# Parameter register

**THE RULE — v2 (standing, non-negotiable).** Never assume you know what a variable or
parameter is. Look it up with **`python3 scripts/ctx/param.py <name>`**, and if it exits 1
**that is a STOP, not a hint** — do not infer it from nearby code; find the owner, get a
primary source or run a dedicated test, add the row, then use it. **Exit 3 is the same STOP
for the opposite reason**: the name IS registered, but its implementation was WITHDRAWN and
no longer exists — see the WITHDRAWN table below. Exit 0 is the only green.

Against every entry there must be a primary source or direct testing evidence for **what it
does** — and, for anything settable, for **how you know a change took effect**. Meaning is
not application. Before running a test that depends on a parameter, name the measurement
that would change if it had applied; if nothing observable would change, the test cannot
produce evidence and should not be run.

A **source comment is a claim, not evidence**, when it describes runtime behaviour: code
states intent, only measurement states behaviour. A hypothesis is not a finding — keep it
OPEN until measured, and retract it where it was recorded.

v2 exists because v1 was followed selectively. Every clause above is here because it was
broken on 2026-08-16, not because it sounds prudent; §12 is the evidence.

This exists because names lie, and on this project they have lied repeatedly with real
cost. In one session (2026-08-15) three separate gauges were read wrong purely because
their names looked self-explanatory:

| gauge | assumed | actually |
|---|---|---|
| `staleRun` | a cumulative count of stale composites | a **run length that resets** — the analyser printed it as a delta, so it read exactly `== compose count` in all 14 phases |
| `paneAge` | age of the displayed pane | queue-residency ms; reported **~27,000 ms** while decoded panes were ~340 ms apart |
| `paired_this` | "a fresh peer frame was spliced" | also true for a **repeat** — an instrument built on it would have reported a hold of 1 forever |

Each survived review because the name sounded right. A name is not evidence.

The next day added four more, so this is a recurring failure and not a bad night:
`missed`/`stalls` on the test pattern (inflated ~30× by a poisoned refresh estimate, §7),
`compose_fps` (excludes catch-up pictures, so it understated the composite by ~29%, §8),
the `cu[plan/take/build]` counters (**refusals**, read as progress — which inverts the
conclusion, §8), and `[COOP-HOLD]` itself, whose unit was wrong three times in a row (§9).
Every one produced a number that looked entirely reasonable.

## How to use this

1. **Before you read a number, look it up here.** Especially before concluding anything
   from it — the three above all produced confident, wrong conclusions.
2. **Before you set a parameter**, check the owner column. If this repo does not own it,
   our comments about it are hearsay.
3. **Before you add one**, add its row with evidence.

    python3 scripts/ctx/param_extract.py --surface all   # what the code actually has
    python3 scripts/ctx/param_check.py                   # diff code vs this register

`param_check.py` fails on any parameter in the code that has no row here. That is what
makes "you will not use one that is not in this register" enforceable rather than a
good intention.

**Coverage — what the check does and does not see.** The generated tables cover five
surfaces the extractor can enumerate: `moonlight.ini` keys, `/tmp` sentinels, `COOP_*`
compile-time constants, `[COOP-*]` diag gauges, and the **test-pattern readout**
(`pattern.html`'s title bar — added 2026-08-16 after that surface cost a session; see §7).
The **host** surface (Apollo config keys) and the **script** surface (shell environment
variables) are curated by hand in §6 only, so a new Apollo key will **not** trip the
checker. Extending the extractor to those is the next piece of work on this register —
until then, treat §6 as the manual half and add to it deliberately.

## Evidence classes

The question that decides the class is **who defines this parameter's meaning**.

| class | means | sufficient evidence |
|---|---|---|
| `SRC` | **We** define it. Our code is the primary source. | file:line of the sites that set/mutate it |
| `DOC` | **Someone else** defines it (Apollo, webOS, moonlight-common-c). Our comments are hearsay. | an upstream citation — their docs, their strings, their source |
| `TEST` | Meaning established by direct measurement on this rig. | the measurement, dated |
| `OPEN` | **No evidence yet. Do not use.** | — |
| `WITHDRAWN` | **The implementation is GONE.** The row is kept for history so the name stays searchable; the thing it names no longer exists in the tree. Do not look for it, do not use it as an example. | the revert (date + commit or section), and what to use instead |

`DOC` and `TEST` can disagree. When they do, **`TEST` wins for this rig** and both stay
recorded — see `minimum_fps_target`, where they do disagree.

## WITHDRAWN — names whose implementation no longer exists

**`param.py <name>` exits 3 on anything in this table, and that is a STOP exactly like
exit 1.** A name here is registered but its code is GONE — reverted, deleted, or never
merged. Without this state a reverted parameter looks identical to a live one: the row is
present, the lookup exits 0, and the rule's cheap check waves it through. That is not
hypothetical. `conv[kr= fb=]` was recommended by two agent charters and by §12 of this
document for **seven days** after the change that created it was reverted, because every
guard the project had said FOUND.

**Add a row here whenever you revert, delete, or abandon something this register names.**
Deleting the row instead is wrong: the name survives in commits, comments, memory files and
charters, so the next reader looks it up, gets NOT REGISTERED, and re-derives it from the
stale mention. Keep the name findable and say it is dead.

This table is parsed by `scripts/ctx/param.py` and `scripts/ctx/param_check.py`. Keep the
name in the first column and in backticks.

| name | withdrawn | why it is gone | use instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| `aurora-launch` / `/tmp/aurora-launch.log` | 2026-10-08 release cleanup | Removed unconditional shared temporary launch-log writes and support-bundle read. Existing legacy file is deliberately not deleted or read by the new implementation. | Existing commons logger; when diagnostic capture is enabled its launch and parsed-selection messages are retained under the selected profile logs directory. |
| `PG_SEARCH_WIN_D` | 2026-10-09, landed working source before build | Fixed1024-decimated-sample search window removed by available-history discovery. Historical §50 value is not current. | coop_pgate.search_win (§81), derived from block/PG_DEC. |
| `output_generation`, `output_submitted`, `output_retries` | 2026-10-09 | Interim worker globals replaced before build by typed output_owner state. Historical §69 names refer to this unbuilt intermediate source. | coop_output.generation/submitted_frames/retries and COOP-AUD.output telemetry (§72). |
| `COOP_AUDIO_DIAL_LONG_AGO`, `sec_last_dial_ms` | 2026-10-09 | Candidate replaces decode-callback one-second redial with a dedicated retained-PCM sender and ACK protocol. Historical measured baseline remains below. | COOP_AUDIO_RETRY_MS and CA4S transport in §71. |

The decay it aimed at is already bounded host-side by the peer's own periodic IDR (`gop_length=300`, ~5 s), which costs nothing because the ENCODER schedules it, whereas a client-forced IDR halved VEGA's throughput (58.6 -> 28.5 fps). | `avcrepeatmax` — the gauge that MEASURES the longest consecutive-repeat run, so the evidence exists before any budget is built. # Curated entries

The generated tables further down cover all 281 parameters. These are the ones that
carry real risk: they have burned us, or they are owned by something other than us.

## 1. Same label, different meaning — the collision list

Eleven labels mean different things depending on which `[COOP-*]` line they appear in.
Reading one for the other is silent and produces a plausible number.

| label | in `[COOP-HH]` | in `[COOP-VT]` / elsewhere |
|---|---|---|
| **`held`** | `coop_hh_heldbroken` — **chain-break freezes** (`session_video.c:3786`, "freeze instead of splicing the hole") | `[COOP-HOLD]`: `coop_hold_run` — **consecutive composites showing the same peer pane** |
| `frames` | — | `coop_vt_frames` (vertical) vs `coop_dump_out_frames` (`[COOP-DUMP]`) |
| `mean` | — | `[COOP-HOLD]` = mean hold **in composites**; `[COOP-JITTER]` = mean delta **in ms** |
| `n` | — | four different backings: `coop_hold_n`, `coop_samples`, `coop_dump_out_frames`, `coop_dump_out_seen` |
| `mended`, `mirrored`, `pocback`, `ready`, `repeated`, `tiles` | `coop_hh_*` | `coop_vt_*` — per-path, never interchangeable |

**`held` is the dangerous one.** `[COOP-HH] held=` counts chain-break freezes and is
usually small; `[COOP-HOLD] held=` is the judder measurement. They are unrelated.

## 2. Clock bases — which timestamps may be compared

`t=` appears in three lines on two different clocks.

| line | expression | base | comparable to |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[COOP-LATE]` | `SDL_GetTicks()/1000` (`session_video.c:2877`) | ms since **SDL init** | `[COOP-HOLD]` |
| `[COOP-HOLD]` | `SDL_GetTicks()/1000` | ms since **SDL init** | `[COOP-LATE]` |
| `[COOP-RATE]` | `coop_now_ns()/1e9` (`session_video.c:1484`) | **`CLOCK_MONOTONIC`** — since boot | neither, without an offset |

`SRC`. **Consequence:** arrival outliers and hold events *can* be correlated directly —
which is exactly the cross-check the co-op investigation needs — but neither can be laid
against `[COOP-RATE]` without first solving for the offset.

## 3. The gauges that were misread

| entry | what it is | ev | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `staleRun` | `coop_stale_run` — **run length, resets on every paired composite**. Not cumulative. | `SRC` | `session_video.c:4735,4737`; shape `count (resettable)` |
| `paneAge` | `now_ms() - coord_q_ms[slot]` — wall-clock ms the pane sat **in the queue** (`gettimeofday`) | `SRC` | stamp `coop_coord.c:204`, difference `coop_coord.c:472` |
| `paired` | `coop_live_paired` — composites that took the splice path, **including repeats** | `SRC` | `session_video.c:4030,4113,4232` |
| `have_peer` | the tiles path's **real** freshness signal — false means this composite re-showed the previous tile | `SRC` | `session_video.c:3808` |
| `recv` | `coord_recv_count` via `coop_coord_received()` — panes **received off the socket** | `SRC` | `coop_coord.c:451` → `:207` |
| `evicted` | `coord_replaced` — panes **dropped from a full queue** (oldest first) | `SRC` | `coop_coord.c:451`ff → `:195` |

**`paneAge` is meaning-established but measurement-contradicted.** The definition is
unambiguous, yet it read ~27,000 ms while panes were ~340 ms apart. Either the queue
genuinely stalled that long, or the gauge is sampled at a moment that does not describe
the pane being shown. Do not quote it until that is resolved.

**`recv` vs `paired` is the ratio every rate test uses**, and they count different
things: panes in, composites out. `recv` far above `paired` is the documented queue-
overrun signature (`session_video.c:3470`).

## 4. `why[1..8]` — catch-up refusal reasons

`[COOP-HH] cu[... why=%u/%u/%u/%u/%u/%u/%u/%u]` is eight separate counters, one per
early-return in the catch-up builder. `SRC`, `session_video.c:779-862`.

| idx | reason |
|---|---|
| 1 | missing input — our AU or the pane is null/empty |
| 2 | no non-VCL header, or it exceeds `out_cap` |
| 3 | peer AU unparseable, or slice 0 missing/empty |
| 4 | peer slice is an **IRAP** (type 16–23) — refuses to build catch-up from a keyframe. **WINDOWS (eeb89fae, issue #27): ALSO counts catch-up STOPPING in front of a queued peer IDR (`coop_coord_next_is_idr`), pane KEPT for the normal path.** Tell them apart: builder refusals also bump `build`, stops do not -- stops = sum(why[1..8]) - build. The builder refusal (pane LOST) is still reachable for an IRAP whose AU is not VPS/SPS/PPS-led (coord flag false), e.g. a CRA |
| 5 | rewritten top slice came out empty (`sn == 0`) |
| 6 | **overloaded**: tiles top-tile empty (`tn == 0`) **or** output would overflow `out_cap` |
| 7 | bottom slice empty (`bn == 0`) |
| 8 | bottom slice would overflow `out_cap` |

**Index 6 cannot distinguish its two causes.** A nonzero `why[6]` does not tell you
which happened; split it before drawing a conclusion from it.

## 5. Latched-at-start parameters

A whole class of bugs: the parameter is read **once**, so changing it on a running
system does nothing while appearing to work. This blinded a full night of visual
testing.

| parameter | latched where | consequence |
|---|---|---|
| capture probe interval (`@repeat N ms` in `/tmp/coop-probe-mv`) | first pass only, `app_webos.c:77-83` | the **command** is re-read each pass, so resolution can change live — the **rate cannot**. Needs an app restart. |
| `/tmp/coop-queue` (queue depth) | once at coord start, `coop_coord.c:226` | deliberate: "the video thread must never `stat()` the filesystem, and a depth that changed mid-session would reorder panes". Clamped `1..COOP_Q_SLOTS`; default 12. |
| `/tmp/coop-vmirror`, `/tmp/coop-hh-tiles` | latched at stream setup, `session.c:380` | arming them mid-session does nothing |
| `/tmp/coop-fps`, `/tmp/coop-fps-peer` (`coop_fps_cap`/`coop_fps_peer`) | once per connection attempt, `session_config_init()` (`session.c:559-608`, function starts `:348`) | editing mid-stream does nothing until the NEXT reconnect; the value in effect for a running session is whatever was on disk when THAT connection was negotiated — see §13 for the full mechanism |
| `/tmp/coop-netcap` (host:port target) | once per stream setup, `coop_netcap_setup()` (`session_video.c:1078-1116`), called from the same setup pass as the dump captures at `session_video.c:2867` | arming/editing it mid-stream does nothing; must be written BEFORE the stream starts, per its own comment at `session_video.c:885` ("read once, at stream start"). §18 covers the whole surface |

`SRC`. **Rule of thumb: if you changed a sentinel and nothing happened, check this list
before concluding the change had no effect.**

## 6. Externally owned — our source is not the primary source

| parameter | owner | ev | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `minimum_fps_target` | Apollo | `DOC`+`TEST` | **DOC:** "The lowest effective FPS a stream can reach. Set 0 for automatic." (Apollo `assets/web/assets/locale/en.json`, `/config/minimum_fps_target_desc`). **TEST:** the configured number is **2× the fps** — `host_encoder.sh:279` sets `FPS_DOUBLING=2`, memory `apollo-minimum-fps-target-half-frames`. **The docs do not mention doubling; the test governs.** |
| `packetsize` | moonlight-common-c | `SRC` | Not named anywhere in `src/` — it rides inside `STREAM_CONFIGURATION`, assigned wholesale at `session.c:353`. The library then **rounds it down to a multiple of 16** (`Connection.c:295`) and subtracts `ENC_VIDEO_HEADER` when encryption is on (`SdpGenerator.c:324-328`). **The negotiated value is not the configured value.** Default 1392. |
| `SSL_CERT_FILE` | OpenSSL | `DOC`+`TEST` | Standard OpenSSL trust-store override, not an Aurora tuning knob. OpenSSL's `SSL_CTX_load_verify_locations(3)` documentation states that it overrides the compiled-in default CA file. The Windows client sets it to the absolute, executable-relative `curl-ca-bundle.crt` only when no readable user/admin value already exists; `build_portable.ps1` copies that file from its explicitly declared, package-manager-verified `mingw-w64-x86_64-ca-certificates` dependency. This is necessary because MSYS2's OpenSSL-backed libcurl otherwise retains an MSYS2-local CA path which is absent on an extracted portable install. **MEASURED 2026-09-23 with the exact MinGW curl/OpenSSL build used for packaging, launched from `%TEMP%`:** an unreadable override failed with curl 77 / HTTP 000; the bundled CA source succeeded against the pinned GameControllerDB HTTPS URL with HTTP 200. **HOW TO VERIFY IT TOOK in the client:** launch the packaged executable from a different working directory with MSYS2 removed from `PATH` and this variable initially unset; the GameControllerDB request must return HTTP 200/304 rather than curl error 77. Removing the bundled file is the negative control and must produce the client's `Portable TLS CA bundle is missing` warning. OpenSSL primary source: https://docs.openssl.org/3.4/man3/SSL_CTX_load_verify_locations/ . |
| `nvenc_preset` | Apollo | `DOC` | 1 = "(fastest, default)" … 7 = "(slowest)" — locale `/config/nvenc_preset_*` |
| `nvenc_spatial_aq` | Apollo | `SRC` | `config.cpp` `bool_f(vars, "nvenc_spatial_aq", video.nv.adaptive_quantization)` — NVENC **spatial adaptive quantisation**: redistributes bits toward low-variance regions. **WHY IT MATTERS HERE:** a smooth gradient is the worst case for flat quantisation, and the user reports blockiness in the pattern's horizontal grey ramp on BOTH hosts' halves. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED — a measurement, not a log line:** count DISTINCT LEVELS across the ramp in a native panel capture and compare with the SOURCE capture of the same pane (`scripts/coop/screenshot.ps1`). Measured 2026-09-01 before enabling: source 350-366 distinct steps, decoded 141-274 — the codec is merging levels. Enabling it should move the decoded count toward the source. NB the panel capture is a JPEG and adds its own blocking, which ADDS steps, so a decoded count BELOW the source is a floor on the real loss, not an artefact of the measurement. |
| `amd_vbaq` | Apollo | `SRC` | `config.cpp` `bool_f(vars, "amd_vbaq", (bool &) video.amd.amd_vbaq)`, passed as the `vbaq` option on both `h264_amf` and `hevc_amf` (`video.cpp`). AMD's equivalent of `nvenc_spatial_aq` (Variance Based Adaptive Quantisation). Same observable as `nvenc_spatial_aq`, measured on the PEER's half. |
| `nvenc_vbv_increase` | Apollo | `SRC` | `config.cpp` `int_between_f(vars, "nvenc_vbv_increase", video.nv.vbv_percentage_increase, {0, 400})` — percentage increase of the NVENC VBV/HRD buffer, 0-400. **WHY IT IS RELEVANT:** both encoders were measured UNDERSPENDING their grant (composite ~30 Mbps of 55 granted, 2026-09-01) and a tight VBV is one mechanism that caps per-frame bits regardless of the target. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** mean/max picture size in the client's `size=` diag field should rise; if it does not move, the VBV was not the limit and the row must not be cited as if it were. UNTESTED as of writing. |
| `fec_percentage` | Apollo | `DOC` | "Percentage of error correcting packets per data packet in each video frame." |
| `nvenc_intra_refresh` | Apollo | `DOC` | "Enable Intra Refresh for some clients to render correctly continuously (e.g. Xbox Client)" |
| `double_refreshrate` | Apollo | `DOC` | "Double the requested refresh rate when creating virtual displays, streamed refresh rate still remain the same." |
| `ensure_active` (`dd_configuration_option` value) | Umbra/Apollo + bundled libdisplaydevice | `DOC` | **WHAT IT DOES (Windows only):** activates the specified display if it is inactive, without deliberately disabling the other displays. Umbra accepts the exact string and maps it to `DevicePreparation::EnsureActive` (`/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build/src/config.cpp:359-370,1194`; `src/display_device.cpp:271-288`); the owner implementation retains the initial topology and appends the target as an extended group only when it is absent (`third-party/libdisplaydevice/src/windows/settings_utils.cpp:194-218`). For a virtual-display application, Umbra replaces `output_name` with the newly created VDD's device id before applying this policy (`src/process.cpp:333-369`), so that VDD is the specified display. **HOW TO VERIFY IT TOOK:** obey §12's Apollo service-start latch, then on a fresh session require `Using the following configuration` to contain `"device_prep":"EnsureActive"` and inspect the `Newly computed display device topology data` log. For a behavioural proof, begin with the target inactive and query `QueryDisplayConfig(QDC_ONLY_ACTIVE_PATHS)` from the interactive Windows session: the target must join the active set while the previously active devices remain. If the target was already active, no topology delta is expected; the dispatch log proves selection, not a behavioural change. A config-file/UI value alone is not proof, and an unrecognised spelling silently maps to `disabled` (`config.cpp:370`). Primary owner documentation: `docs/configuration.md:1017-1061`; log/JSON contract: bundled libdisplaydevice `src/windows/settings_manager_apply.cpp:26-43,157-168` and `src/common/json_serializer.cpp:15,24`. |
| `ensure_only_display` (`dd_configuration_option` value) | Umbra/Apollo + bundled libdisplaydevice | `DOC` | **WHAT IT DOES (Windows only):** activates the specified display if necessary and makes it the only active display. The exact string maps to `DevicePreparation::EnsureOnlyDisplay` at the same Umbra parser/application sites as `ensure_active`; the owner implementation computes `{{target}}` as the active topology (`third-party/libdisplaydevice/src/windows/settings_utils.cpp:194-205`). If no device id was specified it instead keeps the selected primary clone group (`:201-203,252-265`); the virtual-display path does specify the new VDD id before applying the policy (`src/process.cpp:333-369`). **HOW TO VERIFY IT TOOK:** after the §12-required service restart, begin with the target plus at least one other active display, require fresh-session log JSON `"device_prep":"EnsureOnlyDisplay"`, then query `QueryDisplayConfig(QDC_ONLY_ACTIVE_PATHS)` from the interactive Windows session: the active-id set must equal the target (or the documented primary clone group when no id was supplied). Confirm the prior topology returns after normal teardown/revert before calling the test complete. File/UI presence, the computed-topology log alone, and a session-0/SSH display probe are not OS-application evidence; an unrecognised spelling silently maps to `disabled`. Primary owner documentation and logging sources are the same as the `ensure_active` row. |
| `nvenc_constrained` | **ours** (`docs/apollo-gop30.patch`) | `TEST` | **RESOLVED 2026-08-18, and it is LOAD-BEARING — do not remove it.** Its only *signalled* effect is PPS `loop_filter_across_slices` (0 with the key on, 1 with it off); `constrained_intra_pred_flag` is 0 either way. **But that flag is NOT the mechanism:** the composite already sets `loop_filter_across_tiles_enabled_flag = 0` and the seam IS the tile boundary (tiles 1×2, second slice address 4080 = 120×34), so seam deblocking is disabled in BOTH cases — yet `= 0` still shows blocky seam damage on the panel and `= 1` is clean. The working effect is therefore the UNSIGNALLED encode-time prediction restriction (out-of-bounds MVs reading edge padding), which a client cannot reproduce. **NVENC-only.** Owner corrected: absent from Apollo master; our patch adds it. |
| `nvenc_gop_length` | Apollo | `OPEN` | Same: in the binary, not in the locale. |
| `nvenc_twopass` | Apollo | `SRC` | **WHAT IT DOES:** selects NVENC's multi-pass rate-control mode. Accepted values `disabled` \| `quarter_res` \| `full_res`, parsed at `config.cpp:55-67`; an unrecognised value falls back to `quarter_res` **with a warning**. **THE DEFAULT IS `quarter_res`** (`nvenc_config.h:46`, `nvenc_two_pass two_pass = nvenc_two_pass::quarter_resolution;`) and it maps to `NV_ENC_TWO_PASS_QUARTER_RESOLUTION` at `nvenc_base.cpp:242-243`. Our `sunshine.conf` does NOT set the key, so SKYNET runs the default. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED — an unusually clean observable:** `nvenc_base.cpp:474-476` prints the literal ` two-pass` inside the `NvEnc: created encoder ...` log line whenever multiPass != DISABLED, so SKYNET's `sunshine.log` reads `created encoder H.264 P1 async two-pass rfi` today and the word **disappears** when the key is set to `disabled`. Apollo reads `sunshine.conf` ONLY at SERVICE START, so a log line from a session started before the edit proves nothing — restart `ApolloService`, then read the NEW encoder-creation line. **Citations verified against `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build/src`, which is byte-identical to the running binary** (`sunshine.exe` MD5 `7552E097BB13D8E2B121A642894E0714` == the build tree's), Apollo v0.4.6 / commit `0cd32ab`. **PERFORMANCE EFFECT IS UNMEASURED, and this is a mechanism HYPOTHESIS, not a documented remedy:** it is being changed to test whether halving per-frame encoder work stops the 100 ms `NvEnc: frame N encode wait timeout` (hardcoded, `nvenc_base.cpp:590-594`); `upstream-oracle` searched and found **no upstream statement tying two-pass to encode-wait timeouts**. Do not upgrade this row to `TEST` without that measurement. |
| `nvenc_h264_main` | **ours** (Apollo patch, SKYNET's build) | `TEST` | **WHAT IT DOES:** forces NVENC's H.264 encode to **Main** profile instead of High. **WHY IT EXISTS:** mixed-vendor AVC splicing needs the two PPSs to agree, and NVENC High sets `transform_8x8_mode_flag = 1` while AMF omits the PPS extension entirely (= 0). **CORRECTED 2026-09-09 -- the previous sentence here was FALSE.** It read "`coop_avc_compatible()` refuses the pair on that mismatch (`coop_avc.h:17` lists it as unrepairable), so without this key co-op composes nothing at all". It does not, and it cannot: `transform_8x8` is not in `coop_avc_pps_t`, `coop_avc_pps_parse` stops at `redundant_pic_cnt_present_flag` without entering the PPS extension where the flag lives, and `coop_avc_compatible()` (`coop_avc.c:185-213`) compares only width_mbs, height_mbs, poc_type, max_num_ref_frames, entropy_cabac, weighted_pred, weighted_bipred and redundant_present. `grep -rn transform_8x8 src/` returns ONE hit and it is a prose comment. The row's authority was that comment (and it cited the wrong line -- `coop_avc.h:14`, not :17), i.e. a source comment describing runtime behaviour, which register rule 5 forbids. **Consequences of the correction:** (i) the mismatch is NOT refused, it is COMPOSED MALFORMED -- the composite carries the primary's PPS extension byte-for-byte because the PPS branch of `coop_avc_nonvcl_set_rows` deliberately does not re-serialise the tail -- and a silent malformation is far worse than a visible refusal, because `refused_incompatible` stays 0 and `avccomposed` climbs; (ii) there is NO counter for it, so **`avccomposed` climbing does not prove the two vendors' parameter sets agreed**; (iii) a High primary has a SECOND failure mode and we cannot say which you get -- `profile_has_chroma_ext()` is true for profile_idc 100, so a High SPS carrying `seq_scaling_matrix_present_flag` fails the parse at `coop_avc.c:140` and NOTHING composes, uncounted. **The key is still required** -- that rests on the 2026-08-30/31 live mixed-vendor pass which had the patch (`scripts/coop/avc/README.md:325-332`, `transform_8x8_mode_flag` 0 on BOTH), not on this mechanism; no live run has ever been done WITHOUT it for contrast, and the offline experiment that grounds "High breaks it" also carried an ffmpeg-only `max_num_ref_frames` confound (README:71; real Apollo emits 1, matching AMF). **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** not from the conf file — Apollo reads it only at service start. Two primary checks: the key exists in the binary at all (`strings 'C:/Program Files/Apollo/sunshine.exe' | grep nvenc_h264_main` — a STOCK build has no such string, so a stock host silently ignores the line), and the emitted SPS carries `profile_idc = 77` (Main) and the PPS carries `transform_8x8_mode_flag = 0`. **Use `scripts/coop/avc/profile_check.py` on a capture, NOT `avc_hdr.py`** -- corrected 2026-09-09: `avc_hdr.py:parse_pps` stops at `redundant_pic_cnt_present_flag`, which is exactly where the extension carrying that flag begins, so the check this row used to prescribe could never be performed with the tool it named. `profile_check.py` reads both fields and was validated against libx264 high/main/baseline and cross-checked with ffprobe. Measured equal on both hosts 2026-08-30. **VEGA's build is NOT patched with this** and does not need to be — AMF is already Main-compatible on that flag. Evidence: `scripts/coop/avc/README.md:332`. |
| `AV_CODEC_FLAG_LOOP_FILTER` | ffmpeg | `DOC` | **DOC:** ffmpeg's own `AVCodecContext.flags` bit (`libavcodec/avcodec.h`); the `h264_amf` encoder reads it at init — `amfenc_h264.c:203`, `int deblocking_filter = (avctx->flags & AV_CODEC_FLAG_LOOP_FILTER) ? 1 : 0;` — and applies it as a single BOOL property, `amfenc_h264.c:511`, `AMF_ASSIGN_PROPERTY_BOOL(..., AMF_VIDEO_ENCODER_DE_BLOCKING_FILTER, !!deblocking_filter)`. The encoder's own `codec_defaults` table sets `{"flags","+loop"}` (`amfenc_h264.c:578`) — ON is ffmpeg's own default for `h264_amf`. **NVENC never reads this flag** — Apollo's NVENC path never touches `NV_ENC_CONFIG_H264::disableDeblockingFilterIDC` at all, so this flag moves the AMF path only; verified against the encoder source directly, not inferred. **OUR USE:** `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build/src/video.cpp` ~line 1663 sets `ctx->flags = 0;` (Apollo's own "avoid clash on reuse" reset, wiping every ffmpeg default including `+loop`) then re-adds `ctx->flags \|= AV_CODEC_FLAG_LOOP_FILTER;` — restoring what the reset removed, not new behaviour; added 2026-09-02. The comment there also states a measured cause (AMF slices carrying `disable_deblocking_filter_idc=1` with the flag absent) — **treated as a CLAIM here per rule 5, not evidence; the wire-level check is the next row.** **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT:** not a `sunshine.conf` key and not subject to the service-start latch — it is set in Apollo's own encoder-init code path, so it applies on the next AMF encoder init after a binary rebuild+redeploy. Read `disable_deblocking_filter_idc` directly off VEGA's slice headers (next row): flag absent -> every AMF slice idc=1; flag present -> the PPS carries `deblocking_filter_control_present_flag=1` and idc reads 0 before Aurora's own rewrite (below) forces it to 2. |
| `disable_deblocking_filter_idc` | ITU-T H.264 | `DOC`+`SRC` | **DOC:** H.264 clause 7.4.3 (`slice_header()` semantics) — 0 = filter every edge INCLUDING slice boundaries; 1 = filter no edge; 2 = filter every edge EXCEPT slice boundaries. Present only when the PPS's `deblocking_filter_control_present_flag` = 1 (next row); absent, the default is 0 (filter everything). **SRC (our use):** `src/app/stream/video/coop_avc.c:coop_avc_rewrite_slice_impl` (~420-457) reads the field per the SOURCE PPS and writes it per the DESTINATION PPS, forcing `d==0 -> d=2` on every rewritten slice: the co-op seam IS a slice boundary between two independently-encoded panes, so idc=0 would filter each pane's reconstructed edge against the other's — pixels its own encoder never saw — diverging the decoder's reconstruction from the encoder's and feeding the error into later references. Values 1 and 2 are left untouched. **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT:** parse the COMPOSITE's emitted slice headers (not the source pane's) off the wire and confirm every rewritten slice reads idc=2, never idc=0; `coop_avc_compatible()`'s guard (next row) makes idc=0 impossible in the output whenever the destination PPS carries the field at all. |
| `deblocking_filter_control_present_flag` | ITU-T H.264 | `DOC`+`SRC` | **DOC:** H.264 clause 7.4.2.2 (PPS semantics) — 1 = the slice header carries `disable_deblocking_filter_idc` (and, unless idc==1, two offset fields); 0 = it does not, and every slice behaves as idc=0. **SRC (our use):** parsed at `coop_avc.c:177` into `deblock_present` on both SPS/PPS pairs; `coop_avc_compatible()` (`coop_avc.c` ~206) as of 2026-09-02 requires only `pa->deblock_present` — the DESTINATION/primary's PPS — to be true, **not equality** between primary and peer as it read before. **Why:** with AMF's loop filter enabled (`AV_CODEC_FLAG_LOOP_FILTER` row) it stops coding this flag at all (nothing to override — 7.4.3's default is already "filter everything"), while NVENC keeps coding it; requiring equality refused every splice against an AMF encoder with its filter on — measured `refincompat=117, avccomposed=0` before the fix (already-registered COOP-AVC gauges, §COOP-AVC). **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT:** `refincompat` should stop climbing on this specific cause and `avccomposed` climb instead, once VEGA is actually running with the loop filter enabled and a fresh session negotiates against it — a LIVE-TEST observable, not derivable from source alone. |
| `amd_intra_refresh_mb` | **ours** (Apollo patch, mirrors `nvenc_h264_main`/`nvenc_constrained`) | `SRC` | **WHAT IT DOES (mapping, SRC):** `config.cpp:1175` `int_f(vars, "amd_intra_refresh_mb", video.amd.amd_intra_refresh_mb)` reads the key into an `std::optional<int>` (`config.h:80`); `video.cpp:831` maps it onto ffmpeg's own `h264_amf` AVOption `"intra_refresh_mb"` — confirmed against the encoder's own option table, not our comment: `{ "intra_refresh_mb", "Intra Refresh MBs Number Per Slot in Macroblocks", OFFSET(intra_refresh_mb), AV_OPT_TYPE_INT, {.i64=-1}, -1, INT_MAX, VE }` — which the encoder applies as `AMF_ASSIGN_PROPERTY_INT64(..., AMF_VIDEO_ENCODER_INTRA_REFRESH_NUM_MBS_PER_SLOT, ctx->intra_refresh_mb)` only `if (ctx->intra_refresh_mb != -1)`: absent key -> optional empty -> `-1` -> stock behaviour, matching the `video.cpp` comment's own claim (itself a CLAIM per rule 5, not evidence). **NOT WIRED INTO `scripts/coop/host_encoder.sh`:** that script's `KEY_FILTER` (`host_encoder.sh:371`) lists the seven keys it owns and restores on teardown, and `amd_intra_refresh_mb` is not one of them — if it is hand-set in VEGA's `sunshine.conf` (reported live at 1728), this script's restore will NOT remove it. **DO NOT CONFUSE WITH `COOP_INTRA_REFRESH`** (already registered under Shell knobs, `host_encoder.sh:352`) — that shell var only ever writes `nvenc_intra_refresh = 1`; it is NVENC-only and has no AMF/AMD path. **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT — UNVERIFIED, this is the honest state:** whether 1728 on VEGA is live in the RUNNING encoder is not established here. Apollo reads `sunshine.conf` only at service start (§12), and unlike `nvenc_twopass`/`nvenc_intra_refresh` there is no encoder-creation log line naming this property — `amfenc_h264.c` has no `av_log` call anywhere near either the option-table entry or the `AMF_ASSIGN_PROPERTY_INT64` call, so the log-line trick that works for those two does not exist for this one. **TEST DESIGN (do not run — hand to LEAD):** (1) confirm `ApolloService` on VEGA was restarted after the conf write (process start time newer than the file's mtime); (2) either parse a live AMF slice bitstream for the periodic forced-intra-macroblock pattern within otherwise-inter slices (1728 predicts one full sweep of the pane every `ceil(total_mb/1728)` frames), or, cheaper, watch a real co-op run on the panel for whether AMD-side contamination that used to persist indefinitely (`avcconceal` climbing, damage never clearing, per [[coop-seam-mechanism]]) now visibly clears within a bounded number of frames without `avcrefresh` moving. Either result is the proof; the key's presence in the conf file is not. |
| `coop_pad_rows` | **ours** (Apollo patch, `config.h`/`config.cpp`/`process.cpp`) | `SRC` | **WHAT IT DOES:** shrinks the VIRTUAL DISPLAY by `2 * coop_pad_rows` while leaving the ENCODE at the client's requested size, so Apollo's own aspect-preserving centring (`display_vram.cpp`) leaves `coop_pad_rows` BLACK ROWS at the top and bottom of every encoded frame. Applied at `process.cpp` VDD creation AND at both `changeDisplaySettings` call sites — passing `render_height` to those would re-apply the full mode and silently undo the inset. Width is untouched, so the capture fits the encode frame exactly in width and the centring is a pure vertical offset with NO resampling. **WHY:** H.264 edge extension assumes the row above a picture's first row repeats it; at a spliced pane boundary that row is really the OTHER host's pixels, so the assumption is violated every frame. Black padding on both panes makes it TRUE. **Measured 2026-09-05** with the client guard band (which achieves the same padding accidentally, by asking for a taller stream): peer rows 0-8 and 8-16 reproduce inside the composite at **0.088** and **0.011** luma levels. **RETRACTED 2026-09-06:** this row went on to say the peak seam ratio in the first content rows "falls from 1.025 to 0.632" — that number was a bin-alignment artefact in `splice_diff.py` (an 8-row mean compared against a 16-row mean; the same tool bug printed "seam is x0.0" outright on other padded captures, by matching black pad against black pad). Fixed (`d225385f`, `0d204134`) and re-measured like-for-like: **x12.9 unpadded (`gop300`) -> x5.5 padded (`padrows`)** — `coop_pad_rows` roughly HALVES the seam; it does not eliminate it. **WHY NOT THE GUARD BAND:** it makes the COMPOSITE taller than the panel (2240 vs 2160) and the display pillarboxes — measured **64 px and 74 px** of side bars, 3702 of 3840 active. Padding inside the requested height keeps the composite at 2176 and the bars at 0-8 px. **VALIDATION:** refused unless a positive multiple of 16 (an unaligned inset puts content on a half-macroblock boundary) and less than a quarter of the height. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `Co-op seam padding: virtual display WxH inside a WxH encode` in Apollo's log, and the delivered stream's first/last `coop_pad_rows` rows decode as pure black (mean 0.00, min 0, max 0). 0 = stock, byte-identical. **THE PROTECTION IS CONDITIONAL ON A PEER ANCHOR — not previously written down.** The pad is a STATIC BLACK strip, and static content codes as SKIP, which copies from the reference and never self-corrects (the same property that refuted `coop_top_pad`'s black variant, measured drifting 0.00 -> 92-122). The 0.000 bit-exact result was measured WITH `gop_length = 300` on the peer resetting that drift every ~5 s. **A long or absent peer GOP re-opens the seam without changing anything named `pad`** — so `coop_pad_rows` and a bounded peer IDR period are a PAIR, and neither is sufficient alone. **NOT YET VERIFIED LIVE.** **MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE WITH `coop_top_pad`.** That key fills its strip with a LIVE COPY of the content's own top rows, not black. With both set (pad=16, top_pad=16) Apollo's centring gives `offsetY = 24`: composite rows 0-7 black, rows 8-23 a DUPLICATE of content rows 0-15 -- the black-on-black property is destroyed at the top edge, which is the whole mechanism. Never set both. **IT IS GLOBAL -- EVERY VIRTUAL-DISPLAY LAUNCH, NOT ONLY CO-OP (measured 2026-09-29):** VEGA's rig value 16 applied to a plain Desktop session from the Linux test client at 19:51:37 ("Co-op seam padding: virtual display 1920x1048 inside a 1920x1080 encode") -- the "1920x1048 after a single-host stream" noted earlier that day is this, not a virtual-display quirk. A shipped host keeps 0 (`docs/release-defaults.md`); co-op needs it scoped to Co-op Pane launches. |
| `coop_top_pad` | **ours** (Apollo patch, `config.cpp:1115`, `display_vram.cpp`) | `TEST` | **REFUTED 2026-09-05 — implemented, live in both hosts' binaries, and MUST STAY 0.** It reserves a strip at the top of the capture and fills it with a LIVE COPY of the content's own top rows (not black). Measured in three forms and all three made the seam DRAMATICALLY WORSE (strip rows 0-31: control 16.95, `guard=32 + pad=32` 60.17; body 1.34 -> 52.91), because a duplicated region 32 rows away is a PERFECT motion-compensation match, so the encoder is invited to predict the content FROM the strip and import the unprotected edge's damage. A STATIC black variant failed differently: it codes as SKIP, SKIP copies from the reference, and it drifted from 0.00 to 92-122 luma and never recovered. **HAZARD WITH `coop_pad_rows`:** both offset the capture, so with both set `offsetY` becomes 24 and rows 8-23 turn into a DUPLICATE of content rows 0-15 — which destroys the black-on-black property `coop_pad_rows` depends on. **This, not guard+`coop_pad_rows`, is the real double-pad hazard.** Registered here (it was exit-1 NOT REGISTERED while fully implemented, which is itself a STOP) so the next reader finds the refutation instead of the feature. Full data: `scratchpad/run/RUN.md` §AA. |
| `idr_refresh_interval_sec` | **Aurora (client)** | `SRC` | `session_video.c:5028` → `LiRequestIdrFrame()` every `fps × interval` frames; 0 = never. Refreshes **our** host, not the peer (`docs/stack-profile.md:177`). |
| `SS4S_PlayerSetWaitAudioVideoReady` | **ss4s** (upstream `mariotaku/ss4s`, vendored `third_party/ss4s`, `aurora-coop` branch on top) | `SRC`+`TEST` | **WHAT IT DOES (SRC):** declared `include/ss4s/player.h:40`. `src/player.c:49-57` forwards `value` to the active player driver's `SetWaitAudioVideoReady` callback **only when audio and video share one context** (`player->context.video != NULL && player->context.video == player->context.audio`) — true today because webOS's `ndl-webos5` module registers `audio=true video=true` in the SAME module and the same `SS4S_PlayerDriver` struct for both (`ss4s_modules.ini` group `ndl`, weight 50, `os_version = >=5`; `modules/webos/ndl/webos5/ndl_module.c:18` assigns `module->PlayerDriver = &SS4S_NDL_webOS5_PlayerDriver` once, used for both roles), so `SS4S_PlayerOpen`'s `else if (videoPlayerDriver != NULL)` branch (`src/player.c:22-23`) sets `context.video == context.audio`. The NDL webOS5 driver stores the flag as `context->waitAudioVideoReady` (`modules/webos/ndl/webos5/ndl_player.c:67-69`); `LoadMedia` (`ndl_player.c:110-131`) is the enforcement point — when true AND either `mediaInfo.video.type == 0` or `mediaInfo.audio.type == 0`, it logs `"Defer LoadMedia because audio or video has no type"` and returns 0 **without** calling `NDL_DirectMediaLoad`, deferring until whichever of `OpenVideo`/`OpenAudio` runs next completes the pair (`ndl_video.c:192`, `ndl_audio.c:113`, both via `SS4S_NDL_webOS5_ReloadMedia` → `LoadMedia`). **Owner is upstream, not this repo**: introduced by mariotaku/ss4s commit `20096baa` ("added option to start playing when audio and video are ready", 2023-06-11 by the ss4s maintainer), present on `origin/main`; NOT one of the 5 `aurora-coop`-only commits ahead of it (`81bd565 e53431b 8585600 bdf9939 9c1f6b8`). `docs/webos26-media-load.md`'s description of it is therefore citing OUR comment on ss4s's code, and that comment is hearsay per the owner rule — this row is the primary-source citation. Aurora itself calls it `true` exactly once, `src/app/stream/session_worker.c:108`, immediately after `SS4S_PlayerOpen()`, for every session that is not a co-op secondary (secondaries never call `SS4S_PlayerOpen` at all). **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED (TEST):** moonlight-common-c's connection setup opens video before audio, so in this codebase the deferred `NDL_DirectMediaLoad` always lands inside the **`SS4S_PlayerAudioOpen`** window, never `SS4S_PlayerVideoOpen`'s — measured on webOS 26 (11.2.0), `AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG` build: `~/aurora-tv-runs/webos26-20260926/logs/diag-run2-vendor.log:22-29` — `NOTE ARM SS4S_PlayerVideoOpen` / `NOTE DONE SS4S_PlayerVideoOpen -> OK after 0 ms` carries NO NDL Load line (only sets `mediaInfo.video.type`, `audio.type` still 0, guard defers); `NOTE ARM SS4S_PlayerAudioOpen tid=25787 mono_ms 3747019` is followed **at the same `mono_ms`** by `NDL_IMPL_DirectMediaLoad call`. `diag-run3-fixed-armlog.log:7,691-692` corroborates: `DONE SS4S_PlayerAudioOpen -> OK after 656 ms` — the whole vendor Load round-trip is attributed to the Audio open. In a RELEASE build (no vendor log) the same mechanism reads as: `"Starting audio stream..."` (moonlight-common-c's stage message emitted immediately before `SS4S_PlayerAudioOpen` is invoked) is the step that never returns when the deferred load's missing prerequisite never arrives — matches production build `logs/aurora-diag-0928-original-hang.log` (README.txt in that evidence dir). **Module-mismatch caveat, see §12** — the guard silently no-ops whenever audio and video come from different modules (true on non-webOS builds: `alsa`/`pulse`/`sdl` are audio-only in `ss4s_modules.ini`). Not tested with `value=false` in this tree; Aurora never calls it that way, so the "Load fires on VideoOpen instead" branch is a design prediction, not a measurement. |

**One claim explicitly NOT admitted here.** On 2026-08-16 it was reasoned aloud that with
SKYNET drawing 30 fps, `minimum_fps_target`'s floor was padding the encoded stream to ~60
and "that is what the composite tracks", from an observed `compose_fps` of ~60. That
inference is **unsupported**: `compose_fps` excludes catch-up pictures (§8), so it never
measured the emitted rate in the first place. Whether the floor pads a slow host's stream,
and to what, is **`OPEN`** — it needs the host-side encode rate read directly, not inferred
from a client counter.

**Two corrections this surfaced.**

`idr_refresh_interval_sec` is **client-side**, yet `session_video.c:4677` describes it as
"Apollo's default, and what both hosts run". It appears **nowhere** in the installed
Apollo binary (checked ASCII *and* UTF-16, 2026-08-16; Apollo's own idr-related keys are
`idr_interval`, `forced_idr`, `gops_per_idr`). The effect the comment describes is real —
no periodic IDR — but the mechanism is the **client never requesting one**, not the host
declining to emit. Treat any reasoning that depends on the host's IDR policy as
unsupported until re-derived.

`nvenc_constrained` **is now RESOLVED (2026-08-18)** — see §6. The dedicated test was an A/B
bitstream capture off the live host: with the key on, the PPS carries
`loop_filter_across_slices = 0`; with it off, `1`. Nothing else in the PPS moves, and
`constrained_intra_pred_flag` is `0` either way.

**AND A WARNING THAT COST ME A WRONG CONCLUSION THE SAME DAY.** "The only signalled difference"
is not "the only difference", and I briefly recorded it as though it were — concluding the key
was replaceable by a client-side bit flip. It is not. A second A/B on the **composite** (not the
pane) showed `loop_filter_across_tiles_enabled_flag = 0` in *both* cases, and the seam is the tile
boundary, so seam deblocking was already off either way — yet the panel still showed blocky seam
damage at `= 0` and was clean at `= 1`. The effect that matters is therefore encode-time and
unsignalled. **A PPS diff can only ever bound what a key signals; it cannot establish what an
encoder DOES.** Register §12's "fails silently" list is the right frame: this key changes slice
DATA, and slice data is not a flag you can read.

Both keys are also **ours, not Apollo's**:
`docs/apollo-gop30.patch` adds `nvenc_constrained`, `nvenc_gop_length` and `nvenc_slice_rows`,
none of which exist in Apollo master. The register previously listed the owner as Apollo,
which was wrong — verified against `ClassicOldSong/Apollo@master src/config.cpp`.

`nvenc_gop_length` is **inert as shipped** — `host_encoder.sh:44` defaults it to `0`, which the
patch maps to `NVENC_INFINITE_GOPLENGTH` — but it is **NO LONGER UNMEASURED**. Corrected
2026-08-30: it was measured live on SKYNET in BOTH directions and it APPLIES.

| `nvenc_gop_length` | IDRs in 600 delivered pictures | IRAP intervals |
|---|---|---|
| `30` | 21 (`IDR_W_RADL`) | 18, then 30 x 19 |
| `0`  | 2 | pictures 0 and 14, then none in 586 |

**How it was measured** (the method this row's own §6 rule demands, since `AV_PKT_FLAG_KEY`
cannot tell a CRA from an IDR): `scripts/coop/nal_types.py` parses `nal_unit_type` off a
600-access-unit capture taken with the TV's `/tmp/coop-dump` sentinel. Cross-checked against
`ffprobe` on the same files — picture counts and keyframe positions agreed exactly.
**The capture is the HOST'S stream, not the decoder's input**: `coop_dump` is written BEFORE
the splice (`session_video.c:3106`, ":the exact bitstream the host sends"). Do not cite it for
anything about composition — that is the separate `/tmp/coop-dump-out` hook.

Note the two are NOT interchangeable and never were: `nvenc_gop_length` is read only by the
NATIVE NVENC encoder, `gop_length` only by the avcodec path. Both were measured 2026-08-30.

`nvenc_slice_rows` is set by nothing at all and still carries no conclusion.

### `gop_length` — the vendor-neutral sibling (OURS, patch NOT deployed)

| name | surface | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `gop_length` | sunshine.conf | SRC + TEST | **What it does:** sets `ctx->gop_size` AND `ctx->keyint_min` on the **avcodec** encoder path (AMF/QSV/VAAPI/software). Stock Apollo hardcodes both to `INT_MAX` at `video.cpp:1585` (`INT16_MAX` under `LIMITED_GOP_SIZE`) with **no config override of any kind**, so a periodic IDR is unreachable on every non-NVENC encoder. `nvenc_gop_length` cannot substitute: it is read only in `nvenc/nvenc_base.cpp`, the NATIVE encoder, which bypasses avcodec entirely. **How you know it applied:** the IDR interval in the delivered bitstream changes. Do NOT trust `AV_PKT_FLAG_KEY` — it is set for a CRA exactly as for an IDR (measured with ffprobe 2026-08-23). Parse `nal_unit_type`: 19/20 are IDR, 21 is CRA. |

**MEASURED 2026-08-23 on real AMD hardware** (VEGA box: Radeon RX Vega, driver `31.0.12027.9001`,
ffmpeg `N-126242` `hevc_amf`). AMF honours `gop_size` exactly and emits **true IDRs**:

| `-g` | IDR pictures | interval | IDR NAL |
|---|---|---|---|
| 15 | 0,15,30,45,60,75,90,105 | 15 | `IDR_N_LP` |
| 30 | 0,30,60,90 | 30 | `IDR_N_LP` |
| 120 | 0 | (one GOP) | `IDR_N_LP` |

Zero CRA and zero RASL in any AMF run, so AMD needs **no open-GOP flag**. `libx265` is the one
that does: at `-g 30` it puts RAPs on period but emits `CRA_NUT` + RASL leading pictures — an
OPEN GOP that does not reset the reference chain — unless `open-gop=0` is also set. Apollo's
libx265 entry additionally pins `x265-params keyint=-1`, an infinite GOP that silently overrides
`ctx->gop_size`, so the software path needs both fixed.

**STATUS: NOT deployed on SKYNET/PC2** — both still run the STOCK binary (md5
`9e364658ecfca24d8fd4035856701246`); the patch exists only in the `apollo-build` working tree
(`config.h`, `config.cpp`, `video.cpp`) and as a built `build/sunshine.exe` for those two hosts.
Nothing reads this key on SKYNET/PC2 today — do not cite it as live behaviour there until it is
installed and re-measured through Apollo rather than through ffmpeg directly.

**STALE for VEGA as of 2026-08-29**: `gop_length`, `open-gop`, `nvenc_gop_length` and
`keyint_min` are all present as strings in VEGA's live `C:\Program Files\Apollo\sunshine.exe`,
and VEGA's `sunshine.conf` carries `gop_length=30` after a cold boot (config read at service
start). A string in the binary is not proof the value is applied on that path (clause 3,
meaning is not application) — the decisive open question (whether VEGA emits any IDR after
session start, i.e. whether `gop_length=30` actually takes effect on its AMF path) is
UNMEASURED; see `coop-amd-tier2-mcts.md` / `session-handoff-2026-08-29-amdpeer.md` for the
run that raised it. Do not read "both hosts run the STOCK binary" above as covering VEGA.

## 7. Test-pattern readout — `missed` and `stalls` are NOT dropped frames

Read off the panel in every test. Both are derived from an **estimated** refresh interval,
and on a loaded host that estimate is wrong, so both inflate. Measured 2026-08-16 on a
90 s S1 baseline.

| field | what it actually is | ev |
|---|---|---|
| `dt` | interval between **rAF callbacks**, drawn *or gated* — deliberately not between draws, so a `const:30` scenario doesn't report thousands of drops | `SRC` `pattern.html:514` |
| `vsync` | **the window's SHORTEST callback interval**, published as a rate. Not a measured refresh. | `SRC` `pattern.html:532` |
| `missed` | `round(dt / vsync) - 1`, summed — skipped vsyncs *against that estimate* | `SRC` `pattern.html:537` |
| `stalls` | count of frames where that quantity reached **≥ 4** | `SRC` `pattern.html:538` |
| `worst` | max raw `dt`. **No estimate involved — trust this one.** | `SRC` `pattern.html:533` |
| `draw` | frames actually drawn per second. **Trust this one.** | `SRC` `pattern.html:355` |

**Measured falsification (`TEST`, 2026-08-16).** SKYNET reported `missed=5786 stalls=614`
on a run where:

- `worst` was **17.1 ms**, and a stall needs ≥4 skipped vsyncs ≈ **≥24 ms**. No single
  frame in 90 s ever qualified.
- `vsync` published **185.2 fps on a 165 Hz panel** — impossible as a refresh rate, which
  is the tell that the estimate is poisoned by short callbacks.
- the *real* callback deficit was 162.9/s against 165 expected ≈ **2/s**, while `missed`
  claimed **63/s** — over-reporting ~**30×**.
- `draw` held 164.8 fps; 614 genuine ≥24 ms freezes would mean the pane was frozen ~16%
  of the time.
- PC2, with the **same** window `min/max dt`, read `missed=8 stalls=0`. The difference is
  host load: SKYNET also runs Apollo's encoder, WSL2 and the tooling.

**Corroboration (`TEST`, 2026-08-16).** A pattern page captured 2 s after a phase reload
read `missed 0 stalls 0 worst 6.6 ms` on the same host that ended a 90 s phase at
`missed 5786 stalls 614`. The inflation **accumulates as the estimate gets poisoned**; it
is not present from the start, which is why a short sanity check will not show it.

**The file's own claim is now falsified.** `pattern.html:529` states that `stalls`
"survived both bugs (a 1-frame miscount never reaches the >=4 threshold), which is why it
was the trustworthy number". 614 stalls alongside a 17.1 ms worst frame cannot both be
true — a sufficiently poisoned `vsync` reaches the ≥4 threshold on ordinary frames. **Read
`draw` and `worst`; treat `missed` and `stalls` as unreliable on a loaded host.**

## 8. Catch-up — and why `compose_fps` understates the composite

The composite rate is designed to be `max(primary, peer)`, not `primary`
(`docs/coop-rate-decoupling.md`). When the peer is ahead, extra pictures are emitted
pairing the peer's next pane against a **concealed primary tile**. That doc says "built but
NOT wired in" (2026-08-03); it **has since been wired into the tiles path** and is live —
memory recording "catch-up still OFF in tiles" is stale.

| entry | what it is | ev |
|---|---|---|
| `catchup` | `coop_vt_catchup` — catch-up pictures fed **straight to the player** via `SS4S_PlayerVideoFeed`. They do **not** increment `paired` and do **not** pass through `coop_hh_done`. | `SRC` `session_video.c:3667` |
| `cu[gate=]` | `coop_hh_cu_gate` — gate reached with backlog present. **Progress.** | `SRC` `session_video.c:3643` |
| `cu[plan=]` | sequencer did **not** say `TAKE_NEXT` — **a REFUSAL** | `SRC` `session_video.c:3645` |
| `cu[take=]` | take gave nothing, or the verdict refused — **a REFUSAL** | `SRC` `session_video.c:3656` |
| `cu[build=]` | `coop_hh_build_catchup()` refused — **a REFUSAL** | `SRC` `session_video.c:3662` |
| `cu[qmax=]` | `coop_hh_cu_qmax` — LEVEL, deepest queue seen | `SRC` |

**`plan`/`take`/`build` count FAILURES, not work done.** `plan=0 take=0 build=7` means
catch-up almost never refused — it is *working*. Reading them as progress counters inverts
the conclusion completely, and did.

**Measured asymmetry (`TEST`, 2026-08-16).** `catchup` was **10,646** on a run where the
peer led (S1/S5) and **589** where the peer trailed (S4/S10) — catch-up is active only
when the peer is ahead, exactly as designed. That is why a hold measurement is complete in
the peer-slower condition and incomplete in the peer-faster one.

**Consequence for every rate number.** `compose_fps` is `d_paired/dt`, so it counts only
paired composites and **excludes catch-up pictures entirely**. Measured over one run:
paired 26,398 + catch-up 10,646 = **37,044 pictures emitted** against **35,546 peer panes
received** — essentially one picture per peer pane. `compose_fps` understated the real
emission rate by **~29%**, and a `ratio` built from it is wrong by the same factor. When
the peer is ahead, **add `catchup` before drawing any conclusion about composite rate.**

## 9. `[COOP-HOLD]` — the unit, and how to check it

Hold-run length is the direct measure of judder: how many consecutive composites showed
the same peer tile. No other counter can express it (`paired`, `concealed`, `repeated`
count *pictures*, so a frame held twice and one held six times each count once).

**Unit: emitted paired composites.** The instrument got this wrong three times, and every
wrong version produced a plausible number:

1. Placed in the **v5 splice path** — which the primary never runs in tiles mode. Zero
   lines through a visibly stuttering session.
2. Keyed off **peer-AU availability** — which catch-up re-enters several times per
   composite. Reported 900 holds against 600 composites: a hold shorter than one
   composite. The symptom was a rigidly even distribution (2 and 4, never 3 or 5).
3. Anchored at `coop_hh_done` but **ungated** — early-exit pictures reach that label
   without emitting. Holds covered 87.9 units/s against 44.1 composites/s, i.e. every hold
   reported at ~2× its true length.

Now gated on `hh_paired && hh_fresh_peer`, giving the invariant:

> **sum(hold lengths) == `paired`** (modulo a pre-bootstrap offset of ~300, before the
> first real splice). Both are printed on the same line — check it in the first histogram,
> not with a stopwatch.

| entry | what it is | ev |
|---|---|---|
| `hh_fresh_peer` / `fresh_pane_this` | the peer pane actually **changed** — set only at the real-splice site | `SRC` `session_video.c:4203, 4634` |
| `hh_paired` / `paired_this` | **a composite was emitted** — the conceal and repeat paths set these too, so they are *not* freshness | `SRC` `session_video.c:3999, 4082, 4202` (+ the repeat path at `4682`) |
| `[COOP-HOLD] held=` | run length in composites | `SRC` |
| `[COOP-HOLD] hh` / `v5` | which composition path emitted the line | `SRC` |

**Validated (`TEST`, 2026-08-16).** Against S10's deliberately planted stall
(`stall:165+300@5` — freeze 300 ms every 5 s), the instrument recovered the cadence
without being told about it: long-hold events arrived in bursts separated by
**4.73-4.74 s**, worst hold **41 composites ≈ 252 ms** against the planted 300 ms. In S4
it showed holds clustering at 4-6 composites (correct for ~39 panes/s against 163
composites) with a tail of **~34 excursions to 22-43 composites = 135-264 ms**. Recovering
a known planted periodicity is the strongest evidence this gauge has.

**Known remaining limitation:** the instrument counts only paired composites, so it does
not see catch-up pictures — which are exactly the ones that refresh the peer tile when the
peer is ahead. The measurement is therefore only complete when the peer is **slower** than
the composite, which is the judder condition and where catch-up is inactive by definition.

## 10. Tiles-path conceal — measured, and it contradicts a source reading

| entry | what it counts | ev |
|---|---|---|
| `repeated` | `coop_hh_repeated` — composites that **re-showed the peer's last inter slice** (the residual-doubling path) | `SRC` `session_video.c` |
| `skipconceal` | `coop_hh_skipconceal` — composites concealed with the **bit-exact all-skip slice** | `SRC` |
| `irapconceal` | `coop_hh_irapconceal` — unpaired IRAP concealed instead of emitted | `SRC` |
| `canchor` | `coop_hh_conceal_anchors` — re-anchors forced by `COOP_HH_CONCEAL_BUDGET`, *not* a chain break | `SRC` |
| `held` (`[COOP-HH]`) | `coop_hh_heldbroken` — **chain-break freezes**, not hold length (see §1) | `SRC` `session_video.c:3786` |

**`TEST` 2026-08-16, S4+S10 horizontal tiles, 35,998 composites:**

    repeated=0   skipconceal=14780   irapconceal=6   canchor=44   held=303

**This contradicts a source-derived hypothesis that was close to being accepted.** On
2026-08-15 it was concluded from reading the code that the tiles path consults
`coop_skipconceal_enabled` only for a broken chain and one IRAP case, so the ordinary
"peer produced nothing this composite" case falls through to **repeating the peer's last
inter slice**, applying its residual twice — proposed as the cause of the bottom-pane
artefacts. Measured: **`repeated` is 0** and the skip concealer fired on **41% of
composites**. The tiles path conceals; it does not repeat.

The user independently reported seeing **no artefacts** on that run, and the peer pane
image was clean. Counters, image and observer agree — so the artefact mechanism is
**`OPEN` again**, and the earlier reasoning must not be cited as established.

*Reading source is not measuring.* This is the same failure as the rest of this register,
one level up: an unverified claim about what code does, rather than what a name means.

## 11. Conceal A/B — measured, and it validates the RETRACTED mechanism's description

`coop_skip_conceal_h` (config key, `[input]` section) is the real switch. **`/tmp/coop-noskipconceal`
is NOT** — `coop_tunables.c:65` rewrites that sentinel from the config at every session start, so
arming it by hand does nothing and the run silently keeps the old behaviour. Measured 2026-08-16:
a hand-touched sentinel produced a run with `skipconceal=16821`, i.e. no change at all.

| | conceal ON | conceal OFF |
|---|---|---|
| `skipconceal` | 16,821 | **0** |
| `repeated` | 0 | **7,689** (62% of composites) |
| observer | 1-frame flicker at each refresh | **severe artefacts across the whole peer pane** |

**What this settles.** The concealer is load-bearing: without it 62% of composites replay the
peer's last inter slice and the pane degrades badly. So the 2026-08-15 mechanism was **right about
what repeating does** and **wrong that repeating is what happens** (§10 stays retracted; its
*description* is now validated by direct test).

**CORRECTED 2026-08-16 -- the transition attribution above was WRONG.** The flicker rate was
inferred from the ORDER of three phase calls, never measured. Asked directly, the user reports
the flash comes **"every couple of seconds"**. Measured rates: the conceal->fresh transition runs
**32.6-59.4/s** (~75x too fast), while `converted` -- peer IDRs rewritten by
`coop_slice_idr_to_trail()` -- runs a steady **0.43-0.49/s, one every ~2.1-2.3s, flat across
phases**. Only the conversion rate matches. An ordering that is consistent with a hypothesis is
not a measurement of it.

**The suspected defect:** the converted peer IDR carries an EMPTY RPS and rides as slice 1 in a
picture whose slice 0 (ours) declares a reference. HEVC wants the RPS identical in every slice
header of a picture, so the decoder may take DPB state from either -- and the empty one evicts
the previous picture. There is a documented precedent: `nvenc_gop_length=30` (2026-08-02) made
this path fire constantly and the user saw "PC2's whole pane FLASH every few seconds".

**HYPOTHESIS FALSIFIED 2026-08-16.** A safe form of the RPS fix was built (empty-RPS before
bootstrap, keepref after, with fallback) and instrumented with `conv[kr= fb=]` so "no effect" and
"never fired" could be told apart. Measured: **`conv[kr=52 fb=0]`** -- keepref fired for every
conversion, zero fallbacks -- and **the flicker was still there**. The RPS mismatch is real but is
NOT the cause. Reverted; the tree and the TV are back on shipped behaviour.

**A FIX THAT DOES NOT WORK -- do not retry it.** Switching that call to
`coop_slice_idr_to_trail_keepref()` **breaks the bootstrap and the session comes up BLACK**
(every picture suppressed, "before the FIRST composite", no video after 3 launch attempts).
`keepref` is documented "use this when the converted IDR is slice 0"; in tiles the peer's slice
becomes slice **1**, so the empty RPS there is deliberate and decoder-tested. The two
requirements genuinely conflict -- slice 1 needs to be self-contained, slice 0 needs its
reference -- so this cannot be fixed by swapping one call.

## 12. Parameters that apply SILENTLY OR NOT AT ALL -- and how to prove they took

**THE MISSING COLUMN.** Every entry above says what a parameter MEANS. On 2026-08-16 that
was never the problem: five separate tests were void because a parameter was set, verified
present, and **never reached the thing being tested**. A register that only records meaning
cannot prevent that. So for these, record HOW YOU PROVE IT APPLIED.

| parameter | how it fails silently | HOW TO VERIFY IT TOOK |
|---|---|---|
| `/tmp/coop-noskipconceal` and the other mode sentinels | `coop_tunables.c:65` **rewrites them from config at every session start**, so a hand-`touch`ed sentinel is erased seconds later and the run keeps the old behaviour | set the CONFIG key (`coop_skip_conceal_h`), then check `skipconceal`/`repeated` in `[COOP-HH]` actually inverted |
| `nvenc_intra_refresh`, and every Apollo key | **Apollo reads `sunshine.conf` only at SERVICE START.** `host_encoder.sh apply` writes the file and reports success while the running encoder keeps what it loaded hours ago | restart `ApolloService` on BOTH hosts, then verify BEHAVIOURALLY -- for intra refresh, `converted` must fall from its ~53-per-15,000-composites baseline. **`amd_intra_refresh_mb` (§6) is the same trap with NO log-line escape hatch** — `nvenc_twopass`/`nvenc_intra_refresh` at least print into the encoder-creation log line; the AMF path has no equivalent `av_log` call at all, so a conf value cannot be confirmed live without either a bitstream parse or a panel-visible behavioural change (test design in §6's row — hand to LEAD, do not run solo) |
| `COOP_HH_FLOOR` / `minimum_fps_target` | the config number changed on both hosts (60 -> 200, verified) and **the delivered rate did not move** (peer_fps 60.8-61.3 vs ~63) | compare `peer_fps` in `[COOP-RATE]` before and after; the file is not the measurement |
| `COOP_PRIMARY` / which host is primary | the UI writes `coop_primary_uuid`; **`run_coop.sh` DEFAULTS to `COOP_PRIMARY=pc1`** (`run_coop.sh:31`, `case "${COOP_PRIMARY:-pc1}"`) and the caller may override with `COOP_PRIMARY=pc2`, so the two paths can disagree and a scripted matrix may test the opposite roles. It is caller-controlled, NOT forced -- read `COOP_PRIMARY` from the environment of the run you are judging | read the pane labels off the panel -- `pattern.html` prints SKYNET/PC2 in each pane with its scenario |
| any client code change | the build can deploy and the path still never execute | a counter on the changed path, added per change. A LIVE example of the pattern is `converted` / `coop_hh_converted` (`src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:478`, emitted on `[COOP-HH]`). **`conv[kr= fb=]` is WITHDRAWN — see the WITHDRAWN table; do not look for it.** This row recommended it for a week after the revert, which is why that table exists |
| `coop_fps_peer_h`/`_v` (`/tmp/coop-fps-peer`) | **only read inside the block gated by `/tmp/coop-fps` being present** (`session.c:559`, then `:584-594`) — with `coop_fps_cap[o] <= 0` (the shipped default, "free-running both hosts") the whole block is skipped, so the peer sentinel can be written to disk, present, correctly formatted, and **never read at all** | first confirm `/tmp/coop-fps` (the CAP sentinel, not the peer one) exists for the session under test; then verify with the SECONDARY process's own `[COOP] fps cap: requesting %dfps from this host` diag line (`session.c:604`), not the peer file's presence — full mechanism at §13 |
| the 2026-09-02 netcap IRAP-keep fix (`session_video.c:989`, §18) | it only changes what happens to an AU **already sitting ready in the ring** at the moment `coop_netcap_worker()` starts draining it — if no IRAP happened to be resident then (e.g. the connect to the receiver was fast, or the stream's own next organic IRAP arrived right after), the fix has NOTHING to keep and behaves identically to the old unconditional clear. Two of the three recordings on disk (`run7/`, `g0det/`, both PRE-fix) already read `composite_skipped_pre_irap: 0` for this reason — the fix's absence was not exposed in either, so their being clean is not evidence the old code was fine, only that this particular run didn't hit the race | the observable is `composite_skipped_pre_irap` in a capture's `meta.json` (§18) staying low **on a run that would previously have hit the race** — i.e. a slow/late receiver connect, or a long infinite-GOP stretch with few IRAPs, which is exactly the `burst/` shape (`composite_skipped_pre_irap: 9004`, PRE-fix). A clean `run7`/`g0det`-style run proves nothing about the fix either way. No POST-fix capture exists yet (§18) — this row stays OPEN until one is taken under conditions that would have triggered the old bug. |
| `drop`/`err` (`coop_netcap_drop`/`coop_netcap_err`, §18) | a lossy or truncated capture still produces a complete, decodable `meta.json` and `composite.h264` up to the failure — nothing about the FILE says AUs were dropped mid-run unless the `[COOP-AVC] nc[...]` diag line from the SAME session is also read and cross-checked | pull `nc[sent=%u drop=%u err=%u]` from the TV's own diag log for the session under test and confirm `drop=0 err=0` before trusting a capture as complete; `err>0` additionally means the worker stopped early, so also check the recording's AU count/`stop_reason` against the expected run length |
| `AURORA_VARIANT`, `WEBOS_APPINFO_ID`, `AURORA_TMP_SUFFIX` and derived log/socket paths (§22) | Values select compiled/package identity; changing environment or installing does not replace an already-running process. A harness reading the other variant's log can look healthy. | Verify package metadata, effective compile definitions and running executable identity; close/relaunch the selected app and observe fresh session lines in its selected log. `APP_TITLE` only changes the shell banner. |
| `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` (§24) | Compiled ON is inert without a matching client profile; having a file or a matching binary string still does not show override application. | Fresh per-key apply diagnostics for the actual client, teardown restore diagnostics, plus behavioral verification of the encoder settings and a following live-client session with defaults restored. |
| `aurora_gpu_hevc` / `COOP_GPU_HEVC` / `plan.gpu_hevc` (§28) | The gate is 100% vendor detection — `experimental_gpu_hevc` is hardcoded `true`, so `plan.gpu_hevc` reduces to `plan.vendors_known` (BOTH hosts) plus at least one AMD. §25's OWN existing row already documents `EncoderVendor`/`CoopProfileCaps` reading "unknown" for any host polled before pairing, indistinguishable from a stock host that will never answer. So a session can have the feature "on" by every setting that exists, both hosts genuinely AMD/NVIDIA, and still silently take the native/core HEVC path with zero error, because vendor polling timing — not the co-op or GPU-HEVC settings themselves — decided it | `session_worker.c:56-61`'s `[COOP-ENCODER] ... encoder=gpu-hevc-beta\|native-hevc` line, checked on BOTH the primary's and secondary's log for the ACTUAL session under test — not the settings pane, which can show correct GPU names from a stale poll that happened before the vendor fields went unknown |
| `host_uuid` / `host_app_id` (`ares-launch -p` payload, §30) | Both are LENGTH/TYPE-validated only, never validated against reality: a 36-character `host_uuid` that matches no currently-paired host, or a `host_app_id` that matches no id in the loaded app list, is accepted by the parser exactly as if it were correct, then simply fails its match loop later (`launcher.controller.c:513-527`/`:281-298` for the uuid, `apps.controller.c:587-603` for the app id) — no error dialog, no diag line, no exit code. The SAME silent shape covers "typo'd the value" and "value was once correct but the host re-paired / app list changed since" — indistinguishable from outside. `host_app_id`'s auto-launch attempt is additionally ONE-SHOT (`def_app_launched` latches true before the search even runs), so it does not retry if the app list was still loading | on the TV: the app you asked for must actually be on screen, full-screen, not the launcher grid. In the logs: `/tmp/aurora-diag.log` must show `[COOP-ENCODER] role=primary ... host=<the exact host_uuid you sent>` (or, for a non-co-op payload, any post-launch diag line at all) — its total ABSENCE after a launch attempt is the signature of a silent host_uuid/host_app_id mismatch, not a hang |
| `coop` (`ares-launch -p` payload, §30) | Not merely ignored when its precondition fails — ACTIVELY CLEARED. `apps.controller.c:756-765`: `coop:true` only survives if `def_app_from_payload` is also true (i.e. `host_app_id` resolved to a real auto-launch, the row above); if not, `app_launch_set_coop_requested(false)` and `coop_session_clear()` run explicitly, and the stream proceeds as an ordinary SOLO launch with no error — so a broken `host_uuid`/`host_app_id` pair silently downgrades a co-op request into a working solo one, which reads as "it started fine" to anyone not checking for a second pane | `/tmp/coop_secondary.log` (or `/tmp/coop_secondary-beta.log` on the beta variant, §22) must show a FRESH `RUN START ... role=COOP-SECONDARY` for this session's timestamp, plus `[COOP-ENCODER] role=secondary`. Checking only the PRIMARY's own `aurora-diag.log` cannot distinguish an honoured co-op request from a silently-downgraded one — both show a normal-looking solo-shaped stream from that side alone |
| `COOP_PI_ROUTE_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` (Compile-time constants) | Defined independently in TWO files, `src/app/ui/settings/panes/coop.pane.c:65` and `src/app/ui/launcher/coop.dialog.c:76`, with **no shared header** — the classic "two code paths disagree" shape. Editing one site's `250` does not touch the other, and nothing detects or logs a divergence: both compile clean, both link clean, and the settings pane's pad-routing action would silently wait a different length of time than the launcher dialog's for what is otherwise the identical `coop_pi_pads_route()` call | grep BOTH definition sites after any edit and confirm they still match — do not assume one file's value implies the other's. There is no runtime signal that distinguishes "both changed" from "only one changed"; the only observable is the wait before the hub-route error dialog appears, and it must be timed from BOTH entry points (settings pane AND launcher dialog), not just the one that was edited |
| `SS4S_PlayerSetWaitAudioVideoReady` (§6) | The setter's own guard (`src/player.c:51`) requires `player->context.video == player->context.audio` — true only when ONE module supplies both audio and video (webOS's `ndl-webos5` today). On any build/platform where they differ (`ss4s_modules.ini`: `alsa`/`pulse`/`sdl` are audio-only, paired with a separate video module — e.g. non-webOS targets), the call still runs, still takes the mutex, and silently returns having done NOTHING to either driver — `true` is indistinguishable from a no-op from the caller's side, and Aurora's one call site (`session_worker.c:108`) checks no return value (the function is `void`) | `library.c:55,67` logs `"Opened driver: %s"` once for the `Audio` tag and once for the `Video` tag at driver-open time — if those two names differ for the session under test, the setter is a guaranteed no-op regardless of the value passed; if they match (webOS `webOS NDL`/`webOS NDL`), fall back to the TEST proof in §6's own row (vendor-log Load placement) |
| `sec_frame_size` / negotiated Opus duration (§54) | Installed release `06fe12b1` stores/checks the negotiated duration but passes 960-frame capacity to the secondary decoder, including NULL loss callbacks. New local source routes through `coop_audio_decode_opus()` and uses negotiated duration for NULL; deployment remains unverified. A correct init value alone never proves which argument ran. | Instrument the NULL/zero callback argument and returned/forwarded frames. Linux real-codec regression confirms the local helper preserves the specified lost duration; it does not identify the cause of October8 queue growth or prove the installed app contains the fix. |
| `coop_audio_depth_ms` / `/tmp/coop-audio-depth` (§51) | Per-orientation configuration rewrites the sentinel; coordinator reads depth only at start, accepting 5..400 ms. A changed file or wrong orientation can therefore leave the running buffer unchanged. | Reconnect; check the selected orientation and fresh coordinator `Nms jitter buffer` line, then actual queue depth and audio/visual timing. |
| `PGATE_PRED` / `PGATE_BALANCE` (§50) | Environment overrides only exist in builds with `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING`; otherwise `read_env()` is empty. They are read when a gate is created, not continuously. | Use a fresh gate in a verified tuning build; for prediction observe hook reason 3/counters on qualifying onsets, for balance observe `balance_applied` plus actual per-stream output gains after sufficient matches. Exported variables or an unchanged estimate do not prove application. |
| `ENET_NO_INSTALL` (§43) | It is consumed while CMake configures the vendored ENet subdirectory, not while CPack writes the IPK. A stale configure/install manifest or staging tree can therefore retain `lib/static/libenet.a` and the public headers even after the parent source says ON. With CMP0077 NEW (set by this project's CMake 3.19 policy level), a parent normal variable can also be the effective ON value while an older `CMakeCache.txt` entry still reads OFF, so the cache is not application evidence. | Reconfigure and package, then inspect both `build/webos-easy_build/install_manifest.txt` and the IPK's `data.tar.gz` listing: neither may list `lib/static/libenet.a` nor `include/enet/`. Separately, the final executable link command must still name `core/moonlight-common-c/enet/libenet.a`; absence from the package is not evidence that the statically linked code disappeared. |

| `coop_vt_skip_tile` (Compile-time constants) | On webOS/TV builds (the `#else` size, 256 KiB, never widened) an oversized peer/pane slice makes the re-tile call refuse and the whole catch-up/conceal build return 0 — but the ONLY counters that could show this (`cubig`/`cudrop`) are declared and incremented **entirely inside `#ifdef _WIN32`** (`session_video.c:488-491`, `:4524-4526`), so on the TV this exact failure has no counter, no log line, and no other observable at all. It would present only as whatever the caller's OWN fallback does (a held/frozen tile, or a rise in `missing`/`conceal` with no attributable cause) | **OPEN — not measured on webOS.** Test design (hand to LEAD; do not run solo): drive a genuinely large single slice through a real TV co-op vertical session (high-motion/high-bitrate content, the same shape that produced 12/247 oversize slices on Windows at 2560x1440) and watch for a tile freezing or `missing`/`conceal` climbing on `[COOP-VT]` with `cubig`/`cudrop` **absent from the line entirely** (proving the webOS build, not Windows-only instrumentation) at the same moment the panel shows damage. A clean run proves nothing (the same non-hit gap `cubig`'s own row documents for Windows before 09-23) — only a run that actually produces an oversize slice and a simultaneous visible fault would confirm this path. |
| `BUILD_VERSION` / `BRANCH` / `COMMIT` (Umbra configure env, §48) | **Two silent ways to get `0.0.0.<commit>`**: `BUILD_VERSION=... cmake` without `BRANCH` is ignored with no message (`build_version.cmake:20` needs both DEFINED), and none of the three is cached, so a later configure -- including ninja's automatic re-run -- without the env re-versions the build | read the ARTEFACT, not the command: `sunshine.log` `Umbra version: <v> commit: <sha>` (an empty `commit:` = `COMMIT` was missing), `Uninstall\Apollo` DisplayVersion, `sunshine.exe` FileVersion; and the configure output line `Got from CI '<BRANCH>' branch and version '<BUILD_VERSION>'` (`:24`) must be present |
