| `SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER_NAME` / `_WEBSITE` / `_ISSUE_URL` (Umbra CMake cache, §48) | an existing build dir's `CMakeCache.txt` entry **wins over an edited default** in `cmake/prep/options.cmake`: the dir had cached `SudoMaker` and the binary kept logging it. The same cache already holds `.../Umbra/issues`, not the file's `.../umbra/issues` | configure with `-D...`; then the startup log lines `Package Publisher:` / `Publisher Website:` / `Get support:` (`entry_handler.cpp:105-107`) and `grep SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER build/CMakeCache.txt` |
| `notify_pre_releases` / the Umbra home-page card (§48) | **two code paths disagree about its value form (source-derived, OPEN)**: the web UI saves `enabled`, which `to_bool` and Apollo's truthy card read as ON but `UmbraCard.vue:113` (`true`/`'true'` only) reads as OFF; the C++ field is read by nothing | `curl -sk -u USER:PASS https://HOST:47990/api/config` shows the raw string; the card honours it only if the browser's Network tab shows `.../Umbra/releases?per_page=10` rather than `.../releases/latest` (test design in §48) |
| `UMBRA_GPU_HEVC_DIR` (Umbra CMake cache, §47/§48) | left empty it is a configure WARNING only: the package builds, installs, and has no GPU HEVC encoder (currently the case); a host that keeps its hand-copied `gpu-hevc\` still works, so nothing flags the gap until a fresh install | `find <staging>/application -name gpu-hevc` after `cpack`; on the host `UmbraCaps` bit 3 (§47) |

**`rfi` IS A DECOY.** Apollo's `created encoder HEVC P1 async 10-bit two-pass rfi` reads
**identically with `nvenc_intra_refresh` on and off** -- `rfi` is REFERENCE FRAME
INVALIDATION, a different feature. Apollo does implement intra refresh
(`enableIntraRefresh`, `intraRefreshPeriod`, `intraRefreshCnt` are all in `sunshine.exe`),
it simply never reports it. Do not read that line as confirmation.

**`Couldn't find audio sink {0.0.0...}` is not why a session DROPPED**: it appears ~12 ms AFTER
`CLIENT DISCONNECTED` in Apollo's log. But it is not harmless noise either (corrected 2026-09-29):
it is stock Apollo FAILING to put the host's default playback device back -- the device was absent
at teardown (a monitor's HDMI audio while its panel is off) -- and the PC stayed on Steam Streaming
Speakers. Umbra now retries and restores by name: §40.

**The rule this adds:** before running a test that depends on a parameter, ask *what
measurement would change if this had applied* -- and if the answer is "nothing I can see",
the test cannot produce evidence and should not be run.

## 13. Known resolution gaps in the generated tables

The extractor cannot follow these; the meaning is confirmed by hand and recorded here.

| row | why it reads OPEN | actual |
|---|---|---|
| `packetsize` | never named in `src/` | rides inside `STREAM_CONFIGURATION` — see §6 |
| `coop_pad1..4_pane` | ini key built with `sprintf`, storage is an array | `coop_pad_pane[]`, read at `coop_input.c:76`, written at `coop.dialog.c:1152` |
| `known_pad` | same | `known_pads[]`, `app_settings.c:281` |
| `why` | array element with a variable index | `coop_hh_cu_why[1..8]` — see §4 |
| `coop_fps_cap_h` / `coop_fps_cap_v` | ini key built by the `COOP_WRITE`/`COOP_READ_INT` macro (`app_settings.c:259-264` write, `:342-376` read) — the literal key string is assembled at compile time from `"coop_" #field sfx`, never spelled out anywhere, so the extractor's key-name grep never sees it | writes/reads `config->coop_fps_cap[o]` (`app_settings.h:156`) → sentinel `/tmp/coop-fps` (`coop_tunables.c:54`) → consumed in `session_config_init()` (`session.c:559-608`) — full mechanism below |
| `coop_fps_peer_h` / `coop_fps_peer_v` | same macro blind spot | writes/reads `config->coop_fps_peer[o]` (`app_settings.h:157`) → sentinel `/tmp/coop-fps-peer` (`coop_tunables.c:55`) → consumed in `session.c:584-594` **only inside the block gated by the `coop_fps_cap` sentinel being present** — see §12 |
| `coop_bitrate_h` / `coop_bitrate_v` | same macro blind spot as `fps_cap`/`fps_peer` above (write `app_settings.c:281` `COOP_WRITE(bitrate, ini_write_int)`; read `app_settings.c:396` `COOP_READ_INT(bitrate)`) | **RESOLVED below.** Writes/reads `config->coop_bitrate[o]` (`app_settings.h:193`; default `0` = "leave the built-in stream bitrate alone", set in `settings_coop_advanced_defaults()` at `:504`) → read DIRECTLY by `coop_tunables_bitrate()` (`coop_tunables.c:145-152`) — NO `/tmp` sentinel round-trip, unlike `fps_cap`/`fps_peer` — → applied in `session_config_init()` (`session.c:439,442-444`) as `config->stream.bitrate` when `> 0`. Full mechanism, the live value, and the proof gap in the paragraph below this table. |
| every other per-orientation `coop_*_h`/`coop_*_v` key (`slices`, `queue_depth`, `audio_depth_ms`, `bitrate`, `fps`, `height`, `skip_conceal`, `catchup`, `repeat_conceal`, `repeat_pane`, `measure`, `pump`, `self_mirror`) | same macro (`app_settings.c:262-276` write, `:375-389` read) | **NOT resolved by this pass.** Confirmed `param.py` returns NOT REGISTERED for every one spot-checked (`coop_fps_h`, `coop_bitrate_h`, `coop_slices_h`, `coop_queue_depth_h`, `coop_height_h`). The whole `COOP_WRITE`/`COOP_READ_INT`/`COOP_READ_BOOL` macro family shares this extractor blind spot — treat any of them as NOT FOUND until someone does this same walk for that specific name; do not assume registering `fps_cap`/`fps_peer` covers the rest. **`bitrate` (`coop_bitrate_h`/`coop_bitrate_v`) is now ALSO resolved** — see the row above and its paragraph below this table; the remaining names in this list (`slices`, `queue_depth`, `audio_depth_ms`, `fps`, `height`, `skip_conceal`, `catchup`, `repeat_conceal`, `repeat_pane`, `measure`, `pump`, `self_mirror`) are still NOT resolved. |
| `/tmp/coop-spawn` (the EARLIER of its two readers) | the generated Sentinels-table row only sees `access()`/`fopen()` calls on the literal path string; this reader instead hands the literal to a FUNCTION (`coop_session_prepare_scripted(server, "/tmp/coop-spawn")`, `session.c:45`), so the extractor's pattern never matches the call site that actually owns the file | `coop_session_prepare_scripted()` (`src/app/stream/video/coop_session.c:83-118`; `fopen` `:91`, `fscanf "%63s %d"` `:96`) runs FIRST, inside `session_create()` (`session.c:44-49`), before the stream connects — a missing or malformed file here fails the WHOLE launch (`streaming_errmsg = "Invalid co-op peer configuration"`), not just co-op. The generated row's reader (`session_video.c:3331-3346`, at decoder setup, much later) is a SECOND, historical entry point kept for the dialog-driven UI path. **CODE-TRACED (control-flow guarantee, not live-measured): every `return true` inside `coop_session_prepare_scripted()` has already called `coop_session_set()`, so `coop_session_get()` is non-NULL by construction for any process that reached `session_video.c:3331` via the `session.c:44` gate — meaning that function's own `fopen("/tmp/coop-spawn", "r")` fallback at `:3341` is unreachable for a launch-payload-driven co-op session and fires only for the dialog path, which doesn't touch this file at all.** See §30 for the `ares-launch` payload key (`coop`) that arms this whole path. |

**`coop_fps_cap[_h/_v]` and `coop_fps_peer[_h/_v]` — the mechanism, evidence `SRC` (Aurora's request) + `DOC` (whether the host obeys it).**

Full chain: ini key (write `app_settings.c:262-263`, read via suffix logic `:342-359` and `COOP_READ_INT` at `:375-376`) → `config->coop_fps_cap[o]` / `config->coop_fps_peer[o]` (`app_settings.h:156-157`; default `0` = "both hosts free-running", the documented REQUIREMENT, `app_settings.c:483-484`) → sentinels `/tmp/coop-fps` / `/tmp/coop-fps-peer` written by `coop_tunables_apply()` (`coop_tunables.c:54-55`, called on every settings-screen change and on arm) → read back in `session_config_init()` (`session.c:559-608`), which runs **once per session/connection attempt** (function starts `session.c:348`), separately for the primary and the secondary (peer) host connection.

**This is NOT a client-side consumption cap.** `session.c` writes the resolved value into `config->stream.fps` and `config->stream.clientRefreshRateX100`, which flow unchanged into the `STREAM_CONFIGURATION` moonlight-common-c hands to `GS_CLIENT`/`LiStartConnection` (`session_worker.c:34-45`; client built at `:50-52`) — the same field a solo stream's already-registered `net_fps` uses. That is a genuine per-connection REQUEST sent to THAT host at launch, the same field already established to reach and affect the host encoder (memory `coop-165hz-session`, `apollo-minimum-fps-target-half-frames`). Whether Apollo's encoder treats the number as a hard ceiling or a soft target is Apollo's to define (`DOC`, not re-measured in this pass) — same caveat this register already carries for `minimum_fps_target` (§6).

**Primary and secondary get DIFFERENT numbers, and they CAN be set independently — this answers the "host rate vs client cap" question directly: it changes what is requested of each HOST, not what the client consumes.**
- `/tmp/coop-fps` ABSENT (`coop_fps_cap[o] <= 0`, the default) → the whole block `session.c:559-608` is skipped for BOTH sessions → both hosts free-run uncapped, which is the standing requirement (`[[coop-two-free-running-monitors]]`). This is the state a fresh conf ships in.
- `/tmp/coop-fps` present → PRIMARY session clamps `stream.fps` to `coop_fps_cap[o]` directly (`session.c:596-597`).
- SECONDARY session (`coop_secondary_mode() != NULL`, `session.c:584`) computes `peer_fps = coop_fps_cap[o] - 5` (floor 8) UNLESS `/tmp/coop-fps-peer` is ALSO present, in which case it uses that value directly (`session.c:585-594`) — and that becomes the secondary's own clamp, independent of the primary's.
- So `coop_fps_cap_h=X` + `coop_fps_peer_h=Y` (matching orientation) gives the primary a clamp of X and the secondary an INDEPENDENT clamp of Y: a genuine two-different-encoder-rates request, suitable for the rate matrix. **But arming either sentinel is a deliberate DEVIATION from the free-running default** — `session.c:536-558`'s own comment calls the cap "opt-in... restores the old shared-rate behaviour... for an A/B", not the design the mixed-vendor pass ran under. State it explicitly if armed.

**How you know a change took effect:**
- `/tmp/aurora-diag.log`'s `[COOP] fps cap: requesting %dfps from this host` (`session.c:602-606`) is written ONLY when the clamp actually lowered `stream.fps` — its ABSENCE does not prove the sentinel failed to apply, it can mean the negotiated rate was already ≤ the cap. Read the number, not just presence.
- `session_worker.c:34-45`'s `Stream mode WxHxfps, clientRefreshRateX100=...` line is what THIS process actually handed to `LiStartConnection` — the primary and secondary are separate processes with separate logs; check each one's own line for its own value.
- **Neither line proves the HOST's encoder obeyed the request.** That needs a host-side observable — composite/pattern step rate, or `avccomposed`/sec per pane — exactly the trap the existing `COOP_HH_FLOOR`/`minimum_fps_target` §12 row already documents: the config number moving is not the delivered rate moving.
- **Latched per §5:** read once per `session_config_init()` call, i.e. once per connection attempt. Editing a sentinel mid-stream does nothing until the next reconnect.

**`coop_bitrate_h`/`coop_bitrate_v` — the mechanism, evidence `SRC` (the override) + `TEST` (today's live value).**

Full chain: ini key (write `app_settings.c:281`, suffix logic `:270-271`; read via the same suffix logic `:358-375` and `COOP_READ_INT` at `:396`) → `config->coop_bitrate[o]` (`app_settings.h:193`; index `o` is the orientation, `COOP_ORIENTATION_HORIZONTAL` / `_VERTICAL`, `app_settings.h:217-218`) → `coop_tunables_bitrate()` (`coop_tunables.c:145-152`) resolves `o` via `coop_orientation_resolved()` (see the `COOP_ORIENTATION_ENV`/`COOP_ORIENTATION_SEL` row, §25) → `session_config_init()` reads it at `session.c:439` and, when `> 0`, overwrites `config->stream.bitrate` at `:442-444` — the SAME field an ordinary solo stream's `bitrate` ini key sets (`app_settings.c:213,574-575`), so this is a genuine per-connection kbps REQUEST to `LiStartConnection`, not a client-side display cap.

**NOT the same shape as `coop_fps_cap`/`coop_fps_peer`.** No `/tmp` sentinel is involved — `coop_tunables_apply()` (`coop_tunables.c:39-90`) never touches `coop_bitrate[o]` at all; `coop_tunables_bitrate()` reads the in-process `app_configuration` struct directly, so the value that reaches `session_config_init()` is whatever the ini currently holds, not a snapshot taken at the last `coop_tunables_apply()` call. **And there is no peer-specific column**: `session_config_init()` runs this override, UNGATED, for both the primary and the secondary role — the mode-arm call above it is the only part of that function gated to primary-only (`session.c:427`) — so both hosts are asked for the SAME `coop_bitrate[o]` for a given orientation, with no way to request different bitrates for the two co-op hosts the way the fps pair allows.

**UNSET NOW MEANS HALF, from 2026-10-02 (`feat/coop-seamless`).** With `coop_bitrate[o] == 0`, `session_config_init()` sets each pane to `stream.bitrate / 2` instead of copying the Video bitrate, so both PCs together use what one ordinary stream would (user requirement: co-op settings derive from the main ones and stay overridable). A value `> 0` still overrides exactly as above. **Proof it applied:** the `[COOP-ENCODER]` line in `aurora-diag.log` (primary) and `coop_secondary.log` now prints `bitrate=`, and the host log prints the requested `bw.maximumBitrateKbps` -- with the override unset both must be half the Video bitrate.

**Live value, measured 2026-09-18** (`ssh -p 9922 -i "$HOME/.ssh/aurora_tv" ... 'grep -i coop_bitrate .../conf/moonlight.ini'`): the TV currently carries `coop_bitrate_h = 90000` and `coop_bitrate_v = 90000` — both orientations request 90 Mbps, well above `settings_optimal_bitrate()`'s own resolution-based default (`app_settings.c:524`), a deliberate co-op override rather than an accidentally-inherited default.

**How you know a change took effect:**
- **CORRECTION 2026-09-28 (TEST, Windows client log):** the current build DOES log the requested value client-side: `[Session] I Stream mode 1280x1440x165, bitrate 100000 kbps` (seen in every co-op session's aurora-diag.log that day); pair it with the host's `Client Requested bitrate is [N kbps]`. The bullet below predates that line.
- **No log line exists for this override** — unlike `fps_cap`'s `[COOP] fps cap: requesting %dfps` (`session.c:602-606`), there is no equivalent `[COOP] bitrate:` line anywhere in the current tree (confirmed by grep), and `session_worker.c`'s `Stream mode WxHxfps, clientRefreshRateX100=...` line does not print bitrate either. **The only way to confirm the value reached `LiStartConnection` today is to read `config->stream.bitrate` under a debugger or a temporary log**, or infer it indirectly from the HOST's own acknowledged bitrate (Apollo's `bitrate_kbps=` in `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] opened`, §28, for a GPU-HEVC session) — which proves Apollo's own resolved value, not that the client's specific request survived unchanged.
- **Latched per §5, same as `fps_cap`:** read once per `session_config_init()` call, i.e. once per connection attempt, separately for each role. Editing the ini mid-stream does nothing until the next reconnect.
- **This proof is weaker than `fps_cap`'s.** If this value needs to be load-bearing in a future test, add the missing client-side log line first (mirror `session.c:602-606`) rather than trusting silence as confirmation.

---

## 14. Divergence detectors — `[COOP-REF]`, DETECT-ONLY

Added 2026-08-16 after removing `COOP_HH_CONCEAL_BUDGET` (§10). That removal fixed the
flicker and also removed the only thing periodically re-intra'ing the peer tile, so an error
entering by a route the depacketizer cannot see now persists — observed: the bottom tile
corrupted across most-but-not-all of the pane and stayed, with every existing counter flat.

**Nothing acts on these.** They are counters plus a log line. A detector that fired wrongly
would re-create the storm the budget caused, so it earns a trigger only after being measured
against a real corruption.

| entry | what it means | ev |
|---|---|---|
| `refbad` | `coop_hh_refbad` — peer inter slices naming a reference the composite **cannot honour**. Only delta −1 is safe: it is the one case where the previous composite picture holds that pane's previous frame bit-exactly (a skip conceal reproduces it exactly, §10/§11). While a pane trails, the composite emits several pictures per pane, so delta −2 is *not* the pane's previous-but-one. | `SRC` `coop_poc.c coop_slice_refcheck` |
| `used` | `di.first_neg_used` — `used_by_curr_pic_s0_flag[0]`, H.265 §7.4.8. 0 means the nearest negative reference is kept alive but **not used by this picture**, so the delta below does not describe the actual prediction. | `SRC` H.265 §7.4.8 |
| `l0active` | `di.ref_l0_active` — `num_ref_idx_l0_active` (PPS default, or the slice's override). **>1 means the slice MAY reach past delta −1**, which the composite cannot map. Checked separately from the RPS because an RPS may legitimately keep extra pictures alive while only entry 0 is used — testing `num_negative_pics` instead would fire on every healthy slice. | `SRC` H.265 §7.4.7.1 |
| `unresolved` | `di.rps_unresolved` — the delta could not be derived (inter-predicted `st_ref_pic_set`, or an SPS set other than 0). Reported as suspect rather than silently passing. NVENC declares exactly one set. | `SRC` |
| `ppocjump` | `coop_hh_ppocjump` — the peer's **own coded POC** stepped by other than its established step, modulo `1 << log2_max_poc_lsb`. **Distinct from `peergap`**, which watches moonlight's `frameNumber` = Apollo's `frame_nr` (incremented per `encode()` call, `video.cpp`); the coded POC comes from NVENC's picture ordering. The two can diverge, and if they do the pane skipped a picture while `frameNumber` stayed continuous. | `SRC` + `TEST` |

**`TEST` 2026-08-16, `test_coop_poc` against `tests/coop/skynet-4070-reference.h265`
(1,435,824 bytes, a real host capture):** `log2_max_poc_lsb=8` — so POC wraps at 256 and the
modulo in the step check is required, not defensive — and *"POC advances by 1 per picture"*
across the capture, which is the step `ppocjump` learns. Same run also proves the parser
change that added these fields is non-destructive: *"rewrite to the same POC is
byte-identical"* and *"slice payload bytes are unchanged by the rewrite"* both PASS.

**Read-sequence proof:** the capture was added by recording values `skip_st_ref_pic_set()` and
`slice_header_end_bit_x()` already read. Extracting every `bits_u/ue/se` call from both
versions and diffing gives **identical sequences (32 reads)** — so bit consumption, and
therefore every splice path that depends on the header walk, is unchanged.

## 15. Agent (CTM-USBIP) env overrides — C++ `getenv`, invisible to the extractor

The extractor reads shell/ini/C surfaces listed in §13; a `getenv` in the agent's C++ is
not one of them, so these read NOT REGISTERED and must be curated by hand.

| name | default | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CTM_USBIP_PORT` | `kDefaultUsbipPort` (3240) | SRC + TEST | `CTM-USBIP/src/app/agent.inl` in `run_agent()`: `getenv("CTM_USBIP_PORT")` → `atoi` → `g_agent_usbip_server->start(agentUsbipPort, ...)`. **TESTING ONLY** — production leaves it unset. |

**What it does:** moves the USB/IP listener off the fixed 3240 so a SECOND agent instance
can run while the live service holds 3240. Without it the second instance binds its agent
port, fails `CtmUsbipServer::start`, closes the sockets and returns 4 — it looks like
"the agent silently does nothing".

**How I know it took effect (2026-08-21, measured):** the observable is whether a second
instance STAYS UP and accepts a TCP query on its agent port. Unset → process exits, nothing
listening on 48064. With `CTM_USBIP_PORT=3250` → process stayed up, `Get-NetTCPConnection
-LocalPort 48064 -State Listen` returned a listener (pid 15736), and STATUS/DISCOVER both
answered. That is the pair of results the parameter changes.

**TRAP — a background test can put a MODAL DIALOG on the user's screen.** Running
`ctm-usbip.exe` from `out\x64\Release` fails with "The code execution cannot proceed
because avcodec-62.dll was not found" as a **Windows error dialog**, not on stdout: the
redirected log was EMPTY and no port was bound, which reads exactly like a silent exit.
The ffmpeg DLLs (`avcodec-62`, `avformat-62`, `avutil-60`, `swresample-6`, `swscale-9`,
`avdevice-62`, `avfilter-11`) live next to the INSTALLED exe in `C:\Program Files\CTM
Bridge\`. Copy the exe + those DLLs into a scratch dir and run it THERE. This happened on
2026-08-21 and the user saw the dialog over their game.

---

# Generated tables

Regenerate with `python3 scripts/ctx/param_seed.py`. These rows are derived from the
source every time, so they cannot go stale; the curated sections above are what carries
the meaning.

## 27. `[COOP-AGE]` — the DURATION trace, and the feed fault injection (2026-09-12)

Built for passover 07 §3 (Astra), whose first request was: *"Track uninterrupted hold duration in
milliseconds, not only counts."* Every pre-existing co-op gauge is a COUNT, and the reported
symptom is measured in seconds — so a peer half that stops for 40 ms and one that stops for
twenty seconds were indistinguishable in the log. **STATUS: BUILT, NOT YET RUN.** No value below
has been observed in a live session; these rows describe what the code emits, not a finding.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `peer_ms[last= mean= max= n=]` | ours / SRC | `[COOP-AGE]`, AVC path only. Milliseconds between a peer pane's COMPLETE arrival in the coordinator (`coord_q_ms`, stamped in `coop_coord.c`) and the composite take that consumed it. `last` is the most recent, `mean` is over `n` takes since session start, `max` is the worst seen. Sourced by the new `coop_coord_take_next_aged()`; `coop_coord_take_latest()` always reported this age, `take_next()` never did, which is why the AVC path was blind to it. | Non-zero `n` proves the path ran. **TRAP: this is arrival→TAKE, not arrival→display.** It measures the wait for a primary decode-unit callback to come and collect the pane (passover 07 §2B), and says nothing about what happens after the feed. A large `max` with a small `mean` is a stall, not a backlog. |
| `hold_ms[open= max=]` | ours / SRC | How long the peer half has been HELD (concealed rather than refreshed): `open` is the currently running hold, 0 when the peer is fresh; `max` is the longest completed hold this session. Opened where `coop_avc_heldbroken++`, closed when a fresh pane is taken. | `open` climbing across consecutive flushes IS the peer half standing still. **TRAP: distinct from `[COOP-HOLD] held=`**, which is a run length in COMPOSITES on the HEVC paths (§1, §9). Same idea, different unit, different codec path; do not compare their numbers. |
| `broke_ms[open= max=]` | ours / SRC | Uninterrupted duration of a broken-chain episode — opened wherever `coop_avc_chain_broken` is set true, closed when a peer IDR re-anchors it. Passover 07 §2A's deciding measurement. | A break that resolves in tens of ms and one that lasts seconds are the same event in every existing counter; only this separates them. **TRAP: `max` is per session and never decays**, so one early episode dominates it for the rest of the run. |
| `recov[req= anchor=]` | ours / SRC | Milliseconds since the last peer-IDR request was **admitted locally** (`av.request_idr_keep`) and since the last usable anchor was **received**. | **The pair is the measurement, not either number.** `req` small while `anchor` grows = requests are being admitted and not answered, which is exactly the gap passover 07 §2A identifies: the existing latch checks `coop_coord_idr_sent()`, which counts local ADMISSION and establishes neither delivery nor host handling. |
| `/tmp/coop-feed-fail` | ours / SRC | Diagnostic sentinel, `"<site> <every-nth>"` where site is `ord`/`vtcu`/`avccu`/`hhcu`. Fakes the RESULT of every Nth feed at that site while still feeding the decoder, to exercise F2's premise, which has never occurred naturally (0 rejections in 1,131 catch-up feeds). Armed per session; logs `[FEED-FAULT] ARMED`. | **TRAP, and Astra flagged it: this is NOT a reproduction of rejected work** — the decoder still receives the picture, so only the accounting path is exercised. A clean run under injection does not prove the real failure is harmless. Remove the file after any run. |

**Not a heartbeat.** `[COOP-AGE]` prints from the feed path, so if the primary stops delivering,
the line stops with it and a stall shows only as a GAP in flush timestamps. Passover 07 §3.2 asks
for a heartbeat that cannot be censored by the stall it is meant to report; that needs a timer
thread and is **not built**.

## 26. Host-dependent settings — the AUTO policy and `hid_passthrough` (2026-09-11)

**STANDING POLICY, set by the user 2026-09-11.** A setting that cannot work without software
the user may not have installed on their PC is **off by default, carries a note naming what it
needs, and turns itself on when that software is detected**. Aurora must work against stock
Apollo or stock Sunshine; anything else is an accelerator the app notices, never a prerequisite
it assumes. Implemented in `src/app/backend/aurora_host_caps.{h,c}`.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `hid_passthrough` | ours / SRC | **CHANGED 2026-09-11 from a bool to a TRI-STATE.** The ini key still reads `true`/`false` for an explicit choice, and anything else (including the new default `auto`) is AUTO. Backed by `app_settings.h` `hid_passthrough_mode` (`HID_PT_AUTO`/`_OFF`/`_ON`), resolved ONCE per session in `session_config_init()` into the plain bool `session_config_t.hid_passthrough` that the rest of the code reads. AUTO — and an explicit ON — both require the host agent to answer on `hid_passthrough_port`. | `commons_log_warn("Session", ...)` fires when ON was set but nothing answered. The visible proof is the overlay's HID Devices button, which now follows `session_hid_passthrough_active()` rather than the setting. **TRAP, and it is why this changed: enabling passthrough tells the host we have NO gamepads** (`session_worker.c:62` passes a mask of 0), so against a PC without the agent it does not degrade the feature, it removes the controllers entirely, silently. |
| `HID_PT_AUTO` / `HID_PT_OFF` / `HID_PT_ON` | ours / SRC | The tri-state values, `app_settings.h`. AUTO is 0 so a zeroed config is the safe state. | Not observable on its own; see the row above. |
| `aurora_agent_reachable()` | ours / SRC | Non-blocking TCP connect probe (`aurora_host_caps.c`), used to answer "is the host agent running". 300 ms, once per session and once per settings-pane build. Reads `SO_ERROR` after `select()`, because a REFUSED connection also wakes select — without that check "the machine is up" would read as "the agent is there". | The settings row prints what was found, naming the host. **TRAP: it answers "something is listening", not "the right thing is listening"** — it is a presence check, not a handshake. |
| `COOP_COMPAT_MAX_FPS` | ours / TEST | 115. The rate above which the COMPATIBILITY-MODE composite shows a BLACK PANEL. Applied in `session_config_init()` to `config->stream.fps` when the resolved mode is COMPAT, and deliberately NOT applied over an explicit `coop_fps_*` dial. **MEASURED 2026-09-10** by sweep at 3840x2176 mixed co-op, wired path pinned, capture tee off: 60/75/100/110/115 all render; 120 is black. | The session log line `co-op compatibility mode: capping N fps to 115` fires when it bites, and `=== SESSION n ===` then reads `fps=115`. **TRAP: the failure it prevents is INVISIBLE IN EVERY COUNTER** — at 165 the feed is accepted, `errors=0`, `avccomposed` climbs, and the panel is black (observed 2026-09-11). Never conclude a co-op rate is fine from counters. **SCOPE: measured on the AVC composite only** — same-vendor HEVC co-op has run at 165 (2026-08-04), so the cap deliberately does not touch the core path. |
| `gpu` (hosts.ini) | ours / SRC | Per-host GPU make as STATED BY THE USER: `nvidia`, `amd`, `intel`, `other`. Written as a word by `known_hosts.c`, parsed with `coop_gpu_vendor_parse()`, held in `pclist_t.gpu_vendor`, set by `pcmanager_set_host_gpu()` which saves hosts.ini immediately. Absent = never answered, which is what makes the co-op launch screen ask. | The co-op popup asks once per PC and Settings > Co-op > Graphics cards shows the stored answer. **TRAP: a host that REPORTS its encoder overrules this** (`plan_for_pair` checks `encoderVendorKnown` first), so a wrong answer becomes invisible rather than wrong the day the Aurora Apollo build is installed on that PC — do not read this key as "what co-op used". |
| `aurora_apollo_on_host()` / `aurora_apollo_anywhere()` | ours / SRC | "Is this host running the Aurora Apollo build" — true when it emits `EncoderVendor` or a non-zero `CoopProfileCaps`, which only our fork does. | Both settings panes print the result, naming the host. **TRAP: false also means "not polled yet"**, so it may never be read as "the user has the wrong host". |

## 25. Co-op codec selection — vendor detection and `coop_stream_mode` (2026-09-11)

**STATUS: the client half is BUILT AND UNPROVEN; the host half is NOT APPLIED.** Nothing
in this section has been measured end to end yet. The Apollo change that publishes the
two serverinfo fields exists only as `apollo-encoder-vendor-2026-09-11.patch` in this
repo — it is not applied to `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build`, not built, and not
deployed to SKYNET or VEGA. Until it is, **every host answers "unknown"** and the client
takes the core path exactly as it does today. Read every row below as the design
contract, not as an observation.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coop_stream_mode` | ours / SRC | `moonlight.ini` key, int: 0 `COOP_STREAM_AUTO`, 1 `COOP_STREAM_CORE`, 2 `COOP_STREAM_COMPAT`. Declared `src/app/app_settings.h`, clamped on load in `app_settings.c` (out-of-range falls back to AUTO). Consumed by `coop_stream_plan()` (`src/app/stream/video/coop_stream_mode.c`) as the OVERRIDE, and by `coop_stream_mode_resolved()` as the last fallback. AUTO is only ever an input. | The setting alone proves nothing: it is an input to a plan that also depends on what both hosts report. The observable is the codec actually negotiated — `=== SESSION n ===` in the TV diag carries `codec=H264` vs `codec=H265 10bit`. **TRAP: this is a LATCHED-AT-LAUNCH value** (§5). The dialog resolves it once per launch; editing the setting mid-session does nothing, and the secondary never reads it at all when the primary has published (below). |
| `COOP_STREAM_MODE` | ours / SRC | Environment variable, the RESOLVED mode (1 or 2) handed from the primary to the co-op secondary. Written by `coop_stream_mode_publish()` (`coop_session.c`, constant `COOP_STREAM_MODE_ENV`) inside `start_stream()` BEFORE the secondary is spawned; read by `coop_stream_mode_resolved()` in the child. Same mechanism as `COOP_PIN_AUTOENTRY`. | Both processes log their own `=== SESSION n ===` line with a `codec=`; the proof is that the PRIMARY and SECONDARY lines agree. **TRAP, and it is the reason this variable exists:** the secondary is a separate process with its own globals, so it cannot see `coop_session_get()`. The identical gap let it re-arm the ORIENTATION from a stale ini and shred the picture (`session.c:395`). A secondary that falls through to the ini can disagree with the primary about the codec, which is not a degraded picture but a splice that cannot happen. |
| `COOP_PROFILE_CAP_MAIN_CONTROL` (0x1), `COOP_PROFILE_CAP_MAIN_ON` (0x2) | Apollo fork / DOC | Bits of the `CoopProfileCaps` serverinfo field. Bit 0 = this Apollo build HAS the H.264 Main-profile control; bit 1 = it is currently on. Defined client-side in `src/app/stream/video/coop_stream_mode.h`, produced host-side by the unapplied patch from `config::video.nv.h264_main_profile` (`apollo-build/src/config.cpp:1132` → `nvenc_base.cpp:341`). Bit 0 distinguishes our fork from stock: stock Apollo master has NO H.264 profile selector (verified in `git show master:src/config.cpp` — only `nvenc_h264_cavlc`). | Client-side, the bits only reach a warning string. Host-side the applied setting is provable from Apollo's own log: a session created with it on logs `created encoder H.264 ...` after `NvEnc: ...`, and the profile itself is provable only in the BITSTREAM (SPS `profile_idc`, PPS `transform_8x8_mode_flag`). **TRAP: the caps bit says the control EXISTS, not that this session used it.** Bit 1 is read at serverinfo time, and Apollo reads `sunshine.conf` only at service start (§12) — a hand-edited conf that has not been reloaded will report the OLD value. |
| `EncoderVendor` | Apollo fork / DOC | Serverinfo field, one token: `nvidia`, `amd`, `intel`, `software`, `apple`, `unknown`. Derived from `chosen_encoder->name` (the probe's own choice — `apollo-build/src/video.cpp:1078`, set by `probe_encoders()` at `main.cpp:324`), NOT from the DXGI adapter, because a box can hold two adapters and it is the ENCODER that emits the spliced bitstream. Parsed into `SERVER_DATA.encoderVendor` by `core/libgamestream/src/client.c`; mapped by `coop_gpu_vendor_parse()`. **HTTPS + paired only**, like `SessionLocked`. | Absence is indistinguishable from a stock host BY DESIGN, which is why `encoderVendorKnown` is carried separately — the same split `sessionLockedKnown` exists for. To prove the value arrived: the co-op dialog names the vendor per machine in its banner, and that banner is drawn ONLY when there is something to say. **TRAP: an unpaired or HTTP-only probe returns nothing**, so a host polled before pairing reads as unknown forever until re-polled. |
| `CoopProfileCaps` | Apollo fork / DOC | Serverinfo field, int bitmask; see the two bit constants above. `SERVER_DATA.coopProfileCaps`, defaulting to 0 for every host that does not answer. | Same as `EncoderVendor`. 0 is both "stock host" and "our host that failed to answer" — the client must never read 0 as "this host refuses", only as "no claim". |

| `COOP_HDR_ENV` / `COOP_HDR_ALLOWED` | ours / SRC | The constant, and the environment variable it names, carrying the RESOLVED HDR answer from the co-op primary to the secondary ("1" allow, "0" deny). Written by `coop_stream_mode_publish()` beside `COOP_STREAM_MODE`, read by `coop_hdr_allowed_resolved()`, cleared by `coop_session_clear()`. **Absent means "nobody decided", which resolves to TRUE** — the user's own `hdr` setting — so every launch path that predates this (the sentinel harness, a PIN relaunch) behaves exactly as before. | The observable is the negotiated format, not this variable: the TV diag's `=== SESSION n ===` line reads `codec=H265 10bit` when 10-bit was advertised and accepted. **TRAP: deny must be published EXPLICITLY**, because absent and deny are different here; a publisher that only sets the variable when denying would make the primary's decision invisible to the secondary in the allow case and leave it reading its own ini. |
| `COOP_ORIENTATION_ENV` / `COOP_ORIENTATION_SEL` | ours / SRC | Same contract as `COOP_STREAM_MODE`/`COOP_HDR_ENV` above, for orientation. Constant defined `coop_session.c:28`; the environment variable it names carries the RESOLVED orientation (`"0"` horizontal / `"1"` vertical, `app_settings.h:217-218`) from the co-op primary to the secondary. **UNCOMMITTED as of 2026-09-18** (`git diff` shows it new in `coop_session.c`, alongside `coop_session_prepare_scripted()` and `coop_gpu_hevc_resolved()`) — not yet built or live-tested; read this row as the design, not an observation, the same status this section already carries for the codec half. Written by `coop_session_set()` (`coop_session.c:46-48`, from `session->orientation`), called by both the UI dialog and `coop_session_prepare_scripted()` (the sentinel/scripted-harness path, `:124-128`); cleared by `coop_session_clear()` (`:64`). Read by `coop_orientation_resolved()` (`:164-181`), a THREE-tier fallback: an in-process session's own `orientation` field wins outright (same-process short-circuit — the env var this call just set is never consulted in that process); else the env var (the secondary's path); else `app_configuration->coop_orientation` (the ini, the no-dialog launch path). Consumers: `session_config_init()` (`session.c:427-450`, runs in BOTH roles) calls it directly for `coop_tunables_arm_mode()` (primary-only, gated on `coop_secondary_mode() == NULL`) and indirectly through `coop_tunables_bitrate()`/`_fps()`/`_height()` (`coop_tunables.c:145-169`, both roles, each re-resolving independently — see the `coop_bitrate_h`/`_v` row, §13). **Deliberately NOT named `COOP_ORIENTATION`**: `run_coop.sh` already uses that exact name as a SHELL variable carrying `"horizontal"`/`"vertical"` (verified: `run_coop.sh:203,248,262,405,411`), not this enum's `"0"`/`"1"` — a scripted run exporting the shell name into this process would be misread as a number. `coop_session.c:24-27`'s comment states the same reasoning; independently confirmed against `run_coop.sh` rather than taken on the comment's word alone. | **OPEN — mechanism is source-verified, application is NOT yet live-measured** (this code has not been built). Once built, the proof shape is the same as `COOP_STREAM_MODE`: compare the primary's and secondary's own `commons_log_info("Coop", "Tunables applied (%s): ...")` lines (`coop_tunables.c:84-89`, prints `"vertical"`/`"horizontal"`) and confirm they agree. **TRAP, historical and specific to orientation:** `session.c:393-421`'s comment records the EXACT failure this variable exists to prevent, from BEFORE `COOP_STREAM_MODE`/`COOP_HDR_ENV` existed: a stale ini (`coop_orientation=1`, vertical) plus a user's horizontal dialog choice left the primary correctly requesting `3840x1088` while the secondary re-armed from the ini and requested `1920x2160` — "the whole bottom half of the panel shredded, user-reported" (2026-08-15). Re-run that exact scenario with this variable in place before trusting it fixed. |
| `coop_default_primary_uuid`, `coop_default_secondary_uuid` | ours / SRC | `moonlight.ini` strings: the pair the co-op launch popup OPENS on, ahead of the last-used pair. Empty (the default) means "use whatever ran last", which is the behaviour that existed before them. Written by the Settings › Co-op dropdowns; read first in `resolve_hosts()` (`coop.dialog.c`). A uuid that is no longer paired is skipped, not carried. | Open the co-op popup and read the two machine names. **TRAP: these are an INSTRUCTION, `coop_primary_uuid`/`coop_secondary_uuid` are a RECORD** — the latter are rewritten by every launch, these are not, and confusing them means a launch silently overwrites the user's stated default. The popup's own chooser changes the pairing for ONE session and deliberately does not write either pair. |

**HDR IS A SEPARATE GATE, BY REQUIREMENT.** `coop_stream_plan()` answers the codec
question and the HDR question independently: HDR is dropped only when a PARTICIPATING
HOST **is known** not to advertise a 10-bit codec (`SERVER_DATA.supportsHdr`, decoded
from the standard `ServerCodecModeSupport`, so it works on stock hosts), never because
the vendors differ and never because a host has not been polled — silence leaves the
user's `hdr` setting alone. That a compatibility-mode session still ends up 8-bit is a property of the
PROTOCOL, not a policy: every 10-bit format Moonlight defines is HEVC or AV1
(`VIDEO_FORMAT_MASK_10BIT` = 0xAA00, `Limelight.h:236`), so there is no 10-bit H.264 to
advertise. `session.c` therefore applies two statements — a codec mask and a 10-bit mask
— so that if either fact ever changes, only the gate that changed has to move.

**THE DECISION THESE FEED, and what is actually measured in it.** `coop_stream_plan()`
maps a vendor pair to a codec path: same vendor → core (HEVC), mixed → H.264
compatibility mode. The two mechanisms behind that rule are measured and recorded
elsewhere, NOT here — HEVC CTB mismatch (NVENC 32x32 vs AMF 64x64) in memory
`coop-ctb-size-mismatch`, and the H.264 PPS `transform_8x8_mode_flag` disagreement in
Apollo `nvenc_config.h`, whose comment cites a 2026-08-30 bit-exact composition and is
corroborated by the live mixed-vendor AVC run of 2026-08-31. **What has NOT been
measured is this code path**: that the field arrives, that the plan picks what we think,
and that `supportedVideoFormats = VIDEO_FORMAT_H264` actually changes the negotiated
codec. The measurement that would show it is the TV diag `codec=` line on both the
primary and the secondary, on a run where the ONLY change is the vendor pair.

<!-- SEEDED by scripts/ctx/param_seed.py -- curate by hand, then keep it -->

## 16. Apollo session-lifecycle keys — the prep/state command surface

Added 2026-08-22 after these were USED (and PC2's config CHANGED) while all five read
NOT REGISTERED. Apollo owns them, so the citations are into **its** source, the tree we build
from: `C:\Users\<user>\apollo-build`, git `0cd32ab` — the SAME commit both hosts' binaries
report. Where behaviour was measured on this rig, `TEST` is recorded too.

| name | surface | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `global_prep_cmd` | sunshine.conf | DOC + TEST | `src/process.cpp:434` do-loop (`Executing Do Cmd`), `:719` undo unwind (`Executing Undo Cmd`). Measured 2026-08-22 on PC2: arm+revert both fired, ARP `50-3e-aa-52-41-c5 static` → `6c-15-db… dynamic`. |
| `global_state_cmd` | sunshine.conf | DOC + TEST | `src/process.cpp:607` `proc_t::resume()` runs `do` (`Executing Resume Cmd`); `:662` `proc_t::pause()` runs `undo` (`Executing Pause Cmd`). Measured 2026-08-22 22:28:17 on PC2. |
| `terminate_on_pause` | app ctx (C++/apps.json) | DOC + TEST | `src/process.cpp:~655`: if set, logs `Terminating app [X] when all clients are disconnected. Pause commands are skipped.` and returns BEFORE the state-cmd undo. Measured 2026-08-22 22:11:12 for `Virtual Display`. |
| `isolated_virtual_display_option` | sunshine.conf | DOC + TEST | `src/config.cpp:1211` `bool_f(...)`; `src/process.cpp:314` is its **ONLY** use → `VDISPLAY::changeDisplaySettings2(name,w,h,fps,true)`. **It does NOT touch `prep_cmds`.** |
| `exclude-global-prep-cmd` / `exclude-global-state-cmd` | apps.json (per app) | DOC | `src/process.cpp:1311` and `:1339` — `app_node.value("exclude-global-…", false)`; when false the global lists are merged into that app. Not measured here. |

**What the group does:** `global_prep_cmd` runs once per session (do at launch, undo unwound in
REVERSE on terminate); `global_state_cmd` runs on resume/pause transitions within a session.

**THE TRAP THAT COST THIS PROJECT WEEKS — apps built in C++ never got either list.**
The apps.json parse loop is what merges the global lists into an app. Apps constructed in code
(`Virtual Display`, `Remote Input`) skip that loop, so their lists stayed EMPTY and both
`execute()` and `resume()` short-circuit — a Virtual Display session ran NO global prep and NO
global state cmds at all. Our fork's fix is `apply_global_cmds()` (`src/process.cpp:950`,
called at `:1527`), **added 2026-08-13 ~16:54** — absent from `process.cpp.bak-vdd-20260813-165402`,
present after. **A host whose `sunshine.exe` predates that has the bug**; PC2 did until
2026-08-22.

**How I know a change took effect (measured 2026-08-22):** the observable is a
`Executing Do Cmd:` / `Executing Undo Cmd:` line in `sunshine.log` FOR THE APP UNDER TEST, plus
the side effect (here: `arp -a 192.0.2.10` flipping between `…-41-c5 static` and
`…c9-72 dynamic`). PC2 went from **0/4** Virtual Display sessions running prep to **2/2** after
the binary swap, with Co-op Pane (an apps.json app) at 1/1 throughout as the control.

**`isolated_virtual_display_option` — DOC and TEST DISAGREE; TEST wins for this rig.**
The name implies the VDD becomes the only display. Measured on PC2 with the flag `true`, during
a `Virtual Display` session (`dispwake listall` run as an Apollo prep cmd):
`DISPLAY1 ACTIVE PHYSICAL 2560x1440@0,0` **and** `DISPLAY12 ACTIVE VIRTUAL 3840x2160@2560,1440`
— i.e. NOT isolated; the VDD was an empty second monitor, which is why the pane streamed black.
Isolation on PC2 is achieved by dispwake's `physical-off`, not by this flag. **Do not cite this
flag as a cause of prep-cmd behaviour** — that was a retracted claim on 2026-08-22.

**VERSION STRINGS ARE USELESS HERE.** Both hosts report FileVersion `0.0.0.0cd32ab.dirty`;
`.dirty` hides every local edit. Compare **mtime + md5** (PC1 `9e364658…` 08-13 17:45 vs PC2's
old `5b30bee6…` 08-01 17:58).

## 17. `arp-pin.conf` — the TV MAC override (host-side, ours)

Read by `arp-pin.ps1` (staged on BOTH hosts under `…\xlink-build\host\`), which is wired into
`global_prep_cmd` (§16). Format is one `KEY=value` per line; MACs accept `:` or `-` and are
upper-cased internally.

| name | default | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `WIRED` | `50-3E-AA-52-41-C5` | SRC | `arp-pin.ps1`: `if ($line -match '^\s*WIRED\s*=\s*(\S+)')` → `$wired`. The MAC every known TV ip is pinned to (`New-NetNeighbor -State Permanent`). |
| `WIFI` | `6C-15-DB-8D-C9-72` | SRC | same parse block → `$wifi`. Used only to RECOGNISE the TV's other NIC when discovering which ips belong to the TV; never a pin target. |

**What they do:** they identify the TV by HARDWARE, not address — deliberate, because DHCP
moves the TV's ips on every power cycle but the MACs are stable.

**How I know a change took effect (measured 2026-08-22, PC2):** `arp -a 192.0.2.10` flips
from `6c-15-db-8d-c9-72 dynamic` (Wi-Fi, the ARP-flux default) to `50-3e-aa-52-41-c5 static`
on arm, and back to `dynamic` on revert. That pair is the observable — not the log line, which
prints whether or not the pin landed.

**THESE ARE RIG-SPECIFIC AND ARE THE THING THE PLANNED FEATURE REPLACES.** Hardcoded defaults
for one TV; already logged in the release-portability register. The "network path override"
setting is to have Aurora DERIVE the wired MAC at runtime from `/sys/class/net` (wireless dir /
`DEVTYPE=wlan`) instead — brief in memory `aurora-ls2-permission-ceiling`. Register the new
moonlight.ini key BEFORE using it.

## 18. COOP-NETCAP — the continuous capture surface (added 2026-09-02)

**WHY THIS EXISTS.** Every co-op fault worth chasing here develops over MINUTES (peer-pane
decay, seam damage), and no other capture can photograph that: `/tmp/coop-dump-out` stops at
400 AUs (~6.7 s), and `/tmp/coop-dump-ring` can only flush from an IRAP inside its own window
against an INFINITE-GOP stream (periodic IDR is a ruled-out visible flash), so triggering it
would repair the very drift under test. Netcap instead PUSHES a continuous capture off the TV
over TCP to a receiver run on a host — full design in `netcap_recv.py`'s own docstring and
`session_video.c:867-886`. **Two ends, one owner (`SRC` throughout — no upstream project
defines any name on this surface):** the TV client is `session_video.c` (search
`coop_netcap`), the receiver is `scripts/coop/netcap_recv.py`, run manually on a host.

| name | surface | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `COOP_NETCAP_MAGIC` | const | SRC | `session_video.c:889` `#define COOP_NETCAP_MAGIC 0x31434E41u  /* "ANC1" little-endian */` — the 32-bit tag at offset 0 of every 32-byte record header (`coop_netcap_worker`, `session_video.c:1002`, `uint32_t magic = COOP_NETCAP_MAGIC, len = ...`). Independently redefined on the receiving end, `netcap_recv.py:50` `MAGIC = 0x31434E41` — **the two definitions must agree byte-for-byte and there is no shared header enforcing it**; today they do (checked by diffing both literals). **How you know a mismatch took effect:** it does NOT fail silently — `netcap_recv.py:162-164` checks every record and sets `stop_reason = f"BAD MAGIC 0x{magic:08x} ... desynchronised"`, ending the capture immediately with that reason in `meta.json`. A capture that ran to its full `--max-seconds`/`--max-gb` limit never hit this path. |
| `COOP_NETCAP_SLOTS` | const | SRC | `session_video.c:888` `#define COOP_NETCAP_SLOTS 48  /* ~3.7MB at 78KB/AU; deep enough to ride a network hiccup */` — depth of `coop_netcap_ring[]`, the producer/consumer ring `coop_netcap_feed()`/`coop_netcap_worker()` share (indices `coop_netcap_w`/`coop_netcap_r`, modulo this constant). **How you know it applied:** it sets the exact backlog at which `coop_netcap_drop` starts incrementing — `session_video.c:1056-1058`, `if (coop_netcap_w - coop_netcap_r >= COOP_NETCAP_SLOTS) { coop_netcap_drop++; return; }`. Raise it and a network stall that used to drop AUs at 48-behind instead absorbs more before `drop` (below) starts moving; the comment's "~3.7MB at 78KB/AU" is a size CLAIM (rule 5) — the AU size actually seen in a run is measurable from `meta.json`'s `bytes`/`aus` (currently ~60-90 KB/AU across the three recordings on disk, consistent with the comment but not identical). |
| `/tmp/coop-netcap` | sentinel | SRC | Already in §5 (latched-at-start table) — arm with `echo "<host>:<port>" > /tmp/coop-netcap` BEFORE the stream starts (`session_video.c:885`, `coop_netcap_setup()` at `:1078-1116`, read once via `fopen`/`fgets` at `:1102-1109`). **How you know it applied:** `[COOP-NETCAP] armed for <host>:<port>` in the TV's diag log (`:1110-1114`), then `[COOP-NETCAP] connected to <host>:<port>` once the worker thread's socket connects (`:992`). Absence of the second line with the first present means the receiver was not listening yet or was unreachable — check `netcap_recv.py`'s own console output, which prints `netcap: connected from <ip>:<port>` on accept. |
| `ev` | 0 | 1 | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:296` (`coop_hh_skip_events`), incremented `:4728`, reset per stream `:1178`, emitted as `peerskip[ev=]`. Counts PICTURES where the peer frame taken was AHEAD of `plan.min_frame`, i.e. the selector skipped over queued peer frames. **KNOWN DEFECT, measured 2026-09-03: this reads 0 in EVERY run including one with 56.09% corrupt frames**, so it is NOT the signal it was added for. The offline measurement that showed a 7.5x in-burst separation (2.25% of AUs vs 0.30% baseline) was a DIFFERENT quantity — the jump in COMPOSITE frame number between consecutive `kind=1` netcap feeds. **Do not use `ev` as a corruption predictor.** How you know a change took effect: it is nonzero at all. Fix: count the `frame` delta between successive peer feeds at `coop_netcap_feed` (`:1108`). |
| `fr` | 0 | 1 | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:297` (`coop_hh_skip_frames`), total peer frames skipped over, same site and same DEFECT as `ev` above — reads 0 in every run measured. `max=` alongside it is `coop_hh_skip_max`, the worst single gap. |
| `errors` (`diag_feed_errors`) | 0 | 1 | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:1611`, incremented `:6762` on the `else` of `SS4S_VideoFeedResult result == SS4S_VIDEO_FEED_OK`, reset per session `:3224`, printed on the per-frame `[COOP]`/`ok=` line `:6725` and on the session-end line `:3596`. **It counts pictures SS4S REFUSED** — `NOT_READY`, `ERROR`, or a BufferFull turned into `REQUEST_KEYFRAME` — each also logged as a `FEED_ERROR result=` line carrying the raw result code. **REGISTERED 2026-09-06 because a decision was about to rest on it and `param.py` said STOP:** it had prose mentions and no row. **WHAT IT ALSO MEANS, WHICH NOBODY KNEW:** on the AVC co-op path all four `coop_avc_advance_fn()` sites advance the composite's `frame_num` BEFORE the feed result is evaluated, and the catch-up site discards the return value outright — so **a nonzero `errors=` is also a frame_num GAP event**, which the composite may not have, because `gaps_in_frame_num_value_allowed_flag` is inherited from the primary and NVENC writes 0. **MEASURED LATENT, NOT ACTIVE:** `errors=0` in ALL 44 aurora-diag logs on disk, and 0 is the only value that has ever appeared, so no feed has been observed to reject a picture on this rig. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** it is printed unconditionally on every `ok=` line; `errors=0` is not an absence of instrumentation. **DO NOT READ A NONZERO VALUE AS "a few dropped frames"** — read it as a conformance break and go looking for the `FEED_ERROR result=` codes. |
| `ncstuck` (`coop_netcap_stuck`) | 0 | 1 | SRC | **Emitted as `ncstuck=`, NOT `stuck=` — renamed 2026-09-03 on review.** The bare word `stuck` already occurs in COOP-AVC prose ("stuck at 1 with `avcrefresh` climbing"), and while `param.py` no longer green-lights a prose match, a diag FIELD sharing a name with unrelated register prose is a collision waiting to mislead a grep. `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:903` (`coop_netcap_stuck`), incremented `:1010` when the netcap sender abandons a slot after a BOUNDED not-ready spin (~1000 x 1 ms), and logged as `[COOP-NETCAP] slot N not ready after ~1s`. **MUST STAY 0.** Any nonzero value means the single-producer/single-consumer ring invariant broke — the state that silently cost an entire capture on 2026-09-02 (`nc[sent=2 drop=10427]`, a 1-AU file) when the connect-time purge left the read pointer on a cleared slot. How you know it applied: the diag line appears at all; in normal operation neither the counter nor the line is ever seen. |
| `sent` (`coop_netcap_sent`) | gauge | SRC | `session_video.c:910` declares it, `:1025` increments after a fully-written AU (`send()` loop completes both the 32-byte header and the payload). Resets to 0 at every `coop_netcap_setup()` call (`:1100`) — i.e. **once per stream, not once per process**; do not compare `sent` across two sessions run in the same TV process without accounting for the reset. Read on `[COOP-AVC] ... nc[sent=%u drop=%u err=%u] ...` (`:4401,4413`). **How you know it applied:** `sent` climbing roughly 1:1 with `avccomposed` (the TV is submitting most composites to the capture); on the receiver side the SUM should be recoverable from `meta.json`'s `composite_aus + composite_skipped_pre_irap + peer_aus + peer_skipped_pre_irap` for a clean run with no drops/errors, since every `sent` AU is either kept or gated pre-IRAP on the receiving end — this identity is a derivation, NOT independently measured in this pass; treat it as OPEN until checked against a real meta.json where `sent` was also logged from the TV side. |
| `drop` (`coop_netcap_drop`) | gauge | SRC | `session_video.c:910` declares it; two increment sites, both "the ring is full, give up on this AU rather than block the submit thread" — `:1057` (ring at `COOP_NETCAP_SLOTS` backlog) and `:1063` (a `malloc`/`realloc` failure growing a slot's buffer). Resets with `sent`/`err` at every `coop_netcap_setup()` (`:1100`). **How you know it applied:** nonzero `drop` in the `nc[...]` diag field means the capture is LOSSY for that stretch — the composite recording is missing AUs the live stream actually showed, which matters for exactly the kind of "damage that appeared and later cleared" analysis this capture exists for. **This is a silent-failure candidate for §12**, added there below: a lossy capture still produces a plausible, decodable file. |
| `err` (`coop_netcap_err`) | gauge | SRC | `session_video.c:910` declares it, `:1027` increments on a `send()` failure (`n <= 0`), which also sets `coop_netcap_run = 0` — **one `err` ends the worker thread for the rest of that stream**, per the comment at `:1033` ("a half-written AU makes the recording undecodable; stop clean"). Resets at `coop_netcap_setup()` like the other two. **How you know it applied:** `err` going from 0 to 1 and staying there for the rest of the session's `[COOP-AVC]` lines means the capture died early — cross-check against `meta.json`'s `stop_reason` on the receiver, which should show something other than `max-seconds`/`max-gb reached` for the same event (e.g. `"connection closed mid-AU"` or a timeout). |
| `composite_skipped_pre_irap`, `composite_iraps`, `span_s`, and the rest of `meta.json` | script output (host-side, `netcap_recv.py`) | SRC | Written once, on exit, `netcap_recv.py:239-261`. **Two counters are PER-STREAM, not process-wide** — `STREAMS[0]` = composite (the spliced picture we feed the decoder), `STREAMS[1]` = peer (the peer's pane exactly as it arrived, pre-splice), tagged in the wire header's `kind` byte (`session_video.c` `coop_netcap_slot_t`, sent at `:4347`). `composite_skipped_pre_irap` = count of composite-stream records received and DISCARDED before the first composite IRAP arrived (`netcap_recv.py:180-184`); those bytes are never written to `composite.h264`. `composite_iraps` = count of composite-stream records with `irap=1` actually kept (`:198`). **`seconds` and `span_s` are DIFFERENT quantities and have been confused before (flagged explicitly by the lead brief this row answers):** `seconds` = wall-clock CAPTURE WINDOW, `time.time() - t0` from the moment the TCP connection was accepted to receiver exit (`:238`) — it includes any time spent skipping pre-IRAP records. `span_s` = `(last_wall_ns - first_wall_ns) / 1e9` for the COMPOSITE stream only (`:255`), i.e. the wall-clock range of AUs actually KEPT — if the first IRAP arrives late, `span_s` is shorter than `seconds` by roughly that delay. **Top-level `iraps`/`skipped_pre_irap`/`first_wall_ns`/`last_wall_ns` duplicate the `composite_*` fields under legacy short names** (`:249,250,253,254` all assign from `STREAMS[0]`) — read `composite_*`/`peer_*`, the unambiguous names, and treat the short ones as an alias, not a second measurement. **`aus`/`records`/`bytes` ARE combined across both streams** (`n`, and `off = sum(x["off"] for x in STREAMS.values())`), unlike `iraps`/`span_s` — do not assume every top-level field follows the same rule. |

**MEASURED ON DISK, three real recordings (paths in the run brief), used as evidence here rather
than invented:**

| capture | guard | `composite_aus` | `composite_iraps` | `composite_skipped_pre_irap` | `seconds` | `span_s` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `burst/` | 32 | 9003 | 1 | **9004** | 300.008 | 150.214 |
| `run7/` | 64 | 21509 | 9 | 0 | 360.001 | 359.993 |
| `g0det/` | 0 | 37204 | 1 | 0 | 620.304 | 620.306 |

`burst/` is the pathology the brief describes: the receiver discarded the first 9004 composite
records waiting for an IRAP that did not arrive until ~150 s in, so `composite.h264` covers only
the SECOND HALF of the 300 s window while `peer.h264` (`peer_skipped_pre_irap: 0` in all three
files) covers the whole thing — the composite and peer recordings are NOT the same time range in
that file, and any comparison between them must account for that offset, not assume alignment.

**Timeline against the fix under test.** `git log -1 -S'!coop_netcap_ring[i].irap' --
src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` dates the IRAP-keep change (`:989`,
`if (!coop_netcap_ring[i].irap) { coop_netcap_ring[i].ready = 0; }`, replacing an unconditional
clear of the whole ring) to commit `cc94a379`, **2026-09-02T17:08:46+10:00**. `burst/meta.json`'s
mtime is 16:19 the same day — **before** that commit, so its `composite_skipped_pre_irap=9004` is
PRE-FIX data, consistent with (not yet resolving) the prediction under test. `run7/` (13:40) and
`g0det/` (14:52) are also pre-fix but already read `composite_skipped_pre_irap: 0` — the fix is
not needed on every run, only on the specific race the mechanism describes (see the §12 row).
**No POST-fix recording exists on disk as of this pass** — `composite_skipped_pre_irap` dropping
to ~0 and `span_s` matching peer `span_s` on a NEW capture, taken after the TV was rebuilt from
`cc94a379`/`b7c93134` and redeployed (build-verifier's 2026-09-02 17:31 note confirms the binary
swap), is the live-test observable and remains OPEN until that capture is taken.
