## 19. Deterministic pattern (`?det=1`) constants, and `det_scan.py`'s band-vote fields

Added 2026-09-03 after all five names below were about to be quoted in a claim while
`param.py` returned NOT REGISTERED for every one. **Owner is this repo throughout — SRC.**
Neither the extractor's `pattern` surface (which only reads `publishTitle()`'s title-bar
fields, §7) nor `const` (C compile-time only) enumerates JS `const`s or Python script
output, so `param_check.py` cannot see these five and its passing is not evidence they were
registered — that is exactly why this section exists as curated prose.

| name | default / range | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DET_SCROLL_PX` | `4`, clamped to `[1,64]`, settable per-host via `?scrollpx=` | SRC | `scripts/coop/testpattern/pattern.html:458`: `const DET_SCROLL_PX = (...) Math.min(64, Math.max(1, Math.round(v))) : 4`. **What it does:** pixels the detail tile scrolls per STEP (not per draw) in det mode — `detBands()` at `pattern.html:494`, `const sc = (step * DET_SCROLL_PX) % DH`. Purely cosmetic to the verified content: the band digits/cells are unaffected, this only moves the noise/line/gradient background used to force real motion compensation (see the file's own header comment, a CLAIM about encoder behaviour, not measured here). **How you know a change took effect:** the background offset at a given step is `(step * DET_SCROLL_PX) % 1080` — set `?scrollpx=` to two different values on two captures at the same wall-clock step (from the band code) and the visible scroll position must differ by that ratio. No counter publishes this value anywhere (not in the title bar, not in `det_scan.py`'s output) — confirming it applied requires reading the pixel position, not a log. |
| `STEP_MS` | `100`, clamped to `[20,1000]`, settable per-host via `?stepms=` | SRC | `scripts/coop/testpattern/pattern.html:453`: `const STEP_MS = (...) Math.min(1000, Math.max(20, v)) : 100`. **What it does:** the period of the wall-clock step the whole det-mode pane is a function of — `step = floor((Date.now()+TSKEW)/STEP_MS)` (`pattern.html:550`), redrawn only `if (step !== lastStep)`. This is the unit `det_bands.py --step-ms` converts a `step_delta` into skew milliseconds (`det_bands.py:169-172`) and it is what §COOP's `TSKEW` correction (`pattern.html:440-452`) is measured against — **the two hosts must run the SAME `STEP_MS` or `step_delta` is not convertible to a real time difference at all.** It also directly bounds how finely two streams can be aligned BY code: two frames sharing a code could be up to `STEP_MS` apart in real time, so a smaller `STEP_MS` gives tighter code-level alignment at the cost of more redraws (bounded, since `detBands()` only repaints on a step change). **How you know a change took effect:** the band code advances (`floor(now/STEP_MS)`) at `1000/STEP_MS` steps/second — `det_scan.py`'s `stale_runs()` history over a fixed wall-clock window will show that many DISTINCT codes; a run launched with `?stepms=20` that still shows only 10 distinct codes/second did not apply. |
| `bad_pct` | output field, `det_scan.py` | SRC | `scripts/coop/det_scan.py:258`: `"bad_pct": round(100.0 * len(flagged) / n, 2)`, where `flagged` is every sampled frame failing `bad()` (`:144-146`, `not s["agree"]`) and `n` is the TOTAL sampled frames for that pane. **What it does:** the percentage of SAMPLED frames where the 8 band plates (decoded at `det_bands.decode_pane`) did not all agree on one readable code. **CRITICAL COVERAGE CAVEAT, stated explicitly by the tool's own docstring (`det_scan.py:60-63`) and restated here because a prior tool's `bad_pct`-shaped number was already misread as pane-wide corruption once (`burst_scan.py`, superseded by this file 2026-09-03):** each band plate is sampled at its vertical CENTRE over `bh*0.08` rows either side (`det_bands.py:99-101`) and at 16 fixed-fraction columns (`det_bands.py:127-131`) — roughly 21 of 136 pane rows and 18 of 3840 columns per pane, **order 0.007% of pane pixels**. `bad_pct = 0.00%` means "the 8 sampled plates decoded and agreed", **never** "the pane is clean" — corruption anywhere outside those exact rows/columns (which is nearly the whole pane) is invisible to this field by construction, not by a measured gap. **How you know a change took effect:** it is a downstream field, not itself settable — it moves only when the underlying frames' band agreement changes, observable by re-running `det_scan.py` on the same recording (deterministic: same ffmpeg decode, same sample points) and confirming `bad_frames`/`n` matches. |
| `band_disagree` | output field, `det_scan.py` | SRC | `scripts/coop/det_scan.py:261`: `"band_disagree": dict(sorted(bdis.items()))`, a `Counter` incremented once per sampled frame per band index found in that frame's `disagreeing_bands` (`det_scan.py:250-252`), which is computed upstream in `det_bands.summarise()` (`det_bands.py:142`) as `[b["band"] for b in bandlist if b["code"] not in (top, None)]`, where `top` is `Counter(codes).most_common(1)[0]` (`det_bands.py:139`) — **the MAJORITY code among the readable bands, not the correct one**. **What it does:** per-band count, across the sampled recording, of frames where that band's code differed from the modal code of the (up to) 8 bands. **THE TRAP, stated because it inverts the reading of this field once more than half the bands are hit:** `most_common(1)` picks whichever code the MOST bands agree on, with no notion of which bands are actually intact. If splice/corruption hits **5 of 8** bands with one bad code and the **3 remaining CLEAN bands** keep the correct code, the corrupt code is now the majority (`top`), and `band_disagree` counts the 3 CLEAN bands as the disagreeing ones — the exact opposite of which bands are damaged. This field is therefore **majority-DEPENDENT** and only names "the bands that differ from whichever code most bands share" — read it together with `bad_pct`/`agree` (was this frame even majority-corrupt?) before trusting which band index it blames. Below a 4/8 corruption split it correctly identifies the damaged bands; at or above it, it identifies the clean ones instead. **How you know a change took effect:** re-running on the same recording reproduces the same counts exactly (fully deterministic decode); comparing two different recordings requires first checking each frame's readable/total ratio, since the vote's meaning changes with how many bands are corrupt. |
| `band_unreadable` | output field, `det_scan.py` | SRC | `scripts/coop/det_scan.py:262`: `"band_unreadable": dict(sorted(bunr.items()))`, incremented once per sampled frame per band index found in `unreadable_bands` (`det_scan.py:253-255`), computed in `det_bands.summarise()` (`det_bands.py:143`) as `[b["band"] for b in bandlist if b["code"] is None]`. A band's code is `None` when its two sync cells were not separable — `det_bands.decode_pane()` (`det_bands.py:118-121`): `if lw - lb < 25: ... "reason": "sync cells not separable (band likely corrupt)"` — `lw`/`lb` are the mean luma of the white and black sync cells (`luma_at()`, `det_bands.py:93-98`) sampled over the same rows as the data cells. **What it does:** per-band count of frames where that band could not be decoded AT ALL (no code, not even a wrong one), as opposed to `band_disagree`'s "decoded, but to a code differing from the vote". **MAJORITY-INDEPENDENT, unlike `band_disagree`:** an unreadable band is excluded from the vote entirely (`codes = [b["code"] for b in bandlist if b["code"] is not None]`, `det_bands.py:134`), so it never flips with corruption prevalence — a band destroyed badly enough to blow its sync cells shows up here regardless of how many OTHER bands are also damaged. This makes `band_unreadable` the more trustworthy of the two failing-band fields when more than half the bands are affected. **How you know a change took effect:** deterministic given the recording and the `lw - lb < 25` threshold; a band that flips from `band_unreadable` to `band_disagree` (or vice versa) between two decodes of the SAME file would indicate the ffmpeg decode itself is non-deterministic (not expected, not observed) rather than a real change in the source. |

**Why `STEP_MS` and `DET_SCROLL_PX` are not in the generated tables.** `param_extract.py`'s
`pattern` surface only parses `publishTitle()`'s `' label=' + expr` fields (§7); det mode's
JS `const`s are declared and used elsewhere in the same file and never appear in that
regex. `param_check.py` passing (470/470 as of 2026-09-03) says nothing about these five —
it cannot see this surface at all. Do not read a clean `param_check.py` as covering
`pattern.html`'s det-mode constants or any Python script's own output fields; both remain
manual, curated additions here.

---

## 20. Capture-index integrity — `peer_frame_audit.py` fields, and `splice_diff.py`'s shift + IRAP-distance control (added 2026-09-04)

Added because a wrong value of ONE of these silently voided two whole runs and then a wrong
EXPLANATION of it nearly discarded a third. Both are output fields of offline analysis
scripts, the class §19 already notes `param_check.py` cannot see.

| name | value / owner | ev | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `pictures` / `deficit` | output fields, `scripts/coop/peer_frame_audit.py` | TEST | **What they are:** `pictures` = count of VCL NALs (`nal_unit_type` 1 or 5) whose `first_mb_in_slice` is 0, i.e. coded pictures per H.264 7.4.3, parsed straight from the Annex-B bytes with NO decode. `deficit` = `records - pictures`. **What it settles:** whether a capture's `peer_index.jsonl` records correspond 1:1 to coded pictures, which is the premise `splice_diff.py` pairs on. **MEASURED 2026-09-04 on the two runs that failed splice_diff's alignment control: `deficit = 0` for BOTH** (run7 21302/21302, fals_g32_fps57 42977/42977). **This REFUTES the mechanism recorded in `splice_diff.py` on 2026-09-03** ("run7 is from the retired 2-slices-per-pane period", i.e. multiple slices per record) — there is exactly one coded picture per record in both, so multi-slice records are NOT the cause and that sentence was a hypothesis stated as fact. **How you know it applied:** it is a pure function of the file; re-running reproduces it exactly, and `--decode` cross-checks it against the decoder's own output count. |
| `decoded_frames` | output field, `peer_frame_audit.py --decode` | TEST | ffmpeg's OUTPUT frame count for the same stream. **The whole point is that it can differ from `pictures` with NO error reported** — measured run7: 21302 coded pictures, **21294 decoded frames, ffmpeg stderr EMPTY, exit 0**. The decoder simply does not output 8 of the pictures it was given. **This is the real cause of the deficit** the alignment control was catching. Which records are lost is recoverable exactly by joining `ffprobe -show_entries frame=pkt_pos` back to the index `off` field: run7 loses records 10-17; fals_g32_fps57 loses 218-227 and 242-251 — **contiguous blocks, all within the first ~250 records**, none carrying `irap=1`. **Do not generalise the "it's only at the head" part**: it is a measured property of these two captures, not a rule, and it is exactly what makes a single constant shift legitimate for them. |
| `--peer-shift` / reported `peer frame-index shift` | `scripts/coop/splice_diff.py` | TEST | Number subtracted from every peer index before pairing. `auto` measures it EXACTLY and cheaply: decode only the prefix up to `--skip-head` and subtract the output-frame count from the record count (~7% of the file). **Verified against ground truth 2026-09-04: 8 for run7, 20 for fals_g32_fps57, both exact.** **This REPLACED an image-correlation probe** (written 2026-09-03, never validated before the session ended) which is ±1 ambiguous — on fals it returned disagreeing votes of `[20, 21]` and picked the first. **What a nonzero value means for an already-published result:** every peer frame after the loss was compared against the wrong picture, so any run whose alignment control failed was measuring nothing. **Applying it recovers run7 (guard=64): alignment control x0.7 FAIL -> x4.9 PASS.** **Blind spot, stated because the method cannot see it:** losses occurring AFTER `--skip-head` would break the constant-shift premise and are NOT detected here — the ±4-row alignment control is the guard, and `peer_frame_audit.py --decode` is what locates them. |
| `--irap-window`, `irap_dist_median` | `scripts/coop/splice_diff.py` | TEST | Frames since the last **COMPOSITE** IRAP at each sampled point, and the filter that holds it fixed. **Why it exists:** the tool's two decodes are bit-exact only near an IRAP and drift apart afterwards, so this quantity is a first-order driver of the very thing being measured, and it is a property of how a run happened to go — NOT of the guard setting. **Measured 2026-09-03: median 19,234 (g0full, 1 composite IRAP), 17,270 (g0det, 1), 2,404 (g32full, 9).** The guard=32 arm was sampled **8x closer to a resync** than either guard=0 arm, in exactly the direction that manufactures the reported effect, **which is why "guard=32 roughly halves the normalised seam divergence" is VOID and must not be requoted.** It cannot be corrected after sampling, only held fixed at sampling time. **How you know it applied:** without `--irap-window` the tool prints `<== NOT held fixed; do not compare arms on this` next to the median; with it, every arm's printed median and range must fall inside the requested window or the arm is not comparable. **NB the within-run seam-vs-body ratio is NOT affected** — seam and body rows are sampled at identical IRAP distance by construction, which is why that result survives and the guard comparison does not. |

---

## 21. `cfail[...]` — WHY the peer's frame-hold refused (added 2026-09-04)

| name | value / owner | ev | evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `cfail[args noslice sinfo ouridr cap rewrite b_args b_cabac b_poc b_dinfo b_dtype b_emit]` | **ours**, `[COOP-AVC]` diag line, `session_video.c` | TEST | **WHAT IT IS:** twelve counters, one per `return 0` site in `coop_avc_compose_concealed()` (`coop_avc.c`) and `coop_avc_build_skip_slice()` (`coop_avc_conceal.c`), incremented via `coop_avc_cfail()`. The `b_` prefix marks the builder's guards; the rest are the composer's. **WHAT IT ANSWERS:** `avcnopane` counts composite pictures emitted with the peer half NOT CODED AT ALL, and those are **failures of the peer's frame-hold, not missing frames** — measured 2026-09-04, transport during every burst was perfect (60 AU/s, zero frame-number gaps) while 293 of 8589 composite AUs still carried only the primary slice. The hold is the all-skip P slice in `compose_concealed`; twelve refusal sites shared ONE outcome counter, so the log could never say which guard fired. **RELATIONSHIP TO `avcnopane`: `avcnopane` is the SUM of these**, so if they do not add up a refusal path was missed. **ALREADY EXCLUDED FROM THE CAPTURED BITSTREAM, so a nonzero value in these three would contradict a measurement:** `ouridr`/`b_dtype` (all 293 dropped-pane AUs carry a normal `P` slice; the only `I IDR` in the capture kept its pane) and `b_poc` (the composite SPS parses `pic_order_cnt_type = 2`). **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** it is emitted in the existing `[COOP-AVC]` line every 120 composed+suppressed pictures and reset with the other co-op counters at session start; a run with `avcnopane > 0` and all twelve `cfail` fields zero means the instrumentation did not reach the failing site. **DIAGNOSTIC ONLY — nothing branches on these**, so they cannot change what is emitted; that is deliberate, because the change had to be provable without altering the behaviour under investigation. |

Each field is its own gauge. All twelve are `unsigned` counts since session start, reset by
`coop_avc_cfail_reset()`, and all are DIAGNOSTIC ONLY — no code branches on any of them.

| gauge | site that increments it | ev | what a nonzero value means |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `args` | `compose_concealed`, `mine==NULL \|\| out==NULL \|\| st==NULL \|\| !st->ready` | TEST | the hold was called before the composite's parameter sets were ready. `ready=1` is printed in the same line, so a nonzero here alongside `ready=1` means the call raced session setup. |
| `noslice` | `compose_concealed`, no VCL NAL found in our own AU | TEST | our own access unit carried no coded slice. Would also make the paired path impossible, so this should never fire alone. |
| `sinfo` | `compose_concealed`, `coop_avc_slice_info()` failed on OUR slice | TEST | our own slice header would not parse against `src_sps` — a parser/parameter-set mismatch on the PRIMARY side, not the peer's. |
| `ouridr` | `compose_concealed`, `si.idr` | TEST | our picture is an IDR; an all-skip P slice cannot conceal into one. **Measured 2026-09-04: cannot explain the observed drops** — all 293 dropped-pane AUs carried a normal `P` slice. |
| `cap` | `compose_concealed`, any of four capacity checks against `coop_splice_cap` | TEST | the splice buffer was too small. Distinct from `avcoversize`, which is the CALLER's buffer; this one is internal and was previously indistinguishable from every other refusal. |
| `rewrite` | `compose_concealed`, `coop_avc_rewrite_slice()` returned 0 re-homing OUR OWN slice | TEST | renumbering our own slice into the composite's parameter sets failed. Only reachable when `renumber` is true. |
| `b_args` | `build_skip_slice`, null args, invalid SPS/PPS, or `mb_count == 0` | TEST | `mb_count` is `peer_sps.width_mbs * height_mbs`, so this fires if **`peer_sps` was never populated** — i.e. the hold cannot size the peer's pane because the peer's SPS was never seen. |
| `b_cabac` | `build_skip_slice`, `!pps->entropy_cabac` | TEST | the donor PPS is CAVLC; the builder has no CAVLC path. Both vendors are expected to be CABAC, so nonzero contradicts that expectation. |
| `b_poc` | `build_skip_slice`, `sps->poc_type != 2` | TEST | donor SPS POC type is not 2. **Measured 2026-09-04: the composite SPS parses `pic_order_cnt_type = 2`**, so a nonzero value here would mean the DONOR sps differs from the composite's. |
| `b_dinfo` | `build_skip_slice`, `coop_avc_slice_info()` failed on the DONOR | TEST | the donor slice header would not parse. The donor is the peer's last P slice, or OUR own slice when no peer donor was kept. **CAUSE CORRECTED 2026-09-05 — this row previously asserted the wrong thing.** It read as "the peer sent an unusable slice type". Measured: the live caller stores the peer's whole ANNEX-B ACCESS UNIT in `coop_avc_lastpslice`, while every consumer needs a BARE NAL whose byte 0 is the NAL header, so the donor's header was parsed out of the START-CODE bytes. Proven offline by feeding one hand-built P slice both ways: bare -> `ok=1 first_mb=0 slice_type=5 nri=2`; the same bytes behind `00 00 00 01` -> `ok=0 first_mb=10538246 slice_type=1 nri=0`. So this counter has been measuring a PARSE of start-code bytes, not the peer's real slice type. Fixed by unwrapping the donor in `coop_avc_compose_concealed`; `unwrap=` (`donor_unwrapped`) proves the fix is in the build. A non-zero value here AFTER the unwrap is the genuine "peer sent an I slice" case this row originally described. |
| `b_dtype` | `build_skip_slice`, donor `slice_type % 5` not P/SP, or donor is IDR | TEST | the donor is not a usable P header. **Measured 2026-09-04: cannot explain the observed drops** (all dropped-pane AUs carry a normal `P`). |
| `idrreq` | COOP-AVC | `coop_coord_idr_sent()` / `coop_coord_idr_suppressed()` | LEVEL (sent/suppressed) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — the TRUE on-wire peer-keyframe counts, as already printed by `[COOP-VT]` and `[COOP-HH]`. Added to the AVC line 2026-09-05. **`avcrefresh` is NOT a substitute and is wrong in both directions:** it counts every CALL including ones the 120 ms throttle discards (and double-counts when the latch and the cadence fire in one picture), while missing the sequencer's own request on a peer frame-number hole. **Amended 2026-09-06:** the "double-counts when the latch and the cadence fire in one picture" clause still holds, but is now bounded — the latch fires once per BREAK, and the two causes are reported separately as `avcrefresh=t/b/c`, so a divergence between `b+c` and `idrreq` sent is readable rather than ambiguous. The identical HEVC flash was diagnosed in one line by `idrreq=30/358`. |
| `mended` (COOP-AVC) | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_mended`, plus `coop_avc_mend_sticky` | COUNT | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — times a peer IDR cleared `coop_avc_chain_broken`. Mirrors HEVC's `coop_hh_mended`. **Emitted `mended=<total>/s<sticky>` since 2026-09-06.** There are TWO mend sites: the normal take's peer-IDR mend, and the sticky-IDR take (`if (mine_idr)`) added the same day — that branch splices a genuine peer IDR out of the sticky slot and previously left the latch SET through the one event that fixed it. A single total could not say whether the new branch ever runs, which is the changed-path-with-no-counter pattern; `s` is its subset. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `s` moves only on pictures where OUR host emitted an IDR (loss recovery or bootstrap), so it should be small and non-zero over a long session; `s == 0` across many of our IDRs means the sticky slot is empty when we IDR, which is a different problem. |
| `heldbroken` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_heldbroken` | COUNT | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — pictures where the peer's pane was FROZEN because the chain was broken (`avc_len = 0`). **Without this a freeze was invisible**: the frozen picture then conceals and counts as `avcconceal`, indistinguishable from an ordinary rate-mismatch hold — which is why '3 evictions -> 15 s of frozen peer pane' could not be seen in the log. |
| `avcrepeatmax` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_repeat_max` | LEVEL (max run) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — longest run of CONSECUTIVE repeat-fallback pictures. Replaces the never-implemented `COOP_AVC_REPEAT_BUDGET` (see WITHDRAWN) with measurement rather than control: an unbounded repeat run genuinely decays (`repeated=2076 of 8344` once took a pane to black), so this exists to show the evidence before a budget is ever built. |
| `unwrap` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc.donor_unwrapped` | COUNT | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/coop_avc.c` — times the kept conceal donor arrived as a whole ANNEX-B ACCESS UNIT and was unwrapped to its last non-IDR slice NAL. **This is the proof the 2026-09-05 donor fix is in the build:** the live caller always stores an AU, so on any session with a peer P slice this tracks the conceal count. **ZERO here while `donorswap` climbs means the unwrap is NOT in this build** and the donor's header is still being parsed out of start-code bytes (see the `donorswap` row for the measurement). |
| `qoversize` | COOP-AVC | `coop_coord_oversize()` / `coop_coord_oversize_nonidr()` | COUNT (total/non-IDR) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/coop_coord.c` — access units dropped for exceeding `COOP_Q_SLOT_CAP`, and how many of those were NOT IDRs. The drop site ASSUMES an oversized AU is always an IDR (already held whole in the sticky slot) and does not check. **If that is ever wrong the pane vanishes silently**: `coord_replaced` does not move, so no consumer sees an eviction, and `take_next` later reports `skipped == 0`, so the splice runs straight across the hole with the chain flag never set. **A non-zero SECOND number falsifies the assumption** and means a real non-IDR pane was lost. Added 2026-09-05 to make an untested premise testable. |
| `cb[cudrop]` | `coop_avc_cb_cudrop` — CATCH-UP's take reported dropped panes, latching `coop_avc_chain_broken` | added 2026-09-05 to attribute chain breaks. Three sites set that flag and nothing distinguished them, so a 120 fps run showing `avcrefresh=142` could not say whether the peer's chain really broke or the detector is over-eager at that rate. |
| `cb[adrop]` | `coop_avc_cb_adrop` — the NORMAL take reported dropped panes (`coop_coord_take_next`'s `skipped` out-param) | same purpose. Distinguishes 'panes went missing between takes' from an eviction, which is a queue overrun and a different fault. |
| `cb[evict]` | `coop_avc_cb_evict` — `coop_coord_replaced()` moved, i.e. the coordinator queue OVERRAN and dropped its oldest pane | the corrupting case by this codebase's own rule ('a queue that overruns DROPS the oldest pane and tears a hole in the peer's prediction chain, while a queue that underruns merely HOLDS'). |
| `cb[panes]` | `coop_avc_adrop_panes` — total panes reported dropped by the normal take, summed | severity for `cb[adrop]`: one break from 40 lost panes is a different problem from 40 breaks of one pane each. |
| `b_hdr` | `build_skip_slice`: `coop_avc_rewrite_header()` returned 0 — the DONOR slice could not be re-homed, so no header could be built | the conceal produced nothing and the peer's pane is emitted UNCODED (a `avcnopane`). Split out of `b_emit` 2026-09-05: a 120 fps run showed all 144 hold failures as `b_emit` and it could not say which of four sites fired, and they have different fixes — this one is a DONOR problem (see `donorswap`), the others are buffer problems. |
| `b_cabover` | `build_skip_slice`: the CABAC encoder overflowed its 64 KiB payload buffer while writing `mb_skip_flag` for every macroblock | should be unreachable — an all-skip pane is ~1 bit per macroblock, ~2 KiB for a 16,320-MB pane. Non-zero means the macroblock count is wrong, not that the buffer is small. |
| `b_rbsp` | `build_skip_slice`: header + payload exceeded the 70,000-byte RBSP staging buffer | same class as `b_cabover`; treat a non-zero value as a geometry error rather than a sizing one. |
| `b_esc` | `build_skip_slice`: emulation-prevention escaping returned 0, or the escaped slice did not fit the caller's output capacity | a genuine capacity failure. Distinguishes 'the composite buffer was too small' from 'the donor was unusable', which `b_emit` alone could not. |
| `donorswap` | **ours**, `coop_avc.donor_swapped`, `[COOP-AVC]` line | TEST | **PROVES THE 2026-09-04 HOLD FIX FIRED.** Counts times the peer's kept donor was UNUSABLE (not a non-IDR P/SP slice) and `compose_concealed()` fell back to donating our own slice header. Before the fix the fallback tested only `ref_slice == NULL`, so an I-slice donor was passed straight to the builder, refused on its P/SP test, and the pane was dropped. **Read it against `b_dtype`:** the baseline run measured `avcnopane=288, b_dtype=234`; if the fix works `donorswap` should take up roughly that traffic and `b_dtype` should fall toward 0. `donorswap` high AND `b_dtype` still high means the swap happened but the builder still refused, i.e. a different guard. Both zero on a run with `avcnopane > 0` means neither path was reached and the drop has another cause. **CAUSE CORRECTED 2026-09-05 — this row previously asserted the wrong thing.** It read as "the peer sent an unusable slice type". Measured: the live caller stores the peer's whole ANNEX-B ACCESS UNIT in `coop_avc_lastpslice`, while every consumer needs a BARE NAL whose byte 0 is the NAL header, so the donor's header was parsed out of the START-CODE bytes. Proven offline by feeding one hand-built P slice both ways: bare -> `ok=1 first_mb=0 slice_type=5 nri=2`; the same bytes behind `00 00 00 01` -> `ok=0 first_mb=10538246 slice_type=1 nri=0`. So this counter has been measuring a PARSE of start-code bytes, not the peer's real slice type. Fixed by unwrapping the donor in `coop_avc_compose_concealed`; `unwrap=` (`donor_unwrapped`) proves the fix is in the build. A non-zero value here AFTER the unwrap is the genuine "peer sent an I slice" case this row originally described. |
| `b_emit` | `build_skip_slice`, header build or output capacity failed | TEST | the all-skip payload could not be written — bit-writer overflow, RBSP oversize, or escaped output exceeding `cap`. |

## 22. Build identity and co-op harness selection (2026-09-09)

These are **SRC** mappings verified against current source, not claims that a package has
been installed or a runtime test has passed. Verify the named observable on the actual build/run.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AURORA_VARIANT`, `VARIANT` | Eclipse build script / SRC | `scripts/webos/build_for_lg.sh:91-107`: environment selector defaults to `live`; internal `VARIANT` accepts only `live` or `beta`, chooses app id/title, and beta defaults the build directory to `build/webos-beta`. Other values exit 1. `VARIANT` is assigned by the script, not a second environment override. | Inspect the printed variant, generated `appinfo.json` id/title, CMake app-id cache and binary compile definitions. The printed selector alone does not prove the packaged binary identity. Rebuild and close/relaunch the selected app to use new code. |
| `APP_TITLE` | Eclipse build script / SRC | `scripts/webos/build_for_lg.sh:93-107`: internal display string (`Eclipse` or `Eclipse Beta`) printed in the build banner. It is **not passed to CMake**; CMake derives its actual title from the app id when no title is set. | Read generated/package `appinfo.json` title; changing this shell variable alone changes the banner, not the packaged title. |
| `WEBOS_APP_ID` (documentation alias), `WEBOS_APPINFO_ID` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `WEBOS_APP_ID` is not a second setting: the canonical build input is `WEBOS_APPINFO_ID`. It defaults to `com.aurora.gamestream` (`CMakeLists.txt:104-105`); `scripts/webos/build_for_lg.sh:92-110` passes either that live id or `com.aurora.gamestream.beta`. Every read: title and temporary-path variant selection plus build banner (`CMakeLists.txt:111-116,347-353`), packaged manifest substitution (`deploy/webos/appinfo.json:2`, configured by `cmake/PackageWebOS.cmake:4`), package name (`PackageWebOS.cmake:30`) and the install-and-launch target (`:63`). It is also compiled into C as `APPID` (`CMakeLists.txt:353`). | Compare the requested value with the generated and unpacked-package `appinfo.json` `id`, the effective `APPID` compile definition, then the installed `/media/developer/apps/usr/palm/applications/<id>/appinfo.json` and `/proc/<pid>/exe` path after close/relaunch. All must name the same variant. This is covered by the §12 extension below: a cache value, banner or IPK filename alone does not identify a still-running process, and install does not restart it. |
| `NETCAP` | Eclipse tracked harness / SRC | `scratchpad/run_one.sh:49-53`: environment selector defaults to `1`; exactly `0` removes `/tmp/coop-netcap`, every other value writes the capture target before launching the stream. This load-bearing tracked script is outside `scripts/`, so the extractor misses it. | Before launch verify the sentinel absent/present, then inspect the new session for capture arming/connection and sent AU progression. `NETCAP=0` still starts the host receiver and later runs capture-admissibility code unconditionally (`run_one.sh:60,119`); disabling the TV tee does not disable those harness stages. Latency impact of netcap is OPEN, not established by the source comment. |
| `COOP_APP`, `APP` | Eclipse harness / SRC | `scripts/coop/run_coop.sh:45-50`: `COOP_APP` defaults to live app id; internal `APP` is validated as exactly live or beta. It selects package gate, app close/launch calls, config path, host-profile branch, and diagnostic file. `APP_ID` is a separate Umbra/Apollo-compatible host target id, not this webOS app id. | Actual running executable/config path and new session lines in the selected diagnostic file must agree with the requested id. Beta requires its own pairing/configuration. The shell variable alone cannot prove which process is running. |
| `AURORA_TMP_SUFFIX` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `CMakeLists.txt:302-322`: internal string `-beta` iff app id ends in `.beta`, otherwise empty. Builds the three log paths and three co-op socket paths; not a runtime environment selector. | Inspect effective compiler definitions, then the fresh files/sockets created by the selected process. Changing environment after compilation has no effect. |
| `AURORA_DIAG_LOG` | Eclipse CMake and harness / SRC | `CMakeLists.txt:309`: compiled path `/tmp/aurora-diag[-beta].log`. Readers/writers include `session_video.c` (session open/rotation), `coop_coord.c`, `session.c`, `session_worker.c`, audio/input/pointer, webOS probe/wakelock, connection, WoL and PIN logging. Harness independently derives the same path from `COOP_APP` (`run_coop.sh:45-50`) and reads only its session window. | New session marker and increasing mtime on the selected path, attributable to the selected executable. A readable old/live log is not evidence about beta. Filename is compiled; a shell environment assignment cannot redirect the C process. |
| `AURORA_SECONDARY_LOG` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `CMakeLists.txt:310`: compiled path `/tmp/coop_secondary[-beta].log`; append opens in `coop_secondary.c:62,193,420,642,666`, `session_worker.c:64,131`, and secondary-mode branch of `coop_audio.c:93`. | Fresh secondary startup/session records in the variant path, plus secondary process identity. Empty/stale file does not prove spawn or connection. Rebuild required to change path. |
| `AURORA_MULTIVIEW_LOG` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `CMakeLists.txt:311`: compiled path `/tmp/coop_multiview[-beta].log`, opened by `src/app/platform/webos/app_webos.c:99`; failed open returns before reading the probe script at `:109`. | Fresh probe log and fresh advancing TV screenshot timestamps. A missing writable log can prevent capture entirely; compile-time path, not a runtime environment knob. |
| `COOP_COORD_SOCKET` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `coop_coord.h:24-26` fallback `/tmp/aurora-coop.sock`; CMake overrides it with `/tmp/aurora-coop[-beta].sock` (`CMakeLists.txt:320`). `session_video.c` passes it to coordinator start and secondary spawn. | Effective compile definition plus listening UNIX socket and successful secondary connection for that variant. Path existence alone may be a stale socket. Both processes must use the same variant path. |
| `COOP_INPUT_SOCKET` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `coop_input.h:40-42` fallback `/tmp/aurora-coop-input.sock`; `CMakeLists.txt:321` adds the variant suffix. `coop_input.c:58-61` builds both endpoint addresses; `:128,157` unlinks it during listen/stop. | Effective compiler definition, fresh `[COOP-INPUT] coordinator listening on ...` line (`coop_input.c:145`), and a peer-routed input event reaching the intended host. Socket existence alone does not prove delivery. |
| `COOP_AUDIO_SOCKET` | Eclipse CMake / SRC | `coop_audio.h:41-43` fallback `/tmp/aurora-coop-audio.sock`; `CMakeLists.txt:322` adds the variant suffix. `coop_audio.c:127` builds the endpoint, `:189,230` handles stale/closed socket paths. | Effective compile definition, fresh listen/connect diagnostics (`coop_audio.c:218-219`) and audible peer audio or received sample progression in a live session. A stale socket proves neither connection nor audio. |
| `AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG` | Eclipse CMake option / SRC | `CMakeLists.txt` (webOS block): `option(AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG ... OFF)`. When ON it compiles `src/app/platform/webos/vendor_diag.c` into `moonlight-lib`, defines `AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG=1` (turning the `vendor_diag_*` calls in `app.c`, `session_audio.c`, `session_video.c` from inline no-ops into real calls), exports the four PmLog entry points via `-Wl,--dynamic-list=src/app/platform/webos/vendor_diag.list`, and widens the export guard's allowlist to them. Diagnostic builds ONLY (docs/webos26-media-load.md). | Latched at configure time (`CMakeCache.txt` shows `AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG:BOOL=`). Proof it applied: `arm-webos-linux-gnueabi-nm -D --defined-only bin/aurora` lists `PmLogGetContext PmLogSetContextLevel PmLogString_ _PmLogMsgKV`; on the TV, `/tmp/aurora-crash.log` gains `=== diag start pid N` at app launch (the arm at `SS4S_PostInit`). OFF proof: `nm -D` shows only `_init _fini` and `strings bin/aurora` has no `aurora-vendor.log` (both measured 2026-09-26, release ipk `50982e6d...`). |
| `AURORA_VENDOR_LOG`, `AURORA_CRASH_LOG`, `AURORA_STDERR_LOG`, `HANG_FIRST_DUMP_MS`, `HANG_SECOND_DUMP_MS` | Eclipse (vendor_diag.c, CMake) / SRC | Compiled paths, set per variant by CMake (`/tmp/aurora-{vendor,crash,stderr}${AURORA_TMP_SUFFIX}.log`), header defaults `/tmp/aurora-vendor.log` (vendor media libraries' PmLog text, NDL/`smp.*`/`avc.*`), `/tmp/aurora-crash.log` (ARM/DONE per armed vendor call, `=== FATAL signal` records, `=== HANG` dumps at 8 s and 30 s with raw pc/lr/stack words plus `/proc/self/maps`), `/tmp/aurora-stderr.log` (fd 2 redirected at the first arm: loader errors, GLib/GStreamer warnings). Exist only with `AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG=ON`. `HANG_FIRST_DUMP_MS` = 8000 and `HANG_SECOND_DUMP_MS` = 30000 are how long an armed vendor call may block before a `=== HANG` dump; each dump signals every thread (`SIGRTMIN+5`), so poll/select/nanosleep in the target return EINTR: treat behaviour after the first dump as perturbed. | Files are created by the APP uid in sticky `/tmp`: `prisoner` cannot delete them, and each run APPENDS -- split on `=== diag start pid` / `=== vendor log opened pid` and use the newest pid. Raw addresses must be symbolised offline against the TV's own libraries using the maps block in the same record. |
| `ctm_log` (was `g_log` until 2026-09-26) | Eclipse CTM passthrough / SRC | `src/app/hid_passthrough/ctm/ctm_state.c`: the CTM log text buffer. Written by `log_append()`; no reader in the tree. Renamed because as `g_log` the executable EXPORTED it (GNU ld exports an executable global that a linked DSO references) and every library that calls `g_log()` through its PLT (libgstreamer; libglib's own calls bind locally) resolved it to this buffer: the webOS 26 stream-start failure (docs/webos26-media-load.md). | Build-time proof: `cmake/CheckExecutableExports.cmake` runs POST_BUILD on the webOS executable and fails if `nm -D --defined-only` lists anything beyond `_init _fini __bss_start _edata _end` (plus the 4 PmLog symbols in a diag build); it prints `Export guard passed` on success and warns `Export guard SKIPPED` when there is no `CMAKE_NM`. Runtime proof of the original fault: a diag build's `=== FATAL signal 11` record with pc == the address of `B g_log`. |

**§12 silent-application extension:** the build identity, log and socket selectors above are
latched by compilation. Verify packaged metadata **and** executable identity after a rebuild;
installation does not stop a running app. `APP_TITLE` is banner-only in the shell. The selected
harness log must match the compiled log, or successful reads can observe an unrelated session.

## 23. AVC numbering and renderer API ownership (2026-09-09)

Source/API meaning below is evidenced; the application tests are named designs, **not newly
measured runtime results**. Resolve implementation changes against these observables.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `comp_fn` | Aurora / SRC | `src/app/stream/video/coop_avc.h:197`: internal composite picture-number state. Reset to 0 at session reset; `coop_avc_advance_fn()` in `session_video.c` sets the next number to 1 after IDR, otherwise increments modulo the composite SPS width (guarded to 4..16 bits). `coop_avc.c` reads it in paired, concealed and catch-up slice rewrites; the paired IDR path writes 0 instead. This is not either host's input number. | Parse every slice of each accepted composite: both panes must share one number, IDRs carry 0, next reference picture carries 1 after IDR, and non-IDR progression wraps at the SPS modulus. Include catch-up, conceal, repeat and injected feed refusal in a local control-flow test. At registration the four callers advance before feed acceptance; a comment saying "after feeding" does not establish acceptance. Compare number advancement to accepted feeds before claiming this risk fixed. |
| `frame_num` | ITU-T H.264 / DOC | [H.264 (08/2024), clause 7.4.3](https://www.itu.int/rec/T-REC-H.264-202408-I/en): coded-picture identifier carried in `log2_max_frame_num_minus4 + 4` bits; IDR pictures require value 0. Reference-picture progression and permitted gaps follow the clause's constraints, not a generic frame-rate counter. `comp_fn` is Aurora's source state used to write this externally defined bitstream field. | Inspect live output slice headers and SPS; compare all slices in each access unit, IDR resets, modulus and reference progression. Source-host and composite fields must be audited separately because catch-up changes composite picture count. Do not infer conformance merely from `ok=` or the host's frame number. |
| `nal_ref_idc` | ITU-T H.264 / DOC; local storage SRC | [H.264 clause 7.4.1](https://www.itu.int/rec/dologin_pub.asp?id=T-REC-H.264-200903-S%21%21PDF-E&lang=e&type=items): 2-bit NAL header field; for coded slices, zero marks a non-reference picture and nonzero marks a reference picture. IDR cannot use zero; picture slices must agree in reference/non-reference status. Aurora parses `(nal[0] >> 5) & 3` in `coop_avc_slice_info()` and override-writes it in `coop_avc_rewrite_slice_impl()` (`coop_avc.c:230,366`). | Inspect actual output NAL header bits plus matching reference-marking syntax, not only the stored override. Test source/destination zero/nonzero combinations: the existing rewriter gates the source marking read by the destination value (`coop_avc.c:443-466`), so changed zero-ness must be explicitly supported or rejected. Canonical holds must remain reference pictures if subsequent peer slices depend on them. |
| `pic_order_cnt_type`, `poc_type` | ITU-T H.264 / DOC; Aurora alias SRC | [H.264 clause 7.4.2.1.1](https://www.itu.int/rec/dologin_pub.asp?id=T-REC-H.264-201402-S%21%21PDF-E&lang=e&type=items): SPS selector 0..2 for picture-order derivation, with type-2 restrictions on consecutive non-reference pictures. Aurora stores this as `coop_avc_sps_t.poc_type` (`coop_avc.h:28`), reads it in SPS parse (`coop_avc.c:143-147`), rejects type 1 there, compares peer equality (`:195`), and branches slice syntax by it. | Parse both source SPSs and the composite SPS. Canonical-hold tests must show type 2 accepted and unsupported modes refused; never force the SPS field to 2 while keeping incompatible picture syntax. Test composite output ordering across IDR and number wrap, then fresh live panel progression. |
| `max_num_ref_frames` | ITU-T H.264 / DOC; local storage SRC | [H.264 clause 7.4.2.1.1 and 8.2.5.3](https://www.itu.int/rec/dologin_pub.asp?id=T-REC-H.264-201610-S%21%21PDF-E&lang=s&type=items): maximum reference-picture occupancy used for inter prediction and sliding-window reference marking, not a throughput setting. `coop_avc.c:149` stores it, `:196` requires equality between sources, and `:279` preserves it when writing the composite SPS. Equality alone does not restrict both sources to one reference. | Parse both source SPSs and emitted SPS; test single-reference canonical hold acceptance and explicit rejection of unsupported multi-reference inputs. Lowering this field alone does not rewrite source reference dependencies. A decoder reporting no errors is insufficient; a held pane must preserve pixels while the other pane advances. |
| `pic_parameter_set_id` | ITU-T H.264 / DOC | [H.264 clauses 7.4.2.2 and 7.4.3](https://www.itu.int/rec/dologin_pub.asp?id=T-REC-H.264-201201-S%21%21PDF-E&lang=e&type=items): identifies the PPS used by the slice, range 0..255; all slices of one coded picture use the same id. Current Aurora PPS parse discards its id (`coop_avc.c:167`), and slice rewrite copies the source id (`:416`); at registration there is no local `pps_id` member. | Test nonzero, differing source PPS ids: every emitted slice must reference the actual emitted composite PPS id, including synthetic holds. Verify by parsing emitted PPS and slice headers; successful parsing with assumed id 0 is not evidence that arbitrary ids work. |
| `vdec_stream_target_fps` | Aurora / SRC | `session_video.c:70` defaults to 60; renderer setup (`:3260`) assigns positive `redrawRate`, otherwise 60. Existing readers set the player frame-rate numerator (`:3350`) and scale the HEVC scheduled-request frame threshold (`:6708-6709`). It is negotiated/requested timing state, not measured source rate. | Verify the setup value and actual player timing. If used by the new AVC worker to derive a display coalescing interval, record that derived interval and measure peer-only arrival-to-feed delay with primary receipts flat; the worker must release a lone pending peer within that bound. That additional reader was not yet saved at registration, so scheduling behavior remains OPEN until its source and measurement exist. |
| `CAPABILITY_PULL_RENDERER` | moonlight-common-c / DOC | Primary owner checkout `core/moonlight-common-c@9c900f2`, `src/Limelight.h:266-269,911-922`: bit `0x20` requests client-owned video dequeue/complete lifecycle. Every successfully dequeued frame must receive `LiCompleteVideoFrame()` with its decode status. `Connection.c:214-226` rejects a submit callback or simultaneous direct-submit flag; `VideoStream.c:292-302,351` omits its decoder thread in pull mode. | Verify selected capability bits and absent submit callback at connection start, then instrument successful dequeue/completion balance and client renderer thread start/stop. A peer wake test must show accepted peer-only compositions while primary receipts remain flat; configuring pull mode alone does not establish independent progress. `VideoDepacketizer.c:234-311` is the owning wait/poll/wake/complete implementation. |
| `CAPABILITY_DIRECT_SUBMIT` | moonlight-common-c / DOC | `core/moonlight-common-c@9c900f2/src/Limelight.h:239-242`: bit `0x1` dispatches renderer data directly from receive thread and requires a nonblocking renderer. `VideoDepacketizer.c:509-535` bypasses the queue and invokes submit+complete in that branch. `Connection.c:221-226` rejects coexistence with pull mode. | Verify effective bitmask and callback dispatch thread. For a pull renderer this bit must be absent; receiving frames alone does not prove which path ran. |

## 24. Apollo selector and beta-profile build gate (2026-09-09)

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `encoder` | Apollo / DOC | Owner source `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build/src/config.cpp:1190` reads the string from config; `src/video.cpp:2820-2844` prefers the matching named encoder after validation. Names include `nvenc` and `amdvce` (`video.cpp:481,725`); unsuccessful preference logs an error and falls through to further encoder selection. It is a backend preference, not the requested wire codec. | After service reload and new session, inspect actual selected encoder/codec logs (`video.cpp:2936,2946,2957`) and new encoder creation lines. `Trying encoder` lines are probes, not selection proof; a present config value does not prove the matching backend won. §12 service-start config trap applies. |
| `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` | Apollo fork / DOC | Owner `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build/cmake/compile_definitions/common.cmake:136-139`: CMake option defaults ON and emits compile definition `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE=1`. **Updated 2026-09-09 for lease ownership:** apply occurs through `beta_profile::acquire_for_client()` on HTTP launch/resume (`src/nvhttp.cpp:1284,1452`); the typed profile is retained by the actual launch/stream lifetime and restored when its last lease releases (`src/beta_profile.cpp:48-55,123-131`). RTSP control cleanup alone no longer defines stream lifetime. | Verify compile flags, rebuilt/installed/running executable identity, then per-key apply logs for the matching client and the restore log after last lease release. Native `tests/beta_profile/run.py` exercises production parser/lifetime with isolated config; live encoder behavior and following ordinary-client defaults remain separate proofs. A cache ON value or profile-file presence alone proves no session application. |
| `client_uuid`, `client` | Apollo beta-profile file / DOC | Owner `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build/src/beta_profile.cpp:75-94,136-149`: repeated nonempty `client_uuid` entries select exact paired-certificate UUIDs; repeated `client` entries select exact certificate names. **OR matching:** any UUID or name match selects the profile; a UUID line does not disable name matching. This is `beta_profile.conf` syntax, not a sunshine.conf key. | In isolated `tests/beta_profile/run.py`/`lifecycle.cc`, different UUIDs with the same generic name must leave ordinary settings unchanged and select beta overrides only for the beta UUID. Fresh actual-session logs must name that UUID and changed values; incompatible concurrent profiles must reject without mutation. Prefer UUID-only selectors when both installations pair under the same generic certificate name. |

## Gauges (`[COOP-*]` diag labels)

| gauge | line | symbol | shape | ev | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `vmirror` | `Coop` (`"Armed %s: vmirror=%d hh-tiles=%d slices=%d"`) | literal `vertical ? 1 : 0` | LEVEL, computed at print time — NOT a stored variable and NOT the `/tmp/coop-vmirror` sentinel's value (the sentinel is written from this same boolean, but the printed field is the local `vertical` flag, not a file read) | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/video/coop_tunables.c:126-129`, inside `coop_tunables_arm()`. **WHAT IT DOES:** prints `1` iff this session armed VERTICAL orientation, `0` iff HORIZONTAL — it is a restatement of the orientation the line already names ("Armed vertical" / "Armed horizontal"), nothing more. **IT CARRIES NO INFORMATION ABOUT WHETHER A REAL SECOND HOST IS FEEDING THE PEER PANE.** `vmirror=1` is printed for every vertical co-op session without exception, genuine two-host or (hypothetically) not — **[LEAD CORRECTION 2026-09-26, SRC: `session_video.c:3961` gates the vertical composite on `coop_vt_enabled && coop_vt_buf` only -- no coordinator check -- and `:4173-4178` fills the peer column with OUR pane whenever no peer pane has ever arrived. So vertical armed with NO secondary IS a one-host self-mirror for the whole session (`mirrored=` climbs with `frames=`), and CLAUDE.md's use of `/tmp/coop-vmirror` for a single-host self-mirror is RIGHT. What stands: the sentinel is ALSO armed for every two-host vertical session, so `vmirror=1` alone says nothing about whether a real peer fed the pane -- read `mirrored=` against `frames=`/`peer=` and a second host's launch line. Any wording here that vertical has no self-mirror path is WRONG.]** there is in fact no automatic self-mirror composition path in vertical mode at all (see `/tmp/coop-vmirror`'s Sentinels row); the nearest analogue, `coop_vt_mirrored` (`[COOP-VT] mirrored=`), is a one-shot pre-peer bootstrap fallback, not a mode. **COLLISION, RECORD EXPLICITLY:** two log lines from the SAME `"Coop"` tag, printed seconds apart at session start, carry near-identical-looking fields with UNRELATED meanings: `[Coop] Tunables applied (vertical): ... mirror=%d` (`coop_tunables.c:88-91`) prints `config->coop_self_mirror[o]` — the REAL horizontal-only self-mirror setting, `0` for every vertical session by construction — while `[Coop] Armed vertical: vmirror=%d ...` (this row) prints the unrelated orientation flag. Read side by side they can look like agreement ("mirror=0" then "vmirror=1") or, misread quickly, like the opposite — neither line says anything about the OTHER's subject. **HOW YOU KNOW WHETHER THE PEER WAS REAL, since this field cannot tell you:** read `[COOP-VT] ... peer=%u ... mirrored=%u` on the SAME session's periodic diag line instead — `mirrored` climbing in lockstep with `frames` (not frozen at a small constant) is the actual signature of no-real-peer; `peer=` climbing into the thousands with `mirrored` frozen at 1 is the signature of a genuine peer. **MEASURED 2026-09-24** (`aurora-diag.log`, Windows Eclipse client, 4 co-op sessions): all 4 printed `vmirror=1`; the 3 sessions long enough to reach a 600-frame checkpoint (pids 25936, 15356, 1824) each showed `mirrored=1` frozen for their entire run (up to `frames=35400`) while `peer=` climbed to `21454` — i.e. real, sustained peer reception, not a mirror. The 4th (pid 12860, too short for a checkpoint) is independently confirmed real by distinct GameStream host IPs for primary (`192.0.2.19`, PC2) vs secondary (`192.0.2.18`, VEGA) plus an OS-pid cross-check (`(process:20248)` GLib warning, `20248` = the logged secondary pid). Full detail: `scratchpad/run/RUN.md`, `[param-registrar 2026-09-26]`. |

| `slice_type_form` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_rehome_t.slice_type_form` | const/-1 | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/video/coop_avc.h:66`, applied `coop_avc.c` in `coop_avc_rewrite_slice_impl()`. **IT IS THE OTHER HALF'S `slice_type`, and only two things are taken from it: its FORM (0..4 vs 5..9) and, since 2026-09-06, whether its TYPE agrees with the slice being rewritten.** `-1` means "leave this slice's form alone". **WHAT THE FORM IS:** H.264 7.4.3 — `slice_type` in 5..9 asserts that every OTHER slice of the coded picture has that type or that type minus 5. **MEASURED 2026-09-06** (this is what promotes the old source comment from CLAIM to evidence): VEGA's **AMF emits 5..9 for 100% of slices** — 823 slices across the `toppad1` and `swapA` netcap peer streams, `5` for P and `7` for IDR, zero exceptions — while **NVENC emits 0..4** (composite histogram `{0,2}`, 0% in 5..9). **THE TRAP:** the composite's steady state MIXES types (converted all-intra peer = I, primary = P), so carrying a 5..9 form there has each slice asserting a picture-wide type the other denies. Never force the peer's VALUE either — that rewrote a peer P as I at the primary's second IDR and desynchronised the decoder (2026-08-30). **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT:** `python3 scripts/coop/nal_audit.py <stream>` prints `NONCONFORMANT: N picture(s) mix slice types while using the 5..9 form` and exits 1. Proven against a known-bad instant: the pre-fix build emits 47/60 nonconformant pictures on a mixed-type composite, the fixed build 0, and output is BYTE-IDENTICAL where the two halves' types agree. **INERT while SKYNET is primary; it fires the moment VEGA is.** |
| `peerconv` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc.converted_idr` | count (resettable) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/coop_avc.c` (`coop_avc_compose`) — composites where the PEER's slice was an IDR NAL and the primary's was not, so the peer's NAL type was rewritten 5 -> 1 keeping `slice_type` I. A coded picture may not mix IDR and non-IDR slices (H.264 7.4.1.2.4), and refusing instead would drop EVERY composite against an all-intra peer, which is the steady state of the zero-artefact configuration (AMF has no constrained-encoding property, so all-intra is the only setting that stops the AMD peer predicting across the seam). **It climbing at roughly the composite rate is the proof the peer really is all-intra** — with an inter-coded peer it stays near 0. **NAMED `peerconv`, NOT `conv`**: `conv[kr= fb=]` is a WITHDRAWN gauge and a reader looking up `conv` gets exit 3 for a live counter |
| `slices` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc.mine_slices` + `.peer_slices` | two counts, printed `N+M` | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/coop_avc.c` (`coop_avc_compose`) — VCL slices taken from the primary and from the peer in the LAST composite (reset each picture, not cumulative). Exists because nothing else distinguishes "the host ignored the slice request" from "it honoured it and the seam is still wrong": NVENC's constrained encoding is INERT on a single-slice picture, so `2+2` is the precondition for the primary's zero-contamination result and `1+1` silently voids it. Reaching `2+2` requires `CAPABILITY_SLICES_PER_FRAME` advertised for H.264 AND `coop_slices_h = 2` in `moonlight.ini` |

| `droplast` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_droplast` | count (resettable) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — times the sequencer's `drop_lastpeer` verdict made the AVC path DISCARD both hold donors (`coop_avc_lastpane` and `coop_avc_lastpslice`). **WHY IT EXISTS:** discarding them is correct — a donor the composite has moved past would splice a pane it never showed — but it also **silently disables the repeat fallback**, whose only symptom is `avcnopane` rising for no visible reason. Nothing counted it, so a run where the fallback was effectively off looked identical to one where it was never needed. **CORRECTED 2026-09-06, SAME DAY IT WAS ADDED — IT IS A HOLE COUNTER, NOT A DISARM COUNTER.** The clear is normally UNDONE ON THE SAME PICTURE: the hole pane goes on to compose, and the donor keep at the end of the take repopulates both donors. So this counts peer frame-number HOLE EVENTS, which is not what it was added for. It is still the cheapest hole gauge on the path and `seqidr` should track it closely — **a divergence between `droplast` and `seqidr` means a hole took one branch but not the other, and that is worth reading.** The disarm case it was meant to catch is the narrow one where the picture does NOT go on to compose, so nothing repopulates: `droplast` up, `avcrepeat` flat and `avcnopane` rising together. |
| `seqidr` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_idrreq_seq` | count (resettable) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — **the THIRD peer-keyframe requester, and the one nothing counted until 2026-09-06.** `coop_seq_verdict()` sets `request_idr_keep` on every peer frame-number HOLE (`coop_seq.c`), and both AVC take sites honour it — the normal take and catch-up. **`avcrefresh` never included these**, so the wire rate (`idrreq`) could exceed everything `avcrefresh` explained with no way to attribute the difference. **WHY IT MATTERS NOW:** a source comment read "frame_num gaps are INSTRUMENTATION ONLY … they no longer trigger", which is true of `coop_avc_chain_broken` and FALSE of the wire — a reader took gaps to be free. They cost one throttled request each. With the latch now asking once per BREAK, this may be the DOMINANT requester; that is the thing to read first when `idrreq` sent is high. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** the field appears and moves on a run with peer frame-number holes. **CORRECTED 2026-09-06 — the identity this row used to assert (`b + c + seqidr` accounts for `idrreq` sent + suppressed) IS FALSE and was never checkable.** It fails twice over: (1) a FOURTH requester existed and was uncounted — the AVC suppression path (`session_video.c`, `!coop_avc_pending_idr && !hh_paired`) requests on every suppressed picture, which before bootstrap is nearly all of them and mid-session is every `avcnopane`/`avcoversize`/our-IDR-with-empty-sticky picture; and (2) the units never matched — `b` and `c` count CONFIRMED SENDS (the same-day fix that stopped a throttled request latching the break flag) while `seqidr` counts ATTEMPTS, so their sum mixes sends with attempts. **Use `idrall` for the accounting test** (see its own row): it counts attempts at all four AVC sites and gives the exact identity. `seqidr` remains what it says — the sequencer's share, in attempts. |
| `idrall` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_idrreq_all` | count (resettable) | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — **peer-keyframe requests ATTEMPTED by the AVC path, all four requesters, counted in one unit so the accounting closes.** Added 2026-09-06 after a static read of HEAD found the fourth requester (the suppression path) uncounted and the `b + c + seqidr` identity unsatisfiable — see the `seqidr` row. **WHAT IT DOES:** incremented immediately before every `coop_coord_request_peer_idr_keep()` on the H.264 co-op path — the sequencer's hole request at both take sites, the once-per-break latch, the `COOP_AVC_PEER_IDR_MAX_MS` cadence, and the suppression path — whether or not the 120 ms throttle then accepts it. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** the field appears in `[COOP-AVC]`, and the identity `idrall == idrreq sent + suppressed` must hold EXACTLY. On an H.264 co-op session every other `coop_coord_request_peer_idr*` caller in the tree is excluded — the `[COOP-HH]` and `[COOP-LIVE]` sites by `!(vdec_stream_format & VIDEO_FORMAT_MASK_H264)`, the `[COOP-VT]` sites by the `!coop_vt_enabled` guard on the AVC branch — leaving only the manual `/tmp/coop-dump-now` trigger, which fires only when that file is touched. **So a NON-ZERO residual is the detector this row exists to be: it means a requester outside the AVC path put traffic on the wire.** The difference `idrall - (b + c + seqidr)` is the suppression path's share plus the attempts `b`/`c` refused to record because the throttle swallowed them; it is NOT expected to be zero. NOT LIVE-TESTED — added offline, never yet read off a run. |
| `avcrefresh` | COOP-AVC | `coop_avc_refreshes`, split `coop_avc_idrreq_break` / `coop_avc_idrreq_cadence` | count (resettable) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — **emitted `avcrefresh=<total>/b<break>/c<cadence>` since 2026-09-06.** Peer-keyframe requests this path CALLED (not what reached the wire — that is `idrreq`). **THE OLD DESCRIPTION, "repeat budget spent", NAMED A MECHANISM THAT NEVER EXISTED**: `COOP_AVC_REPEAT_BUDGET` was audited as never implemented (WITHDRAWN table) and `COOP_AVC_REFRESH_PICTURES`, the 600-composite timer, was deleted 2026-08-31 (also WITHDRAWN). There are exactly two callers now, and the split exists because the total could not tell them apart: **`b`** = the chain-broken latch, which since 2026-09-06 asks **ONCE PER BREAK** (`coop_avc_break_asked`) instead of once per picture; **`c`** = the `COOP_AVC_PEER_IDR_MAX_MS` cadence. **What the split decides:** a rising `b` with few breaks means the latch is re-asking and one-per-break regressed; a rising `c` against a peer whose `gop_length=300` should re-anchor every ~5 s means the peer is not honouring requests. Before the split, `avcrefresh=63` over 15 s could not distinguish those. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** the field prints three numbers, and `b` <= the number of distinct breaks (`cb[cudrop+adrop+evict]`). |

| `cubig` | COOP-VT | `coop_vt_cu_big` | count (resettable) | SRC+TEST | **WINDOWS-ONLY** (rpsfix3, 2026-09-23/24) — declared `session_video.c:491` inside the `#ifdef _WIN32` block opened at `:488`; the webOS/TV build compiles no such symbol and never prints this label. Reset with the other co-op counters at session start, `:3459` (same gate). **WHAT IT DOES:** increments once per catch-up-built peer slice whose re-tiled length exceeds 256 KiB — `:1525 if (pl > 256u * 1024u) { coop_vt_cu_big++; }`, inside `coop_vt_build_catchup()` — i.e. a slice the OLD `coop_vt_skip_tile` buffer would have refused. That buffer is sized `1u << 20` (1 MiB) under `_WIN32` (`:522`) versus `256u * 1024u` (256 KiB) on every other build (`:524`, the unchanged webOS size) — the 1 MB widening is what stopped these slices being silently dropped by the re-tile call, per the diagnostic cited in the comment at `:520-523` ("12 of 247 ordinary peer slices > 256 KB, 0 of 152 catch-up ones"). **COLLISION, RECORD EXPLICITLY — do not confuse with `cb[cudrop]` at this register's §21 (`cfail[...]`, line ~958):** that is `coop_avc_cb_cudrop`, a DIFFERENT symbol on the H.264 `[COOP-AVC]` splice path, which counts `coop_coord_take_next`'s dropped-panes out-param latching `coop_avc_chain_broken`. Confirmed by grep: no `coop_avc.c`/`coop_avc.h`/`coop_avc_conceal.c` exists anywhere in this tree (that whole path was deleted, uncommitted, on this branch — see the WITHDRAWN table's `avccomposed`/`avcbroken` family). `cubig`/`cudrop` here are `[COOP-VT]`-line HEVC counters on the LIVE co-op path; the AVC `cb[cudrop]` row is a different line, a different meaning, and its own backing code is gone. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** printed as `cubig=%u` on the `[COOP-VT]` diag line only in a Windows-compiled build (`:4505-4513`; the `#else` branch a few lines below omits it entirely) — its absence from a webOS log is expected and is NOT evidence of anything. **MEASURED**, `scratchpad/run/RUN.md`, run `rpsfix3` (exe `5d8d0e9a`, 2026-09-23/24 session): final diag line read `cubig=267` against `frames=11400 recv=11458 cudrop=1`, rising in bursts tied to scroll activity (16 by 23:55:16, 91 by 23:56:20, 249 by 23:58:04, 267 by 23:58:18), not steadily — catch-up rate itself was not constant (flat 888 23:56:20-23:57:00, 1384 after 23:58:18). Both panes screenshotted clean at +15s/+76s/+3m08s/+3m32s across four scroll bursts. **The falsifier pass ruled the underlying MECHANISM (a >256 KB catch-up re-tile silently drops a pane) ESTABLISHED** offline (tile clean through pic 209, damaged from pic 210 on — the first picture carrying a >256 KB peer slice) — but **"1 MB alone fixed it" remains a HYPOTHESIS, not a finding**: `rpsfix3` also carries the keepref + conceal-POC changes from `rpsfix2`, so this n=1 run cannot isolate the buffer-size change by itself; a decisive run needs baseline + the 1 MB widening + these two counters, and nothing else changed. |
| `cudrop` | COOP-VT | `coop_vt_cu_drop` | count (resettable) | SRC+TEST | **WINDOWS-ONLY**, same `#ifdef _WIN32` declaration site as `cubig` (`session_video.c:491`), same session-start reset (`:3459`), absent from the webOS build. **WHAT IT DOES — two distinct increment sites, both inside the vertical catch-up loop, both gated `#ifdef _WIN32`:** (1) `:4065` — the sequencer wanted to take a peer pane off the coordinator queue but either nothing was there or the take was refused: `if (cl == 0 || !cv.accept) { if (cl > 0) { coop_vt_cu_drop++; } break; }`. It only counts when `cl > 0`, i.e. a pane genuinely existed and was taken off the queue (`coop_coord_take_next`) and then never fed — an empty queue alone does not increment it. (2) `:4074` — `coop_vt_build_catchup()` itself returned 0 after a successful take, so the POC is rolled back: `if (cn == 0) { coop_vt_poc--; coop_vt_cu_drop++; break; }`. Site (2) is where an oversize-refusal (the `cubig` class) would land before the 1 MB widening; site (1) is unrelated to slice size (an `!cv.accept`/chain-verdict refusal) — so `cudrop` is the BROADER "took a pane off the peer's chain and then lost it" counter, of which `cubig` explains only a subset of occurrences. **COLLISION, RECORD EXPLICITLY — same note as `cubig` above:** this is UNRELATED to `cb[cudrop]` / `coop_avc_cb_cudrop` at this register's §21 (line ~958), which lives on the deleted `coop_avc.c` H.264 splice path (confirmed absent from the tree by grep) and counts a different event on a different, dead path. Do not read one row for the other. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** printed as `cudrop=%u` on `[COOP-VT]` (`:4505-4513`), Windows-build only. **MEASURED**, RUN.md, run `rpsfix3` (exe `5d8d0e9a`): final `cudrop=1`. The falsifier pass found this same value **already present at frames=600 with `cubig=0`** — i.e. that one drop is NOT a size drop (it is the site-(1) `!cv.accept` class, not the oversize-refusal class `cubig` tracks) — and it stayed at 1 through the rest of the run (frames=1800..13200), so the 1 MB widening did not make this loss class worse. **Accounting caveat:** the run log's own "recv == peer+catchup+evicted+cudrop" closure was reported exact at frames=11400/13200, but the falsifier pass found the frames=1800 checkpoint OFF BY ONE (1769+298+13+1=2081 vs recv=2082, attributed to residue in the 16-slot `COOP_Q_SLOTS` queue) — treat the identity as approximately-closing, not exact at every sample point. |
| `cuirap` | COOP-VT | `coop_vt_cu_irap` | count (resettable) | SRC | **WINDOWS-ONLY** (added 2026-09-27, issue #27), declared beside `cudrop`/`cubig` in the same `#ifdef _WIN32` block, reset with them at session start. **WHAT IT DOES:** counts the times the VERTICAL catch-up loop stopped IN FRONT of a peer IDR -- `coop_coord_next_is_idr(cp.min_frame)` (a read-only peek of the coordinator queue, also `_WIN32`) said the pane `take_next` would hand out is an IDR, so catch-up breaks and leaves it for the normal path, which converts it. Before this, catch-up TOOK the IDR and `coop_vt_build_catchup()` refused it (IRAP beside an all-skip tile), so the re-anchoring keyframe was lost (`cudrop`) and later panes predicted from a picture the decoder never had -- persistent blocks in static areas (runs W4/W5 horizontal, where the HH loop's equivalent was `why[4]`; the HH loop now counts its stops in `why[4]`, and the builder's own IRAP refusal is unreachable from there). **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `cuirap=%u` on the Windows `[COOP-VT]` line; for horizontal, `why[4]` > 0 with `build` NOT rising alongside it, and `converted` rising (the normal path took the IDR). The picture: no persistent blocks in the secondary's tile after a bootstrap that evicted peer panes. |
| `demoted` | COOP-VT | `coop_vt_demoted` | count (resettable) | SRC | **WINDOWS-ONLY** (added 2026-09-28, issue #42), declared beside `cudrop`/`cubig`/`cuirap` in the same `#ifdef _WIN32` block, reset with them at session start. **WHAT IT DOES:** counts OUR (primary) IDR pictures that had no peer IRAP to pair with and were folded into a TRAIL_R picture on the composite's POC timeline (`coop_slice_idr_to_trail_keepref` at `coop_vt_poc + 1`), instead of going out beside the peer's repeated P-slice -- the picture FFmpeg rejected (`Ignoring POC change between slices`). Counted at the DECISION, before the compose: read it with `refused`. First one also logs `[COOP-VT] our IDR had no peer IRAP to pair with -- folded into a TRAIL picture at composite POC N (#42)`. | PROVE: trigger a primary encoder restart without a peer IDR (scratchpad/g7run.sh: this PC's virtual display 1280x1440 -> 1920x1080 -> back). Known-bad 50061f21 (G7A): 2 "Ignoring POC change", pane corrupted. 30f91cb8 (G7B): 0, `demoted=1`, pane whole (DDA pictures). With the clearing re-armed (next build) two restarts should read `demoted=2`. |
| `demotefail` | COOP-VT | `coop_vt_demote_fail` | count (resettable) | SRC | **WINDOWS-ONLY** (2026-09-28, #42). Times the demotion was eligible (our IDR, peer not IRAP, a composite picture exists) but not done: our AU had more than one slice, or `coop_slice_idr_to_trail_keepref` refused (returned 0, e.g. slice > 1 MB demote buffer). The picture then goes out exactly as before the fix. | Expected 0; any non-zero value means the old rejection path ran -- check for "Ignoring POC change" in the same window. |
| `idrstale` | COOP-VT | `coop_vt_idr_stale` | count (resettable) | SRC | **PC-DECODER BUILDS ONLY** (`COOP_PC_DECODER`: Windows + desktop Linux; added 2026-10-04), reset with `demoted` at session start. **WHAT IT DOES:** counts OUR IRAP pictures where the sticky peer IDR was SUPERSEDED -- its peer frame number is below the last pane the composite accepted (`coop_seq_idr_superseded`: `idr_frame + 1 < peer_next`) -- so it was NOT paired; the take is treated as "no IDR", which asks the peer for a fresh one (KEEP request) and lets #42 fold our IDR into a TRAIL picture (`demoted` rises with it). Only taken when #42 can fold: a last peer pane exists, `coop_vt_poc_init`, and our AU is single-slice. First one also logs `[COOP-VT] our IRAP found only a superseded peer IDR (frame N, composite past M) -- not paired`. **WHY:** the 10-04 session (VEGA primary/PC2 peer) shows VEGA's mid-session IDR paired with PC2's IDR from 36 s earlier (`demoted`, `demotefail`, `mirrored` all unchanged across it -- INFERRED from counters, not observed in a bitstream). | PROVE: mid-session, make the PRIMARY host IDR while the peer streams (open a fullscreen game on it / change its display mode). Expect `idrstale` +1 and `demoted` +1 in the same 600-frame window, the one-shot line, a `peer asked for an IDR` line in the secondary log right after, and on screen the peer pane must NOT jump back to an older picture. Known-bad (pre-fix): `demoted`, `demotefail`, `mirrored` all unchanged across the primary's IDR. |

| `idr_sent` | DIAG (untagged — see note) | `diag_idr_sent` | count (resettable, per-session at `session_video.c:2536`) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:5392` (increment); printed at `session_video.c:5381-5382` (periodic `[%lu] ok=... idr_sent=...` line) and `:2896` (`=== session ended ===` summary) — **narrower than "the client requested an IDR from the host"**. It counts ONLY calls to `LiRequestIdrFrame()` made from the `SS4S_VIDEO_FEED_REQUEST_KEYFRAME` branch (`session_video.c:5388-5398`) of the per-frame `SS4S_PlayerVideoFeed()` result handler, gated by `IDR_REQUEST_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` (1000ms, `:5390`, `:58`). On this TV's actual decoder module (webOS SMP, `third_party/ss4s/modules/webos/smp/src/smp_video.c:71-84`), that result is returned for exactly one condition: `StarfishPlayerFeed()` reported `SMP_FEED_BUFFER_FULL` — i.e. this is a **client hardware-decoder buffer-full signal**, not a generic "decoder wants a keyframe" or "stream looks broken" signal. **It does NOT include**: the periodic HEVC `idr_refresh_interval_sec` keyframe request (`LiRequestIdrFrame()` called directly at `session_video.c:5348`, no counter touched at all), or any co-op peer-chain-break IDR request (`coop_coord_request_peer_idr()`/`_keep()`, counted separately by `coord_idr_sent` and printed as `idrreq=` in `[COOP-HH]`/`[COOP-VT]` lines — see that row). **`idr_sent=0` therefore does NOT mean "the TV requested zero IDRs of the host" in general** — it means the buffer-full path never fired (and never had to throttle either, see `idr_throttled`). Verify a change took effect: force `SMP_FEED_BUFFER_FULL` (e.g. saturate the platform decoder) and watch `idr_sent` increment in the diag line, or watch for the `[%lu] IDR_SENT frame=... ok_so_far=...` line it also emits (`:5393-5396`) |
| `idr_throttled` | DIAG (untagged — see note) | `diag_idr_throttled` | count (resettable, per-session at `session_video.c:2537`) | SRC | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:5400` (increment); printed alongside `idr_sent` at `:5381-5382` and `:2896`. Increments when the SAME `SS4S_VIDEO_FEED_REQUEST_KEYFRAME` condition (buffer-full on webOS SMP, see `idr_sent`) fires again inside the 1000ms `IDR_REQUEST_MIN_INTERVAL_MS` window — in that case `LiRequestIdrFrame()` is NOT called, the frame is silently dropped (`return DR_OK`, not `DR_NEED_IDR`), and only this counter moves. `idr_throttled=0` alongside `idr_sent=0` means the throttle never had anything to suppress — the underlying buffer-full condition never occurred even once, a STRONGER statement than merely "the throttle didn't trigger." Verify a change took effect: force two buffer-full signals within 1s of each other and watch `idr_throttled` increment on the second while `idr_sent` does not, or watch for `[%lu] IDR_THROTTLED frame=... size=...` in the diag log (`:5401-5404`) |

**Collision note**: `diag_idr_sent` (label `idr_sent=`, this table) and `coord_idr_sent` (accessed via `coop_coord_idr_sent()`, label `idrreq=` in `[COOP-HH]`/`[COOP-VT]`, see that row above) are two unrelated counters with confusable C symbol names — one is the client-decoder buffer-full throttle above, the other is the co-op peer-chain-break IDR request counter in `coop_coord.c`. Grepping source for "idr_sent" finds both; the printed diag labels (`idr_sent=` vs `idrreq=`) are the only reliable disambiguator.

**Collision note**: `cudrop`/`cubig` (this table, `[COOP-VT]`, `session_video.c:491`, Windows-only) are UNRELATED to `cb[cudrop]` (§21 `cfail[...]`, line ~958, `coop_avc_cb_cudrop`, `[COOP-AVC]`). The bracketed form lives on the H.264 splice path whose backing files (`coop_avc.c`/`.h`/`coop_avc_conceal.c`) do not exist in this tree (confirmed by grep — deleted, uncommitted, on this branch); the bare form here is a live HEVC counter. Same shape of trap as `held` above — read the LINE TAG (`[COOP-VT]` vs `[COOP-AVC]`) before the label.

**§12 candidate — considered, not added**: `idr_sent`/`idr_throttled` do not silently fail to apply (they are directly and only incremented at the point `LiRequestIdrFrame()` is called or skipped, in the same branch), so they are not a silent-failure case in the §12 sense. What they ARE is narrower in scope than their name suggests, which is a clause-3/clause-5 naming-trap, not a §12 apply-vs-not-apply gap — recorded here and in the collision note instead.

**Coverage gap, parallel to `SHOT_DIR`**: `param_extract.py`'s `surface_gauge()` (`scripts/ctx/param_extract.py:246-295`) only extracts `fprintf(diag_log, ...)` calls whose format string starts with a `[COOP-*]` tag (`:265-267`, `if not tag: continue`). The `idr_sent=`/`idr_throttled=` lines have no such tag (`[%lu] ok=...` and `=== session ended ===`), so — like `scripts/tv/*` for the script surface — these are structurally invisible to `param_check.py` regardless of registration; `--surface gauge` will never flag them missing. This row exists because they were checked by hand, not because the checker caught them. Any other diag counter without a `[COOP-*]` tag (`ok=`, `errors=`, `frame=`, `size=`) has this same gap and is likewise unregistered; not resolving those here per scope, flagging the pattern only.

Verify a change took effect: confirm which host the `AuroraPattern` scheduled task actually started on (e.g. `ssh "$COOP_PEER_SSH" hostname`, or watch that host's desktop for the moving test pattern) — do not infer routing from the printed label, which is `COOP_PEER_LABEL` and can be wrong independently |

Verify it took effect: the peer's blanked rows are visibly flat in the composite, or `ssh "$COOP_PEER_SSH" 'Test-Path <path>'` before the run. Default byte-identical to the PC2 value it replaced |

Unset = the old TV matchers (3840x~1088, 1920x2160/2176), byte-for-byte the old behaviour. Set: launch.sh's local wait and both hosts' `place.ps1` get `-Geom WxH`, which match that width (height within 64) and FAIL CLOSED. **Why it exists:** on the Windows client nothing matched the TV sizes and the fallback chose the widest screen -- the user's own lit monitor (run W1, 09:46). **Proof it applied:** the run prints `PLACE-GEOM: label=SKYNET chosen=<WxH> matched=yes`; the peer's placement is only visible in the stream (Session 0 cannot see VEGA's VDD). |
