| `RATE_TOP` | `${COOP_REFRESH_HZ:-165}` | 6 | SRC | `scripts/coop/stress_coop.sh:126` — **NOT an externally-settable knob**: it is a local derived from `COOP_REFRESH_HZ` (already registered), added in 08e9f657 with that knob. Every cell in `stress_matrix.tsv` is written as a ratio against a 165Hz virtual display, but the AVC co-op composite is capped near 63fps by the TV's H.264 decoder (MaxMBPS 2,073,600, Level 5.2 — see `tv-decoder-ceiling-model` memory), so the whole matrix has to be re-expressed at whatever top rate the rig can actually hold. `RATE_TOP` is that rescaled ceiling. It does two things: (1) drives the preflight rate-gate (`stress_coop.sh:1015-1024`) — `rg_floor = RATE_TOP * 120/165`, and the run refuses to start if either host can't draw at least that fraction; (2) at `stress_coop.sh:1066-1067`, gates a BLIND string substitution over the matrix's `s1`/`s2` scenario columns — `s1="${s1//165/$RATE_TOP}"` — replacing every literal `165` in those cells with the new top rate, run only `if [ "$RATE_TOP" != "165" ]`. **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT** (two independent, code-verified observables): (a) `events.jsonl`'s `phase_start` event (`run_phase`, `stress_coop.sh:730-732`) records the POST-substitution `pc1`/`pc2` scenario strings verbatim (`kvs pc1 "$s1"` / `kvs pc2 "$s2"`); (b) `$s1`/`$s2` are also passed as the `-Scen` argument to `place.ps1`, which sets pattern.html's `?scen=` query param, and pattern.html echoes that back verbatim as its own on-screen `scen=` readout (`pattern.html:357,620`) — reading the pane confirms the value the browser was actually given, independent of the driver's own log. **CAVEAT, confirmed against `stress_matrix.tsv:34`**: row S11 (`both-jitter`, `jitter:60..165`/`jitter:60..165`) DEGENERATES TO A CONSTANT (`jitter:60..60`) if `RATE_TOP` is set to `60` — the substitution is a blind string replace with no awareness of what the number means in each cell, so a rescale that collides with a row's own lower bound silently removes that row's entire point (a beat-frequency test between two free-runners) with no error |

Selects which co-op layout's COMPOSITE geometry the escalation targets: `h` stacks two panes (height doubles, e.g. panes 3840x1088 -> composite 3840x2176), `v` puts them side by side (width doubles). It also decides whether `/tmp/coop-vmirror` is armed. Verify a change took effect: the printed `comp=WxH` column changes shape between runs (taller for `h`, wider for `v`), and the TV's `[COOP-HH]` vs `[COOP-VT]` diag lines say which path ran — do NOT infer the mode from the flag you passed |
| `CODEC` | `hevc` | 1 | SRC | `scripts/coop/decoder_ceiling.sh` — CLI (`--codec hevc\|h264`), NOT an env var (same unconditional-assignment pattern as `MODE`). Sets `hevc = true\|false` in the TV's `moonlight.ini` and selects BOTH the block size used to round panes up (16 for H.264 macroblocks, 32 for HEVC CTBs) and the throughput unit reported (`MB/s` for H.264 per level `MaxMBPS`, `luma/s` for HEVC per level `MaxLumaSr`). Verify a change took effect: the TV's diag line reads `codec=H264` or `codec=H265` — the script prints it in the trailing `[...]` column of every row. **Do not trust the flag alone**: the app REWRITES `moonlight.ini` when it closes, so an edit made in the wrong order is silently lost |

A working composite measures tens of thousands of distinct colours (77,700 on a 4K HEVC desktop, 2026-08-30); a failed decode is **1 colour at 100%**, so the two states are four orders of magnitude apart and the exact threshold is not delicate. Exists because the counters cannot be used for this — every measured black-panel run read `ok=` climbing with `errors=0` ([[screenshot-every-test]]). Verify a change took effect: a run's `colours=` column against the printed PASS/BLACK verdict |

| `COOP_INPUT_PENDING_CAPACITY` | `128` messages | bounded coordinator input backpressure queue | SRC+TEST | **OURS.** Defined in `src/app/stream/input/coop_input.c`. It bounds messages retained after the non-blocking coordinator-to-secondary socket reports backpressure. Mouse-position and sensor states may replace older equivalent states; a controller state may do so only when its button bits and active mask are unchanged (axes/triggers only). Controller-button, arrive/remove, touch, mouse-button, and scroll transitions remain distinct FIFO entries. The UI-loop `coop_input_coord_pump()` drains on `POLLOUT`, so a final controller release is not stranded merely because no later input arrives. **HOW TO PROVE IT TOOK:** `tests/app/test_coop_input_lifecycle.c:testBackpressureKeepsFinalStateAndOrderedEdges` stops reading the peer, sends 200,000 same-button controller states until the socket saturates, appends uniquely numbered mouse-button edges plus controller-down/touch/controller-release transitions, then pumps/drains and requires every edge in order plus the final release. Measured 2026-09-23: PASS natively and under ThreadSanitizer, alongside stop/send/pump descriptor-reuse and secondary callback-vs-detach races (3 tests, 0 failures). **BOUND:** if a peer remains unwritable long enough to fill all 128 entries exclusively with non-coalescible transitions, the next event is dropped rather than blocking the UI; this is a failure bound, not permission to grow latency without limit. |

| `coop_vt_skip_tile` | `1u << 20` (1 MiB) under `#ifdef _WIN32`; `256u * 1024u` (256 KiB) on every other build (webOS/TV included) | static per-tile scratch buffer: 10 call sites | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:519-525` (declared right after the horizontal-only `coop_vt_skip_pane[256u*1024u]`, no relation between the two names beyond both being scratch buffers). **WHAT IT DOES:** one shared static buffer that every vertical catch-up/conceal builder re-tiles a single slice INTO before copying it to the output access unit — `coop_slice_to_tile()`/`coop_slice_set_address()` are handed `sizeof(coop_vt_skip_tile)` as their hard cap at all 10 use sites (`:1446-1452` HH-tiles keepref, `:1493-1498` and `:1521-1529` `coop_vt_build_catchup()` tile-0/tile-1, `:4303-4315` another catch-up caller, `:5058-5063` and `:5140-5145` the HH-tiles conceal sites). A slice larger than the buffer makes the tiling call refuse (return 0), and the caller falls through to its own failure path (return 0 / hold / conceal) — this buffer's size is a hard ceiling on what one slice may be, independent of any other setting. **PLATFORM SPLIT IS NOT COSMETIC:** raised to 1 MiB, Windows-only, by commit carrying the `rpsfix3` fix (2026-09-23/24) after a diagnostic found 12 of 247 ordinary peer slices at 2560x1440 exceeded the OLD 256 KiB size (comment at `:519-522`); the webOS/TV build still ships the original 256 KiB and has NEVER been widened. **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT — WINDOWS ONLY:** `[COOP-VT] ... cubig=%u` (this register's Gauges `cubig` row, `coop_vt_cu_big`) counts slices exceeding a 256 KiB threshold at the ONE catch-up call site gated `#ifdef _WIN32` (`:4524-4526`) — a nonzero, climbing `cubig` with `cudrop` NOT climbing in step is the observable that the 1 MiB buffer accepted slices the 256 KiB build would have refused. **ON WEBOS/TV THERE IS NO OBSERVABLE AT ALL for this exact failure** — see the §12 row added for this parameter. |
| `COOP_DECODE_BUDGET_MBPS` | `24.0` | 24 Mbit/s | **TEST** | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:88`. **WHAT IT DOES:** the DELIVERED-bitrate budget for the composite. Every 2 s the client sums the bytes it actually fed, divides by the window, and appends `mbps=` to `[LATENCY]`, with an `*** OVER DECODE BUDGET ***` suffix above this value. It changes nothing about the stream -- it is purely a warning, because the fault it names has NO other observable. **EVIDENCE (measured 2026-09-09, latency from the pattern's det-code clock `pattern.html:550` against wall clock, three points, same rig):** 3840x2176 @120 static desktop **5.3 Mbps -> latency LOW**; @120 test pattern **29.5 Mbps -> 16.5 s**; @60 test pattern **19.9 Mbps -> 1.9 s**. **WHY BITRATE AND NOT PIXEL RATE:** the first two points have IDENTICAL pixel rate (1002 Mpixel/s) and opposite outcomes, so a geometry x fps budget is the wrong variable and warns on a healthy static desktop -- that mistake was made and corrected the same day, see the WITHDRAWN entry for `COOP_DECODE_BUDGET_MPIXEL_S`. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `mbps=` appears on every `[LATENCY]` line; the suffix appears only above 24. **TRAP:** across those runs `ok=` advanced at the full rate with `errors=0 queued=0 evicted=0 avcbroken=0`, and `host_ms`/`sub_ms` read 4.3/1.1 at 16.5 s versus 3.6/2.4 at 1.9 s -- IDENTICAL while real latency differed by 14.6 s. The backlog is entirely AFTER the feed, in the TV's pipeline; `SS4S_PlayerGetVideoLatency()` cannot see it either (its stats counter has no callers in the webos5 module). **24 IS A BRACKETED ESTIMATE FROM n=3** -- the boundary lies in 20..29 Mbps at this geometry and is probably resolution-dependent. Narrow it with a sweep before trusting it as a threshold rather than a hint. |

| `COOP_AVC_PEER_IDR_MAX_MS` | `15000u` (a probe at `60000u` was reverted the same hour 2026-09-07; `6000u` until 2026-09-06) | 1 | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — **maximum time the AVC co-op peer's pane may go without an IDR anchor.** Added 2026-09-05 after measuring both failure modes: nothing requested peer keyframes at all (`coop_avc_chain_broken` fires only on a hard fault, so `avcrefresh` was 0 in every earlier run) and VEGA emitted NO IDR across a 150 s capture, leaving its pane unreadable garbage on the panel; while a conceal-count budget of 90 fired every ~2.2 s and put `peer_iraps` at 67 in 150 s against a natural baseline of 8 — a keyframe storm. **Time, not conceal count:** conceals track the RATE MISMATCH, a normal designed state (a peer at half rate conceals ~50% of pictures BY DESIGN), so budgeting them fires during healthy operation; elapsed time since the last anchor tracks DECAY, which is what needs bounding, and behaves the same at any rate. Reset when a peer IDR re-anchors the chain; the request is additionally rate limited in `coop_coord` to one per **120 ms** — `COOP_IDR_REQ_MIN_MS_VT` (`coop_coord.c:128`), the ONLY name that exists. **CORRECTED 2026-09-06: this row said "one per 500 ms (`COOP_IDR_REQ_MIN_MS`)" — the number was wrong by 4x AND the constant it named does not exist (`param.py` exit 1).** Three source comments carried the same false 500 ms and were fixed 2026-09-05; this row was missed, so the register was the last place still asserting it. The real value is what sets the request CEILING: 120 ms is 8.3 requests/sec, above the 3.5/s the user rejected as "very consistent flashing", which is why the latch now asks once per BREAK rather than once per picture (see `avcrefresh`). **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `[COOP-AVC] no peer keyframe for 6000ms -- requesting one (#k)` in aurora-diag.log and `peer_iraps` settling near span/6 s. **RAISED 6000 -> 15000 ON 2026-09-06, AND THE ORIGINAL PREMISE WAS FALSIFIED.** 6000 was "about one `gop_length=300` GOP" assuming VEGA runs at 60 fps. It does not. Peer scheduled-IRAP intervals re-derived from `peer_index.jsonl`: **`cb120` (09-05 18:52, the current configuration) median 6.20 s, max 11.61 s, 27 of 33 gaps over 6 s**; `back60` (older) median 15.21 s, max 37.85 s. **6000 therefore sat BELOW the peer's median scheduled interval**, so once the cadence was lifted out of the conceal branch it would have fired before the encoder's own IDR most of the time and become the DOMINANT requester — pre-empting the encoder-scheduled IDR that [[coop-seam-anchor-fix]] measured as the entire reason the seam is currently invisible (a CLIENT-forced IDR halves VEGA 58.6 -> 28.5 fps; a scheduled one costs nothing). 15000 clears `cb120`'s whole observed range with margin and deliberately does NOT clear `back60`'s 37.85 s: this is a BACKSTOP against a peer that has STOPPED anchoring, so it must sit above normal operation and still fire when anchoring truly stops. **IT IS CALIBRATED, NOT CHOSEN — re-measure whenever VEGA's `gop_length` or frame rate changes.** The clock is also now reset only on a CONFIRMED send (`coop_coord_idr_sent()` compared across the call): resetting before the throttleable request meant a swallowed request still bought a full silent interval. **RAISED AGAIN 15000 -> 60000 ON 2026-09-07, AND THIS ONE IS AN EXPERIMENT, NOT A TUNED VALUE.** Live: the user reported a subtle flash plus periodic full-composite breaks on the peer's half, breaks measured **~15 s apart — exactly this constant**. The measurement underneath: over a clean 60 s window VEGA produced **4 IDRs against 4 cadence firings, one for one, none extra**. **VEGA EMITS NO SCHEDULED IDR AT ALL**, so this cadence stopped being the rare backstop it was designed as and became the only thing refreshing the peer — and a client-forced IDR is the expensive kind. `gop_length=300` IS in VEGA's `sunshine.conf` and Apollo loaded it 4 s after the write, so the setting is applied and the ENCODER is not acting on it, consistent with Apollo pinning `gop_size` to `INT_MAX` before any config override on the avcodec path (VEGA's AMF path). **HOW TO READ THE EXPERIMENT:** if the flash and breaks drop to a quarter of their frequency, our own forced IDR is confirmed as the cause; the run also measures how far the peer's pane decays across a 60 s gap. **THE REAL FIX IS HOST-SIDE** — make `gop_length` reach the AMF encoder so VEGA schedules its own IDRs. **60000 WAS REVERTED THE SAME HOUR, AND THE REASON IS THE IMPORTANT PART.** The user: *"We were never meant to have any kind of visible refresh, even slower this is still going against my requirements."* Slowing a visible flash is masking, which was ruled out at the start of this work. **NO VALUE OF THIS CONSTANT CAN SATISFY THE REQUIREMENT:** any setting that lets the cadence fire produces a visible flash on VEGA, and any setting that stops it firing lets the peer's pane decay unbounded — because VEGA never refreshes itself. **Do not tune this knob to chase the flash.** It stays at 15000 as the least-bad backstop, not as a solution. The fix is host-side: make VEGA's AMF encoder refresh on its own so this cadence never fires. |

| `COOP_IPC_IO_DEADLINE_MS` | `1000u` | shared absolute deadline for one video IPC header plus payload | SRC+TEST | **OURS.** Defined in `src/app/stream/video/coop_ipc.h`; both `write_all()` and every stage of `coop_ipc_recv_au_meta()` subtract from the same monotonic start stamp, so progress does not restart the budget. An idle non-blocking header read still returns immediately; the deadline begins to matter only after bytes of a frame are in flight. Expiry returns `ETIMEDOUT` (`message-timeout` on receive), after which the coordinator discards the whole peer link because a partial stream frame cannot be resumed as a new message. **HOW TO PROVE IT TOOK:** `tests/coop/test_coop_ipc.c` creates non-blocking Unix socket pairs whose peer never drains a 3 MiB send and whose peer supplies only half a header. Measured 2026-09-23: send returned `ETIMEDOUT` at **1000 ms**, read returned `ETIMEDOUT` / `message-timeout` at **1001 ms**; both test assertions reject an unbounded wait and a per-poll/per-chunk deadline. This is a failure bound, not a normal latency target: any expiry resets the link rather than accepting a truncated frame. |

| `COOP_PI_ROUTE_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS` | `250` | 2 (1 call site per definition) | SRC+TEST | **OURS — defined TWICE, independently, no shared header:** `src/app/ui/settings/panes/coop.pane.c:65` (used at `:339`) and `src/app/ui/launcher/coop.dialog.c:76` (used at `:1844`), both currently `250` but NOT the same symbol — editing one site does not change the other; confirm both if this value is ever retuned. Each call site passes it straight through as the `timeout_ms` argument of `coop_pi_pads_route()` (`src/app/stream/input/coop_pi_pads.c:197`, prototype+doc `coop_pi_pads.h:89`), fired when the settings pane or the launcher dialog pins a discovered Xbox pad on the Pi hub to a chosen PC (or clears the pin). **WHAT IT DOES:** bounds, on a monotonic clock, how long the client waits after broadcasting an `XLINK_PI_ROUTE_V1` UDP route request for the Pi to echo back the matching `ROUTE_ACK <addr>` reply; unrelated packets, acks from another endpoint, and acks for a different applied route are ignored while still waiting. Returns `false` — both callers then show `show_hub_route_error`/`show_hub_route_failure` — if no matching ack arrives inside the window. **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `tests/coop/test_coop_pi_pads.c` is the primary evidence: against a real loopback UDP server, a genuine reply (~30ms of noise then the exact ack) returns `true` with `valid_elapsed` asserted `>= 20 && < 200` ms (i.e. it keeps waiting for the EXACT ack rather than accepting the first packet), and a silent server with the deadline shortened to 70ms returns `false` with `timeout_elapsed` asserted `>= 55 && < 200` ms (i.e. it genuinely blocks up to the deadline, not one immediate nonblocking poll) — `tests/coop/test_coop_pi_pads.c:129,133,141,145`. On a live run the observable is the hub-route error dialog appearing (or not) roughly this many ms after the pad-routing action, not before; the dialog appearing IMMEDIATELY would mean the wait was skipped. |

Registered 2026-09-06 after `param.py` exited 1 on it: the SENTINEL had a row but the app_settings FIELD THAT WRITES IT did not, so a reader checking the name they had in hand got a STOP while the name one layer away read green. **Read-once semantics matter:** the value in force for a running session is whatever was on disk when the coordinator started, and `coop_coord.c` clamps it to `1..COOP_Q_SLOTS` (16). The compiled default was raised 12 -> 16 on 2026-09-05, so a setting of 0/absent now yields the maximum and this knob can no longer INCREASE depth — see the `/tmp/coop-queue` row for why depth was the wrong lever anyway. |

| `/tmp/coop-guard` | 1 | SRC | `src/app/stream/session.c:545` — **the DUAL GUARD BAND.** Integer row count; each host encodes this many rows MORE, so the composite is `2 x (1088 + guard)` rows and EVERY row is displayed (scaled into the 2160 panel). **The earlier shape `primary_visible + primary_guard + peer_guard + peer_visible` is WRONG and is corrected here 2026-09-03** — it implied a guard BLOCK sitting between the panes, which would put peer row r opposite primary row r-guard and silently misalign any row-to-row comparison. There is no such block: `session.c:545-580` does `half += guard; config->stream.height = half` for BOTH hosts from the same `/tmp/coop-guard`, `coop_avc_compatible()` refuses unequal `height_mbs` so no composite forms unless both honoured it, and the captures measure exactly 3840x2240 and 3840x2304 = 2 x (1088+guard). Each pane is simply 1088+guard rows of that host's OWN desktop. **NOT the retired `guard_band.sh` desktop-blacking overlay** — that blacks DESKTOP rows and must out-reach the motion vector (measured contamination 495 rows vs H.264 `MaxVmvR` +/-512, so it could not be sized and was retired 2026-08-31); THIS adds rows to the ENCODE, so a cross-seam MV resolves to that host's OWN reconstructed pixels in both the standalone and composite decode. **What it does:** read once in `session_config_init`, **clamped to 128 (`session.c:576`)** and floored to a whole 32-row CTB, then added to the half-height pane request. Nothing else changes: `coop_avc.c:522-536` already derives composite rows as `src_sps.height_mbs + peer_sps.height_mbs` and `peer_first_mb` as `width_mbs * src_sps.height_mbs`. **How you know it applied:** the client's own `[COOP] half-height:` diag line now prints `guard=N`, and the negotiated height changes — 0 -> `requesting 3840x1088 (composite 3840x2176)`, 64 -> `requesting 3840x1152 (composite 3840x2304)`; the host's Apollo log independently reports `Desktop resolution [3840x1152]`. **Sizing rule (TEST, 2026-08-30):** `guard_rows >= 1.8 x frames_between_refresh` — contamination climbs the band at ~1.8 rows/frame under aggressive synthetic motion, so 64 holds ~35 frames; a REAL desktop pane measured ZERO because static content codes as zero-MV SKIP. **Default 0 = byte-identical to every run before 2026-09-01.** **THE BINDING CONSTRAINT IS BITRATE, NOT MACROBLOCK RATE — and "32 IS THE CEILING" IS RETRACTED (2026-09-03).** This row asserted a MaxMBPS ceiling (`guard 64 -> 2304 rows = 34,560 MBs = 2,073,600 MB/s = exactly Level 5.2`) and a clamp of 32. **Both were wrong, and the row contradicted its own implementation from the day it was written**: commit `eca2822e` raised the clamp to 128 in `session.c` and left this text saying 32. The MaxMBPS arithmetic landing on 100.0% was a COINCIDENCE — an A/B/A with the SAME geometry and bitrate as the only variable (`session.c:551-566`) shows a 2304-row composite is fine at <=55 Mbps and collapses at 80: `30000 -> 2130 of 2160 OK; 40000 -> 2127 OK; 55000 -> 2125 OK; 80000 -> 496 FAILS`, and the 80 Mbps failure is not even repeatable in size (1154 rows one run, 496 another) — a decoder falling over on throughput, not a geometric mis-size. Consistent with memory `tv-decoder-ceiling-model`, whose MaxMBPS central claim is RETRACTED. **What survives as measurement** is the panel observation itself: guard 0 displays 2160 rows and guard 32 displays 2147, both fine, and guard 64 degraded rather than refusing — but the CAUSE was the bitrate of those runs, not the macroblock rate. **So 32 is not a ceiling and is not required**; the current geometric limit is 128 (1088+128 = 1216/pane = 76 MB rows, 38 CTB rows, composite 2432 = 36,480 MBs, inside Level 5.2's MaxFS 36,864), and a deeper guard is affordable if the bitrate comes down. Two earlier clamps were wrong: `full/4` (permitted 512 rows) and then 64, both chosen on arithmetic alone. **THE GUARD ROWS ARE DISPLAYED — nothing crops them**, so the composite stretches 2304 into the 2160 panel (vertical squash rises 0.7% -> 6.25%) and the guard appears as a ~60-panel-row band at the seam. That band is the guard WORKING: the 2026-08-30 bit-exactness was measured over the VISIBLE region excluding those rows (`halfdiff.py`'s `visible_h`/`peer_top_in_comp` arguments). **`guard=N` in the diag proves the REQUEST, not the encode** — the 2026-08-31 VEGA failure verbatim. The wire-level proof is free and adjacent: `session_video.c:3989-3999` prints `firstmb=`/`rows=` from the RECEIVED SPSs, so honoured-by-both reads `firstmb=17280 rows=144` and not-honoured reads `firstmb=16320 rows=136` = VOID; honoured by only one is loud, not silent (`coop_avc_compatible` rejects unequal `height_mbs`, `refincompat` climbs, no composite forms). Also: `watch_tv.py`'s `DEFAULT_BOUNDARY` is 0.5037 for the 2176 geometry — on a guarded run the true seam is **0.5**, so pass `--boundary 0.5` or the +/-2% seam band lies entirely inside the guard rows. **MEASURED 2026-09-05 FALSE AS A CONTENT CLAIM:** the extra rows are BLACK LETTERBOX PADDING, 16 per EDGE, produced by Apollo centring an 1088-row capture inside a frame whose taller mode the VDD never adopted. The protection is REAL — **re-measured like-for-like 2026-09-06 at x12.9 unpadded -> x5.5 padded (roughly HALVING the seam), superseding this row's earlier "seam peak ratio 1.025 -> 0.632", which was a `splice_diff.py` bin-alignment artefact (an 8-row mean against a 16-row mean) fixed in `d225385f`** — but it is black-on-black edge extension, and it is ACCIDENTAL -- a VDD that DOES adopt the taller mode gives zero padding while this knob still reads as armed (confirmed 2026-09-06 to vary run-to-run at the SAME setting; see memory `coop-guard-band-single-sided`, which retracted its own "every run" universal claim the same day). **SUPERSEDED by `coop_pad_rows`**, and NOT additive with it: set both and you get `coop_pad_rows` rows per edge while paying the pillarboxing (composite 2240, side bars 64-74 px on a 2160 panel) for nothing. |
| `/tmp/coop-peer-top` | 2 | TEST | `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:2919; src/app/stream/input/coop_pointer.c:119` — **PANE ORDER.** Non-zero puts the PEER's pane at the TOP of the composite and ours at the bottom; absent = the pre-2026-09-05 arrangement, byte-identical. Read ONCE per session into `coop_avc.peer_top`, which selects `peer_first_mb`/`mine_first_mb` and the slice EMISSION ORDER in both `coop_avc_compose()` and the catch-up builder; `coop_pointer.c` reads the same file so cursor routing follows the picture. **WHY:** the seam damages whichever pane has an unprotected CONTENT edge at it. Guard rows sit at a host's BOTTOM edge (protecting the TOP pane); NVENC's out-of-bounds restriction protects a pane's TOP edge (suiting the BOTTOM pane). Today SKYNET holds both and VEGA neither. **Both mechanisms measured 2026-09-05** by removing SKYNET's own protection: `nvenc_constrained=0 guard=0` grossly damaged at the seam, `nvenc_constrained=0 guard=32` clean (panel shot + composite frame 7500 from the recorded bitstream, both arms). **HOW YOU KNOW IT APPLIED:** `[COOP-AVC]` logs `peertop=1` and `firstmb`/`minefirstmb` SWAP (firstmb 0, minefirstmb non-zero); `[COOP-PTR] pointer split armed` reports `primary pane BOTTOM (peer_first=1)`. `peertop=1` with `minefirstmb=0` means the geometry never re-derived. **TRAP:** slice order is a CONFORMANCE requirement — arbitrary slice order is Baseline/Extended only and we encode Main (`nvenc_h264_main`), so the top pane's slices must be emitted first. **NOT YET VERIFIED LIVE** at registration. |

| `/tmp/coop-vmirror`, `coop-vmirror` | 2 | SRC+TEST | `src/app/stream/session.c:477` (checked at `access("/tmp/coop-vmirror", F_OK)`, session.c ~505) `; src/app/stream/video/session_video.c:2650`. **[LEAD CORRECTION 2026-09-26, SRC: `session_video.c:3961` gates the vertical composite on `coop_vt_enabled && coop_vt_buf` only -- no coordinator check -- and `:4173-4178` fills the peer column with OUR pane whenever no peer pane has ever arrived. So vertical armed with NO secondary IS a one-host self-mirror for the whole session (`mirrored=` climbs with `frames=`), and CLAUDE.md's use of `/tmp/coop-vmirror` for a single-host self-mirror is RIGHT. What stands: the sentinel is ALSO armed for every two-host vertical session, so `vmirror=1` alone says nothing about whether a real peer fed the pane -- read `mirrored=` against `frames=`/`peer=` and a second host's launch line. Any wording here that vertical has no self-mirror path is WRONG.]** **WHAT IT DOES:** the VERTICAL-ORIENTATION MODE selector — set true for EVERY vertical co-op session (real two-host included, self-mirror or not) by `coop_tunables_arm_mode()` (`coop_tunables.c:98-100`), which unconditionally pairs it with `/tmp/coop-hh-tiles` set false; the two are mutually exclusive and both latched at stream setup (§5). At runtime its ONLY effect is geometry: `config->stream.width` is halved for the request THIS host sends, and — only when `coop_gpu_hevc_resolved()` is also true — `config->stream.height` is padded to a multiple of 32. **IT IS NOT A SELF-MIRROR / SINGLE-HOST DIAGNOSTIC FLAG**, despite the name and despite an existing claim in this register at §28 ("the `/tmp/coop-vmirror` self-mirror test branch") and in `CLAUDE.md`'s testing-rules section (which lists it alongside the genuine self-mirror sentinel `/tmp/coop-hmirror`) — both are CORRECTED by this row; see the §28 annotation. The real horizontal-only self-mirror mechanism is the DIFFERENT sentinel `/tmp/coop-hmirror` / `config->coop_self_mirror[o]` (`coop_tunables.c:83`, comment "SELF-MIRROR IS HORIZONTAL-ONLY"), which `session_video.c:4539`'s composition gate explicitly excludes when `coop_vt_enabled` (vertical mode) is set — so vertical mode has NO client-side "duplicate my own pane" composition path at all; the closest analogue, `coop_vt_mirrored` (`[COOP-VT] mirrored=` gauge), fires only as a ONE-TIME bootstrap fallback before the peer's first pane ever arrives (`session_video.c:4176`, comment "any mirrored picture after the peer is established would be a real defect") and is NOT gated by this sentinel. **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT:** the requested geometry line in the diag log, `[COOP-VT] half-width: requesting WxH from this host (composite WxH)` — present only when this sentinel is armed; absent, `session.c` instead takes the horizontal half-HEIGHT branch. **MEASURED 2026-09-24** (`%APPDATA%\com.limelight\moonlight-tv\aurora-diag.log`, Windows Eclipse client, 4 co-op sessions, primary always PC2 `192.0.2.19`): all 4 printed `Armed vertical: vmirror=1 hh-tiles=0 slices=1` (see Gauges `vmirror` row — that field is a SEPARATE diag value, not this sentinel) and `[COOP-VT] half-width: requesting 1280x1440 ...`. Do not confuse this sentinel, its `vmirror=` print, and `config->coop_self_mirror`'s `mirror=` print (on the adjacent "Tunables applied" line) — three different things, two of which print within a few log lines of each other. |

A config without it whose rect equals a monitor's usable area exactly is adopted as maximized (app_ui_adopt_legacy_maximized). Proof it applied: after a restart the window is maximized (title bar reachable, not a normal window with its frame off-screen), and a local-host session's display change leaves the client maximized at 2560x1369 (measured, run W3) instead of shrinking to 2542x1351. webOS writes it but runs fullscreen. |

## COOP-AVC — the H.264 co-op splice gauges (added 2026-08-31)

**WITHDRAWN, 2026-09-16 — READ THIS BEFORE ANYTHING BELOW.** The entire H.264 co-op splice
implementation this section documents (`coop_avc.c`, `coop_avc.h`, `coop_avc_conceal.c`) was
deleted the same night, in an uncommitted change on branch `diag/feed-accounting`. `param.py`
still returned exit 0 for `avccomposed`, `avcbroken`, `avcrefresh` and roughly twenty other
names from this section at the time of writing — see the WITHDRAWN table near the top of this
document for the individual rows (grouped by family), each with what to use instead where
anything exists. Everything from here down is HISTORY: correct at the time it was written,
describing code that is no longer in the tree. Do not treat a name below as registered just
because it appears in a table on this page — check `param.py <name>` first, exactly as the
STOP rule at the top of this document requires.

**WHY THESE EXIST AT ALL.** Mixed NVIDIA+AMD co-op is impossible in HEVC — the two vendors'
encoders pick different CTB sizes and neither exposes a control — so co-op across those hosts runs
in H.264, through `coop_avc_compose()`. **The `[COOP-AVC]` line is the ONLY evidence that path
ran**: every other `[COOP-*]` line is printed inside the HEVC branch that H.264 skips, so without
it a bad H.264 co-op session produces no `[COOP-*]` output whatsoever and "compose never called",
"refused", "oversize" and "composed but the TV mangled it" are indistinguishable.

**LABEL COLLISION, DELIBERATELY AVOIDED.** `repeated` already names THREE different variables in
this diag stream (`coop_hh_repeated` under COOP-HH, `coop_repeated` under COOP-LIVE,
`coop_vt_repeated` under COOP-VT — see §1's collision list, and `held` above all). Every AVC label
is therefore prefixed `avc*`, so a grep for `avcrepeat=` cannot match anything else. **Do not
"tidy" these back to short names.**

**HOW YOU KNOW EACH MOVED — and what ZERO means, since several are diagnostic by staying at zero:**

| gauge | increments when | ZERO means |
|---|---|---|
| `avccomposed` | a composite was built | the splice never produced a picture — read the refusals |
| `avctaken` | a peer pane was obtained, BEFORE compose | the coordinator delivered nothing; not a splice fault |
| `avcsuppressed` | a picture was DROPPED rather than fed | nothing was ever dropped |
| `avcoversize` | a composite exceeded `buffer_size` | never hit |
| `avcrepeat` | the peer's last pane was re-sent | either every picture had a fresh pane, or none did |
| `avcnopane` | no pane and no usable repeat | the floor always held |
| `avcrefresh=t/bB/cC` | a peer keyframe was SENT — `B` on the latching edge of a chain break, `C` by the `COOP_AVC_PEER_IDR_MAX_MS` cadence (**15 s since 2026-09-06, not 6 s — the 6 s value sat BELOW the peer's measured 6.20 s median scheduled-IRAP gap and would have pre-empted the encoder-scheduled IDR that makes the seam invisible**) | nothing asked: the chain never broke AND the peer anchored inside every cadence window. NOT the same as "none reached the wire" — read `idrreq` for that. **Both halves count CONFIRMED SENDS, not attempts** (corrected 2026-09-06): the 120 ms throttle makes a request a silent no-op, so latching on the attempt froze the peer's pane. For attempts in one unit read `idrall` |
| `refincompat` | the two encoders disagree on something a rewrite cannot fix | the panes are compatible |
| `refidr` | our AU and the peer's disagreed on IDR-ness | the hosts stayed IDR-paired |
| `dbrelax` (`coop_avc.deblock_relaxed`) | a composite was built while the two PPSs disagree on `deblocking_filter_control_present_flag` (`coop_avc.c:561`, `if (st->src_pps.deblock_present != st->peer_pps.deblock_present) { st->deblock_relaxed++; }`) — added 2026-09-02 alongside the deblock-relaxation fix (§6, `deblocking_filter_control_present_flag` row) so that fix could not go silent the way `nvenc_intra_refresh` did | the two encoders' PPSs agree on the flag (e.g. both AMF with the loop filter off, or both NVENC) |

**`avcoversize` is not a theoretical arm.** `buffer_size` is the INCOMING access unit's grown
allocation while a composite is roughly twice that, so it is reachable. It exists because that
arm previously dropped every picture in silence.

**`refidr` counts CORRECT behaviour, not an error.** A coded picture may not mix IDR and non-IDR
slices (H.264 **7.4.1.2.4**), and two free-running hosts disagree about when to IDR. Refusing is
the only legal option; emitting the picture is what would be wrong.

**`avcrepeat` measures DAMAGE ACCUMULATING, not a harmless freeze.** A repeated inter slice
re-applies its motion vectors AND residual against a different reference, so the pane advances by
the same motion twice and accumulates the residual twice. Measured on the HEVC path:
`repeated=2076 of 8344` decayed the bottom pane **all the way to black with only coloured edges
left**. Only the SKIP concealer is bit-exact ([[coop-conceal-reference-frames]]). A climbing
`avcrepeat=` with a static `avcrefresh=` is therefore a warning, not reassurance.

| name | surface | class | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dbrelax` (`coop_avc.deblock_relaxed`) | gauge | SRC+TEST | **WHAT IT COUNTS (SRC):** `coop_avc.h:143` field, incremented `coop_avc.c:561` on every compose where the primary and peer PPS disagree on `deblocking_filter_control_present_flag` — the case the 2026-09-02 relaxation (§6) exists to carry rather than refuse. Emitted on `[COOP-AVC] ... dbrelax=%u` (`session_video.c:4401,4412`). **HOW YOU KNOW A CHANGE TOOK EFFECT — already measured live, both directions (`scratchpad/run/RUN.md`, 2026-09-02):** with VEGA running the deblocking-enabled Apollo build, `dbrelax=1543` while `avccomposed=1543` (every single composite took the relaxed path); with VEGA rolled back to the pre-deblock build (hash-verified `sunshine.exe.bak-predeblock-20260902`), the same 900 s test read `dbrelax=0`. The counter tracks the config change it was built to prove, cleanly, in both states. **Do not read a nonzero `dbrelax` as a fault** — §COOP-AVC's own framing applies: it is proof the relaxed path fired, not that anything is wrong. |

### COOP-AVC additions 2026-08-31 — the concealer and the broken-chain freeze

**`avcconceal` REPLACES `avcrepeat` as the normal gap filler, and the difference is measured.**
A repeat re-sends the peer's last slice, which replays its residual AND its motion vectors against
a different reference: the pane advances by the same motion twice, accumulates residual twice, and
**smears**. An all-skip slice codes every macroblock P_Skip -> MV(0,0), no residual -> the peer's
half is reproduced **PIXEL-EXACTLY** (verified: 26 consecutive concealed frames, ZERO bytes
differing). So `avcconceal` climbing is NOT a warning the way `avcrepeat` climbing is.
`avcrepeat` is retained only as a fallback counter and should now stay at 0.

**`avcbroken` / `avcpeergap` are the freeze mechanism.** A gap in the peer's frame numbers means
the pane it just sent predicts from a picture the composite never showed. Splicing it paints
damage that then propagates and — measured on the panel 2026-08-31 — **is never repaired**: clock
region debris was still there 88 minutes after it appeared, with `avcrefresh=0` all session,
because nothing asked for a re-intra. So on a gap the path now freezes the pane by concealing and
drives a paired IDR. **How you know it worked:** `avcpeergap` increments, `avcbroken` goes to 1,
`avcrefresh` increments, and `avcbroken` returns to 0 when a peer IDR arrives. **`avcbroken`
stuck at 1 with `avcrefresh` climbing means the re-anchor is never completing** — that is the
failure to watch for, and it would present as a permanently frozen peer pane.


## 28. `auroraGpuHevc` / `aurora_gpu_hevc` / `COOP_GPU_HEVC` — the GPU HEVC beta opt-in (2026-09-16)

**OURS, split across two repos, invented for this feature — not stock Apollo/Sunshine.**
Client: this repo. Host: the fork at `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build` (WSL path; base
commit `adc5c5a0bd80831ce495434bb16aee2cd4175fb8`). Four names carry ONE decision across
a compile boundary, the wire, and two processes — each gets its own row key, because a
reader who greps for one of them and stops at exit 0 has found only a quarter of the
mechanism. **Added 2026-09-16, same night:** `AURORA_GPU_HEVC_EXPERIMENTAL` — the fourth
row, the COMPILE-TIME half of the gate, below the wire/process rows it feeds.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `auroraGpuHevc` | ours (client + Apollo patch) / SRC | Query-string arg the CLIENT appends to `/launch` and `/resume` (`core/libgamestream/src/client.c:599`, gated on `hnd->aurora_gpu_hevc` — setter `gs_set_gpu_hevc()` at `client.c:500`, declared `client.h:104`, backing field `priv.h:19`). Apollo parses it at `nvhttp.cpp:397`: `launch_session->aurora_gpu_hevc = get_arg(args, "auroraGpuHevc", "0") == "1"`. **This ONE line is compiled out when `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` is OFF** (§24's existing row — the CMake option defaults ON, but the documented release-build invocation at `cmake/compile_definitions/common.cmake:135` is `-DAPOLLO_BETA_PROFILE=OFF`). Every downstream read of the resulting field is NOT gated by that macro — verified by tracing `#ifdef`/`#endif` nesting through `rtsp.cpp`, `video.cpp`, `video.h`, `rtsp.h`: none of the four read-sites in the next row fall inside an `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` block. Apollo echoes the resolved value back unconditionally as `root.auroraGpuHevc` on BOTH the launch-app response (`nvhttp.cpp:1283`) and the `/resume` response (`nvhttp.cpp:1459`). | The client checks its own ack: `client.c:644-650`, `xml_search_ex(..., "auroraGpuHevc", ...)`. A missing/mismatched ack (echoed `0` when the client sent `1`) makes the client **fail the launch outright** — `"Host did not accept the experimental GPU HEVC encoder; refusing vendor fallback"` — a hard abort, not a silent vendor substitution. **TRAP, VERIFIED 2026-09-16:** this abort happens AFTER the game has already launched HOST-SIDE — `server->currentGame = appId` executes at `client.c:626`, inside the same `/launch` response handling, BEFORE the ack-check block at `:644-653` runs. So a host that rejects the ack (non-beta build, or a genuine mismatch) leaves its game process running with NO client session ever opening — the client sees only a local `GS_FAILED`, and the host has no way to know the launch was refused downstream. **BINARY-PRESENCE CHECK (does not substitute for a live run):** confirmed 2026-09-16 via `findstr` against the installed executable — `auroraGpuHevc`, `AURORA-GPU-HEVC` and `GPU HEVC requires HEVC` all present in `C:\Program Files\Apollo\sunshine.exe` on BOTH SKYNET and VEGA, both at SHA256 `dfbc4e94fc511b091d6d35bdb9efa3bf134065071f4d2cfbecb6c88f689a5242`. **This proves the strings are COMPILED IN — it does not prove the ack is EMITTED at runtime**, and it does not by itself prove `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` was ON for this specific build (a build with the macro off would still contain these literal strings anywhere they appear outside the `#ifdef` at `nvhttp.cpp:397`, e.g. the log format string in `video.cpp:2185` and the 400-response text in `rtsp.cpp:1153` are both unconditional, so their presence in the binary is not itself proof the PARSE line compiled in). The parse line's own presence is not independently checkable by string search since it contains no unique literal beyond `"auroraGpuHevc"`, which the wire-arg read and the two echo `tree.put` calls also contain — string search cannot distinguish "parse compiled in" from "parse compiled out, only the echo survives." **CODE-TRACED, NOT LIVE-MEASURED for the negative case:** every downstream read is unconditional and the ack-check aborts on mismatch, so tracing the control flow finds no path where the client's `1` reaches an unhonoured `aurora_gpu_hevc=false` silently — a source read, not a live test against an actual non-beta build. **THE POSITIVE CASE IS NOW LIVE-MEASURED, 2026-09-16 ~21:46, both hosts:** Apollo logged `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] opened 3840x1088 depth=8 HDR=0 bitrate_kbps=38988 rate_control=bitrate experimental=1` on BOTH SKYNET and VEGA — proving the `nvhttp.cpp:397` parse is compiled IN and firing on both installed binaries, and that `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` was ON for both. **The release-build (OFF) case remains unmeasured** — mark that half OPEN if it needs to be load-bearing (test design: build Apollo with `-DAPOLLO_BETA_PROFILE=OFF`, launch with `auroraGpuHevc=1`, confirm the client's error string appears rather than a silent core-HEVC session). |
| `aurora_gpu_hevc` | ours (Apollo patch, `rtsp.h`/`rtsp.cpp`/`video.h`/`video.cpp`/`process.cpp`) / SRC | The HOST-side session/config field the wire arg above sets. `rtsp_stream::launch_session_t::aurora_gpu_hevc` (`rtsp.h:56`, default `false`, NOT macro-gated) is copied into the per-monitor `video::config_t::aurora_gpu_hevc` (`video.h:51`) at `rtsp.cpp:1039`. The comment at `video.h:51` — "Explicit session opt-in; never a global encoder replacement" — is a CLAIM of intent, not verified behaviour by itself; the four read-sites are what actually make it true. FOUR independent, unconditional (not macro-gated) effects: **(1) encoder bypass** — `video.cpp:2184-2186`, `make_encode_device()` returns `disp.make_gpu_hevc_encode_device(config)` INSTEAD of the normal encoder list, logging `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] explicit experimental session, CTB32/minCB16, no CPU fallback`; this bypasses the host's own `encoder=` config key entirely (e.g. `amdvce` does not block it — it never competes with that selection). **(2) format gate** — `rtsp.cpp:1153`, HTTP 400 `"GPU HEVC requires HEVC 4:2:0"` if set and the negotiated format is not HEVC 4:2:0. **(3) pad suppression** — `process.cpp:303` (inside `#ifdef _WIN32`): `pad = aurora_gpu_hevc ? 0 : config::video.coop_pad_rows`, feeding `vdisplay_height = render_height - 2*pad` at `process.cpp:308`; see this register's `coop_pad_rows` row (§6) for the padding mechanism this overrides. **(4) resume geometry** — `process.cpp:635`, `prepare_gpu_hevc_resume()`, called from both the launch-as-resume path (`nvhttp.cpp:1340`) and the `/resume` endpoint (`nvhttp.cpp:1484`), ONLY when `no_active_sessions`; early-returns `true` (no-op) when `!session.aurora_gpu_hevc`, otherwise resizes/recreates the existing virtual display to the new request's geometry and REFUSES the resume (503, `"Beta HEVC could not resize the resumed virtual display"`) rather than silently keeping stale geometry. | **(1)** Apollo's own log line `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] explicit experimental session...` INSTEAD OF `Creating encoder [h264_amf]` / `[hevc_amf]` / `[hevc_nvenc]`. **(3) MEASURED NEGATIVE CASE, 2026-09-16 21:03:31-33, VEGA** (`coop_pad_rows=16`): a co-op session requested `[3840x1088x115]` and came up `Desktop resolution [3840x1056]` (= `1088 - 2*16`) with `Creating encoder [h264_amf]` — proves `aurora_gpu_hevc` was FALSE for that session. The positive case (full 1088 held, `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC]` logged) is NOT yet captured in this session's evidence — OPEN, needed before this row's mechanism can be called proven live. **(4) TRAP:** the resume geometry check is SKIPPED when Apollo believes a session is already active on that host (`no_active_sessions` false) — a stale/orphaned session there means a resume silently keeps the OLD virtual-display geometry with **no error at all**, the one place in this parameter's path that is not self-defending. |
| `COOP_GPU_HEVC`, `coop_gpu_hevc_resolved`, `plan.gpu_hevc` | ours / SRC | NOT the wire parameter — client-internal names for the same decision, and the ones that actually gate every CLIENT-side behaviour below `gs_set_gpu_hevc()`. Chain: `coop_stream_plan_ex()` (`src/app/stream/video/coop_stream_mode.c:73-96`) computes `plan.gpu_hevc` = `experimental_gpu_hevc` AND `plan.vendors_known` AND `vendor_is_tested(primary->vendor)` AND `vendor_is_tested(secondary->vendor)` AND (`primary->vendor == COOP_GPU_AMD` OR `secondary->vendor == COOP_GPU_AMD`) — i.e. BOTH vendors must be known and be NVIDIA-or-AMD, AND at least one must be AMD; an NVIDIA+NVIDIA pair never sets it. **`experimental_gpu_hevc` is hardcoded `true` at the only production call site** (`coop_session_plan_for_pair()`, `coop_session.c:79`, used by both the UI dialog `coop.dialog.c:294` and the scripted/harness path `coop_session.c:108`) — there is no runtime opt-out left; the entire gate is vendor detection. `coop_session_set()` (`coop_session.c:29-38`) copies `plan.gpu_hevc` into the file-static `g_session.gpu_hevc` (assigned at `coop_session.c:110`) AND `setenv("COOP_GPU_HEVC", session->gpu_hevc ? "1" : "0", 1)` (`coop_session.c:37`), so a LATER-SPAWNED co-op secondary process — a separate address space, the same trap this register already documents for `COOP_STREAM_MODE`/`COOP_HDR_ALLOWED` in §25 — inherits the decision through its environment rather than shared memory. `coop_gpu_hevc_resolved()` (`coop_session.c:58-63`, declared `coop_session.h:76`) reads `g_session.gpu_hevc` when a valid in-process session exists, else falls back to `getenv("COOP_GPU_HEVC")`. `coop_session_clear()` unsets the env var (`coop_session.c:53`). Consumers, THIS WORKING TREE, UNCOMMITTED (the H.264 co-op path was deleted 21:13-21:14 tonight — cite these as CURRENT, not stable): `session_worker.c:55`, `gs_set_gpu_hevc(client, app_launch_coop_requested() && coop_gpu_hevc_resolved())` — the ONLY place the wire parameter actually gets armed; `session.c:496` rounds the composite height to a multiple of 32, but ONLY inside the `/tmp/coop-vmirror` self-mirror test branch (`access("/tmp/coop-vmirror", F_OK) == 0`) — correcting an overread, this is NOT a general effect on real two-host co-op **[CORRECTED 2026-09-26 — this clause is itself wrong, and the error is exactly the trap this register exists to catch: a plausible-sounding label read as evidence]: `/tmp/coop-vmirror` is NOT a self-mirror test branch.** **[LEAD CORRECTION 2026-09-26, SRC: `session_video.c:3961` gates the vertical composite on `coop_vt_enabled && coop_vt_buf` only -- no coordinator check -- and `:4173-4178` fills the peer column with OUR pane whenever no peer pane has ever arrived. So vertical armed with NO secondary IS a one-host self-mirror for the whole session (`mirrored=` climbs with `frames=`), and CLAUDE.md's use of `/tmp/coop-vmirror` for a single-host self-mirror is RIGHT. What stands: the sentinel is ALSO armed for every two-host vertical session, so `vmirror=1` alone says nothing about whether a real peer fed the pane -- read `mirrored=` against `frames=`/`peer=` and a second host's launch line. Any wording here that vertical has no self-mirror path is WRONG.]** `coop_tunables_arm_mode()` (`coop_tunables.c:98-100`) sets it true for EVERY vertical co-op session, real two-host included — it is the VERTICAL ORIENTATION selector, paired with `/tmp/coop-hh-tiles` for horizontal; the genuine horizontal-only self-mirror sentinel is the DIFFERENT file `/tmp/coop-hmirror` (`config->coop_self_mirror`). So the height-rounding here IS a general effect of running vertical mode with GPU-HEVC resolved, not something confined to a diagnostic branch. Measured against 4 real two-host sessions in `aurora-diag.log` 2026-09-24 (PC2 `192.0.2.19` primary <-> VEGA `192.0.2.18` / a second host secondary; `[COOP-VT] mirrored=1` frozen while `peer=` climbed into the tens of thousands, i.e. genuine sustained peer reception) — all 4 armed `/tmp/coop-vmirror` and printed `vmirror=1`. Full evidence: this register's Sentinels `/tmp/coop-vmirror` row and Gauges `vmirror` row; `scratchpad/run/RUN.md` `[param-registrar 2026-09-26]`; `CLAUDE.md`'s testing-rules section carries the SAME error (lists `/tmp/coop-vmirror` alongside `/tmp/coop-hmirror` as if both were self-mirror sentinels) and should be corrected too, not just this row. `session.c:831-836` forces `supportedVideoFormats = VIDEO_FORMAT_H265` (+ `H265_MAIN10` under HDR) and `colorSpace = COLORSPACE_REC_709`; `session.c:843` forces `colorRange = COLOR_RANGE_LIMITED` unconditionally, overriding the user's own SDR full-range preference. | `session_worker.c:56-61` writes `[COOP-ENCODER] role=%s encoder=%s formats=0x%x host=%s` to `AURORA_SECONDARY_LOG`/`AURORA_DIAG_LOG` — resolved by `CMakeLists.txt:313-314` to `/tmp/aurora-diag${AURORA_TMP_SUFFIX}.log` (primary) and `/tmp/coop_secondary${AURORA_TMP_SUFFIX}.log` (secondary), suffix `-beta` on the beta variant, empty on this one (§22) — **so the two roles land in TWO DIFFERENT FILES, and a reader checking only the app's own diag log sees ONE role and can conclude nothing about the other.** `encoder=` reads `gpu-hevc-beta` or `native-hevc` — check that the PRIMARY's and SECONDARY's lines AGREE, same shape as the existing `COOP_STREAM_MODE` proof in §25. **LIVE-MEASURED 2026-09-16, both roles, confirming this mechanism works exactly as specified above (no longer OPEN):** `/tmp/aurora-diag.log` carried `[COOP-ENCODER] role=primary encoder=gpu-hevc-beta formats=0x100 host=CBA83FB7...`, and `/tmp/coop_secondary.log` carried `[COOP-ENCODER] role=secondary encoder=gpu-hevc-beta formats=0x100 host=2ECF9FAD...` — both roles agree, both resolved to `gpu-hevc-beta`. Build-time coverage exists and is not a substitute for a live check: `tests/coop/test_coop_session.c:47,82,114-122` exercises the session-valid path, the env-fallback path, and clear-unsets-env. **THE TRAP MOST LIKELY TO BITE A LIVE RUN:** the whole gate collapses to vendor detection, and §25's existing row already documents that `EncoderVendor`/`CoopProfileCaps` read as unknown for any host polled before pairing, with no distinguishing error — "0 is both 'stock host' and 'our host that failed to answer'". If either paired host's vendor was never (re-)polled since pairing, `plan.gpu_hevc` computes `false` with zero error anywhere in either process, and the session silently takes the native/core HEVC path. The `encoder=gpu-hevc-beta`/`native-hevc` log line is the ONLY place this is visible before the panel itself — check it every run; do not infer readiness from the settings pane showing the right GPU names, since that pane reads the same possibly-stale vendor fields. |
| `AURORA_GPU_HEVC_EXPERIMENTAL` | ours / SRC | Compile define, `CMakeLists.txt:262`: `target_compile_definitions(moonlight-lib PRIVATE AURORA_GPU_HEVC_EXPERIMENTAL=1)`, UNCONDITIONAL — not an `option()`, always `=1` for any build from this tree. The adjacent comment (`CMakeLists.txt:260-261`) — "Co-op has one HEVC launch path; mapped AMD pairs use the beta host encoder. This is no longer an optional app variant or a cached build switch" — is a CLAIM about intent, and it is CORROBORATED here: the macro's ONLY consumer anywhere in `src/` is one `#ifdef` in `coop.dialog.c:1801`, choosing between two BANNER STRINGS shown when `!plan->vendors_known && !plan->forced` — cosmetic UI text, not a functional gate. **CORRECTION, VERIFIED BEFORE WRITING THIS ROW:** the claim that this define is what `coop_stream_plan_ex()`'s `experimental_gpu_hevc` argument depends on does not hold against the current tree — grepped `coop_stream_mode.c`, `coop_stream_mode.h`, `coop_session.c`, `coop_session.h` for the macro: ZERO occurrences in all four. `experimental_gpu_hevc` is the literal `true`, hardcoded and unguarded, at the sole call site `coop_session_plan_for_pair()` (`coop_session.c:79`; see the `COOP_GPU_HEVC` row above). A build with this macro removed would behave IDENTICALLY to today except for which banner sentence displays — it would still request the beta encoder for any vendor-known, AMD-paired session. **This row exists to stop a reader trusting the macro's name over its measured behaviour** — "EXPERIMENTAL" reads like a kill switch and, in this tree, is not one. | The macro is unconditional, so there is nothing to "take effect" per build beyond its presence, which every build from this `CMakeLists.txt` already has. Proving it does what its name implies needs a test, not a read — OPEN, test design: build twice (with, then with the `target_compile_definitions` line removed), launch a co-op session with one vendor deliberately unknown on both builds, and confirm the ONLY difference is the banner sentence in `coop.dialog.c` — `plan.gpu_hevc` and the `encoder=gpu-hevc-beta` log line must be identical on both, which would confirm the macro is inert for anything but that string. Not run tonight; handed back per this role's scope on live tests. |
| `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] opened` | ours (Apollo patch, `gpu_hevc_device.inc`) / SRC | The BOOST_LOG line Apollo prints once per successful GPU-HEVC device init — distinct from the wire-arg/ack rows above (this is the HOST's own diagnostic, not a protocol field). Source `gpu_hevc_device.inc:71-72`, inside `d3d_gpu_hevc_encode_device_t::init()`, printed immediately after `open_fn()` (the `agh_open` DLL export) succeeds: `"opened " << config.width << "x" << config.height << " depth=" << colorspace.bit_depth << " HDR=" << args.hdr << " bitrate_kbps=" << args.bitrate_kbps << " rate_control=bitrate experimental=1"`. A FAILED open instead logs `"open failed: "` + the DLL's own `detail` buffer (`:70`) — the two are mutually exclusive per attempt, so "opened" is itself the positive proof this path was taken, not merely attempted. **LIVE-MEASURED 2026-09-16** (cited by the `auroraGpuHevc` row above): both SKYNET and VEGA logged this line ~21:46. | Grep the host's own `sunshine.log` for the literal line — presence proves `d3d_gpu_hevc_encode_device_t::init()` reached its last statement for THIS device init. **TRAP:** prints once per device init, not once per session — a session that internally reinits its capture/encoder (see §31's `[AURORA-REINIT]`) logs it again, so counting occurrences is a reinit count, not a session count. |
| `frame=` (in `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] frame=...`) | ours (Apollo patch, `gpu_hevc_device.inc`) / SRC | Per-encode diagnostic, `gpu_hevc_device.inc:146-147`, inside `encode(uint64_t index, ...)`: `if (idr \|\| index % 300 == 0) BOOST_LOG(info) << "frame=" << index << " bytes=" << packet.bytes << " IDR=" << idr << " QP=" << packet.qp << " passes=" << packet.encode_passes << " submit_packet_ms=" << packet.submit_to_packet_ms;` — `index` is the real frame index passed into `encode()`, so the NUMBER is genuine when it prints. | **THE GUARD IS THE TRAP: it prints for at most 1 in 300 calls to `encode()`** (more often only while `idr=true` keeps firing). Counting `frame=` LOG LINES is a count of how often the print condition was true, NOT a count of frames encoded — the gap between two consecutive logged `index` values can be up to 300. **No separate unconditional per-frame counter exists in this file** (grepped: `encode()` has exactly one `BOOST_LOG` call, this one) — nothing in Apollo's own log can currently answer "how many frames did this session encode" for the GPU-HEVC path. Do not report a count of matching log lines, or a raw delta of `index`, as a frame count without this caveat. |
| `bitrate_kbps` (in `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC] opened`) | ours (Apollo patch, `gpu_hevc_device.inc` / `aurora_gpu_hevc.h`) / SRC | Field of the `agh_config` struct handed to `agh_open()` at device init (`gpu_hevc_device.inc:66-67`): `uint32_t(config.bitrate)` — Apollo's own resolved per-session bitrate at OPEN time (not re-traced upstream of `config` in this pass) — read back and logged as `args.bitrate_kbps` at `:72`. | **A REQUESTED value at OPEN time, once — not a live/measured delivered bitrate**, and it does not appear in the `frame=` line (`bytes=` there is per-packet size, not a rate). To approximate a delivered rate, sum `bytes=` across `frame=` lines over a time window (subject to that row's own 1-in-300 sampling gap) or measure client-side. A session that reinits its capture/encoder logs `opened ... bitrate_kbps=` again with whatever applied at THAT reinit, which can differ from the first — read the timestamp, not just the value, when more than one `opened` line falls inside a session's log window. |

**Corrections to the brief this section was built from** (team-lead message, 2026-09-16,
verified against source before writing the rows above): the `#ifdef APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE`
guard around the wire-arg PARSE (`nvhttp.cpp:397`) was not mentioned and is load-bearing.
The `tree.put("root.auroraGpuHevc", ...)` echo lines sit OUTSIDE that guard, not inside
it — confirmed by tracing preprocessor nesting, not assumed. The "resume can inherit the
previous session's value" claim is real but is about the VIRTUAL DISPLAY's geometry
persisting when `no_active_sessions` is false, not about `aurora_gpu_hevc` itself going
stale: each `/resume` re-parses its own args through a fresh `make_launch_session()` call
(confirmed at `nvhttp.cpp:1457`), so the boolean itself cannot carry over from a prior
session. `coop_stream_mode.c:94` computes `plan.gpu_hevc`, but the call site actually
exercised by this repo's UI and scripted paths reads it back through
`coop_gpu_hevc_resolved()` in `coop_session.c` — a separate accessor with its own
env-fallback path for the secondary process (third row above), which the brief did not
distinguish from the planner field. And `plan.gpu_hevc` requires an AMD host on at least
one side, not merely "both vendors known" as summarised — an NVIDIA+NVIDIA pair is
excluded by design, matching passover doc 22's "NVIDIA+NVIDIA keeps hardware HEVC and the
core compositor."

**Extractor gap:** `param_extract.py --surface all` finds nothing for this parameter —
expected, it is a wire query-arg plus a compile-time macro plus C struct fields, none of
the ini/conf-key shapes the extractor scans, so this section had to be curated entirely
by hand like §15.

`session-handoff-2026-09-12-vendor-detect-and-latency-trace.md` recorded as "built but NOT
deployed" — **that status is STALE**: `coop_trace_open()`/`_pic()`/`_identity_set()`/`_close()`
are now called live from `session_video.c` (lines 3220, 1655, 663, 3607), a file that is
MODIFIED not new. Flagging the discrepancy here rather than editing that memory file, which
is outside this role's scope.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/tmp/aurora-agetrace` (sentinel), `/tmp/aurora-coop-trace.csv` (output) | ours / SRC | The sentinel gates a PER-PICTURE CSV trace of co-op timing/provenance. `coop_trace_open()` (`coop_trace.c:101-126`) is called UNCONDITIONALLY at every session start (`session_video.c:3220`) but returns `false` immediately if `access(AURORA_TRACE_SENTINEL, F_OK) != 0` (`coop_trace.c:103`) — so an ordinary run with the sentinel absent is byte-unchanged, per the comment at `session_video.c:3216-3219`, which the code's own early-return structure supports (every other `coop_trace_*` call is a no-op against `trace_f == NULL` / `coop_trace_active() == false`). When armed, it writes a header row, then a `hb,` row roughly every `COOP_TRACE_HB_MS` from a DEDICATED HEARTBEAT THREAD (`trace_hb_thread`, `coop_trace.c:72-99`) — built specifically so a STALLED feed path still produces rows, because the feed path's own `since_pic` cannot report its own silence — plus one `pic,` row per composed picture (`coop_trace_pic()`, called `session_video.c:1655`). Closed at session end (`coop_trace_close()`, `session_video.c:3607`), which appends a `# closed at ... after N rows` trailer specifically so a trace missing that trailer reads as CRASHED, not ENDED (comment at `session_video.c:3603-3605`). | File existence + growth: after arming the sentinel and starting a co-op session, `/tmp/aurora-coop-trace.csv` should appear with a header (`# eclipse co-op per-picture trace`) within the session-open window, then grow by roughly 2 rows/s (the heartbeat alone) even with zero co-op activity. **OPEN — mechanism is source-verified, live firing is not**: no session in this working tree has armed the sentinel and inspected the resulting file. `trace_room()`'s cap message (`"TRACE STOPPED AT ITS ROW CAP"`, `coop_trace.c:64-66`) is the observable for `COOP_TRACE_MAX_ROWS` specifically — appears exactly once, at row 450000, and the trace keeps running uncapped-looking in every other respect (the session is NOT affected, only the file stops growing). |
| `COOP_TRACE_MAX_ROWS` | ours / SRC | `#define COOP_TRACE_MAX_ROWS 450000u` (`coop_trace.c:21`), read at `coop_trace.c:61` (`trace_room()`) and referenced in its own cap message. **WHAT IT DOES (comment, corroborated by the code around it):** bounds the trace file so a forgotten sentinel cannot fill `/tmp` — stated at `coop_trace.c:18-20` to be a TMPFS on the TV, so an unbounded trace "takes the whole app down, not just the trace." At ~115 composites/s this is roughly an hour of `pic,` rows before the cap silently (to the session — loudly, once, to the file) stops further writes. | See the sentinel row above — same observable, the one-time cap message. |
| `COOP_TRACE_HB_MS` | ours / SRC | `#define COOP_TRACE_HB_MS 500u` (`coop_trace.c:27`), read at `coop_trace.c:75` (`usleep(COOP_TRACE_HB_MS * 1000u)` inside the heartbeat thread's loop). **WHAT IT DOES:** sets the heartbeat's period — 500 ms, "well under the seconds-scale symptom" per the comment at `coop_trace.c:23-26`, which explicitly cites `docs/passover/07-*.md` §3.2's requirement for "a periodic heartbeat so a stopped feed path cannot censor its own stall." | `hb,` rows in the trace file spaced ~500 ms apart during a session with the sentinel armed; a gap longer than a few multiples of this value between `hb,` rows (not `pic,` rows) means the HEARTBEAT THREAD ITSELF stalled or died, which is a different, worse fault than the feed path it exists to watch. |
| `COOP_IPC_F_HOST_MS_ABSENT` (`0x01u`), `COOP_IPC_F_PTS_ZERO` (`0x02u`) | ours / SRC | Provenance bit-flags for the peer's timing fields, carried across the co-op secondary->primary IPC link. Set in the secondary process at `coop_secondary.c:646-649`, from `decodeUnit->frameHostProcessingLatency == 0` and `decodeUnit->presentationTimeMs == 0` respectively — per the comment at `coop_ipc.h:40-45`, citing `docs/passover/07-*.md` §3.1: Moonlight/Apollo can substitute rate-control time or synthesize presentation time, and these two protocol-visible cases must be flagged rather than silently averaged in with genuine timestamps. Carried opaque through `coop_ipc.c:136` (a straight `meta->flags` copy on the primary's deserialize, the ONE "read" site `param_extract.py` found) into the trace's `xflags=%u` field (`coop_trace.c:172,179`). **TRAP: never bit-tested anywhere in this tree.** Both flags are pass-through-only; the only place they become meaningful is a HUMAN reading the trace CSV's `xflags=` column against the header comment it writes for exactly this purpose (`coop_trace.c:119`, `"# flags: 1=host_ms absent/NA  2=pts zero"`). If a future change needs these to gate BEHAVIOUR rather than annotate a trace column, that would be new code, not a change to these two constants. | `xflags=` in a `pic,` trace row: bit 0x01 set means that picture's peer `phost`/`xhost` field is not a genuine host-processing-latency measurement; bit 0x02 set means `xpts` is not a genuine RTP presentation time. Requires the sentinel armed (see above) — same OPEN status, no live trace has been inspected yet. |
