## 30. `ares-launch -p` webOS payload keys — `host_uuid`, `host_app_id`, `coop` (2026-09-17)

**OURS — this repo's own JSON parser, not a webOS or Apollo contract.** All three are fields
of the `-p '{...}'` payload `ares-launch` hands `com.aurora.gamestream` as `argv[1]`, e.g.
`ares-launch -d tv com.aurora.gamestream -p
'{"host_uuid":"CBA83FB7-192B-2C0A-0F10-2487C7D92B85","host_app_id":2079486299,"coop":true}'`.
Parsed ONCE by `app_handle_launch()` (`src/app/platform/webos/app_webos.c:189-331`) into
`app_launch_params_t` (`src/app/app_launch.h:8-16`: `default_host_uuid` (`uuidstr_t`),
`default_app_id` (`int`), `coop_requested` (`bool`)). Registered after `param.py` returned
exit 1 on `host_uuid`/`host_app_id` and "MENTIONED BUT NOT REGISTERED" on `coop`. **No real
collision on `coop`** — its hits were the substring `coop` inside unrelated `/tmp/coop-*` and
`nvenc_spatial_aq` prose (§5, §6); grepped the whole file for the bare row key `` `coop` ``
and nothing else claims it.

**SHARED PRECONDITION, SOURCE-VERIFIED — the app must NOT already be running.**
`app_handle_launch()` is called exactly once, from `main()` (`src/main.c:53`), at process
start; nothing else in this tree re-invokes it — grepped `src/app/platform/webos/` for any
relaunch/reactivate handler (`relaunch`, `SDL_APP_DIDENTERFOREGROUND`, `SDL_APP_*`) and found
none. So if Aurora is already open on the TV, this `ares-launch` cannot deliver any of the
three keys to that process at all. **The other half of that claim — that webOS/LSM responds
to a second `ares-launch` by refocusing rather than restarting — is webOS's to define, not
ours**; memory `aurora-ls2-permission-ceiling` records it that way (`TEST`, 2026-08-04), not
re-measured fresh tonight. For a run that must land these keys, confirm the app is closed
first (`ops-playbook` separately records `ares-launch --close` itself as unreliable).

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `host_uuid` | ours / SRC | JSON string, the paired host to auto-select. Parsed `app_webos.c:267-271`: `jobject_get(params_obj, "host_uuid")`, and only if `jis_string()`, `uuidstr_fromchars(&params->default_host_uuid, buf.m_len, buf.m_str)`. `uuidstr_fromchars` (`third_party/commons/util/uuidstr/uuidstr.c:11-17`) zeroes the field outright if the string's length isn't exactly `UUIDSTR_LENGTH` = 36 (`uuidstr.h:6`) — it validates LENGTH only, never UUID *format* (hex digits/dash positions), so a 36-character garbage string is accepted and simply fails to match anything downstream. Absent, wrong JSON type, or wrong length -> `default_host_uuid` stays all-zero (`calloc`'d struct) -> `uuidstr_is_empty()` true. Consumed in TWO places, both `src/app/ui/launcher/launcher.controller.c`: `populate_selected_host()` (`:513-527`) walks `pcmanager_servers()` and calls `pcmanager_select()` on an exact `uuidstr_t_equals_t` match (case-insensitive, 36-byte compare); `on_pc_added()` (`:244-261`) re-runs the same match when a host arrives via discovery AFTER fragment init — the common cold-start case, per that function's own comment — and additionally calls `select_pc()` to actually open it (the init-time path alone only auto-opens a host that was ALREADY marked `selected` from a prior session's saved preference). **A value that matches no currently-paired host is a silent no-op** — no log, no error, same shape as `host_app_id` below. | **LIVE, 2026-09-17, independently re-grepped from the saved logs (not taken on trust):** `~/aurora-tv-runs/reopen-20260917/tv-aurora-diag.log:55335` `=== RUN START 2026-09-17 15:25:16 pid=20197 role=primary ===`, then `:55338` `[COOP-ENCODER] role=primary encoder=gpu-hevc-beta formats=0x100 host=CBA83FB7-192B-2C0A-0F10-2487C7D92B85` — the exact uuid from the payload under test, on the primary. **TRAP:** the match is exact-byte only, so a STALE paired-host uuid (TV re-paired since, or that host's Apollo identity regenerated) fails exactly the same silent way as a typo — the two are indistinguishable without separately checking `pcmanager`'s current server list. |
| `host_app_id` | ours / SRC | JSON number OR string, the Apollo app id (memory `apollo-app-id`: `crc32(name + sha256(cover_image))`) to auto-launch on the `host_uuid` host. Parsed `app_webos.c:272-285`: if `jis_number()`, `jnumber_get_i32()` (falls back to 0 on conversion failure); else if `jis_string()`, `strtol(..., NULL, 10)` (negative result clamped to 0). Absent or wrong JSON type -> `default_app_id` stays 0 (`calloc`'d). **Only takes effect paired with a MATCHING `host_uuid`** — `launcher.controller.c:281-298` (`select_pc()`) sets `arg.def_app = params->default_app_id` only inside the branch where `uuidstr_t_equals_t(uuid, &params->default_host_uuid)` is true, so `host_app_id` sent without a `host_uuid` that actually resolves is inert. From there `apps.controller.c:587-603` (`appload_loaded()`): `if (fragment->def_app > 0 && !fragment->def_app_launched)`, linear-searches the loaded list for `apps->items[i].base.id == fragment->def_app`. **`def_app_launched` latches `true` BEFORE the search, unconditionally** — a wrong id does not merely fail once, it can never retry for that apps-fragment's lifetime even if the list is still populating. No match -> `app` stays `NULL` -> `launcher_launch_game()` is never called -> **nothing happens, nothing is logged**, matching memory `apollo-app-id`'s "getting it wrong is completely silent". | **LIVE, 2026-09-17:** `~/aurora-tv-runs/reopen-20260917/tv-coop_secondary.log:1681` `session_begin(host=2ECF9FAD-3BC3-AA06-C712-DE0D490935E0 app=2079486299) -> 0` — `2079486299` is the exact `host_app_id` from the payload under test. **Read this evidence carefully, it is NOT this key's own path:** the secondary's app id comes from `/tmp/coop-spawn`'s second field via `coop_session_prepare_scripted()` (§13's extractor-gap row), a completely separate mechanism from this JSON key — it matches only because tonight's harness happened to write the same id into both places. The direct proof for `host_app_id` itself is that the PRIMARY's requested app launched at all (no error dialog on the panel), which the run reaching `[COOP-ENCODER] role=primary` (`tv-aurora-diag.log:55338`) confirms indirectly, since that line is only reached after a real app launch succeeded. |
| `coop` | ours / SRC | Opt-in for a scripted co-op session. Accepts JSON `true`/`false`, a JSON number (nonzero = true), or the exact 4-character string `"true"` (case-insensitive `strncasecmp`) — any other string, including `"1"`, resolves `false` with no error. Parsed `app_webos.c:304-316`, published process-wide by `app_launch_set_coop_requested()` into the file-static `coop_requested_at_launch` (`src/app/app.c:37,39,41`) — ONE global, not scoped per-fragment or per-attempt. Absent or wrong JSON type -> `coop_requested` stays `false` (`calloc`'d). Read back via `app_launch_coop_requested()` at 21 call sites (`session.c` x9: `44,371,379,503,666,814,831,843,870`; `session_video.c` x8: `1748,3007,3330,3421,4410,5948,6241,6370`; `apps.controller.c` x2: `686,756`; `session_worker.c` x2: `55,56` — reproduce with `grep -rn app_launch_coop_requested src/app/`); this row covers only the ones that gate whether co-op happens AT ALL, not the geometry/format/HDR details once it has. **Gate 1 — is the request even honoured:** `apps.controller.c:756`, `payload_wants_coop = controller->def_app_from_payload && app_launch_coop_requested()`; `def_app_from_payload` is set ONLY at `apps.controller.c:600`, inside the SAME `host_app_id` auto-launch success path above — so **`coop:true` sent without a `host_uuid`+`host_app_id` pair that actually resolves to a real launch is silently discarded**, and `apps.controller.c:762-765` explicitly clears it (`app_launch_set_coop_requested(false); coop_session_clear();`) rather than leaving it armed for a later manual launch to inherit. **Gate 2 — can the session start at all:** `session.c:44-49`, `app_launch_coop_requested() && coop_secondary_mode() == NULL && !coop_session_prepare_scripted(server, "/tmp/coop-spawn")` -> `session_create()` returns `NULL`, `streaming_errmsg` = `"Invalid co-op peer configuration"`, and the WHOLE launch aborts, not just co-op falling back to solo — see §13's `/tmp/coop-spawn` row for what makes that call fail. **Gate 3 — the secondary process:** `session_video.c:3330-3346` spawns the coordinator + secondary only when `app_launch_coop_requested() && !coop_spawned` is true AND EITHER `coop_ui_session != NULL` OR `access("/tmp/coop-spawn", F_OK) == 0`. | **LIVE, 2026-09-17:** a secondary process exists at all — `tv-aurora-diag.log:55407` `=== RUN START ... role=COOP-SECONDARY ===` and `tv-coop_secondary.log:1683` (+14 more) `[COOP-ENCODER] role=secondary encoder=gpu-hevc-beta formats=0x100 host=2ECF9FAD-...` — which can only happen if Gates 1, 2 and 3 all passed: `coop:true` was read, it matched a successful `host_app_id` launch, and `/tmp/coop-spawn` parsed. **TRAP:** because Gate 1 clears a mismatched `coop:true` SILENTLY rather than erroring, a run that intended co-op but landed as an ordinary solo stream looks IDENTICAL in the primary's own `aurora-diag.log` to a deliberate solo launch. The only tell is the ABSENCE of a `role=COOP-SECONDARY` RUN START and `[COOP-ENCODER] role=secondary` line in `/tmp/coop_secondary.log` (or that file's mtime not advancing) — check the SECONDARY's log, not just the primary's, before trusting a co-op run started. |

**Extractor gap:** none of the seven `param_extract.py --surface` scans (`ini`, `sentinel`,
`const`, `gauge`, `pattern`, `script`, `host`) cover a webOS `-p` JSON payload — curated
entirely by hand, the same reason as §15/§19/§28.

## 31. `[AURORA-REINIT]` / `[AURORA-REASSERT]` — DXGI capture-loss recovery on VEGA (2026-09-18)

**OURS, Apollo fork only, `src/platform/windows/display_base.cpp` (+ one line in `video.cpp`) —
not stock Sunshine/Apollo.** Added today diagnosing a co-op peer pane that stayed black
indefinitely: `AcquireNextFrame` was returning `DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST` (0x887A0026) roughly
every 1.85 s, forever, on VEGA specifically, whenever a fullscreen-exclusive game (measured:
Castle Crashers) held the OTHER orientation's resolution while the session requested this one.
`[AURORA-REINIT]` instruments the four reinit CAUSES; `[AURORA-REASSERT]` is the recovery
attempt and its two outcomes. **NOT covered by `param_check.py`**: `param_extract.py`'s
`surface_gauge()` (`scripts/ctx/param_extract.py:246-248`) hardcodes exactly ONE file — this
repo's own `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c` — and has no path into the separate Apollo
tree at all, so these BOOST_LOG labels (same gap as `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC]`, §28) never appear in
`--missing` and must be checked by hand.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[AURORA-REINIT]` | ours (Apollo patch) / SRC + TEST | Four BOOST_LOG(info) call sites, each naming a DIFFERENT reason the capture loop returns `capture_e::reinit` — deliberately distinct labels so the cause is never ambiguous from the log alone: `next_frame -> reinit [0x...]` (`display_base.cpp:153`, any `AcquireNextFrame` failure in `{WAIT_ABANDONED, DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST, DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED}`, hex status printed); `release_frame -> reinit (ACCESS_LOST)` (`:185`, `ReleaseFrame` itself lost access); `factory not current (display/GPU/HDR state changed)` (`:317`, `IDXGIFactory::IsCurrent()` false — display/adapter/HDR topology changed); `artificial (switch_display_event)` (`video.cpp:1362`, a deliberate internal display-switch request, NOT a fault). | **MEASURED 2026-09-18, VEGA, 12:36-12:46 session:** `next_frame -> reinit [0x887A0026]` (= `DXGI_ERROR_ACCESS_LOST`) x85, `release_frame ACCESS_LOST` x5, `factory not current` = 0, `artificial` = 0 — proving the fault is an external party revoking the duplication, not our own display/HDR/geometry state changing and not an internal switch request. Saved log: `~/aurora-tv-runs/reopen-20260917/d0918-vega-sunshine.log`. **TRAP:** `artificial` is instrumented on the ASYNC capture loop only (`video.cpp` around `:1359`); the SYNC loop (`:2402`) signals the same condition through a different variable (`ec`) that carries no `[AURORA-REINIT]` line — so `artificial=0` is evidence only for a PARALLEL_ENCODING session (AMF/NVENC both qualify; confirmed by diff-sentinel before this row was written). |
| `[AURORA-REASSERT]` | ours (Apollo patch) / SRC + TEST | The recovery action and its outcomes, all in `display_base.cpp`'s `aurora_reassert_output_mode()` (`:237-274`) and the `capture_e::reinit` handler around it (`:389-418`, `:427-441`). Re-applies the output's current `DEVMODEW` via `ChangeDisplaySettingsExW(..., CDS_RESET, ...)` (`:269`) — `CDS_RESET` re-applies even an UNCHANGED mode, which is what drops an exclusive-fullscreen owner back to the desktop. If the display's CURRENT mode already differs from the session's requested geometry, the call substitutes the session's own width/height/fps instead of re-asserting the (wrong) current one (`:254-266`, the `restore_session_mode` branch) — the log line's opening words say which: `"restored session mode "` vs `"re-applied current mode "` (`:270`). Gated by counters at namespace scope (`:222-229`), `std::atomic`, because the capture object is destroyed and rebuilt on every reinit (a plain member would reset each cycle and never reach a threshold): `aurora_recent_losses`/`aurora_window_start_ms` count reinits inside a ROLLING window, `AURORA_LOSS_WINDOW_MS` = 10000 ms (`:229`) — a window, not a consecutive-run counter, and that distinction is load-bearing (next column) — until `AURORA_LOSSES_BEFORE_REASSERT` = 3 (`:227`) are seen, which triggers one reassert attempt; `aurora_reasserts` (max `AURORA_MAX_REASSERTS` = 3, `:228`) is the total mode-set budget for the LIFE OF THE PROCESS, restored only by a fresh process, never by a frame arriving (`:429-437`'s comment states this as deliberate). Three log shapes: `"restored session mode WxH@FHz on \\.\DISPLAYn (was W'xH', session wants WxH, rc=)"` / `"re-applied current mode ..."` on each attempt (`:270-273`); `"capture recovered after N reassert(s)"` once, on the first OK frame after at least one attempt (`:438-441`, latched by `aurora_recovery_logged`); `"gave up after 3 attempts: another owner keeps taking this display. Set the game to WxH, or close it."` once, when the budget is exhausted and losses keep arriving (`:411-414`, latched by `aurora_gave_up`). | **MEASURED 2026-09-18 15:23, VEGA, build `cb3e6fd35a30c6020403717d` — the DRIFTED-MODE case, recovered:** `restored session mode 3840x1088@165Hz on \\.\DISPLAY8 (was 1920x1080, session wants 3840x1088, rc=0)` then 388 ms later `capture recovered after 1 reassert(s)`; `rc=0` = `DISP_CHANGE_SUCCESSFUL`. Corroborated end-to-end: TV `[COOP-HH] paired`/`converted` climbing and a screenshot showing the peer pane live with the game running (was black all day). **MEASURED 2026-09-18 15:27, VEGA — the UNRECOVERABLE case, same build:** `re-applied current mode 1920x2176@165Hz ... (was 1920x2176, session wants 1920x2176, rc=0)` x2 then `gave up after 3 attempts...`; the display was ALREADY at the session's own geometry, so `CDS_RESET` alone could not dislodge the game — a forced `SW_MINIMIZE` on the game did not help either (losses continued after minimising); only closing the game stopped it. **So "gave up" is not a bug report, it is the documented ceiling** — this patch fixes ONE variant (drifted mode) and converts the other (game keeps the output regardless of mode) from an indefinite silent black screen into one line naming the fix. **TRAP: a first design of this gate FAILED and is superseded — do not resurrect it as an example.** An earlier build counted "consecutive reinits with no frame in between" via a since-renamed/removed counter (`aurora_barren_losses`, per diff-sentinel's 13:41 review of that build; absent from the source cited above) and never reached 3 (32 losses / 0 reasserts measured 15:10), because brief successes between losses reset a consecutive counter — the deployed windowed design specifically does NOT reset on a stray frame (`:434-437`'s comment). That same review separately flagged that `aurora_reasserts` was being cleared on every OK frame (unbounded total mode-sets over a session) and that the loss counter was never cleared after a reassert (attempts 2/3 landing on a mode change that had not settled) — **both are FIXED in the deployed source cited above**: `aurora_reasserts` has no clear-on-frame anywhere in the current file (grepped), and `:409-410` clears `aurora_recent_losses`/`aurora_window_start_ms` immediately after each `aurora_reassert_output_mode()` call. Re-verify against `display_base.cpp` directly before citing either blocker as still open — they described a build that is no longer what VEGA runs. |
| `aurora_recent_losses`, `aurora_window_start_ms`, `aurora_reasserts`, `aurora_gave_up`, `aurora_recovery_logged` | ours (Apollo patch) / SRC | The five counters/flags backing the row above, all `display_base.cpp:222-226`, namespace-scope `static std::atomic`. Not independently logged as numbers except `aurora_reasserts`, which appears as the `N` in `"capture recovered after N reassert(s)"` and as the `3` in `"gave up after 3 attempts"` (that `3` is the constant `AURORA_MAX_REASSERTS`, not a live read of the counter). | No observable beyond the `[AURORA-REASSERT]` lines themselves — these are implementation state, not a separate diagnostic surface. Listed here only so a reader grepping the SOURCE for one of these symbol names lands on this section rather than concluding it is unregistered. |

**Scope: this fires on ANY session, co-op or not, GPU-HEVC or not.** MEASURED 2026-09-18 15:30: a
SOLO VEGA stream with no co-op payload and the stock `hevc_amf` encoder (not `[AURORA-GPU-HEVC]`
at all) lost the duplication 173 times in ~50 s under the same game-holds-the-wrong-mode
condition and never opened an encoder. The fault is in Apollo's DDA capture layer, upstream of
every encoder path this project has built.

**WGC backend is NOT an escape hatch on VEGA** — `capture = wgc` in `sunshine.conf` fails
outright at startup (`Screen capture is not supported on this device for this release of
Windows: failed to acquire device: [0x80070424]`), confirmed 2026-09-18 and immediately
reverted (`sunshine.conf.bak-prewgc-20260918`). Do not suggest it as a workaround for this
fault on this hardware without re-testing.

## 32. Desktop automatic bitrate and configuration preservation (2026-09-23)

These are client-owned policy states added during the Windows release review. They are not
host parameters and are outside `param_extract.py`'s enumerated surfaces, so they are curated
here. Linux deliberately shares the same code; only native-display and UTF-8 file pointers are
platform-specific.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `settings_optimal_bitrate` | ours / SRC | `src/app/app_settings.c:681-734`. Returns the ordinary-stream default in kbps from the Moonlight community resolution curve (360p/480p/720p/1080p/1440p/4K factors 1/2/5/10/20/40), linearly interpolated by pixel count; FPS scales linearly through 60 and by `sqrt(fps/60)` above 60. Invalid geometry falls back to 1080p60. It does **not** own co-op's explicit per-orientation override. | `tests/app/test_settings.c:testDefaultBitrateCurve` asserts 720p60=10000, 1080p60=20000, 1440p60=40000, 4K60=80000 and 4K120=113000. At runtime the observable is the bitrate slider after a fresh launch/native display resolution has been applied; an existing positive INI `bitrate` must remain unchanged. |
| `stream_bitrate_defaulted` | ours / SRC | `src/app/app_settings.h:49`; initialized true at `app_settings.c:159`, cleared by a positive INI value at `:791-795` or slider input at `src/app/ui/settings/panes/basic.pane.c:202`. True means bitrate follows geometry/FPS and is omitted from `moonlight.ini`; false means user-owned and persistent. It is ownership state, not another bitrate. | `testDefaultBitrateOwnership`, `testWriteINI`, `testAutomaticBitrateLoadsAfterGeometry`, and `testSettingsUtf8PathRoundTrip` distinguish automatic recomputation/omission from explicit persistence. **Trap:** a positive legacy value such as 300000 remains explicit by design; upgrading must not silently rewrite it. |
| `settings_refresh_auto_bitrate` | ours / SRC | `src/app/app_settings.c:736-745`. Recomputes `stream.bitrate` through `settings_optimal_bitrate()` only while `stream_bitrate_defaulted` is true. Called after complete INI parsing, after SDL identifies the restored window's actual monitor, and when the UI changes resolution/FPS. | The ownership tests above change geometry once in automatic state and once after setting a custom 45000: only the first call changes bitrate. A live settings check should show the slider follow resolution/FPS until the user moves it, then stay fixed. |
| `settings_save_allowed` | ours / SRC | `src/app/app_settings.h:222`; initialized true, then set false at `app_settings.c:252-279` when an existing `moonlight.ini` cannot be opened/closed or parsed. `settings_save()` refuses every write while false (`:313-315`), preventing defaults or a partially parsed structure from replacing a recoverable user file. Missing (`ENOENT`) means first launch and remains writable. | `testUnreadableOrMalformedSettingsAreNeverOverwritten` writes malformed content, observes `settings_read=false` and `settings_save=false`, then re-reads the original marker. Desktop primary also shows a visible warning (`src/app/app.c:152-160`); the headless co-op helper never opens a dialog. |
| `known_hosts_save_allowed` | ours / SRC | `src/app/backend/pcmanager/priv.h:36`; `known_hosts_parse_impl()` sets it true for a valid file or `ENOENT`, false for every other open/parse/close failure (`known_hosts.c:268-294`). Every hosts save stops while false (`:103-108`), so an empty discovered list cannot erase pairings after a Unicode/permissions/syntax error. | Application is observable as the existing `hosts.ini` remaining byte-for-byte present after shutdown when parsing fails. UTF-8 path parsing is covered by `test_known_hosts:test_parse_utf8_path`; a malformed-file manager-level persistence test remains desirable. |

## 33. Windows/co-op protocol constants exposed by the desktop port (2026-09-23)

These names are implementation contracts rather than user tuning knobs. They are registered
because `param_check.py` intentionally treats protocol constants, compile guards, and harness
environment inputs as parameters too; changing one side without its consumer is still a real
configuration error.

| name | value / owner | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_SHUTDOWN` | `2u`, ours | Video-IPC control code in `src/app/stream/video/coop_ipc.h`; coordinator sends it before teardown and the secondary exits its control loop when received. | `tests/coop/test_coop_ipc.c` round-trips the value and `test_coop_coord_concurrency.c` requires it after the IDR control. A number change must be made on both processes at once. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_READY` | `3u`, ours | Secondary-to-coordinator declaration that host lookup, exact app resolution, and session start completed; consumed by `coop_coord.c`. | A co-op start is not marked ready until this byte crosses the live control link; startup tests and a live `secondary ready` diagnostic distinguish it from process creation alone. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_CONFIG` | `4u`, ours | Secondary startup failed while decoding/validating its launch configuration. | `coop_secondary.c` emits it on configuration failure and `coop_coord_start_error()` maps it to the configuration-specific UI error. Injecting that return is observable as that message, not a generic timeout. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_HOST` | `5u`, ours | The selected secondary host UUID is absent from the secondary's host list. | `coop_secondary.c` emits it at the failed host lookup; `coop_coord_start_error()` reports the host-specific failure. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_QUERY` | `6u`, ours | The selected host could not return a usable application list. | Emitted only after the app-list request fails and mapped to the query-specific launch error. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_APP` | `7u`, ours | No exact `COOP_SESSION_APP_NAME` entry was found, or a supplied cached ID resolved to another name. | `tests/coop/test_coop_session.c` covers exact-name resolution and stale IDs; the live UI names the missing host entry instead of launching a different app. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_SESSION` | `8u`, ours | The secondary GameStream launch/session setup failed after host and app resolution. | Emitted by `coop_secondary.c` on connection setup failure and mapped to the session-specific UI error. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_CONNECTION` | `9u`, ours | Catch-all for a secondary IPC connection that disappears or never becomes usable. | Coordinator initializes and resets its start error to this value around link failure; timeout/disconnect tests observe bounded failure rather than a hung start. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_BUSY` | `10u`, ours (added 2026-09-27, issue #33) | The secondary's GameStream launch was refused because its host is already running another app (`coop_secondary_launch_failed()` matches "already running" in the launch error). `coop_coord_start_error()` maps it to "The second co-op PC is already running another app. Close that stream or app on it, then start co-op again." | MEASURED 2026-09-27 (issue #33): PC2 streaming Virtual Display, SKYNET+PC2 co-op -> that exact message within seconds instead of the 30 s start timeout. A number change must be made on both processes at once. |
| `COOP_IPC_CTL_FAIL_LAUNCH` | `11u`, ours (added 2026-09-27, issue #33) | The secondary's GameStream launch failed for any other reason (the host refused the app). Mapped to "The second co-op PC refused to start the co-op app." | SRC only: emitted by `coop_secondary_launch_failed()` for a launch error without "already running"; the live #33 runs exercised only the BUSY path. |
| `COOP_MSG_CHANNEL_INITIALIZER` | `fd=-1`, static pthread mutex, ours | Complete static initializer for the small-message input/audio transport in `src/app/stream/coop_msg_channel.h`. It establishes the detached state before any UI callback can send. | `tests/coop/test_coop_msg_channel.c` and `tests/app/test_coop_input_lifecycle.c` initialize channels with it, then exercise attach/send/detach and descriptor reuse under sanitizers. Do not replace it with zero-fill: descriptor 0 is valid. |
| `COOP_MSG_FRAME_MAGIC` | `0x434D4731u` (`CMG1`), ours | Windows stream-socket message marker in `src/app/stream/coop_msg_channel.c`; POSIX `SOCK_SEQPACKET` does not need the extra header. | `test_coop_msg_channel` sends framed partial writes and receives the original message; corruption produces `COOP_MSG_RECV_ERROR` rather than exposing bytes as input/audio. Both processes ship in one package and must share the value. |
| `COOP_MSG_FRAME_BYTES` | `2 * sizeof(uint32_t)`, ours | Size of the Windows framing header: magic plus payload length. It owns buffer sizing and the transition from header to payload reads. | The large-message/partial-write test crosses the boundary repeatedly and verifies exact payload length/content. Changing this without the serializer/parser fails that test. |
| `COOP_SESSION_APP_NAME` | `"Co-op Pane"`, client/host contract | Exact Apollo/Sunshine application name required on both selected hosts (`src/app/stream/video/coop_session.h`). IDs are host-local and therefore may not be hardcoded or copied between PCs. | `tests/coop/test_coop_session.c` gives the two hosts different IDs, requires exact-name lookup on both, and rejects a stale ID that names another app. Fresh-user host setup is documented in the Windows build guide/README. |
| `COOP_WIN_COMPAT_H` | include guard, ours | Prevents duplicate declarations from the force-included Windows POSIX compatibility surface (`src/app/platform/windows/coop_win_compat.h`). It has no runtime value. | A Windows build compiles the shared co-op sources with the header force-included and their POSIX includes redirected; duplicate typedef/wrapper errors indicate the guard did not apply. |
| `COOP_WIN_HAVE_SOCKLEN_T` | compile guard, ours | Marks the local `socklen_t` compatibility typedef as supplied so Windows SDK/header combinations cannot define it twice. It has no runtime value. | The MinGW Windows build of every shared co-op source is the application check; a conflicting SDK declaration is a compile failure. |
| `COOP_WIN_RUNTIME_SUFFIX` | `Aurora\\runtime`, ours/platform | Exe-independent per-user subdirectory below `%LOCALAPPDATA%` (or the Windows temp fallback) used for translated `/tmp` sentinels and local sockets. | A clean-user Windows co-op launch creates/resolves the directory through `coop_win_runtime_root_w`; Unicode/UNC path builds exercise the wide path boundary. This is a relative product namespace, never a user/profile path. |
| `COOP_IP_HINTS` | optional space-separated IPs, test harness | Fast-path candidate addresses for `scripts/coop/run_coop.sh`; every candidate is accepted only when live `/serverinfo` returns the requested UUID, then the harness scans if none match. This is not read by the client or shipped package. | Override with the current lab subnet during a harness run and observe the printed UUID-derived primary/peer addresses. A hint alone can never select a host; UUID verification is the application signal. |
| `COOP_PEER` | `pc1|pc2|vega`, test harness | Names the peer role in `scripts/coop/run_coop.sh`; the script rejects equality with `COOP_PRIMARY` and derives the peer UUID/address as one unit. It is lab harness vocabulary, not a client setting. | The harness prints both resolved roles and refuses an equal primary/peer before launch; live logs must then show traffic from the derived peer UUID. |

## 34. Explicit co-op intra-refresh request (2026-09-23)

This replaces the platform-specific `/tmp/coop-live` check that had leaked into
moonlight-common-c's SDP generator. The stream configuration now carries the policy decision
explicitly, so webOS, Windows, and the future Linux client use the same connection field.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `enableIntraRefresh` | ours (Aurora moonlight-common-c fork) / SRC | `STREAM_CONFIGURATION.enableIntraRefresh` is a per-connection boolean. In `core/moonlight-common-c/src/SdpGenerator.c`, a nonzero value on a Sunshine/Apollo connection emits `x-ss-video[0].intraRefresh=1`; zero omits the attribute, as does any non-Sunshine connection. `LiInitializeStreamConfiguration()` zero-initialises the field. Aurora sets it nonzero only from the co-op launch state, so an ordinary stream retains the zero/default protocol shape without a filesystem sentinel or platform path dependency. Apollo's receiving chain is `rtsp.cpp` (`intraRefresh` defaults to `"0"`, then parses this SDP attribute into `client_config.enableIntraRefresh`) -> `nvenc_base.cpp` (consumes that field when configuring NVENC); those Apollo-owned meanings come from those source sites, not Aurora's comments. | The SDP generator logs the exact branch as `[SDP] enableIntraRefresh=%d emitted=%d`: a real co-op connection to Apollo must report `1/1` in **both** client processes, while a real regular stream must report `0/0`. `1/1` proves the request was emitted; the paired Apollo/session negotiation must also contain `x-ss-video[0].intraRefresh=1` for that same connection. **TRAP:** Apollo does not log the resulting NVENC intra-refresh state, so the SDP line proves signalling, not encoder behaviour or visual repair. Whether persistent pane damage actually clears remains a separate live visual test. |

## 35. Rolling composite capture and explicit recovery probe (2026-09-23)

These two client-owned sentinels already existed in the shared co-op engine but previously
appeared only in the generated sentinel inventory, which `param.py` correctly treats as a
mention rather than a registration. On Windows, `coop_win_compat.h` maps their `/tmp` names to
the per-user `%LOCALAPPDATA%\\Aurora\\runtime` directory; the product never requires a literal
`C:\\tmp` or developer-created directory.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/tmp/coop-dump-ring` | ours / SRC | Read once during video setup in `src/app/stream/video/session_video.c`. Its integer content is the rolling composite capture size in MiB; missing disables the ring, invalid/non-positive content defaults to 96 MiB, and values above 256 MiB clamp to 256. The ring stores access units actually submitted to the decoder and is diagnostic only until triggered. | Setup logs `[COOP-DUMP] rolling capture armed: NMB ...`; allocation failure logs `allocation FAILED`. That line, not file existence, proves the latched-at-start value applied. **Trap:** changing the file during a stream does nothing until the next stream, and allocating the ring alone neither requests an IDR nor writes a dump. |
| `/tmp/coop-dump-now` | ours / SRC | A runtime mtime trigger polled from the video feed only when the rolling ring is armed. A changed mtime calls `LiRequestIdrFrame()` for the primary and `coop_coord_request_peer_idr()` for the peer, waits for a composite IRAP, then writes `/tmp/coop_composed.h265`. It is therefore also an explicit two-host recovery probe: it deliberately perturbs both reference chains and must not be used while passively measuring natural decay. | The primary log must say `[COOP-DUMP] ring: dump requested -- asked both hosts for a keyframe`; the secondary log must add `peer asked for an IDR (#N)`, and the coordinator's `idrreq` sent count must rise. A later `ring flushed` or `gave up waiting` line distinguishes an arriving composite IRAP from a request that never completed. For a recovery test, take advancing screenshots immediately before and after the trigger: a pane change without these request logs is not evidence that this parameter applied. **Trap:** setup resets the remembered mtime to zero without sampling an existing file, so a file left present before startup fires on the first poll. Keep it absent until the intended trigger; later triggers require a genuinely newer filesystem timestamp. |

## 36. Desktop decoder error policy (2026-09-23)

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `err_recognition` / `AV_EF_EXPLODE` | FFmpeg / SRC | `AVCodecContext.err_recognition` controls decoder error recognition. `AV_EF_EXPLODE` makes FFmpeg HEVC return `AVERROR_INVALIDDATA` on the exact `Ignoring POC change between slices` warning observed on Windows; without that bit, it continues using the current picture POC. Primary source: [FFmpeg n9.0.1 hevcdec.c, hls_slice_header](https://github.com/FFmpeg/FFmpeg/blob/n9.0.1/libavcodec/hevc/hevcdec.c). Earlier Windows snapshots did not set this field; the hardware-backend change added EXPLODE. This describes parser policy, not proof that it caused all visible damage. | Latched at decoder open; log `Decoder error policy: err_recognition=0x...` proves the configured context value. For the live A/B, hold executable, hosts, layout and hardware decoding constant. Prediction: POC warnings no longer yield `Error parsing NAL` / `avcodec_* -1094995529` solely from that warning. Inspect advancing screenshots independently: removing rejections is not itself a clean-picture result. No software decode or shared co-op changes. |

## 37. `ui_scroll.c` layout constants — wheel step and scrollbar grab slop (2026-09-26)

New file, uncommitted (`diag/feed-accounting`), ours. Both are plain `#define`s that name
neither `COOP`, `VT`, nor `HH`, so `param_extract.py`'s compile-time-constant surface (which
scans `#define (COOP|VT|HH)_*` — see §13) never sees them; `param.py` exits 1 for both
regardless of code presence. Same gap as the already-noted `SECTION_COL_PCT` (not yet its own
row — cite this section's convention if it needs one). Curated here rather than force-fit into
the generated "Compile-time constants" table, matching §32/§33's treatment of client-owned UI
constants outside the extractor's five enumerated surfaces.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `WHEEL_STEP_DPX` | `36`, ours / SRC | `src/app/ui/ui_scroll.c:5`; consumed once, `ui_scroll_wheel()` at `:91` (`LV_DPX(WHEEL_STEP_DPX)`), the pixel distance one mouse-wheel notch scrolls the hit-tested container (`:86-104`). Reached only through `wheel_scroll()` in `src/app/lvgl/input/lv_sdl_drv_wheel_input.c:58-84` (call site `:82`), itself compiled only `#ifndef TARGET_WEBOS` (guard at `:13` and `:51`). On webOS the same wheel event takes the `#ifdef TARGET_WEBOS` branch at `:36-43` instead and moves LVGL encoder focus (`data->enc_diff`) — `WHEEL_STEP_DPX` is **not linked into that build at all** (a diff-sentinel pass this session confirmed the webOS `sdl_input_read` object disassembly byte-identical to HEAD). SDL preciseY (`SDL_VERSION_ATLEAST(2,0,18)`) means a touchpad can scroll a fraction of one step. | Windows/desktop dev build only: scroll a settings pane N notches with a real or posted wheel event and measure the pixel delta between before/after screenshots — it should be `N * LV_DPX(36)` (measured 2026-09-26 on a 2560x1392 dev build: 104 px/notch to a clean end). On the SAME notch on webOS the correct observable is d-pad-style FOCUS movement with ZERO pixel scroll — any pixel scroll on the TV from this event would mean the guard failed. Re-measure per notch; do not assume linearity holds across builds/DPI without checking `LV_DPX`'s scale factor on that display. |
| `BAR_GRAB_SLOP_DPX` | `3`, ours / SRC | `src/app/ui/ui_scroll.c:9`; consumed once, `ui_scroll_bar_press()` at `:148` (`ver.x1 - LV_DPX(BAR_GRAB_SLOP_DPX)`), widening the vertical scrollbar thumb's left hit edge so a press slightly left of the drawn thumb still grabs it for dragging (function `:131-`, full geometry check `:131-160`). Reached from `src/app/lvgl/input/lv_sdl_drv_pointer_input.c:77`, which carries **no** `TARGET_WEBOS` guard anywhere in the file (confirmed by grep) — this path is live on BOTH the Windows pointer and the TV's magic-remote pointer. It only fires while `app->session == NULL` (no stream active; `pointer_input.c` ~line 74), matching CLAUDE.md's `hid_passthrough` note that the mouse belongs to the host during a stream. | A left press within 3 dpx left of the drawn thumb must drag the bar and must NOT click the row beneath it; a press further left must click normally. Measured on the Windows dev build 2026-09-26 ("thumb drag works"); **the TV beta check is OPEN** — not yet measured on webOS despite the code path being reachable there. Test design for that check: on the beta app id with no stream active, press within/just-outside the 3-dpx band on the TV's rendered thumb (via the magic-remote pointer) and observe drag-vs-click in a screenshot pair, per this repo's screenshot-every-test rule. |

## 38. Local-host co-op session — client marker file and the display hook's local-mode branch (2026-09-26)

New, uncommitted (`diag/feed-accounting`), ours: `src/app/platform/windows/local_host_win.{c,h}` (new
files), wired into `src/app/stream/session_worker.c`; the reader half is uncommitted edits to
`xlink/dispwake/apollo-display.cmd` (identical copy `fork/host/apollo-display.cmd`, diffed byte-equal).
**What the contract is for:** when the Windows Eclipse client is ALSO one of the two co-op hosts, the
display hook's normal TV behaviour (turn the physical panel off, leave the VDD as the only display) would
blank the screen the client's own window is on and pull the capture inside the client's own pane — so the
hook needs to know, at `resume`, that this particular session is local and behave differently.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `local-host-session` (`%ProgramData%\Eclipse\local-host-session`) | ours / SRC | WRITER `local_host_session_begin()`, `local_host_win.c:101-119` (line numbers refreshed 2026-09-26; was `:70-86` before the window-home guard grew the file), called from `session_worker.c:74` **before** `gs_start_app`, gated `#ifdef _WIN32` — only if `address_is_local()` (`local_host_win.c:28-52`, a UDP `bind()` probe against the resolved host address, so it follows DHCP with no adapter enumeration) says the stream's host address is THIS machine. First line is the decimal `GetCurrentProcessId()` + `\r\n` (`local_host_win.c:114`, "ASCII, CRLF: the hook reads it with cmd's `set /p`"). Removed by `local_host_session_end()` (`:121-131`) at `session_worker.c:266`'s `thread_cleanup` label — runs on every exit path through that label, not just a clean stop. READER: `apollo-display.cmd`'s `:is_local` subroutine (new label, both copies identical) — treats the session as local **only if `tasklist` shows the named PID running AS `moonlight-tv.exe`** (`/FI "PID eq N" /FI "IMAGENAME eq moonlight-tv.exe"`; the file text is read through `set /a` as a NUMBER, never expanded into a command — diff-sentinel findings 2+4), so a stale file naming a dead or reused PID is ignored AND deleted. Consumed at `resume` only; `pause`/`sessionend` do NOT re-check this file (see `local-mode.flag` below — the reason a second, host-side file exists at all). **Consequence added 2026-09-26 (same guard, not the same file):** the instant `local_host_session_begin()` returns true, `session_worker.c:81` also calls `LiSetLocalInputBlocked(true)` (own row below); it is cleared at the same `thread_cleanup` label, `session_worker.c:269`. MEASURED 20:52: Apollo's `sunshine.log` for that session shows CLIENT CONNECTED with **zero** `Gamepad N will be` lines, against **4** in every prior local-host session (one physical DS4, forwarded to this PC's own Apollo, created a virtual pad the client itself then picked up and re-forwarded, looping to the 4-pad cap). | **Client side:** `[LOCAL-HOST] host=<addr> is this machine -> local-host-session written` in the session's diag log (`session_worker.c:79`, into `AURORA_SECONDARY_LOG` when a secondary, else `AURORA_DIAG_LOG` — `win_posix_bits.h:26,29`), plus `commons_log_info("Session", "Host %s is this machine: local-host session announced", ...)`. **Host side:** `C:\Users\<user>\xlink-build\dispwake\apollo-display.log` gains `local client pid N - panel stays ON, VDD made primary` (from `:is_local` finding `ISLOCAL=1`) rather than the plain `resume` line every TV session prints; a PID that fails the check instead logs `local-host file names pid N, not a running Eclipse client - ignored and removed` and falls through to the ordinary physical-off path. **MEASURED SO FAR (2026-09-26 ~20:05, per the arming session): hook control-flow only, against a STUB `dispwake.exe` (8 cases at 20:05; re-run after the review fixes at 20:18: live fake-Eclipse pid, live NON-Eclipse pid, injection text `1&echo INJECTED`, dead pid — all as designed, RUN.md) — confirms the `.cmd` branches (`:is_local` -> `do_resume_local` -> retry loop) take the paths this row describes.** Since then, MEASURED LIVE 20:30-20:53 (RUN.md "LIVE: local-host phase 1"): the file being written/removed, the window-home guard reading its presence, and a real panel screenshot with the client back on the physical panel are all now confirmed together — no longer OPEN for the file mechanism itself. Still open: mouse/keyboard handoff to the correct pane (Phase 3) and other-player-pad isolation (Phase 2). |
| `local-mode.flag` (`%ProgramData%\Eclipse\local-mode.flag` — MOVED 20:18 from next to `apollo-display.cmd`: installed, the script dir is Program Files, which the non-elevated hook cannot write; diff-sentinel finding 3) | ours / SRC | Written by `resume`'s local branch (`do_resume_local`, `echo local> "%LOCALFLAG%"`) the moment `:is_local` finds a live PID — **before** the `virtual-primary` retry loop runs, so it records the DECISION even if the dispwake call that follows never succeeds. Read by `pause` (`do_pause`: if present, run `dispwake physical-primary` and return, skipping the normal `physical-on`) and by `sessionend` (`do_sessionend`: if present, delete the flag and run `dispwake physical-primary` then `physical-on` — physical-primary first so a LEAKED VDD cannot keep the saved VDD-primary layout (diff-sentinel finding 1) — with no wake-blink retry sequence, then return, skipping the normal forced-wake path). A non-local `resume` deletes a stale copy (`if exist "%LOCALFLAG%" del`) before starting its own physical-off loop. **Exists because of a specific ordering fact, stated in the source comment as intent (Rule 5 — not evidence on its own, but the mechanism is directly checkable in the diff):** the CLIENT deletes `local-host-session` at its own `thread_cleanup` (which race-wise happens close to when the stream tears down), so by the time Apollo's `pause`/`sessionend` commands run, `local-host-session` may already be gone and `:is_local` would report `ISLOCAL=0` — `local-mode.flag` is the host-side memory of "this session started local" that survives the client's own cleanup. | No diag log of its own; the observable is BEHAVIOURAL — `apollo-display.log` shows `pause: local mode - panel back to primary` / `sessionend: local mode - panel back to primary, verifying it` lines (new, this diff) instead of the ordinary `pause: restoring physical display` / the forced wake-sequence block, and the file's presence/absence on disk between resume and sessionend. **Same OPEN status as the row above**: not yet exercised end-to-end against a real session, only against the stub dispwake's control flow. |

### The window-home guard, its log, and blocking local input (added 2026-09-26, MEASURED live)

New in the same diff, same files: `src/app/platform/windows/local_host_win.{c,h}`, `src/app/ui/root.c`
(`#ifdef _WIN32`), `src/app/stream/session_worker.c`, and the moonlight-common-c fork
(`patches/client-deps/moonlight-common-c.patch`). This is the mechanism RUN.md's "LIVE: local-host
phase 1" and "CONTROLLER FEEDBACK LOOP" entries (2026-09-26 20:30-20:53) measured directly.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LOCAL_HOST_WINDOW_POLL_MS` | ours / SRC | `#define LOCAL_HOST_WINDOW_POLL_MS 500` (`local_host_win.h:8`) is the period, in ms, of the `lv_timer` `app_ui_init` creates only under `#ifdef _WIN32` (`src/app/ui/root.c:119`, `lv_timer_create(local_host_window_timer_cb, LOCAL_HOST_WINDOW_POLL_MS, ui)`), which calls `local_host_window_tick()` (`local_host_win.c:227-291`) on the UI thread. This timer runs on **every** Windows session, local-host or not — it always learns the window's home monitor from calm periods (`win_home`, `local_host_win.c:140-147`) — but the tick only ever **moves** the window while `local_session_active()` (`local_host_win.c:195-198`, tests presence of `local-host-session`, own row above) is true. | Compile-time constant — no live knob, so "how you know a change took effect" reduces to a rebuild: a smaller value narrows the `LAYOUT_SETTLE_TICKS` window in wall-clock terms (fewer ms per tick to reach the same tick count) and a larger one costs more `EnumDisplayMonitors` calls per second. At the shipped value the guard fired within one tick of the layout change in every 2026-09-26 run (RUN.md: displaced immediately, no missed cycle observed), so 500 ms has not yet been shown to be too coarse. Not itself independently observable at runtime — only its downstream effect (the `local-host.log` lines below) is. |
| `LAYOUT_SETTLE_TICKS` | ours / SRC | `#define LAYOUT_SETTLE_TICKS 4` (`local_host_win.c:151`, ~2 s at the poll period above). `layout_signature()` (`:169-173`) FNV-1a-hashes every monitor's device name + rect from `EnumDisplayMonitors`, order-independent (summed, not chained). A window found off `win_home.home` within this many ticks of a signature change is classed SYSTEM displacement (`:263-267`, sets `displaced=true`, logs `window: on %ls after a display change`); once `calm` (ticks since the last signature change, capped at this value, `:250`) reaches it with no move, a later move IS the user's, and that monitor is adopted as the new home (`:270-281`) instead of being fought. Exists per a **source comment, Rule 5 — a claim, not evidence on its own** (`:149-150`): "Windows repositions windows a little after the monitor list changes." | MEASURED indirectly, not the constant itself: every 2026-09-26 run that started with a stored panel-off layout produced the "on ... after a display change" line (displacement correctly detected within the window). **Trap (was silent until the 21:3x post-review edit, now logged):** a move landing ≥ `LAYOUT_SETTLE_TICKS` ticks after a layout change is adopted as home; during a local session that adoption now logs `window: now on <dev>, adopted as home (was <dev>) -- no recent display change` (`local_host_win.c:283`), outside one it is still silent by design — so a slow SYSTEM displacement reads as an adoption line, not as nothing, and if the client is later put back on the panel within 2 s of a *subsequent* layout change, the guard will drag it back onto whatever it silently adopted (the VDD). The only live evidence so far (20:42, 20:52) shows the delay well inside the window; the silent branch itself is UNMEASURED. |
| `local-host.log` (`%ProgramData%\Eclipse\local-host.log`) | ours / SRC | Written by `local_host_log()` (`local_host_win.c:77-99`), called from `local_host_session_begin`/`local_host_session_end` (own row above) **and** from `local_host_window_tick`'s four log sites (`:254`, `:265`, `:274`, `:289`) — session lifecycle and window lifecycle share one file. Append-only (`_wfopen(path, L"ab")`, `:85`), one line per state transition (none per tick), format `HH:MM:SS.mmm pid=<pid> <message>\r\n`. The **directory** (`%ProgramData%\Eclipse`) is created only by `session_file_path(..., create=true)`, called only from `local_host_session_begin` — so a machine that has never run a local-host session has NO such file at all, even though the window-guard timer itself runs unconditionally (see `LOCAL_HOST_WINDOW_POLL_MS` above); its window-tracking lines only ever appear once a session has run at least once. Both the primary and a co-op secondary process append to the same file (RUN.md (c): "ab" is seek-to-end then WriteFile, so two processes can lose a whole line to a race, never interleave mid-line). Exists because `aurora-diag.log`/`AURORA_SECONDARY_LOG` is **rewritten** when the video session starts, erasing every line written during the connect — exactly when a local-host session moves displays around (source comment `:74-76`; this is the mechanism behind `docs/eclipse-windows-issues.md` issue #9). | MEASURED LIVE (RUN.md "20:30-20:44"): `20:42:21.374 window: on \\.\DISPLAY5 after a display change, home \\.\DISPLAY1 (local session: yes)` -> `20:42:27.672 window: moved to \\.\DISPLAY5 by a display change -> returned to \\.\DISPLAY1`, repeated `20:52:18.184` -> `18.274`, each pair co-timed with a panel screenshot showing the client back on the physical panel — the pairing (log line + screenshot), not either alone, is what proves application. Trap: ~0.5 KB/session, unbounded, no rotation — negligible today, a housekeeping item if local-host sessions become routine. |
| `LiSetLocalInputBlocked` / `localInputBlocked` | ours (Aurora's moonlight-common-c fork, NOT upstream) / SRC | `bool` argument. Declared in the patched `Limelight.h`, defined in the patched `InputStream.c` (`patches/client-deps/moonlight-common-c.patch`) as a setter for a static `volatile bool localInputBlocked` in that file. `inputSendThreadProc`'s dequeue loop frees every packet holder instead of sending it while set — one choke point for mouse, keyboard, controller, touch and pen input, all of which funnel through the same packet-holder queue; the check site itself (`if (localInputBlocked) { freePacketHolder(holder); continue; }`) carries no comment, so its meaning travels ONLY via the `Limelight.h` declaration and the call sites below — the "undocumented at the point of consumption" shape this register exists for. `session_worker.c:81` sets it `true` immediately after `local_host_session_begin()` succeeds (own row above — i.e. only when this client's own host is this same machine); `session_worker.c:269`, the `thread_cleanup` label (every exit path, same guarantee as `local_host_session_end()`), sets it back to `false`. Both call sites are `#ifdef _WIN32` only. | MEASURED 20:52 (RUN.md "CONTROLLER FEEDBACK LOOP"): before this existed, every local-host session (20:36:54, 20:39:42, 20:42:25) produced Apollo `sunshine.log` lines `Gamepad 0..3 will be DualShock 4 controller` from **one** physical DS4 — the client forwarded the pad to its own local Apollo, Apollo created a ViGEm pad on the same PC, the client picked that up as a NEW pad and forwarded it too, looping to the 4-pad cap (keyboard has the same shape: keys injected into the client's own foreground window). With the block active, the 20:52 session's Apollo log shows CLIENT CONNECTED and **zero** `Gamepad N will be` lines. **Trap/consequence:** this also blocks ALL of this PC's own live input to the locally-hosted game for the session's duration — the local game must read this PC's devices natively instead; mouse/keyboard handoff to the correct co-op pane is an open follow-on (RUN.md "Phase 3"), and traffic on the co-op peer socket is unaffected (only this process's own outbound queue is drained). |

### Co-op controller routes after "Both" was removed (added 2026-09-27)

`coop_padN_pane` rows above are seeded; these are the hand-written meanings the diff-sentinel 09-26 21:31
review found unregistered (`param.py` exit 1) when the dialog's third "Both" column was removed.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coop_pad_pane` | ours / SRC | `int coop_pad_pane[4]` (`app_settings.h:133`), persisted as `coop_pad1_pane`..`coop_pad4_pane` (`app_settings.c:388-391`). Value = a `COOP_PAD_*` route for gamestream pad slot N. DEFAULTS (`app_settings.c:222`): pads 1/3 -> `COOP_PAD_PRIMARY` (0), pads 2/4 -> `COOP_PAD_SECONDARY` (1) — the seeded rows' "0 for all four" is wrong for 2 and 4. Parse (`app_settings.c:905-914`) coerces any value other than 0/1 (including a stored 2 = BOTH) to PRIMARY. Read by `coop_input.c:109` (`coop_pad_route`), whose range check still admits BOTH, but the parser means it can no longer arrive there from a conf file. | Unit test `testCoopPadBothReadsAsPrimary` (tests/app/test_settings.c) proves the coercion. Live: the pad's input lands in the pane its route names (screenshot the pane reacting); a changed value applies at the next stream start. |
| `COOP_PAD_BOTH` | ours / SRC | Enum value 2 in `app_settings.h:236`, "sent to both hosts -- NOT OFFERED any more; parsed as PRIMARY". No producer survives in the UI or parser (diff-sentinel 21:5x enumerated them). Kept only so `coop_input.c:110-120` still compiles and so the number is not reused. | Nothing observable should produce it; a conf holding `coop_padN_pane = 2` reads back as 0 (the unit test above). |
| `COOP_ASSIGN_BOTH` | ours / SRC | Enum value 2 in `coop_assign.h:54`, must equal `COOP_PAD_BOTH` (same conf numbers). `coop_assign.c:83,93` still treat it as a load on every host and valid only with >1 host; the dialog no longer creates it. | Same as above: not reachable from the UI; covered by the existing coop_assign tests. |
| `PAD_ROUTE_COUNT` | ours / SRC | `#define PAD_ROUTE_COUNT 2` (`coop.dialog.c:76`): number of controller-destination columns in the co-op dialog (was 3 with "Both"). Sizes `pad_col[]`/`pad_col_title[]` (`:163-164`) and `pad_route_order[]` (`:566`); left/right navigation wraps modulo it (`:1130`). | Visible: the dialog shows exactly two controller columns, one under each PC card; LEFT/RIGHT on a controller row alternates between them. |
| `PAD_SECTION_COL_PCT` | ours / SRC | `#define PAD_SECTION_COL_PCT SECTION_COL_PCT` (`coop.dialog.c:74`, = 36): width percent of each controller column, equal to the Computers cards' width so each column sits under its PC. | Visible: controller columns align under the PC cards in a screenshot of the dialog. |

**Scope decision on `dispwake` verbs (`virtual-primary`, `physical-primary`, and the pre-existing
`physical-off`/`physical-on`/`physical-wake`) — NOT REGISTERED, deliberately.** These are `argv[1]`
string dispatch on a self-built CLI tool (`dispwake.cpp:main()`, plain `if (cmd == "...")` chain), not a
configuration surface: nothing is *set* by choosing a verb, and every one of this register's seven
surfaces plus every curated cross-process section (§16, §17, §30, this one) covers a name that is
**defined in one place and consumed at a distance, undocumented at the consumption site** — the exact
shape that produces a wrong guess. `dispwake`'s verbs fail that test in both directions: (1) the tool
prints full usage, including every verb and a one-line description, when run with no arguments
(`dispwake.cpp` ~line 622); (2) each exit code is documented in an 18-line comment directly above
`arrangeGroups()` (`dispwake.cpp:505-521`) covering exactly `virtual-primary`/`physical-primary`, and
the ONE place that consumes those exit codes — `apollo-display.cmd`'s `do_resume_local` — carries its
own adjacent comment naming what exit 3 means and why it retries (`"Exit 3 means the panel is dark ...
light it, then retry"`, directly above the `errorlevel 3` check). There is no reader of these codes
anywhere else in the tree. Registering them would duplicate documentation that is already colocated
with both its definition and its only use, which is not this register's job — its job is names that
travel WITHOUT their meaning. Per the task's own instruction, the pre-existing verbs
(`physical-off`/`physical-on`/`physical-wake`) are therefore also left unregistered; `physical-off` stays
in the register only as the already-flagged "mentioned but not registered" prose reference at §16 (a
separate, smaller issue — a bare mention with no row — not resolved by this entry).

## 39. Co-op loading gate and desktop video scaling (2026-09-27)

Ours unless stated. Added after the evidence-auditor found these names used without rows (`param.py` exit 1) while
fixing issue #29 (commit 238893ec: vertical co-op held "Connecting..." until the backstop).

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coop_first_real_splice` | ours / SRC | `static bool` (`session_video.c:125`), reset every session (`:~3512`). Means "the composite has put a picture on screen whose PEER tile is anchored (intra data)". Writers: horizontal tile path (`:~5345`, when `hh_peer_intra`), legacy two-slice splice (`:~5760`, peer NAL type 16..23), and since 238893ec the vertical compose (`:~4466`, when `vt_peer_intra`). The first two sit inside `if (coop_live && !coop_vt_enabled && ...)` (`:~4557`), so before 238893ec a vertical session had NO writer. Read only through `coop_composite_started()`. | Windows: the diag line `Co-op loading dialog released after N ms (composite anchored\|backstop)` (`streaming.controller.c:230`). MEASURED once (RUN Q1 2026-09-27): 1000 ms, composite anchored, vertical. TRAPS: (1) if the flag is already set when USER_STREAM_OPEN arrives, no timer starts and NO line is printed (`streaming.controller.c:~489-507`) -- an absent line proves nothing; (2) on Windows the line reaches `aurora-diag.log` through stderr and is OVERWRITTEN when the session start rotates that file with `"w"` (>5 MB; issue #9) -- move the log aside before a timing run; (3) on webOS `commons_log` goes to PmLog, not the diag file. The old backstop duration was never timed. |
| `coop_composite_started` | ours / SRC | Accessor `coop_composite_started()` (`session_video.c:126`) returning the flag above. Polled every `COOP_FIRST_PICTURE_POLL_MS` by `coop_first_picture_cb` (`streaming.controller.c:~219`), which closes the progress dialog when it is true or when `coop_wait_ms` reaches `COOP_FIRST_PICTURE_TIMEOUT_MS`; checked once at USER_STREAM_OPEN to decide whether to hold the dialog at all (only when `app_launch_coop_requested()`). Per process: only the primary's UI shows the dialog. | As above. |
| `vt_peer_intra` | ours / SRC | Local `bool` in the vertical compose (`session_video.c:~4276`). Set while walking tile 1's PEER VCL NALs (`if (side && vt_have_peer)`) when the ORIGINAL `nal_unit_type` is IRAP 16..23 -- read BEFORE the IDR->TRAIL conversion rewrites it. Committed to `coop_first_real_splice` only if the compose succeeds (`ok && out > 0 && out <= buffer_size`). Never set by a mirrored tile 1 (walks our own buffer) or a skip-conceal tile (`continue`s before the walk); IS set by a refused skip-conceal that falls back to a REPEATED peer IRAP (intra data, same as horizontal). | Via the release line on a VERTICAL session. |
| `hh_peer_intra` | ours / SRC | Horizontal counterpart (`session_video.c:4808`): `pe_irap` of the peer's slice read before conversion (`:5221`), also set when an IDR is converted to TRAIL (`:5282`). Gates the horizontal writer above. | Release line on a HORIZONTAL session. |
| `coop_vt_enabled` | ours / SRC | `static bool` (`session_video.c:396`) = `access("/tmp/coop-vmirror", F_OK) == 0` at session start (`:3448`) -- i.e. VERTICAL co-op (the `vmirror` sentinel is the vertical arm, see `Armed vertical: vmirror=1`). On Windows `/tmp` is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Aurora\runtime`. Excludes the horizontal/legacy splice block. LATCHED at session start. | `[COOP-VT]` lines appear only when it is set. |
| `SDL_HINT_RENDER_SCALE_QUALITY` | SDL2 (external, 2.32.10 headers in msys64) / DOC | SDL's own header (`SDL_hints.h`): `"0"`/`nearest` nearest sampling, `"1"`/`linear` linear filtering, `"2"`/`best` = linear; default nearest. Set to `"1"` by `src/app/lvgl/lv_disp_drv_app.c:14` before the renderer is created. The desktop video texture (`third_party/ss4s/modules/ffmpeg/ffmpegvid.c:705`, IYUV streaming) is scaled into the window by `SDL_RenderCopy` with an aspect-fit dst rect (`:854-868`). That SDL reads the hint when a texture is created is from SDL's source, not verified here. | Geometry MEASURED: a maximized 2560x1369 client shows a 2560x1440 composite at 2434x1369 (0.951), x=63 -- every Windows DDA crop since 09-27 uses that rect. Fullscreen (`fullscreen = true`, the app default) shows it 1:1. Linear-vs-nearest in effect: UNMEASURED. |
| `SDL_HINT_JOYSTICK_ALLOW_BACKGROUND_EVENTS` | SDL2 (external, 2.32.10, `C:\msys64\mingw64\include\SDL2\SDL_hints.h:681-693`) / DOC | SDL's header: `"0"` = joystick and gamecontroller events are NOT delivered while the app is in the background, `"1"` = they are; default `"0"`; may be set at any time. Set to `"1"` by `local_input_arm()` (`src/app/platform/windows/local_input_win.c`) for a co-op session in which one pane is THIS PC, and restored (an unset hint as `"0"`) by `local_input_disarm()`. Needed because the game on this PC's virtual display holds the focus whenever the cursor is handed over, and pads routed to the OTHER PC come through this client. | UNMEASURED (2026-09-27). Test: SKYNET+VEGA co-op, pad routed to VEGA, cursor handed to SKYNET's game -> the pad must keep driving VEGA's pane (VEGA `Gamepad` input, picture). `local-host.log` "input: armed ... pads read in the background" says the arm ran, not that SDL honoured it. |
| `SDL_HINT_MOUSE_FOCUS_CLICKTHROUGH` | SDL2 (external, 2.32.10) / DOC + TEST | SDL's header (`C:\msys64\mingw64\include\SDL2\SDL_hints.h:1566-1576`): `"0"` = ignore mouse clicks that activate a window (default), `"1"` = generate events for them. Upstream source `src/video/windows/SDL_windowsevents.c:333-356` (tag release-2.32.10): a button press while `focus_click_pending` is set is DROPPED unless the hint is true. Set to `"1"` by `local_input_arm()` (`local_input_win.c`) for a co-op session with a pane on THIS PC, restored (unset as `"0"`) by `local_input_disarm()`. Needed because after the hotkey returns an ASSIGNED mouse, the game on the virtual display still has the focus, so the click on this PC's pane is exactly an activating click. | MEASURED 2026-09-28 02:12 (K4, build 1d4c5a99, SKYNET+VEGA vertical, mouse assigned to SKYNET): after each of two hotkey returns, one click on the client logged `input: click in this PC's pane -> the assigned mouse goes over again` + `handed over #N` in `%ProgramData%\Eclipse\local-host.log`, cursor then on the virtual display. Control: K3 on f7544cb0 (no hint) -- the same click produced no line. Proof of application = that log line; without it the click never reached the client. |
| `input_names_start()` / `last_keyboard` | ours / SRC (`src/app/platform/windows/input_names_win.c`) | Background thread with a message-only window registered for RAW INPUT of the KEYBOARD class only (usage page 0x01, usage 0x06, `RIDEV_INPUTSINK`); every `WM_INPUT` from a keyboard stores its device handle in `last_keyboard`. `input_device_name()` then names THAT device as the keyboard and passes it over for the mouse. Why keyboard only: SDL2 2.32.10 registers the MOUSE class (`SDL_windowsmouse.c:59`, relative mode) and the PAD classes 1/4, 1/5, 1/8 (`SDL_rawinputjoystick.c:2089`) but never the keyboard -- raw input is one target window per class per process, so registering a class SDL uses would take its messages away. Started once from `app_init()` after SDL video. | Prove by `%ProgramData%\Eclipse\local-host.log`: `input-names: keyboard watcher started (raw input, keyboard class)` at launch (or `NOT started (... error N)`), then `input-names: typed on \\?\HID#VID_xxxx&PID_yyyy...` at the first real key press and whenever a different keyboard is used. The dialog alone cannot prove it on SKYNET: diff-sentinel 2026-09-28 showed the fallback (keyboard = a device other than the mouse) already names both rows right there ("USB Receiver (Mouse)", "Keychron Link (Keyboard)"; before: both "USB Receiver", K4dlg.png 02:11). TRAP: injected keys (`SendInput`/`keybd_event`, i.e. every test tool here) carry no device (`hDevice` NULL) and are ignored -- the watcher needs one REAL key press. |
| `coop_pointer_set_local_pane()` / `local_pane` | ours / SRC (`src/app/stream/input/coop_pointer.c`) | Which co-op pane's host is THIS machine: 0 primary, 1 peer, -1 neither. Set by `start_stream()` (`coop.dialog.c`) from `local_host_is_this_pc()` on both hosts' addresses (always -1 off Windows); LATCHED by `coop_pointer_start()`. Nothing is ever sent to that pane; the pointer entering it calls `local_input_hand_off()` instead. | `[COOP-PTR] pointer split armed: ... this PC's pane PRIMARY|PEER|none` in aurora-diag.log; `tests/coop/test_coop_pointer.c` case 8 (nothing sent to the local pane, the other pane untouched). |
| `COOP_KBM_MOUSE` / `COOP_KBM_KEYBOARD` | `"@mouse"` / `"@keyboard"`, ours (`src/app/app_settings.h`) | Reserved names under which the mouse and the keyboard are claimed per PC in hosts.ini, exactly like a controller's `pad=` claim, while `coop_kbm_assign` is on. Never in `known_pads`; the settings board and the co-op dialog add them as items after the controllers and keep them OUT of the controllers' even spread. | `pcmanager_node_has_pad(node, "@mouse")` true for the PC it was moved to; the dialog pre-fills from it. |
| `coop_pointer_set_pins()` / `mouse_pin`, `keys_pin` | ours / SRC (`coop_pointer.c`) | The session's assigned mouse and keyboard as panes (0 primary, 1 peer, -1 follows the pointer), set by `start_stream()` from the dialog's items; LATCHED at `coop_pointer_start()`. An assigned mouse never crosses (clamped into its pane); assigned keys go to their pane wherever the pointer is. With this PC as a host they become `local_input_arm(..., mouse_here, keys)`. | `tests/coop/test_coop_pointer.c` case 9; `[COOP-PTR] assigned: ...` line. |
| `EDGE_MARGIN` | `6` px, ours (`local_input_win.c`) | Distance kept between a placed cursor and the edge that would send it straight back: a hand-over lands 6 px inside the virtual display's divider edge, a return lands 6 px into the other pane. Without it the cursor would cross back on the next watcher tick. | `local-host.log` "handed over ... -> display (x,y)" and "back ... -> client (x,y)" show the placed points. Size chosen, not measured for feel. |
| `RETURN_HOTKEY_ID` (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Z) | ours (`local_input_win.c`) | Global hotkey registered by the watcher thread ONLY while the cursor is handed over; brings it back for games that recentre or clip the cursor. Same keys as moonlight's "ungrab input" combo (`KeyComboUngrabInput`, `session_keyboard.c`), which Eclipse otherwise only logs. | `local-host.log` "back #n (hotkey)". Registration can fail if another program owns the combo -- UNMEASURED whether anything on SKYNET does. |

## 40. Client latency counters — the `[LATENCY]` diag fields and the stats overlay (2026-09-28)

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sub_ms` (`[LATENCY]`) / "submit" (overlay) | ours / SRC | Mean of `LiGetMillis() - decodeUnit->enqueueTimeMs`, taken AFTER the feed returns (`session_video.c:6287`), over the frames of the window: from moonlight-common-c handing us a complete frame to the feed call returning. On WINDOWS the FFmpeg module decodes, reads back (D3D11VA) and converts INSIDE that call, so `sub_ms` INCLUDES the decode; on webOS the NDL feed returns before decoding. Integer-ms samples. | Read it together with `dec_ms`: on Windows `dec_ms` <= `sub_ms` always (it is a part). TRAP: do not add them on Windows -- the overlay says "submit X ms (decode Y ms)" and the quality dot leaves the decode out (`streaming.controller.c`, #32). |
| `dec_ms` (`[LATENCY]`) / "decode" (overlay), `avgDecoderLatency`, `has_decoder_latency` | ss4s stats (ours via `patches/client-deps/ss4s.patch`) / SRC | `SS4S_PlayerGetVideoLatency()` averaged over 1 s. MEANS DIFFERENT THINGS PER PLATFORM: webOS NDL = render-buffer length x feed interval (`ndl_video.c:163`, a queue-depth proxy, see the comment at `session_video.c:~6380`); WINDOWS (FFmpeg module, since 2026-09-28) = feed start -> the frame published for the UI thread (decode + D3D11VA readback + I420 conversion), BeginFrame/EndFrame in `ffmpegvid.c` Feed. `-1.0` = no samples (before 2026-09-28 it was ALWAYS -1 on Windows: the module had no PlayerDriver, #32). `has_decoder_latency` latches true at the first sample and is never cleared. | Windows before/after: V5 on 0e1b4bd8 logged `dec_ms=-1.0` and the overlay "Decoder latency: not available"; a build with #32 must log a positive `dec_ms` and show "submit X ms (decode Y ms)". TRAP (pre-existing, SS4S): the stats ring's `size` is a `uint8` while capacity is 2 x fps, so at >=128 fps about one read in 256 returns -1, which the latched flag shows as "decode 0.00 ms" (diff-sentinel 2026-09-28). |

## 40. Default playback device restore, host audio, Nintendo pads, remote slots (2026-09-29)

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `localAudioPlayMode` | GameStream launch/resume query; OWNER Umbra (Sunshine) | 1 = play on the host's own output; 0 = Umbra moves the host's DEFAULT playback device to its virtual sink (Steam Streaming Speakers) for the session. `nvhttp.cpp:1281` -> `session.host_audio` -> `rtsp.cpp:1009` `HOST_AUDIO` -> `audio.cpp:173` (virtual sink only when `!HOST_AUDIO`). Sent from `localaudio` (libgamestream `client.c:618`); Eclipse forces 1 for a host that is THIS PC (`session_worker.c`, local-host block). | Umbra log `Selected audio sink: virtual-Stereo{...}` = 0, a plain device id = 1. TRAP: on `/resume` Umbra honours it only with no active session (`nvhttp.cpp:1455`). |
| `audio_restore_sink.txt` | Umbra state file (config dir); ours, `src/audio.cpp` | Line 1 endpoint id, line 2 friendly name of the last default device that was NOT the virtual sink. Written at every audio start while the default is a real device; read when the default is found ON the virtual sink (an earlier restore failed / crash), which is then restored at session end. | Log: `Default audio device is the virtual sink, left over ...; will restore [name]`, then `Restored default audio device [name] after N s` or `... by name [name]`. MEASURED on VEGA 2026-09-29, tests A (stock: stuck) and B/C/D (fixed), RUN.md. |
| `nintendo_layout` | moonlight.ini `[input]`; ours (`app_settings.h`, `input_gamepad.c`) | 0 off (Nintendo pads follow `swap_abxy`), 1 buttons as printed (A = A), 2 Xbox positions (B = A). Applies ONLY to pads detected as Nintendo (SDL controller type, else USB vendor 057e); `swap_abxy` keeps swapping every other pad. Unset (-1) until read: an existing config gets `swap_abxy ? 2 : 1`, a new one 1. `SDL_HINT_GAMECONTROLLER_USE_BUTTON_LABELS` is pinned to 1 (`app_input.c`) so SDL's A is the PRINTED A. | Log `Controller #N (...) connected -- Nintendo pad, A/B X/Y as printed / swapped (Xbox positions)`. MEASURED on the Linux client 2026-09-29 with a uinput 057e:2009 pad (default, and migration from `swap_abxy = true`). |
| remote-like pad slots | rule, ours (`input_gamepad.c` `next_gamepad_gs_id`) | A controller whose NAME reads as a remote (`remote`, `rcu`, `air mouse`, `media center`, `ir receiver`) takes host slots from the TOP (3, 2 ...); real pads fill from 0 -- games that only listen to player 1 (or 1-4) kept the pad as player 2 behind a remote. webOS still ignores remotes outright. | Log `Controller #3 (<remote>) connected` while a pad plugged in later is `#0`. MEASURED on the Linux client 2026-09-29 (uinput "Amazon Fire TV Remote" before an Xbox pad). |
| `COOP_PC_DECODER` | compile-time; ours (`coop_coord.h:31`) | Defined on Windows and desktop Linux: the co-op splice rules measured on PC hardware decoders (D3D11VA, NVDEC; VA-API measured 2026-09-29) -- keepref, own-slice retiming, catch-up buffer. webOS / Steam Link keep their own. | `docs/eclipse-linux-issues.md` L11: NVDEC mis-decoded the peer tile without it (0/399 frames differ with it). |
| `diag_logging` / `DIAG_LOGS_KEEP` / `DIAG_LOGS_SHARE_PORT` | moonlight.ini top level + constants; ours (`util/diag_logs.[ch]`, About pane) | ON: at start stderr (and stdout on Linux/webOS, where webOS's logger also prints) is reopened onto `<conf>/logs/eclipse-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.log`; `logs/index.txt` keeps the newest `DIAG_LOGS_KEEP` (5). Takes effect at the NEXT start. "Share logs..." (any value) serves them read-only on TCP `DIAG_LOGS_SHARE_PORT` (47900, next free up to 47909) under a random 8-hex token path while its dialog is open: kept logs, `AURORA_DIAG_LOG`, `AURORA_SECONDARY_LOG` (ANSI colour stripped), `moonlight.ini` and `hosts.ini` with any key containing `pin_code`, `password`, `token` or `secret` served as `<removed>`. | Log `Kept diagnostic log started ...` / `Sharing logs at http://ip:port/token/` / `Stopped sharing logs`. MEASURED Linux client 2026-09-29: kept file + index created; page served; wrong token 404; all.txt 5 sections, fake PIN absent, 0 ESC bytes; after Stop sharing, connection refused. The TV accepts inbound LAN TCP (measured on 47900 as the dev user). |
| `DIAG_LOGS_BUNDLE_NAME` / Share logs per platform | constant `"eclipse-logs.txt"`, ours (`util/diag_logs.[ch]` `diag_logs_write_bundle`, About pane) | Desktop "Share logs..." writes `<conf>/logs/eclipse-logs.txt`: the SAME text the page's `all.txt` serves (one writer, `out_t` = socket or FILE), replaced each time; "Open folder" = `SDL_OpenURL(file:///<logs dir>)`. WINDOWS: bundle + folder ONLY, NO network listener -- the first listen raised Windows Firewall's prompt (PickerHost FirewallNotificationDialogServer), which took focus from the fullscreen app and left the dialog's buttons dead; dismissing it created two "Query User" BLOCK rules. LINUX: bundle + page. WEBOS: page only (its files are out of reach). | Log `Wrote the log bundle <path>`. MEASURED 2026-09-29: Linux (Xvfb) bundle 6 sections = page's sections, PIN 0 hits, 0 ESC, Open folder keeps the dialog, Stop sharing -> refused; Windows on VEGA (exe cadcf5c3) bundle 7 sections, PIN 0 hits, 0 ESC, listeners 47900-47909 = 0, no firewall prompt, Open folder -> "logs - File Explorer", Close dismisses. |
| `audio: coordinator link failed at <step> errno=N` | log pattern, ours (`coop_audio.c` `coop_audio_secondary_link_failed`), co-op secondary log | Why the co-op SECONDARY's audio link to the primary's mixer is down; `<step>` = socket / connect / nonblocking / attach / config. Logged once per distinct cause. Before 2026-09-29 every step failed silently. | MEASURED 2026-09-29 (Windows client on VEGA, co-op VEGA + PC2): "coordinator link failed at connect errno=5 (Input/output error)" then "audio: connected to coordinator", primary "peer=up msgs=102"; the secondary then forwarded 102 frames and stopped because the host went silent. A first dial that races the coordinator used to wait for the 200th packet (a silent host sent none: "peer=down msgs=0" for a whole session, run 1) -- FIXED the same day, see the next row. |
| co-op secondary audio re-dial: `sec_last_dial_ms` / `COOP_AUDIO_DIAL_LONG_AGO` | ours (`coop_audio.c` `coop_secondary_aud_feed`), 1000 ms | The secondary dials the primary's mixer at audio init, then from the audio feed AT MOST ONCE A SECOND and at once on the first packet after a quiet spell (was: every 200th packet). A send failure resets it to "long ago" (the next packet re-dials). | MEASURED 2026-09-29 (Windows client 51b85bde, co-op VEGA + PC2): init dial lost the race (errno=5), PC2 silent -> link down; a 5 s tone on PC2 -> "audio: connected to coordinator" on its first packet, primary "peer=up msgs=84 ... peak peer=8354", 1002 messages over the 5.06 s tone (the whole of it). |
| kept log on webOS: `stdout = stderr` | ours (`diag_logs.c` `diag_logs_start`, glibc only) | After the dup2 of stderr onto fd 1, the stdout STREAM is pointed at stderr's, because webOS's logger (`logging_pmlog.c`) prints info/debug lines to stdout. | MEASURED on the TV (webOS 26, beta, 2026-09-29): WITHOUT it the kept log held only stderr lines (3, then 7 after a clean close -- not one info line, even at exit); WITH it, 49 lines incl. "System: webOS 11.2.0 (HE_DTV_W25O_AFABATAA)" and "Launched with parameters". Cause of the lost stdout lines not established. |
| `wired_pin_mode` (+ launch `auroraWiredMac` / `auroraOwnMacs` / `auroraOwnIps`) | moonlight.ini; ours. webOS only (UI and launch path under `TARGET_WEBOS`) | 0 AUTO (ask only a host whose serverinfo `UmbraCaps` has bit 0), 1 OFF, 2 ON (ask any host; stock hosts ignore the parameters). Both need a wired card that is up (`stream/wired_nic.c`: not wireless, `eth*`/`en*`, operstate up, IPv4; fastest wins). The launch then carries that card's MAC, all the TV's MACs and IPv4 addresses (`gs_set_wired_pin`, also on /resume). Replaces the rig-only `arp-pin.ps1` (§17), whose MACs were hard-coded and which ran for every client. | TV log `Wired pin: asking <host> to keep the stream on <if> ...` / `no wired network card is up` / `<host> does not offer it`. Not yet run on the TV: webOS 26 shows NO interface for the plugged-in UE300 (r8152 is built into the 6.12 kernel; see RUN.md 09-29). |
