| `UmbraCaps` / `wired_pin_state.txt` | Umbra serverinfo + config-dir state file; ours (`src/wired_pin.[ch]`, `nvhttp.cpp`, `process.cpp`) | `UmbraCaps` is sent by every Umbra build from 2026-09-29 (bit 0 = can pin; Windows); Eclipse also takes its PRESENCE as "this host runs Umbra" (`aurora_host_caps.c`), since `EncoderVendor`/`CoopProfileCaps` come from a patch no deployed host carried. The pin: after the launch's do-commands, each claimed IPv4 that is ON-LINK (best route has no next hop) and whose current neighbour entry is absent or points at one of the claimed MACs gets a Permanent entry to the wired MAC; the launch's source address must be one of the claimed IPs. Reverted in `proc_t::terminate` after the undo-commands; `wired_pin_state.txt` lists live pins and is reverted at the next start after a crash. | Umbra log `Wired pin: <ip> -> <mac> (interface N) until the session ends` / `... belongs to another device here, left alone` / `released <ip>`. MEASURED on VEGA 2026-09-29 with a crafted /launch from SKYNET: own IP pinned Permanent, PC2's IP refused, released at /cancel (entry back to Reachable, state file empty). |

## 41. Support logs: report, crash and frozen-UI records, run marker, host log (2026-09-30)

How a user gets the file and how to read it: `docs/support-logs.md`. Everything here was measured on Linux, Windows
(VEGA) and the TV (webOS 26) on 2026-09-30 unless the row says otherwise.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ECLIPSE_BUILD_COMMIT` | compile definition, moonlight-lib PRIVATE; ours (`CMakeLists.txt`: `git rev-parse --short=8`, `-dirty` when the tree is uncommitted) | The commit in every report's `App:` line (`unknown` without git). The version (1.0.8) is not bumped per build, so the report also carries the running binary's SHA-256 (`Binary:` line, hashed on a worker thread, logged when done). | `[Report] App: Eclipse 1.0.8 for <platform>, commit <8 hex>[-dirty]`; `sha256sum` of the shipped binary equals the `Binary:` hash. Trap: the hash, not the commit, identifies the file. |
| `[Report]` start report / full report | log pattern; ours (`util/diag_report.c` `diag_report_log_start` / `diag_report_log`) | START (`=== Eclipse diagnostic report, at start (before video, audio and input are up) ===`, right after the kept log opens): build + binary, OS / firmware / kernel, CPU, memory, GPUs + drivers, storage, network, settings. FULL (`=== Eclipse diagnostic report ===`, end of `app_init`): adds SDL drivers, displays / panel, media modules + caps, audio devices, controllers and SDL joysticks, computers. The Share bundle / page starts with a fresh full report. | A run that died while starting has ONLY the start block: that is its purpose. `SDL x (video and audio not started yet)` in it is expected. |
| `[Host]` lines | log pattern; ours (`diag_report_log_host`, from `pclist.c` upsert / mark_offline) | One line per computer whenever what it reports changes (FNV-1a signature per uuid, 16 slots), so a status poll does not flood the log. | "GPU not reported" = the host does not send it (stock hosts), not a detection failure. |
| `Session start` / `[Stats]` / `Session end` / `Stream totals` | log patterns; ours (`stream/session_worker.c`, `stream/video/session_video.c` `vdec_stat_submit` / `vdec_stats_log_totals`) | `Session start: host …, codecs offered …`; while a log is kept a `Stats` line every 10 s (fps and kbps = `receivedBytes * 8 / ms` of the last window; frames lost COUNTED SINCE THE PREVIOUS LINE -- it used to be the last ~2 s window only, which showed "0 lost" on every line of a stream that lost 15%; decode time; plus "the decoder is the limit" when that explains the losses); `Session end after …` / `FAILED before connecting` / `FAILED TO START` with the interrupt reason and the moonlight-common-c error in words (`conn_error_text`, e.g. `NO VIDEO TRAFFIC`, code -100); `Stream totals`. | A stream's kept log has all four (Linux and webOS streams; the Windows failed path, 2026-09-30). Trap: kbps was 1000x too high before it was computed per ms. |
| `codecModeSupport` | `SERVER_DATA` field; ours (`libgamestream/client.[ch]`, `pcmanager_common.c` `serverdata_clone`) | The codec mask the host ADVERTISES (`ServerCodecModeSupport`), kept apart because a session overwrites `serverInfo.serverCodecModeSupport` with the negotiated one. `serverdata_clone` copies it with `umbraCaps`(+Known) and `sessionLocked`(+Known). | Reading `serverCodecModeSupport` after a session gives the negotiated mask, not what the host can do. |
| `UMBRA_CAP_HOST_LOG` / `umbralog` (`GET /umbralog`) | `UmbraCaps` bit 1 (0x2), `aurora_host_caps.h`; Umbra `nvhttp.cpp` `umbralog()`; Eclipse `gs_get_host_log` + `session_log_host` | Umbra from d4d79b59 serves the tail of its own log over HTTPS to a PAIRED client with all permissions: `GET /umbralog?bytes=N` (4096..1048576, default 262144), starting at a whole line, secrets redacted (password / passwd / salt / token / secret / credential / cookie, and config keys containing pass / salt / token / secret / cred / key / pin). Eclipse fetches 256 KB at the end of every session (primary process, 8 s timeout) into the kept log between `===== HOST LOG: … =====` and `===== END HOST LOG =====`. | VEGA / PC2 serverinfo `UmbraCaps` 3; a TV session's log carried the block (683 lines). Without bit 1: `Host log: this host does not offer its log`; a dead host: `Host log: could not fetch it … Timeout`. SKYNET is still d35d3270 (no bit 1). |
| `running.txt` (the run marker, `<conf>/logs/running.txt`) | file; ours (`util/diag_logs.c` `diag_logs_run_begin` / `diag_logs_run_end`, `app.c`) | Written at start (`started <time>, Eclipse <version>`), removed as the LAST step of `app_deinit`; primary process only. Found at the next start = the last run did not end cleanly: that start keeps its log EVEN WITH `diag_logging` OFF (`The previous run (…) did not end cleanly … Keeping this run's log although "Keep diagnostic logs" is off`) and on desktops rewrites `eclipse-logs.txt` at once (`Share bundle refreshed after that`). | kill -9 / End task / crash → the next start keeps; a clean close (SIGTERM, WM_CLOSE, the webOS app manager's close) removes it. A failed start (`app_init` returns -1) leaves it too, on purpose. |
| `CRASH` record (`=== CRASH: …`) | log pattern; ours (`util/crash_log.c`) | POSIX: `=== CRASH: signal N (NAME), fault address X (code C)`, or for a signal SENT by a process (`si_code <= 0`) `sent by pid P (uid U), code C` / `raised by this process itself (abort/raise), code -6`; then pc, on ARM lr / sp, pid; ARM 128 stack words; glibc backtrace; `/proc/self/maps`; `=== END CRASH ===`. Windows: exception code, `module+offset`, access-violation detail, StackWalk64 frames. Installed when a kept log starts. | Test hooks below. Trap: before 2026-09-30 a kill / raise printed the sender's pid as the "fault address" (the TV's own pid, 0x44b8). ARM backtraces come out EMPTY: the stack words are the walk. |
| `UI THREAD STUCK` record (`=== UI THREAD STUCK: …`); `STALL_FIRST_S` 5 / `STALL_STARTUP_S` 20 / `STALL_SHUTDOWN_S` 15 / `STALL_AGAIN_S` 25 / `STALL_MAX_RECORDS` 6 | constants + log pattern; ours (`util/crash_log.c`; heartbeat `crash_log_ui_alive` once per `app_run_loop` pass, `crash_log_ui_shutdown` at `app_deinit`) | With a kept log, a watchdog thread counts ITS OWN seconds without a main-loop pass (a suspended process is not a stall): at 5 s (20 before the first pass, 15 once closing) `[Watchdog] The UI thread has not run for N s…` and the record with the UI thread's stack; again 25 s later; at most 6 per run; `The UI thread is running again, after about N s stuck`. Windows: suspend → registers + a copy of the stack → resume → StackWalk64 on the copy (nothing that takes a lock runs while it is suspended). Linux / webOS: `SIGRTMIN+5` snapshot (pc / lr / sp, 256 ARM stack words, glibc backtrace off ARM), maps once per run. | Test hooks below. Trap: `SIGRTMIN+5` is also `vendor_diag.c`'s sampling signal: in an `AURORA_VENDOR_DIAG` build this watchdog is off (vendor_diag has its own hang watch); if something else owns the signal the record says `(no stack: …)`. A modal message box or a dragged window is a real UI stall and is recorded. |
| `ECLIPSE_TEST_CRASH` (`early` / `1`) / `ECLIPSE_TEST_HANG` (seconds) | environment, TEST ONLY, desktop builds; ours (`app.c`) | Only while a log is kept: `early` = null write right after the start report (a crash while starting); `1` = once the app is up; `HANG=N` = the UI thread sleeps N s on its first main-loop pass. | Without a kept log (setting off, last run clean) they do nothing. |
| `aurora_test_crash` / `aurora_test_hang` / `AURORA_TEST_HOOKS` | webOS launch parameters, BETA builds only (`AURORA_TEST_HOOKS=1` for `AURORA_VARIANT=beta`); ours (`platform/webos/app_webos.c`) | `{"aurora_test_crash":true}` → `raise(SIGSEGV)` in `app_handle_launch`; `{"aurora_test_hang":N}` → the UI thread sleeps N s on its next pass. The dev shell runs as another uid and cannot signal the app, so these are the TV's only way to test the records. | The live app id never has them. `ares-launch` runs the beta with the TV in STANDBY too (it logged normally, 2026-09-30). |
| `crash_frames` (`scripts/support/crash_frames.py`) | tool; ours (`scripts/support/crash_frames.py`) | Names the frames of CRASH / UI THREAD STUCK records against the EXACT shipped binary (every package keeps its symbols): Windows `module+offset` + PE ImageBase; Linux `(+0xOFF)` backtrace lines; webOS pc / lr / stack words through the record's own maps (ET_EXEC absolute, ET_DYN minus the file's offset-0 mapping, Thumb bit on ARM only, `nm -S` sizes drop words past a function's end). | 2026-09-30 records named: Windows `app_init` / `app_run_loop`, Linux the same, TV `app_handle_launch` / `app_run_loop`. Trap: the binary must be the one whose sha256 the log names; stack-word hits are possible return addresses, stale words appear too. |
| `PCManager` / `worker_context_finalize` ("`<action> failed on <computer or address>: <reason> (code N)`") | log pattern; ours (`backend/pcmanager/worker/request.c` `worker_context_finalize`, name from `worker_name`) | A host action the USER asked for -- Pairing, Quitting the running app, Wake-on-LAN, Adding a computer by address -- that failed, with the host's own reason (`Bad status <code>: <message>` from `xml_status`) or the transport error (cURL). Before 2026-09-30 it was shown in a dialog only (e.g. "Unable to quit game ... same client", which was really a TIMEOUT while the host reverted its display: PC2's log said `Restoring HDR settings failed due to retry timeout!`). Adding shows the address (and a port only when one was typed); the others the computer's uuid (match it to its `[Host]` line). Status polls and discovery are not named: their outcome is the `[Host]` lines. | VEGA: `Adding a computer by address failed on address 192.0.2.250: cURL error: Timeout was reached (code -5)`; TV: `... Couldn't connect to server`. Trap: pairing's `arg1` is the PIN -- it is never printed (only adding's hostport is). |
| `lv_drv_sdl_key` / `SDL key repeats` (dropped in the UI key driver) | rule; ours (`lvgl/input/lv_drv_sdl_key.c` `sdl_input_read`) | An `SDL_KEYDOWN` with `repeat` set is not passed to LVGL (nor to a session: `stream_input_handle_key` ignored repeats already). LVGL repeats a held key itself (long-press repeat), so held arrows still repeat. Reason: on webOS the key that focuses a text field opens the system keyboard mid-press, its release goes to that keyboard, and SDL repeated the key forever -- the dialog's focus bounced in and out of the field ~2x/s and the remote looked dead until Back. | TV 2026-09-30: before, one UP into the Add-computer address field = 32 `Starting/Stopping text input` cycles; after, 1 start and no flapping through 112 typed presses. Trap: after such a leak SDL still thinks the key is down, so the user's NEXT press of that key arrives flagged as a repeat and is dropped once. |
| `the decoder is the limit` (Stats / Stream totals hint) | log pattern + threshold 0.75; ours (`stream/video/session_video.c` `vdec_stat_submit`, `vdec_stats_log_totals`) | Appended when frames were "lost on the network" while the decoder was busy >= 75% of the time (decoder latency x received fps): "the decoder is the limit (N ms a frame = at most ~M fps): these were most likely dropped in this device, not on the network". moonlight-common-c counts frames the receive path could not finish (the decoder runs on the receive thread: direct submit) as network drops ("Sending RFI request for unrecoverable frame"), so a saturated decoder looks exactly like a lossy network. | VEGA (Windows, D3D11VA + readback, 2026-09-30): 4K = 32 ms a frame, ~30 fps cap, 14-17% "lost" with ZERO UDP receive errors and zero NIC discards; the same host at 1080p = 8 ms, 0 lost; the TV at 4K = 10-15 ms, 0 lost. 0.85 missed a 0.81 start window, hence 0.75 (TV 0.57, VEGA 1080p 0.49 stay clear). The cause on Windows is known: D3D11VA frames are read back and converted for the shared SDL renderer (third_party/ss4s/modules/ffmpeg/ffmpegvid.c header). |
| `settings_open_staging` "wb" (moonlight.ini line endings) | rule; ours (`app_settings.c`) | The settings file is written in BINARY mode: the ini writer (`third_party/commons/io/ini-writer`) ends every line with "\r\n" itself, and Windows text mode doubled the CR -- every line of moonlight.ini ended "\r\r\n" (99 of 99 on VEGA, the original file too). Linux/webOS bytes unchanged. | VEGA 2026-09-30: after one save, 99 lines, 0 CR CR LF, 99 CR LF; the old "\r\r\n" file was read fine first (inih strips trailing whitespace). Linux: 102 lines CR LF, 0 doubled. Trap: a script editing moonlight.ini on Windows must expect "\r\r\n" in files written by older builds. |

## 42. GitHub Actions repository-visibility gate (2026-09-30)

Externally owned and invisible to `param_extract.py`'s seven project-specific surfaces. This row
is scoped to this repository's `push` and `pull_request` build workflows.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `github.event.repository.private` | GitHub Actions / DOC | Boolean from the repository object in the event payload that triggered the run: `true` means the repository is private and `false` means it is not private. GitHub's [Actions context reference](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/contexts#github-context) defines `github.event` as the full triggering webhook payload. GitHub's [`push`](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/webhook-events-and-payloads#push) and [`pull_request`](https://docs.github.com/en/webhooks/webhook-events-and-payloads#pull_request) payload contracts both require `repository`; the pull-request contract explicitly calls it the repository where the event occurred, distinct from the separate head/source repository. GitHub's immutable OpenAPI repository schema defines `private` as a Boolean, [“Whether the repository is private”](https://github.com/github/rest-api-description/blob/67abf4515e6df41e32e1c182fd097b9599e1a32f/descriptions/api.github.com/api.github.com.yaml#L89526-L89528), and includes it in the schema's [required fields](https://github.com/github/rest-api-description/blob/67abf4515e6df41e32e1c182fd097b9599e1a32f/descriptions/api.github.com/api.github.com.yaml#L89727-L89763). This is an event-payload snapshot, not a live visibility query. | On a harmless `push` or `pull_request` run, print only `jq -c '{private: .repository.private}' "$GITHUB_EVENT_PATH"` and compare it with the event repository's visibility; the expected payload fragment is `{"private":true}` while private and `{"private":false}` once public. For the actual gate, use the direct positive Boolean condition `if: ${{ github.event.repository.private }}`: the upload step must run and create the named snapshot artifact while private; after publication it must be marked **Skipped** and no snapshot artifact may exist. **OPEN:** no Actions run was triggered while registering this field. GitHub documents that a missing context property evaluates to an empty string and that [empty strings are falsy](https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/expressions#literals), so this positive test fails closed on an event without the field; do not invert it into `== false` as a safety gate. **Trap:** the gate prevents new uploads only; it cannot remove artifacts retained from earlier private runs, which must be expired or deleted separately before changing repository visibility. It does not belong in §12 because it is evaluated once by GitHub before the gated step and has direct run/skip and artifact-presence observables rather than a silently applied runtime setting. |

## 43. webOS external-source pins and package-only install controls (2026-09-30)

These names are outside `param_extract.py`'s seven project-specific surfaces. `SOURCE_DIR` is
registered for the four named webOS fallback `ExternalProject` targets below; `INSTALL_DIR` is
documented for `ext_sdl2_backport` only because the pending licence install does not consume it.
Neither generic name is a project-wide path alias.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SOURCE_DIR`, `<SOURCE_DIR>` (named CMake `ExternalProject` targets only) | Kitware CMake / DOC; target applications are Aurora/commons-c SRC | CMake's [`ExternalProject` directory-options and property documentation](https://cmake.org/cmake/help/v3.24/module/ExternalProject.html#directory-options) defines this as the directory into which downloaded content is unpacked, replaces `<SOURCE_DIR>` in step command lines with that target property, and says `ExternalProject_Get_Property(<target> SOURCE_DIR)` writes the value to an ordinary variable named `SOURCE_DIR`. The supported licence-package uses are separate retrievals for `ext_sdl2_backport`, `ext_inih`, `ext_mbedtls`, and `ext_opus`, after each target has been created. None supplies an explicit source directory/prefix, so the documented default is `<current-binary-dir>/<target>-prefix/src/<target>`; each current generated `*-urlinfo.txt:7` records that exact shape. | After configure, compare each retrieved value with its own target's generated `source_dir=` record, then hash the exact source notice: `ext_sdl2_backport/share/licenses/SDL2/LICENSE.txt` = `97f35b302b361680ec1e891e95d2d52097bb95abff361434916d99dc1305f127`; `ext_inih/LICENSE.txt` = `9ae3b39e83e9158e44b67733baa3bb2d84e80efdcfb14a5820210a42c7abdf7f`; `ext_mbedtls/LICENSE` = `cfc7749b96f63bd31c3c42b5c471bf756814053e847c10f3eb003417bc523d30`; `ext_opus/COPYING` = `8338ce8d922bb4416ce3dd1e5680173332435e3f0755007ac7801ccd674fe682`; and `ext_opus/AUTHORS` = `f9fd849e652219ce98e008303370b352d37663d0ffb27b8dc514ad93c9c7438b`. The unpacked IPK must contain the five corresponding notice files with those hashes; the SDL bytes are deliberately installed a second time under the controller-database notice name, but that duplicate is not a fifth external-source input. **MEASURED 2026-09-30:** the package build selected all four fallback targets and the unpacked `com.aurora.gamestream_1.0.8_arm-legalnotices-UNTESTED-20260930.ipk` contained every named file with the registered digest. Package SHA-256: `10d7464cb129df89c6c7d3c06ddb926c32e06c6733cee66db64bf09e51e32f64`; it was not deployed. **Collision:** CMake also has a distinct target/directory property named `SOURCE_DIR` (for example `third_party/commons/lvgl/sdl_img/CMakeLists.txt:10` asks target `lvgl` for it). `ExternalProject_Get_Property` overwrites the same ordinary variable on every call, so retrieve and consume it per named target; never infer it from the bare name. A download method may delete or replace previous contents. |
| `INSTALL_DIR`, `<INSTALL_DIR>` (CMake `ExternalProject` context only) | Kitware CMake / DOC; `ext_sdl2_backport` application is commons-c / SRC | The same [CMake primary documentation](https://cmake.org/cmake/help/v3.24/module/ExternalProject.html#directory-options) defines `INSTALL_DIR` as the prefix substituted for `<INSTALL_DIR>` and explicitly warns that the property alone does **not** configure an external project to install there. `ExternalProject_Get_Property()` stores it in the same-named ordinary variable. For `ext_sdl2_backport`, the default is `<current-binary-dir>/ext_sdl2_backport-prefix`; this module's release branch makes it real by explicitly copying `<SOURCE_DIR>` to `<INSTALL_DIR>` (`ExternalSDL2BackportForWebOS.cmake:25`) and declares the SDL library below it as a byproduct (`:26`). Current `build.make:106` expands the destination to `build/webos-easy_build/ext_sdl2_backport-prefix`. | Require the generated copy command to name the expected target prefix, then require `INSTALL_DIR/lib/libSDL2-2.0.so.0` and `INSTALL_DIR/share/licenses/SDL2/LICENSE.txt` after the external target completes. The current installed-copy licence is byte-identical to the source licence (same SHA-256 `97f35b...`). The unpacked IPK, not variable presence, proves packaging. **MEASURED 2026-09-30:** the legal-notices verification IPK contained `lib/backports/libSDL2-2.0.so.0`, `licenses/SDL2-zlib.txt` and the duplicate controller-database attribution, both notice copies hashing to the registered `97f35b...`; the package was not deployed. **Trap:** this generic variable is overwritten by each `ExternalProject_Get_Property(... INSTALL_DIR)` call, and other external targets use it too; retrieve it for the named target in the scope where it is consumed. Outside this module's explicit copy/source-build commands, `INSTALL_DIR` alone promises no installed contents. |
| `SDL2_BACKPORT_RELEASE` | commons-c `ExternalSDL2BackportForWebOS.cmake` / DOC; Aurora-selected value / SRC | `CMakeLists.txt:169-173` sets `release-2.30.12-webos.5` for webOS. When the variable is defined, the owner module's `:18-27` selects the prebuilt-release branch, maps a matching release label to archive `SDL2-2.30.12-webos.tar.gz`, downloads it from the same GitHub release label, skips configure/build, and copies the extracted tree to the external project's install prefix. Defining this therefore selects a release archive instead of the `SDL2_BACKPORT_REVISION` source-build branch. The regex replacement is archive-name construction, not an explicit validity check. | **MEASURED for the current build:** generated `ext_sdl2_backport-urlinfo.txt:9` names `https://github.com/webosbrew/SDL-webOS/releases/download/release-2.30.12-webos.5/SDL2-2.30.12-webos.tar.gz`; extracted `include/SDL2/SDL_revision.h` says `SDL-release-2.30.12-webos.5-0-g9f30a1f`; the declared runtime byproduct exists. The 2026-09-30 legal-notices verification package contained the runtime and its byte-exact licence at SHA-256 `97f35b...`. For a changed value, require all three to change consistently and hash the packaged SDL2 licence. **Supply-chain trap:** this `ExternalProject_Add(URL ...)` has no `URL_HASH`; the label, URL and embedded revision show selection, not cryptographic authentication of downloaded bytes. It does not need a separate §12 entry because the generated URL, extracted revision and byproduct are direct application observables. |
| `ENET_NO_INSTALL` | moonlight-common-c's vendored ENet / DOC | Owner source `core/moonlight-common-c/enet/CMakeLists.txt:5,103-120`: Boolean option, default OFF. ON skips only ENet's `install(TARGETS ...)` and `install(DIRECTORY include/ ...)` rules; it does not suppress `add_library(enet STATIC ...)`. The parent unconditionally adds the ENet subdirectory and links `moonlight-common-c` to `enet` (`core/moonlight-common-c/CMakeLists.txt:12-16,34`). Thus ON removes the otherwise separately installed archive/headers from the consumer package while keeping ENet compiled and statically linked. It does not remove ENet's licence/acknowledgement obligation. | **MEASURED 2026-09-30:** after setting ON before `add_subdirectory(core/moonlight-common-c)`, a fresh reconfigure/build/package left both `build/webos-easy_build/install_manifest.txt` and the unpacked IPK free of `lib/static/libenet.a` and `include/enet/`, while `build/webos-easy_build/CMakeFiles/moonlight.dir/link.txt` still named `core/moonlight-common-c/enet/libenet.a`. The verification package is `dist/com.aurora.gamestream_1.0.8_arm-enetfix-UNTESTED-20260930.ipk`, SHA-256 `59f30b5ea013c7fa5540fcf63d44a4de21ec9187121a537cd1288b8822abba7f`; it was not deployed, and the canonical IPK was restored afterward. This belongs in §12: a cache value or source assignment is not proof, especially because CMP0077 NEW lets an effective parent normal variable coexist with a stale cache entry. |
| `GCDB_REVISION` | Aurora webOS GameControllerDB generator / SRC; pinned upstream Git object / TEST | Immutable upstream commit to use for every SDL_GameControllerDB file fetched by `scripts/webos/gen_gamecontrollerdb.sh`: `5a12daa568d19344f9b6e9286ef5929833b25c7c`. The repository's already-audited Windows packager owns the cross-platform source pin at `scripts/windows/build_portable.ps1:41-45`; webOS must reuse that object rather than independently pinning today's branch head. Direct retrieval of the immutable raw files on 2026-09-30 reproduced the registered database and licence digests. This pins the webOS build-time seed and its licence; it is not the mutable branch name. The runtime updater in `third_party/commons/device/gcdb-updater/gamecontrollerdb_updater.c` independently follows `master`. | **MEASURED 2026-09-30:** the generator fetched the immutable URL, and the unpacked verification IPK's database began with this exact commit id. Its transformed output was byte-identical to a fresh verified transform (SHA-256 `4a044bfceb1abd37938287f9b148b93420efeb1afadb24b83da0cc1249b34eb8`). The package still needs the separately attributed GameControllerDB licence file at SHA-256 `97f35b302b361680ec1e891e95d2d52097bb95abff361434916d99dc1305f127`; an identical SDL2 licence digest does not replace that attribution. A header containing the revision remains only a claim unless the byte check passed. Upstream `master` resolved to newer object `555ce569...` on 2026-09-30, proving why the moving URL is not an acceptable package input. |
| `GCDB_SHA256` | Aurora webOS GameControllerDB generator / SRC; upstream bytes / TEST | Expected SHA-256 of the **raw, unfiltered** `gamecontrollerdb.txt` bytes at `GCDB_REVISION`: `07ec5b753e685c4829987919b27905cc3c45c8e18c11b3046e275fcf38b0f3cb`. This is the audited cross-platform value in `scripts/windows/build_portable.ps1:42`; re-downloading the immutable raw URL and hashing its bytes reproduced it on 2026-09-30. It is neither the digest of the Linux-to-webOS transformed output nor the digest of upstream `LICENSE`. | The generator downloads first, compares the raw file to this value, and exits nonzero before emitting a usable package asset on mismatch; `PackageWebOS.cmake`'s `execute_process(... COMMAND_ERROR_IS_FATAL ANY)` then aborts packaging. **MEASURED 2026-09-30:** the normal generator/package path succeeded and produced the byte-identical transformed asset recorded in the `GCDB_REVISION` row; an isolated copy with the expected digest replaced by 64 zeroes exited 1 with zero output bytes. Merely printing this value or hashing only the filtered file would not prove it applied. This is not a §12 setting because the mismatch behavior is a hard failure with a direct package result. |

## 44. Launch-media decoded-variant similarity gate (2026-10-01)

This is a repository-owned publication validator, not a streaming-quality or codec-performance claim. It compares only the two delivery encodes that the media contract requires to represent the same edit frame for frame.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SSIM_MINIMUM` | `0.95`, Eclipse launch tooling / SRC + TEST | `scripts/launch/check_media.mjs` runs FFmpeg's `ssim` filter over the decoded `coop-hero-loop.webm` and `coop-hero-loop.mp4` after assigning each stream a frame-index-derived timeline at the pair's validated common frame rate (`setpts=N/(frameRate*TB)`) and normalising to `yuv420p`. `SSIM_MINIMUM` is the lowest accepted score for both the aggregate `All` result and every paired frame. It is a same-edit/transcode guard layered after exact frame-count and decoded-timestamp checks; it is not evidence of visual quality, host performance, latency or co-op correctness. The source constant is the owner because this repository defines both delivery formats and the acceptance policy. | The observables are FFmpeg's aggregate `All` value, the per-frame values/count, and the validator exit status/error. **MEASURED 2026-10-01, temp-only dedicated calibration:** a 7.0 s 1920x1080@30 `testsrc2` source was independently encoded as VP9/WebM (`libvpx-vp9`, CRF 32, `cpu-used=5`, 6,447,947 bytes) and H.264/MP4 (`libx264`, CRF 23, `veryfast`, `yuv420p`, fast-start, 4,968,251 bytes). Frame-index-normalised comparison at the measured common 30 fps rate produced 210 paired frames, aggregate `All=0.993333`, per-frame minimum `0.991550` and maximum `0.996383`; the complete copied-validator fixture exited 0 with `Launch-media check passed: 8 delivery files.` Replacing only the MP4 with same-duration/resolution/FPS `testsrc` content (142,281 bytes) and updating its manifest hash produced 210 comparisons, aggregate `All=0.600290`, minimum `0.591765` and maximum `0.611638`; the validator exited 1 with both `decoded frame 76 SSIM 0.591765 is below 0.95` and `decoded aggregate SSIM 0.60029 is below 0.95`. Thus the named measurements change and the gate demonstrably separates the controlled same-source and wrong-content cases at this value, without the prior framesync/outlier ambiguity. **Trap/open real-media check:** synthetic calibration establishes the guard, not the quality of future camera/game footage. Record the first real dual-export score; if an apparently correct pair falls below 0.95, first investigate edit, timing and encode settings rather than weakening the threshold. Any changed value requires a new register row/evidence update and rerun of both pass and intentional-mismatch fixtures. |

## 45. Public launch-media manifest schema (2026-10-01)

These are repository-owned JSON contract fields, not streaming or encoder controls. They are registered because the launch gate consumes their values and refuses publication when the record is missing, internally inconsistent or not tied to the reviewed files. `docs/launch/templates/media-manifest.template.json` is the human starting point; `scripts/launch/check_media.mjs` is the executable owner.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `media-manifest.json`, `schemaVersion`, `walkthrough`, `builds`, `sourceInventory`, `deliveries` | Eclipse launch-media manifest / SRC + TEST | `site/media/media-manifest.json` is the required public provenance record and its exact top-level keys are those listed here for schema version 1. `walkthrough` records the hosted film, `builds` the three demonstrated binaries, `sourceInventory` the public-safe source evidence and `deliveries` the seven reviewed repository files. No additional root key is permitted. | The delivery contract and inventory allowlist require the exact filename; the full checker reports it missing and cannot pass without it. `validateManifest()` calls `rejectUnknownKeys()` with exactly this field set and requires `schemaVersion === 1`; the unit manifest passes, while an unknown/malformed structure adds an error. **Trap:** renaming the file, adding a field to the template without adding it to the validator, or adding it to code without registering its meaning recreates an undocumented release input. |
| `url`, `durationSeconds`, `sourceId`, `signedOutPlaybackReviewed`, `captionsEnabledReviewed`, `reviewedBy`, `reviewedAt` | Eclipse launch-media walkthrough/source records / SRC + TEST | The hosted walkthrough uses a public credential-free HTTPS `url`, a finite 90–105 second `durationSeconds`, one inventory `sourceId`, two true playback/caption review attestations, a public-safe reviewer label and an ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` review date. The referenced source must be `walkthrough-master` and its duration must agree within 0.05 s. In inventory records, `durationSeconds` is finite and positive for recorded sources and omitted for stills. | The valid 100 s YouTube-shaped unit fixture returns no errors; the reserved `example.com` mutation is rejected. Source lookup/type/duration and the two booleans are validated before the release check can pass. **Trap:** a reachable URL alone is not review evidence; the booleans attest signed-out playback and enabled captions only after human review. |
| `eclipse`, `umbraHostA`, `umbraHostB`, `commit`, `version`, `artifactSha256` | Eclipse launch-media build record / SRC + TEST | `builds` contains exactly one Eclipse client and the two Umbra host artifacts shown. Each `commit` is a full 40- or 64-hex object id, `version` has 2–4 dot-separated numeric components and `artifactSha256` is the 64-hex digest of the demonstrated binary. | The sample manifest with three distinct `1.0.0` build records passes. `rejectUnknownKeys()`, the commit/version/SHA-256 regexes run for all three records, and the transcript validator separately requires the same public build values. Only an exact already-validated version token is exempted from the transcript's dotted-quad/IP privacy scan; arbitrary dotted text is not. **Trap:** source commit identity does not prove which binary was recorded; the artifact digest is independently mandatory. |
| `id`, `kind`, `publicLabel`, `sha256`, `frameRate`, `frameCount`, `publicSafe`, `liveCapture`, `rightsReviewId`, `privacyReviewId`, `consentReviewId` | Eclipse launch-media source inventory / SRC + TEST | `id` is a unique public slug; `kind` is one of `continuous-live-take`, `screen-capture`, `screenshot`, `photograph` or `walkthrough-master`; `publicLabel` is non-placeholder public text; `sha256` identifies the source bytes. Recorded kinds require finite positive `durationSeconds`/`frameRate` and a positive safe-integer `frameCount`, with `frameCount / frameRate` agreeing with duration within 0.05 s; still kinds omit the three measurements. `publicSafe` must be true. `liveCapture` explicitly distinguishes a live application capture from other material. The three review IDs resolve to the private rights/privacy/consent registers without exposing them. | The unit fixture proves accepted 30 fps sources with 900/3000 frames and stills with no timing fields; the measurement unit test rejects zero/non-finite rate/count values. The validator rejects duplicate IDs, unsupported kinds, malformed hashes, inconsistent timing, unreviewed sources and unreferenced inventory entries. **Trap:** `liveCapture=true` is an attestation backed by the run sheet, not something a hash can infer. |
| `lineage`, `rightsReviewed`, `privacyReviewed`, `accessibilityReviewed` | Eclipse launch-media delivery record / SRC + TEST | Every required delivery has its exact file `sha256`, exactly one `lineage` record, all three human-review booleans set true, and the shared reviewer/date fields. Delivery hashes must match the repository bytes. The two hero encodes must name identical intervals from one live continuous take; captions/transcript must reference only the reviewed walkthrough master. | `validateManifest()` compares the seven declared digests to hashes calculated from `site/media`, requires each review flag, requires one lineage item and enforces per-file source kinds. The complete fixture passes and the full checker fails closed when files/records are absent. **Trap:** matching hashes establish byte identity, not rights, privacy, accessibility quality or truth; those remain separate human attestations. |
| `sourceIn`, `sourceOut`, `sourceTimecode`, `sourceFrame` | Eclipse launch-media lineage record / SRC + TEST | Clock fields use `HH:MM:SS.mmm` and cannot exceed the referenced source duration. A video interval requires `sourceOut > sourceIn`. `sourceFrame` is either `original-still` for an allowed still source or a zero-based nonnegative safe integer below `frameCount`; when paired with `sourceTimecode`, it must equal `round(seconds * frameRate)`. Posters come from the same verified hero run; picker media may be an original live screenshot or an exact frame from live video. | The good poster fixture maps 6.000 s × 30 fps to frame 180. Dedicated mutations prove that a missing frame, contradictory frame 10 and unsafe integer are rejected. The validator also bounds the frame and enforces exact hero/still/picker lineage shapes. **Trap:** a timecode without the matching decoded frame number is not exact provenance; variable-rate sources must be normalised or represented by evidence the schema can validate before use. |

## 46. Public media-directory text budget (2026-10-01)

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PUBLIC_MEDIA_README_MAX_BYTES` | `102400` bytes, Eclipse launch tooling / SRC + TEST | Maximum byte length accepted for `site/media/README.md`, the only non-delivery file allowed in the public media directory. `scripts/launch/check_media.mjs` owns the value as `100 * 1024`; the cap keeps a public static asset bounded before its UTF-8 content is scanned for paths, private addresses, placeholders and other unsafe text. It is not a repository-wide Markdown limit. | `inventoryMediaDirectory()` reads the file's actual `stat.size`; 102400 bytes remain eligible for the privacy scan, while 102401 bytes produce an `exceeds 102400` error before content acceptance. The unit test exercises both sides of that boundary and separately proves a private `/root/...` path is rejected. **Trap:** being under the byte cap does not make the content public-safe, and a privacy-clean README does not authorise any unlisted media file or subdirectory. |

## 47. Co-op out of the box: built-in pane, per-launch host tuning, encoder override (2026-10-02)

User goal 2026-10-02: a fresh install pairs a second PC and starts co-op with nothing to set up; co-op settings are
derived from the Video settings (like bitrate), forced with a documented override, or taken unchanged (like HDR).
All rows are OURS (Eclipse `feat/coop-seamless`, Umbra `codex/mixed-beta-profile`).

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `coop_encoder` | moonlight.ini; Eclipse `app_settings.[ch]`, `coop_stream_mode.[ch]` / SRC + unit TEST | 0 AUTO (default): `coop_stream_plan_choose()` = the automatic plan (NVIDIA+NVIDIA native NVENC, any tested AMD -> GPU HEVC on both). 1 NATIVE: each PC's own encoder, `forced`. 2 GPU_HEVC: GPU HEVC on both, `forced`. Out-of-range reads as 0. Replaces the ignored legacy `coop_stream_mode`, which is kept only so old ini files still load. | `[COOP-ENCODER] ... encoder=gpu-hevc-beta|native-hevc` in `aurora-diag.log`; the co-op dialog banner names a forced choice. `tests/coop/test_coop_stream_mode.c` covers all three. Trap: NATIVE on a pair with an AMD card is a grey/corrupt pane by construction (CTB 32 vs 64). |
| `coop_host_tuning` | moonlight.ini; Eclipse `app_settings.[ch]`, `session_worker.c` / SRC | Default ON. On co-op launches only, calls `gs_set_coop()` so `/launch` and `/resume` carry `umbraCoop=1`. Off sends nothing and each host keeps its ordinary settings. | `[COOP-ENCODER] ... host_tuning=1`; on the host, `NvEnc: constrained encoding enabled for 1 slices/frame (co-op pane)` in the Umbra log. Off + a fresh NVIDIA host = seam damage (see `nvenc_constrained`). |
| `umbraCoop` (`/launch`, `/resume`, `/applist` query arg) | GameStream query arg; Umbra `nvhttp.cpp` / SRC | On `/launch`/`/resume`: `1` = co-op with tuning, `0` = co-op without (Eclipse's `coop_host_tuning` off), ABSENT = a client that predates the argument, which gets tuning exactly when it launches/resumes the co-op pane (built in or same-named apps.json entry; `nvhttp::resolve_coop_flag`), so old Eclipse/TV builds keep the rig's knobs. `launch_session_t::umbra_coop` -> `video::config_t::umbra_coop` scopes: `nvenc_constrained` AUTO (-1) becomes ON, `gop_length` (avcodec + x265) and `nvenc_gop_length` (native NVENC GOP/idrPeriod) apply, `coop_pad_rows` applies (non-GPU-HEVC). Without it none of them touch the session. On `/applist`: lists the co-op pane even when it is not running. Eclipse sends it on EVERY `/applist` (`gs_applist`) and on co-op launches while `coop_host_tuning` is on. Stock hosts ignore unknown args. | Host log: the constrained line above; for `coop_pad_rows` the `Co-op seam padding: virtual display WxH` line appears only on co-op launches (it used to appear on Desktop streams on VEGA, 09-29). `gop_length`: periodic IDRs only in co-op sessions. Trap: `gop_length`/`coop_pad_rows` in sunshine.conf NO LONGER affect ordinary streams -- by design. |
| `UmbraCaps` bit 2 (`0x4`, `CAP_COOP_PANE`) | Umbra serverinfo; `nvhttp.cpp` / SRC | This Umbra build understands Eclipse co-op: builds the co-op pane in, hides it from `/applist` unless running or `umbraCoop=1`, honours `umbraCoop` on launch. Always set by builds from 2026-10-02. Also makes bit 3 meaningful. | `curl -sk https://HOST:47984/serverinfo` (paired cert) or the client log's Host line `umbraCaps=`. |
| `UmbraCaps` bit 3 (`0x8`, `CAP_GPU_HEVC`) | Umbra serverinfo; `proc::gpu_hevc_encoder_installed()` / SRC | `<exe dir>\gpu-hevc\aurora_gpu_hevc8.dll`, `aurora_gpu_hevc10.dll` and `kernels\` exist (the files `gpu_hevc_device.inc` loads); only in `APOLLO_BETA_PROFILE` builds. Eclipse: bit 2 without bit 3 = KNOWN no -> `plan.host_support_missing` + dialog banner; no bit 2 = unknown, assumed present as before. | Remove/rename the folder on a host and restart Umbra: bit 3 clears and the co-op dialog names the missing encoder. |
| `COOP_PANE_UUID` / `COOP_PANE_NAME` | Umbra `process.h` / SRC | `4E4F11CD-A1C7-4A7B-A04F-C7581FEB2A56` / `"Co-op Pane"`. `proc::parse()` adds the built-in pane when the virtual display driver is OK and no apps.json entry has that name (an existing one is kept). `virtual-display`, `auto-detach`, `wait-all=false`, global prep/state cmds, and **`terminate-on-pause=false`** -- RESUMABLE (user 2026-10-02: Disconnect must leave co-op to resume). Safe because `prepare_gpu_hevc_resume` now resizes EVERY co-op pane's virtual display on resume (GPU HEVC 32-aligned, native even, keeping a `coop_pad_rows` inset) and Eclipse's Quit quits BOTH hosts (`session_worker.c`, `[QUIT@..] co-op peer .. quit rc=`). `/applist` hides only THIS built-in (by UUID); a same-named apps.json entry stays visible for older clients. Eclipse resolves it by the NAME (`COOP_SESSION_APP_NAME`). The default apps.json no longer carries it. | `/applist?umbraCoop=1` lists it; plain `/applist` does not unless it runs. Umbra log at start: `Co-op Pane: built in (id N)`. Disconnect: `Session pausing for app [Co-op Pane]` on both hosts; reopening co-op: `Session resuming for app [Co-op Pane]` (+ `resumed virtual display verified WxH`). Quit: both hosts end the pane. |
| `UMBRA_GPU_HEVC_DIR` | Umbra CMake cache path, `cmake/packaging/windows.cmake` / SRC | Folder with the built GPU HEVC encoder (`aurora_gpu_hevc8.dll`, `aurora_gpu_hevc10.dll`, `kernels/`, from Eclipse `experimental/gpu-hevc`, `-DBUILD_HOST_DLLS=ON`); installed to `gpu-hevc\` in the application component. Unset = configure WARNING and a package without the encoder. | The NSIS/ZIP staging tree has `gpu-hevc\`; a host installed from it reports `UmbraCaps` bit 3. **Currently EMPTY in the build cache, so today's installer ships no `gpu-hevc\` -- file:line, the FATAL_ERROR on a missing member, and the upgrade behaviour are in §48.** |

**`/tmp/coop-audio-dump` (2026-10-03, ours, SRC):** read once when the co-op audio coordinator starts (`coop_audio.c` `dump_start`). Present = write the mixer's two inputs, exactly as summed and before any gain, to `coop-audio-self.wav` (primary host) and `coop-audio-peer.wav` (second host) beside the diagnostic log; content = seconds (default 120, 5..600). Diagnostic only, off by default. **Proof it applied:** `[COOP-AUD] input capture -> <path>` at start and `[COOP-AUD] input capture closed: N frames` at the end, and two WAVs of equal length. Purpose: measure the offset and waveform similarity of the same sound from both hosts (duplicate-narration filter).

**Duplicate suppression -- `coop_audio_dedupe` / `/tmp/coop-audio-dedupe` / `COOP_AUDIO_DEDUPE_MAX_LAG_MS` (2026-10-03, updated 2026-10-06, ours, SRC + TEST).** `coop_audio_dedupe` (ini bool, default **true**, co-op settings "Play shared sounds once") turns on duplicate filtering inside `coop_audio_mix()` when MIXING two audible hosts. **Since 2026-10-06 the filter is the passive gate `coop_audio_pgate.c` (5 ms; see the echo-filter A/B switch row below); the single-offset filter described in the rest of this row is the A/B fallback (`/tmp/coop-audio-gate` = `off`) and the fallback when the gate cannot be created.** The single-offset filter `coop_audio_dedupe.c`: it finds the offset between the two hosts' copies of a shared sound (GCC-PHAT, +/- `COOP_AUDIO_DEDUPE_MAX_LAG_MS` = 400 ms) and turns down the LATER copy per frequency bin by gain only (never subtraction; floor -26 dB); both streams then carry +512 frames (10.7 ms at 48 kHz). `/tmp/coop-audio-dedupe` = `on`|`off`, re-read ~1/s, overrides the setting live for A/B by ear. **Proof it applied:** `[COOP-AUD] play shared sounds once: on|off, filter PASSIVE GATE by default` at start (`play shared sounds once -> on|off` on every change); the ~5 s stats line carries `dedupe=on|off` for the SETTING, and its `lag=.. rho=.. amount=.. quieting=..` fields describe the single-offset filter only, so they read zero while the gate runs. **Measured offline (tests/coop/test_coop_audio_dedupe.c):** synthetic +80/+250/-120 ms: duplicate -20.7..-21.6 dB, own sounds -0.6..+0.2 dB, earlier stream 0.00 dB, unrelated audio 0.00 dB; real Halo audio, both sides through libopus 128k, +120 ms: lag exact, shared-only -26..-28 dB in every band, with the other player's sounds bursting -12.8 dB average (the filter holds back ~30 ms around them) and those sounds kept at -1.2 dB. **NOT yet measured:** the real offset between two games' copies (use `/tmp/coop-audio-dump`), and a live listen.

**Echo-filter A/B switch -- `/tmp/coop-audio-gate` / `COOP_AUDIO_GATE_MAX_LAG_MS` (updated 2026-10-06, ours, SRC + bounded live TEST).** **Since 2026-10-06 the passive gate `coop_audio_pgate.c` is the DEFAULT filter** behind `coop_audio_dedupe`; before that (from commit `6daaf613`, 2026-10-05) it ran only when this file said `on`. In `coop_audio.c:149-226`, it is re-read every 200 mix calls (~1 s at the current packet size): `off` or `0` selects single-offset dedupe (the A/B fallback); an absent file, `on`, or any other nonempty token selects the passive gate. An empty file or an open failure other than absence preserves the current selection. A selection change recreates the selected filter. `COOP_AUDIO_GATE_MAX_LAG_MS` = 1000 ms bounds the passive gate's offset search; its one-block latency is 240 frames at 48 kHz (5 ms; `coop_audio_pgate.c:303,401`). Both filters remain governed by `coop_audio_dedupe` / `/tmp/coop-audio-dedupe`; processing requires mix mode, neither host marked as this client PC, and a successfully allocated filter (`coop_audio.c:709-718`). **Proof of selection:** at coordinator start `[COOP-AUD] play shared sounds once: on, filter PASSIVE GATE by default` (an ` -- UNAVAILABLE (allocation failed)` suffix means no filter runs); a later switch logs `[COOP-AUD] echo filter -> dedupe (single offset)` or `-> PASSIVE GATE`. **Proof of passive-gate processing:** changing offsets/prediction counts in `[COOP-AUD] pgate: offsets(ms) ... | balance ... | predictions ...` alongside an enabled mixed session; a switch line or a static stats line alone does not establish processing. The ordinary stats line still reports the single-offset dedupe object's lag/rho/amount, so zeros there do not establish passive-gate inactivity. **Bounded live evidence (Windows, 2026-10-05):** the user-tested session saved in `scratchpad/coop-audio-live-20261006/coop-aud-log.txt` (the directory was misdated; the test was on 2026-10-05) selected PASSIVE GATE, captured 25,201,200 input frames per file (525.025 s at 48 kHz), and ended with changing passive-gate telemetry (332 confirmed / 2352 failed predictions; 7 dropout detections). These logs establish that the Windows mixer exercised the passive gate; they do not quantify echo removal, prove audible quality, or establish a TV live-listening result. **`pgate:` lines since 2026-10-06** print only while the gate is actually processing (setting on, mix mode, neither host this PC, gate allocated), so their presence now means the gate ran. **`PGATE_*` environment variables** (`coop_audio_pgate.c` `read_env()`, ~30 tuning knobs such as `PGATE_PRED`, `PGATE_HOLD_MS`, `PGATE_CLOSE_REL`, `PGATE_SENTINEL`, `PGATE_DROPOUT`, `PGATE_WINS`) are OFFLINE simulator/bench knobs compiled in only with `-DCOOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING`; shipping builds read no environment and always run the evaluated defaults. `tests/coop/sim_coop_audio_pgate.c` refuses to build without the flag so a sweep cannot be silently ignored. Proven equivalent 2026-10-06: the real 10-05 capture (`sec1005`) through the old and new gate gave byte-identical stats and output audio. **Previous spectral gate, retained for offline work only:** the following controls, telemetry and measurements belong to `coop_audio_gate.c`, which this sentinel no longer selects; they do not describe or tune `coop_audio_pgate.c`. `GATE_MODE`, `GATE_COH_LO`, `GATE_COH_SPAN`, `GATE_ACT_ON`, `GATE_ACT_OFF`, `GATE_SMOOTH`, `GATE_SH_LO`, `GATE_SH_HI`, `GATE_DOM_LO`, `GATE_DOM_HI`, `GATE_DOM_SMOOTH`, `GATE_TRACE`, `GATE_STRACE`, `GATE_TRACE_BAND` are historical PROTOTYPE environment overrides for offline sweeps (defaults = the evaluated configuration, mode 4); not for users. **Historical application proof (old gate only):** `[COOP-AUD] echo filter -> GATE (multi-offset)` when switched, and a `[COOP-AUD] gate: cut self=..dB peer=..dB offsets(ms:amount) ...` line beside each ~5 s stats line. **Historical offline measurements (old gate only; 2026-10-04 Halo capture, tests/coop/truth_coop_audio_gate.c + eval_coop_audio_gate.c):** ground truth echo -9.5 dB / own -3.9 dB (oracle -18.0/-3.9); real cutscene 36-40 dB of echo removed up to 2.6 kHz; busy gameplay -1.9 dB, only in bands measured as a real +59.8 ms majority copy; CPU 4.4 % of a Ryzen 5600X core, **22.5 % of a TV (Cortex-A78) core** (dedupe 1.1 % / 6.2 %).

**Passive gate (historical offline prototype measurements) -- `COOP_PGATE_BANDS` / `COOP_PGATE_MAX_OFFSETS` / `PGATE_CLOSE`, `PGATE_OPEN`, `PGATE_HOLD_MS`, `PGATE_PRED`, `PGATE_RISE` environment tunables (2026-10-05, ours, SRC + offline TEST).** The following records the prototype's successive offline configurations and measurements, not one current default configuration. `coop_audio_pgate.c` implements the user's design: a 5 ms waveform match of the later stream against the earlier one at every known offset, both directions, per band (3 bands, `COOP_PGATE_BANDS`, summing back to the input exactly), closing that band's gate on a match; an offset bank of up to `COOP_PGATE_MAX_OFFSETS` fed by a 10/s background search; a PREDICTIVE close one-two blocks ahead from the earlier copy's onset. It is now wired into the mixer through the echo-filter live-test switch documented immediately above. Defaults: close 0.65 / open 0.40 (share = rho^2), hold 200 ms, rise x4. The `PGATE_*` names are prototype environment overrides for offline sweeps, not for users. **Proof it applied (offline):** `tests/coop/sim_coop_audio_pgate.c` real/truth modes. **Measured:** ground truth echo -15.0 dB, earlier copy -0.01 dB, true pauses -0.58 dB, known-offset onset median 5 ms; real cutscenes -17..-22 dB (runs 1-2), -4..-6 dB (run 3); idle -0.1 dB; CPU 1.2 % of a Ryzen 5600X core, 6.8 % of a TV core. **Live status:** the Windows live session was user-tested; see the switch paragraph above for its dated, bounded evidence. The offline results here are not measurements of that live session. **v2 (2026-10-05):** 6 bands (`COOP_PGATE_BANDS`), per-channel matching (`PGATE_STEREO`), thresholds relative to a learned per-band ceiling (`PGATE_CLOSE_REL` 0.70 / `PGATE_OPEN_REL` 0.45 / `PGATE_CLOSE_MIN` 0.55, `PGATE_CEIL`), host level balance from clean matches (`PGATE_BALANCE`; learned +4.37 / +4.66 dB on the two captures vs +4.6 measured by hand), bass witness at any offset (`PGATE_ANYWITNESS`) or alone when strong and sustained (`PGATE_BASSALONE`), the both-streams rule switchable (`PGATE_CONFLICT`), per-band windows (`PGATE_WINS`, default 40,30,10,5,5,5 ms). Measured: ground truth echo -17.8 dB (v1 -15.0), earlier copy -0.01, pauses -0.74; real runs 1-2 -23.1 / -19.6 dB (v1 -22.0 / -17.2); run 3 VEGA -3.9..-4.2, PC2 top band -8.2 (v1 -2.1); idle -0.1. CPU 3.3 % PC core, 12.3 % TV core. **v3 (2026-10-05, "S" sounds):** `PGATE_SIBILANT` / `PGATE_ENV` (0.80): during a line (mid bands matching at that offset) the treble also closes on a log-energy ENVELOPE match -- the games rendered narrator "s" hiss as different noise with the same envelope (waveform 0.16-0.57, envelope 0.84-0.90). `PGATE_LINK`: a channel closed on a strong match pulls the other channel's band closed when that channel's share >= the open threshold -- the right channel's own positional sound hid the centred narrator (run 2 at 120.8 s: L 0.9+, R 0.1-0.7) and its treble reopened mid-"s". Measured: S-burst leaks in runs 1-2 20 -> 1; run 1 lines -29.6 dB, run 2 -21.3 dB; ground truth per-line median -23.4 dB; earlier copy -0.01; idle 0.0/-0.1. **v4 (2026-10-06, dropouts):** prediction safeguards -- `PGATE_PRED_BANDS` (2: a line starts in several bands), `PGATE_LANDING` / `PGATE_LANDING_AGE` (a predicted close reopens at once when the slave's band shows no rise where the copy should land), `PGATE_PRED_FAILS` / `PGATE_PRED_ONE` / `PGATE_PRE_GAIN` (kept OFF: each slowed real line onsets 5 -> 30-35 ms), `PGATE_DRIFT_VOTES` (3: drift steps only after 3 blocks agree; was ~20 noise steps/s), `PGATE_DROPOUT` (when one stream falls to digital silence, the other's copy is let through). Measured: failed predictions' band-closure time 127 s -> 35 s per 190 s (each ~12 ms), onset still 5 ms; run 3 PC2 stream dropout at 302.60 s no longer a 20 ms hole; ground truth -17.5 dB, per line -23.1, earlier copy -0.01. **v5 (2026-10-06, speech classes; see `docs/coop-audio-speech-risks.md`):** `PGATE_ENV` default 0.60 (was 0.80), `PGATE_ENV_ALL` (during a line every band above 250 Hz may close on an envelope match), `PGATE_ENV_LINE` (>= 3 bands with moving, matching envelopes at a known offset = a line; for whispers/breaths), `PGATE_FAST_TRACK` (a just-lost line searches +-8..48 samples per block; offsets keep a `home` where lines start), `PGATE_NASAL_WITNESS` (250-600 Hz witnesses the bass). Measured on the truth simulator's speech classes: voiced -23.1 dB/line, speech with "s" -13.8 (treble -15.3), plosive -15.4, nasal -20.3, noise-only lines -2.8..-7.0 (were -0.2..-0.4), speed-varied 0.25% -10.7 / 1% -2.8; real run 1 lines -32.5 dB, idle 0.0. CPU 16.1 % TV core. **Performance (2026-10-06), same output bit-for-bit on every simulator measure:** loudness sums from the 1 ms frame record (was 46% of CPU), open gates pass the input straight through, both search directions in 3 FFTs (was 6), single-precision 4-way matching sums, frame positions without 64-bit division (5% on the TV's 32-bit ARM), silent bands skipped once per block. TV 17.4 % -> ~8.3 % of a Cortex-A78 core; PC 3.7 % -> 1.9 % of a Ryzen 5600X core. `PGATE_SENTINEL` (default OFF): idle offsets check 2 mono bands first -- ~0.5 % TV saving but the slowest 10 % of line onsets 265 -> 860 ms. Compiler: `-O3` / `-ffast-math` on this file gave 8.6 / 8.0 % -- not adopted. **Round 2 (2026-10-06):** planar per-channel band storage, slave-window energy computed once per block (lazily), 8-way single-precision matching sums, FFT with contiguous per-stage twiddles and multiply-free first stages, single-precision 1 ms frame sums, coarse-to-fine offset refinement. Measured interleaved on the TV: v5 15.5 % -> 7.8 % of a core; PC 1.9 %; output hash identical to round 1. NEON intrinsics for the matching were tried and were NOT faster on the TV's core (short windows, de-interleaving loads) -- removed. `PGATE_TIMING` (compile-time) reports time per phase for offline benchmarks.

## 48. Umbra host build: version, installer identity, publisher metadata, pre-release notices (2026-10-03)

Owner: the Umbra tree (`/mnt/c/Users/<user>/apollo-build`, branch `umbra/thin`, commit `9977e0fc`; WSL path as written) inherits ClassicOldSong/Apollo's cmake, which inherits LizardByte Sunshine's. Rows below say which lines are UMBRA's (delta vs the v0.4.6-based beta base `6a9480df`: `git diff 6a9480df HEAD -- cmake` touches `common.cmake`, `packaging/common.cmake`, `packaging/windows.cmake`, `prep/options.cmake` and NOT `prep/build_version.cmake`). Evidence read 2026-10-03 from that source, its `build/` dir (`CMakeCache.txt`, `CPackConfig.cmake`, `build.ninja`, `cpack_artifacts/.../project.nsi`) and SKYNET's live install (`sunshine.log`, `reg query`, Start Menu). The PC2/VEGA log lines in the brief were NOT re-read here. All configure-time rows share one trap: **the value is a property of the `cmake` invocation or of the cache, not of the source file you edited** -- see the §12 rows.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `BUILD_VERSION` (env, configure time) | Apollo/Sunshine cmake `cmake/prep/build_version.cmake`, unchanged by Umbra / DOC + TEST | `:6-8` copies the env var to the cmake var. `:20-28`: only when **both** `BRANCH` and `BUILD_VERSION` are DEFINED in the environment and `BUILD_VERSION` is non-empty, `PROJECT_VERSION` = `BUILD_VERSION` with one leading `v` removed (`:25-26`) and `CMAKE_PROJECT_VERSION` too (`:27`, which CPack uses). MAJOR/MINOR/PATCH are regex-parsed from it (`:112-121`; measured `CPackConfig.cmake:129-131` = 0/4/6 for `0.4.6-umbra.2`). Compiled in as `PROJECT_VERSION="0.4.6-umbra.2"` (`build.ninja`: 50 compile lines + the RC) -> log line `main.cpp:136`, `/api/config` field `version` (`confighttp.cpp:1006`), `windows.rc:23,26` FileVersion/ProductVersion, `CPACK_PACKAGE_VERSION` (`packaging/common.cmake:6`). Umbra scheme `<Apollo release>-umbra.<n>` (our recipe, a doc = hearsay for behaviour: Umbra `docs/umbra-windows-build.md:51`). Env absent -> else-branch `:29-72`: `0.0.0` + `.<short commit>` unless the GIT branch (`git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`, `:38`) is `master`, + `.dirty` when `git diff -b --quiet` fails. Both vars defined but `BUILD_VERSION` EMPTY: neither branch runs and the version stays plain `0.0.0` (source-derived, not run). | **NOT CACHED** (`build/CMakeCache.txt` holds no `BUILD_VERSION`/`BRANCH`/`COMMIT`, checked): any later configure without the env -- including the automatic re-run ninja does when a CMake input changes (CMake behaviour, not reproduced here) -- silently re-versions the build to `0.0.0.<commit>[.dirty]`. Read the ARTEFACT, never the command line: SKYNET `sunshine.log` 14:43:02 `Umbra version: 0.4.6-umbra.2 commit: 9977e0fc`; `HKLM\...\Uninstall\Apollo` DisplayVersion `0.4.6-umbra.2`; `(Get-Item sunshine.exe).VersionInfo.FileVersion` = `0.4.6-umbra.2`; `project.nsi:1021-1022`; the web UI Umbra card (reads `version`). The configure output also prints `Got from CI '<BRANCH>' branch and version '<BUILD_VERSION>'` (`:24`) only when the gate passed -- its absence means the version was NOT taken from the env. |
| `BRANCH` (env, configure time) | same file / DOC + TEST | `:3-5` copies it to `GITHUB_BRANCH` (consumed only by the Linux flatpak manifest, `packaging/linux/flatpak/dev.lizardbyte.app.Sunshine.yml:56`, and the message at `:24`). Its whole effect on a Windows build is the gate at `:20-21`: it must be DEFINED, any value, empty counts (the file's own comment `:19`). It is NOT git's branch -- the `master` test in the else-branch uses `git rev-parse` (`:38`). Umbra's recipe passes `BRANCH=release` (a placeholder, `docs/umbra-windows-build.md:51`). | The silent failure is **`BUILD_VERSION=... cmake` WITHOUT `BRANCH`**: the gate is false, the env is ignored with no message, and the build reads `0.0.0.<commit>` (source-derived from `:20`; not run). Proof is the same artefact list as `BUILD_VERSION`. Sibling env vars in the same file: `COMMIT` (`:12-14` -> `GITHUB_COMMIT` -> `PROJECT_VERSION_COMMIT`, `:141`; the `commit:` in the log line -- NOT gated on BRANCH, unset = `commit: ` empty; 50 compile lines carry `"9977e0fc"`), `CLONE_URL` and `TAG` (-> `GITHUB_CLONE_URL`/`GITHUB_TAG`, consumed only by the Homebrew/Arch/flatpak templates `packaging/sunshine.rb`, `packaging/linux/...`, no Windows output). |
| `CPACK_PACKAGE_INSTALL_DIRECTORY` | CMake CPack owns the variable (msys2 ucrt64, cmake 4.4.0); the pin is UMBRA's / DOC + SRC + TEST | `cmake/packaging/windows.cmake:108` `set(... "Apollo")`; upstream had `"${CPACK_PACKAGE_NAME}"` (`windows.cmake:80` at `6a9480df`), and Umbra's project name is `Umbra`, so unpinned the installer would target `Program Files\Umbra` beside the old Apollo and repoint `ApolloService` at an empty `config\`. CPack derives `CPACK_PACKAGE_INSTALL_REGISTRY_KEY` from it (`Modules/CPack.cmake:707-710`), so this ONE pin fixes both the folder and the Uninstall key name; the installer's uninstall-before-install lookup reads `Uninstall\Apollo` (`project.nsi:1526-1532`). The reason is Umbra's own comment (`windows.cmake:102-107`) = intent, not evidence. | `build/CPackConfig.cmake:123-124` (`INSTALL_DIRECTORY` and `REGISTRY_KEY` both `Apollo`); `project.nsi:28` `InstallDir "$PROGRAMFILES64\Apollo"`, `:513`/`:1453` `Uninstall\Apollo`. SKYNET (read 2026-10-03): `C:\Program Files\Apollo` exists, `C:\Program Files\Umbra` does not, `Uninstall\Apollo` exists and `reg query ...\Uninstall /f Umbra /k` finds 0. **Packaging-time only**: it changes nothing on an already-installed host; check the generated `project.nsi`, not the cmake line. |
| `CPACK_NSIS_DISPLAY_NAME` | CMake CPack owns it; the value is UMBRA's / DOC + SRC + TEST | `windows.cmake:109` `"Umbra"`. CPack marks it set (`CPack.cmake:868`) and then uses it as `CPACK_NSIS_PACKAGE_NAME` (`:878-879`; otherwise that is `CPACK_PACKAGE_INSTALL_DIRECTORY`, i.e. `Apollo`). NSIS template: PACKAGE_NAME -> `Name` (`NSIS.template.in:34`) and the options-page header (`:755`); DISPLAY_NAME -> the Uninstall `DisplayName` (`:668`). The Start Menu folder is NSIS MUI's default `$(^Name)` (NSIS documentation, not re-read here; the generated script sets no `MUI_STARTMENUPAGE_DEFAULTFOLDER`, `project.nsi:567-571`), so it follows `Name`. The shortcut file name `Umbra.lnk` is a literal (`project.nsi:1066`), not derived. | `CPackConfig.cmake:71-72,109`; `project.nsi:34` `Name "Umbra"`, `:1018-1019` DisplayName. SKYNET: `Uninstall\Apollo` DisplayName `Umbra`; `ProgramData\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Umbra\` holds `Umbra.lnk` + `Uninstall.lnk` and there is no `Programs\Apollo\`. **DISPLAY TEXT ONLY**: the registry key and folder stay `Apollo`, so never script against `Uninstall\Umbra` (it does not exist). |
| `SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER_NAME` (also `SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER_WEBSITE`, `SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER_ISSUE_URL`) | Sunshine/Apollo CMake option; the defaults are UMBRA's / DOC + SRC + TEST | `cmake/prep/options.cmake:2-7`: CACHE STRINGs, Umbra defaults `PreceptorOfMagic` / `https://github.com/PreceptorOfMagic` / `https://github.com/PreceptorOfMagic/umbra/issues` (upstream `SudoMaker` / `sudomaker.com` / `ClassicOldSong/Apollo/issues`). Consumers: compile definitions `compile_definitions/common.cmake:145-147` -> startup log `entry_handler.cpp:105-107`; `compile_definitions/windows.cmake:47` (`PROJECT_VENDOR`, RC); `packaging/common.cmake:5,11` -> `CPACK_PACKAGE_VENDOR` / `CPACK_PACKAGE_CONTACT`, which becomes the NSIS Publisher AND the vendor segment of the install-folder key `HKLM\Software\<vendor>\Apollo` (`project.nsi:569,1014`). | **TRAP, measured: a value already in an existing build dir's `CMakeCache.txt` WINS over an edited default** -- that dir had cached `SudoMaker` and the binary logged `Package Publisher: SudoMaker` until reconfigured with `-D` (the lead's measurement; the cache now holds the new value). A second live example in the same cache: `SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER_ISSUE_URL=.../Umbra/issues` (capital U) is not the `.../umbra/issues` default in `options.cmake:6` -- the cache holds an earlier value (harmless: GitHub paths are case-insensitive). Proof: SKYNET `sunshine.log` 14:43:02 `Package Publisher: PreceptorOfMagic`, `Publisher Website: https://github.com/PreceptorOfMagic`, `Get support: https://github.com/PreceptorOfMagic/Umbra/issues`; `build.ninja` `SUNSHINE_PUBLISHER_NAME=\"PreceptorOfMagic\"`; `Uninstall\Apollo` Publisher `PreceptorOfMagic`; the install-folder key is `HKLM\Software\PreceptorOfMagic\Apollo` (SKYNET holds no `HKLM\Software\SudoMaker\Apollo`). Reconfigure with `-D`; editing the default or the file alone does nothing to a build dir that has the cache entry. |
| `UMBRA_GPU_HEVC_DIR` | Umbra CMake cache path (`cmake/packaging/windows.cmake:34`) / SRC + TEST | Detail beyond the §47 row. `:35-48`: if set, every one of `aurora_gpu_hevc8.dll`, `aurora_gpu_hevc10.dll`, `kernels` must exist or configure ends in `FATAL_ERROR` (`:36-40`); then the DLLs and `kernels/` are installed to `gpu-hevc\` in component `application` (`:41-43`). If empty: only `message(WARNING ...)` (`:45-47`), no `gpu-hevc\` in the package. **Currently EMPTY**: `build/CMakeCache.txt:724` `UMBRA_GPU_HEVC_DIR:PATH=`. It is a CACHE variable, so unlike `BUILD_VERSION` it persists across re-runs once set. | Measured 2026-10-03: the staged installer has none -- `cpack_artifacts/_CPack_Packages/win64/NSIS/Umbra/application/` has no `gpu-hevc`, `install_manifest_application.txt` 0 entries, `project.nsi` 0 hits. So installing it leaves a host's hand-copied `gpu-hevc\` alone: the uninstaller only `Delete`s listed files and `RMDir`s non-recursively (`project.nsi:737-983`), and its one `RMDir /r "$INSTDIR"` (`:1225-1226`) is behind a Yes/No prompt that is answered NO only in SILENT mode (`/SD IDNO`; interactively it is the user's answer, so an interactive uninstall can still remove `gpu-hevc\`) -- source-derived; the lead's observation was that upgraded hosts kept their DLLs. When set, the install OVERWRITES same-named DLLs on a host. Verify a package with `find .../application -name gpu-hevc` and on the host `UmbraCaps` bit 3 (§47). |
| `notify_pre_releases` (sunshine.conf; web UI "PreRelease Notifications") | Apollo/Sunshine key (`docs/configuration.md:241`: "Whether to be notified of new pre-release versions of Sunshine", default disabled); Umbra's `UmbraCard.vue` also reads it / DOC (host side) + SRC (card) | Host side is INERT: `config.cpp:600` default false, `:1308` `bool_f` (`to_bool` accepts true/yes/enable/enabled/on/any `1`, `:858-868`) -> `config.h:340`, and nothing else in `src/` reads the field (grep: those three hits only; identical lines in `origin/master`, `config.cpp:598,1301`). Its only consumer is browser JS: `/api/config` returns every key of `sunshine.conf` as the RAW TEXT written (`confighttp.cpp:1010-1012`, same loop in `origin/master`, `confighttp.cpp:1013`). Apollo's card `index.html:112` `notifyPreReleases = config.notify_pre_releases`, used at `:42,:47` as a truthy test (same code in `origin/master` `index.html:105`, `:38,:43`). Umbra's card `UmbraCard.vue:113` `=== true || === 'true'`; if so `latest()` (`:95-97`) fetches `.../releases?per_page=10` (newest non-draft, pre-releases included) instead of `.../releases/latest`, for both the Apollo and the Umbra repos. The UI saves the pair it detects: default `"disabled"` (`config.html:154`) so ticking writes `enabled` (`Checkbox.vue` `mapToBoolRepresentation`), and unticking DELETES the key (`config.html:436-448`, value equals default). | **OPEN -- two paths disagree, source-derived, not run.** UI-saved `enabled` is truthy to Apollo's card and true to `to_bool`, but FALSE to `UmbraCard.vue:113` (only `true`/`'true'`), so ticking the box may not make the Umbra card consider pre-releases; and a hand-written `disabled`/`false` is truthy to Apollo's card. Test (lead conducts): tick the box in the web UI, Save (`/api/config` re-reads the file per call, `confighttp.cpp:1010`, no restart needed), `curl -sk -u USER:PASS https://HOST:47990/api/config` and read `notify_pre_releases` (expect `"enabled"`), open the home page with the browser Network tab: a request to `.../PreceptorOfMagic/Umbra/releases?per_page=10` = the card honours it; only `.../releases/latest` with the box ticked = the bug is real (fix `UmbraCard.vue:113` to accept `enabled`/`enable`/`yes`/`on`/`1`). Re-run with `notify_pre_releases = true` hand-written to separate the value form from the plumbing. |

## 49. Co-op local-host audio and the silent-host audio clock (2026-10-03)

Registered by the param-registrar on 2026-10-03 because `param_check.py` reported these three compile-time names UNREGISTERED after commit `c9b5d182` (co-op audio mix). Eclipse-side constants, class SRC; behaviour claims in the source comments are CLAIMS (marked), the measured audio-mix results are in `scratchpad/run/RUN.md` (2026-10-02/03 entries, n=1 each) and not repeated here.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `COOP_LOCAL_AUDIO` (macro `COOP_LOCAL_AUDIO_ENV`) | process env var Eclipse sets and reads ITSELF, `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c:175` / SRC | A per-process flag, not a setting. `coop_audio_prepare_local_host()` (`:194`) first UNSETS it (`:195`), then on Windows with this PC being a co-op host sets `local` (`:204,:233`: SDL audio unavailable, no virtual sink on this PC, the default output is already the sink, or it has no name -- the safe mode) or `stream` (`:236`: this PC's own host's sound is streamed and mixed, and Eclipse's output is pinned to the chosen device). `coop_audio_release_local_host()` unsets it (`:186-190`). Only reader: `coop_audio_local_streamed()` (`:181`, true only for `stream`) -> `session_worker.c:211` `config.local_audio = !(app_launch_coop_requested() && coop_audio_local_streamed())`, i.e. `localAudioPlayMode` (§40). Nothing outside `coop_audio.c` reads the env var. | **Setting it from outside does nothing**: it is rewritten at the start of every prepare (`:195`). Observable: the log line `[COOP-AUD] local host: ... STREAMED and mixed; Eclipse output pinned to "<device>"` (= `stream`) or `... plays its own sound, NOT mixed` (= `local`), and on the host the Umbra log `Selected audio sink:` (virtual sink = streamed, §40 `localAudioPlayMode`). Non-Windows builds never set it (`#ifdef _WIN32`, `:197`), so `local_audio` stays 1 there. The log text is code intent, not a measurement of what the speakers did. |
| `COOP_VIRTUAL_SINK_NAME` | Eclipse constant `"Steam Streaming Speakers"`, `coop_audio.c:179`; the NAME is owned by Umbra/Sunshine / SRC + DOC | `strstr` against every SDL output-device name (`:210`) to decide whether this PC has the virtual sink, and against the chosen/default device (`:228`) to detect "the default already IS the sink" (a previous session did not restore it). Umbra's own match is `match_steam_speakers()` on the ADAPTER friendly name (`apollo-build/src/platform/windows/audio.cpp:902-904`) -- a different field from SDL's device name; that they coincide on every host is NOT verified (OPEN). A host with a differently-named virtual sink is not recognised and falls back to `local` (no loop). | The `[COOP-AUD]` line names it: `no "Steam Streaming Speakers" on this PC, ...` -> `local`. To test a host: `SDL_GetAudioDeviceName` output vs the Umbra log `Selected audio sink:` on a stream to that host. |
| `COOP_PUMP_IDLE_MS` | Eclipse constant `40` (ms), `src/app/stream/audio/session_audio.c:36` / SRC | AUDIO only: how long our host's audio feed may be quiet before the co-op audio pump thread (`coop_pump_main`, started by `coop_pump_start`, `:77`, which needs `coop_audio_coord_active()` and a decoder) takes over as the mix clock, feeding the other PC's PCM over silence (`:54`, compared against `SDL_GetTicks()` minus `last_self_feed_ms`). The source comment's premise -- hosts send no audio while nothing plays, "first audio packet 39.3 s in" -- is a CLAIM from the 2026-10-02 session. **Not the video pump**: `coop_pump_h/_v` / `/tmp/coop-pump` (Sentinels table; read at `session_video.c:2485`) is an unrelated knob. | At session end the log prints `Co-op audio clock: <N> frames mixed while our host was silent` (`session_audio.c:98`): N > 0 = the pump ran, N = 0 = it never took over (the host never went quiet for 40 ms, or the coordinator was inactive). Changing the constant needs a rebuild; it is not read from config. |

## 50. Release installation paths and build feature gates (2026-10-08)

Source declarations and primary specifications below establish meaning; application observables are test designs until recorded as measured in the release run log.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `XDG_DATA_HOME` | freedesktop.org Base Directory specification / DOC + SRC | Absolute per-user data directory, default `$HOME/.local/share` when unset/empty. https://specifications.freedesktop.org/basedir/latest/ . Used by `deploy/linux-portable/install-desktop-entry.sh` for launchers/icons and SDL2 `SDL_GetPrefPath` for `com.limelight/moonlight-tv` preferences. | Install under an isolated absolute override; inspect desktop entry/icon and launch app to observe its preference files there. A relative value is invalid per specification; installers must reject it. |
| `XDG_STATE_HOME` | freedesktop.org Base Directory specification / DOC + SRC | Absolute persistent state directory; unset/empty default `$HOME/.local/state`. `deploy/linux-portable/eclipse` places redirected non-terminal logs under `eclipse/`. | Launch with stderr redirected and isolated override, observe `eclipse/eclipse.log`; changing an existing process environment cannot relocate an already-open log. |
| `XDG_CACHE_HOME` | freedesktop.org Base Directory specification / DOC + SRC | Absolute cache directory; default `$HOME/.cache`. Also used by Eclipse `src/app/platform/sdl/path_sdl.c:path_cache_parent` then appends `moonlight-tv`. Existing shell-knobs row refers separately to developer network cache. | Inspect newly created cover-art cache beneath the isolated override. Never delete the cache base itself on uninstall, only the application subdirectory. |
| `HOME` | OS process environment / DOC + SRC | User home used for the XDG defaults above and portable build workspace. On webOS it is the app install tree, NOT a desktop user's home (`platform/webos/path_webos.c`). | Run per-user installer with isolated HOME and unset XDG values, inspect writes remain below it; never apply desktop uninstall rules to webOS HOME. |
| `ECLIPSE_LINUX_WORK` | Eclipse build environment `scripts/linux/portable/env.sh:4` / SRC | Dependency/sysroot workspace (default `$HOME/eclipse-linux`), defines sysroot-jammy, hosttools, built dependencies. Builder-only; not a distributed application configuration. | Build log/compiler sysroot and manifest must resolve to requested workspace; check resulting ELF GLIBC symbol ceiling independently. |
| `FEATURE_EMBEDDED_SHELL` | Eclipse CMake/generated feature config / SRC | Internal feature selecting legacy external Moonlight Embedded fallback: root CMake defaults OFF but enabled on Linux; `src/app/stream/CMakeLists.txt` includes wrapper/worker and `session.c` selects it only when native decoder is invalid. | Inspect generated feature header and linker inputs; when disabled there must be no external embedded executable launch path. Requires rebuild, never a live setting. |
| `--coop-secondary` | Eclipse internal argv, `src/app/stream/video/coop_secondary.c:coop_secondary_parse_args`, `src/main.c` / SRC | Followed by coordinator endpoint; starts the same binary headlessly with co-op network forwarding, no UI. Parent launches it with the app-launch file separately; not an end-user installation option. | A process test observes a distinct child PID and owned endpoint, then no child after parent termination; a live test additionally requires peer video on the panel. Missing endpoint argument does not enter secondary mode. |
| `-PackageDir` / `-Makensis` | Eclipse `scripts/windows/build_installer.ps1` / SRC | Verified portable payload folder (default dist/eclipse-windows-x64) and path/name of the NSIS compiler (default makensis.exe). Installer generation verifies every SHA256SUMS row and refuses extra payload files. | Alter a file -> build refuses; compile -> output exe plus matching sha256. These select builder inputs, not runtime defaults. |
| `-Action` / `-InstallDir` / `-KeepSettings` / `/KEEPSETTINGS` | Eclipse installer `deploy/windows/manage-install.ps1` and `eclipse.nsi` / SRC | Prepare validates and replaces owned payload during upgrade, preserving preferences; Remove stops exact-path client processes then deletes declared owned files and preferences unless KeepSettings is selected. NSIS silent uninstall accepts /KEEPSETTINGS, default removes profile. | Isolated native installer smoke: install, start exact-path dummy worker, upgrade, uninstall, verify PIDs/registry/shortcuts/files removed and unrelated files/processes preserved. Profile deletion test must isolate the user to avoid touching real pairings. |
| `BUILD_HOST_DLLS` | Eclipse GPU encoder CMake option, `experimental/gpu-hevc/CMakeLists.txt:51` / SRC | Default OFF. ON adds aurora_gpu_hevc8/10 shared-library targets; requires MSVC and static-CRT x265 builds. It does not install them into Umbra; UMBRA_GPU_HEVC_DIR does that. | CMakeCache true + generated targets; compile both, inspect PE imports, compare staged/installed DLL hashes and host capability bit. |
| `OPENCL_HEADERS` | Eclipse GPU encoder CMake PATH, `experimental/gpu-hevc/CMakeLists.txt:5` / SRC | Search hint for Khronos OpenCL CL/cl.h headers. find_path may also search system paths; pin and verify OPENCL_INCLUDE_DIR to prove selected headers. | CMakeCache resolved OPENCL_INCLUDE_DIR and source commit of that directory, then compiler include command. |
| `X265_SOURCE` | Eclipse GPU encoder CMake PATH, `experimental/gpu-hevc/CMakeLists.txt:57` / SRC | x265 source root used for common/encoder/public headers by both DLL builds; static libraries configured separately. | Compiler include paths and source commit; must match the static libraries' source/build manifest. |
| `X265_BUILD_8` / `X265_BUILD_10` | Eclipse GPU encoder CMake PATH, `experimental/gpu-hevc/CMakeLists.txt:59-63` / SRC | Build folders with Release/x265-static.lib and generated x265_config.h, respectively 8/10-bit. Missing files fail configure; existing wrong ABI is NOT verified by the file check. | CMake library input + DLL link success and live corresponding bit-depth encode; archive headers/libs hashes and x265 source provenance. |
| `SDL2_BACKPORT_REVISION` | commons-c `third_party/commons/cmake/ExternalSDL2BackportForWebOS.cmake:29-47` / DOC (owner source) | When SDL2_BACKPORT_RELEASE is NOT defined, selects a git revision of webosbrew/SDL-webOS and compiles it for WEBOS.
