A defined RELEASE (even empty) takes precedence. | ExternalProject configure/build logs and source HEAD equal pinned revision; resulting IPK SDL bytes equal locally linked library. Source build requires LICENSE.txt at source root (prebuilt uses share/licenses/SDL2). |
| `SUNSHINE_EXECUTABLE_PATH` | Umbra/Apollo CMake `cmake/prep/init.cmake`, generated sunshine.service / DOC (owner source) | Executable used by user service ExecStart (default `sunshine`, absolute installation path permitted). Distinct from the executable's display/product name and from process identification for removal. | Inspect generated/installed user unit ExecStart and running process executable path. Package removal must resolve exact installed binary rather than kill all processes named sunshine. |
| `CPACK_RPM_PRE_UNINSTALL_SCRIPT_FILE` | CMake CPack RPM generator / DOC | Path whose contents become the RPM %preun scriptlet; https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/cpack_gen/rpm.html#variable:CPACK_RPM_PRE_UNINSTALL_SCRIPT_FILE . No default; Umbra uses it for pre-removal host shutdown. | Inspect final package with `rpm -qp --scripts`; exercise uninstall and observe only this package's service/PIDs stop before executable removal. |
| `CPACK_DEBIAN_PACKAGE_CONTROL_EXTRA` | CMake CPack DEB generator / DOC | List of extra control.tar files, e.g. postinst/prerm. https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/cpack_gen/deb.html#variable:CPACK_DEBIAN_PACKAGE_CONTROL_EXTRA . File must carry executable permission unless strict-permission setting overrides. | `dpkg-deb --ctrl-tarfile` confirms scripts and modes; install/remove test proves prerm invocation before payload deletion. |
| `--root` / `--output` | Eclipse `scripts/release/source_bundle.py` / SRC | Source checkout root and output tar.gz for the PROJECT source component. Includes pinned submodules and audited applied patches, not separately built external dependencies. | Archive fixtures check hashes, expected added source files and rejection of unknown inputs; a Git-free direct CMake rebuild exercises archive completeness. |
| `-Configuration` | Eclipse `scripts/windows/build_portable.ps1` / SRC | Debug/Release/RelWithDebInfo CMake build type. Default Release; release workflow explicitly uses RelWithDebInfo to match the tested Windows compiler optimization profile. | CMakeCache CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE + BUILDINFO.txt and compiler invocations in build.ninja; install hash must match this output. |
| `CMAKE_C_FLAGS` / `CMAKE_CXX_FLAGS` | CMake language-wide compiler flags / DOC | Initial compiler flags for each language across configurations. https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/variable/CMAKE_LANG_FLAGS.html . webOS release wrapper uses GCC file/macro/debug prefix maps so diagnostic paths do not disclose builder home directories. | Compiler command lines contain mappings; inspect final unpacked executable/libraries with strings, not only cache presence. ExternalProject builds require explicit forwarding. |
| `CMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_ARGS` | commons-c ExternalProject helper input / DOC (owner source) | The helpers forward this as the child cache string `-DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_ARGS:string=...` (`third_party/commons/cmake/ExternalSDL2BackportForWebOS.cmake:12-14`), NOT as individual compiler flags. The current Buildroot SDK does not consume that cache value: setting it for prefix maps would silently do nothing (SRC inspection 10-08). | Child cfgcmd.txt only proves the cache string reached configure, NOT compiler application. Inspect compile commands and output strings. Use a wrapper toolchain applying add_compile_options for this SDK. |
| `CMAKE_C_COMPILER` / `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER` | CMake compiler-selection cache variables / DOC | Select C/C++ compiler executable for a build tree; https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/variable/CMAKE_LANG_COMPILER.html . webOS wrapper supplies the SDK's absolute cross-compilers so moving the toolchain wrapper cannot accidentally select host GCC. | Fresh CMakeCache compiler paths + compile command; final ELF machine must be ARM. Changing compiler in an existing cache may force CMake regeneration. |
| `CMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE` | CMake cross-compilation input / DOC | Path loaded early to establish target toolchain; https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/variable/CMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE.html . Release wrapper includes the real SDK then adds compiler prefix maps; common ExternalProjects forward the wrapper path. | Root and every external cfgcmd/toolchain carry wrapper; final ELF ARM and strings source-prefix audit prove target and flag application. |
| `PKG_CONFIG` | pkg-config tool override; CMake/vcpkg build input / DOC (owner source) | Path of native pkg-config/pkgconf tool. vcpkg scripts/cmake/vcpkg_find_acquire_program(PKGCONFIG).cmake accepts environment override rather than acquiring its obsolete bundled tool. Eclipse portable/env.sh also sets its chosen tool. | vcpkg/build command logs name actual tool and no acquisition of absent runtime; generated dependency configure records/pkg-config invocation prove selection. Builder-only, not a packaged runtime default. |
| `VCPKG_KEEP_ENV_VARS` | Microsoft vcpkg environment configuration / DOC | Semicolon-separated environment-variable names preserved in vcpkg's sanitized build environment; values do not affect ABI hashes. https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/vcpkg/users/config-environment#vcpkg_keep_env_vars . Used only to preserve native PKG_CONFIG tool selection for source-built nefconc. | Build logs/configure tool path show actual pkgconf invocation; setting this alone proves nothing about code generation or ABI. Rebuild output and dependencies still require independent verification. |
| `SUNSHINE_ENABLE_CUDA` | Apollo `cmake/prep/options.cmake` and `cmake/compile_definitions/linux.cmake` (upstream adc5c5a0) / DOC | Default ON requests CUDA language/compiler discovery and builds cuda.cpp/cuda.cu plus SUNSHINE_BUILD_CUDA when available. Does not by itself prove a host can encode/capture with its installed driver. | Configure must print CUDA compiler/architectures; build commands compile CUDA translation units and final binary contains that path. Real NVIDIA Linux stream remains a separate live gate. |
| `CUDA_FAIL_ON_MISSING` | Apollo options + Linux compile definitions, upstream adc5c5a0 / DOC | Default ON makes requested-but-missing CUDA compiler a fatal configure error. OFF permits configuring without CUDA support; that is not evidence of a complete NVIDIA build. | Configure with no nvcc errors when ON; final source list/configure must establish CUDA inclusion, not simply cache presence. |
| `SUNSHINE_ENABLE_DRM` / `SUNSHINE_ENABLE_WAYLAND` / `SUNSHINE_ENABLE_X11` | Apollo `cmake/prep/options.cmake` and `cmake/compile_definitions/linux.cmake`, upstream adc5c5a0 / DOC | Linux capture build gates default ON; find platform dependencies and include corresponding capture paths. These select compiled support, not runtime permission/display availability. | Build/configure dependency checks and compiled target sources reflect requested paths; actual X11/Wayland/KMS streaming needs live tests. |
| `BUILD_TESTS` / `BUILD_DOCS` (Umbra CMake) | Apollo `cmake/prep/options.cmake`, upstream adc5c5a0 / DOC | Default OFF gates upstream test/documentation build targets. Distinct from application release configuration or debug settings; disabling tests does not imply they passed. | Generated build target inventory shows test/docs targets as requested; execute specific tests separately and record results. |
| `CMAKE_CUDA_HOST_COMPILER` | CMake official https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/variable/CMAKE_CUDA_HOST_COMPILER.html / DOC | Selects host C++ compiler used by NVIDIA CUDA compiler; set before CUDA language enable, not a replacement for the main project C++ compiler. Used to select CUDA-supported GCC12 while project uses GCC13 on Ubuntu24.04. | CUDA compiler command contains selected host compiler (-ccbin); successful compilation plus compiler identification logs. |
| `SUNSHINE_ASSETS_DIR` | Apollo common/unix compile definitions and common packaging, upstream adc5c5a0 / DOC | Defaults to assets; Unix prefixes relative values with CMAKE_INSTALL_PREFIX, and compiled asset path plus install destinations use it. Release Linux passes share/sunshine with /usr prefix. Windows normally uses assets beside executable. | Generated compile definitions and unpacked package asset locations must agree; launch/find-web-assets test is separate application proof. |
| `BUILD_DIR` (webOS wrapper internal) | Eclipse `scripts/webos/build_for_lg.sh:77-93` / SRC | Resolved absolute build folder derived from CMAKE_BINARY_DIR or build/webos-easy_build. Internal shell variable, not an independently accepted runtime/build environment override; used for generated wrapper toolchain and compiler prefix maps. | Generated eclipse-release-toolchain.cmake location/content and final build metadata identify the chosen build folder. |
| `CPACK_DEB_COMPONENT_INSTALL` | CMake CPack DEB https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/cpack_gen/deb.html#variable:CPACK_DEB_COMPONENT_INSTALL / DOC | OFF creates one combined binary package; ON emits component packages. Umbra release OFF keeps binary, removal helper and maintainer hooks in one package instead of installing the same prerm into a notices-only component. | Count generated DEBs and inspect control + payload: one package contains sunshine, umbra-stop-host, notices and executable prerm. |
| `CMAKE_INSTALL_FULL_LIBEXECDIR` / `CMAKE_INSTALL_FULL_BINDIR` | CMake GNUInstallDirs https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/module/GNUInstallDirs.html / DOC | Absolute computed installation directories based on prefix and LIBEXECDIR/BINDIR settings. Umbra removal hook embeds these exact package locations for its private pidfd helper and sunshine executable. These are build/package paths, not discovery of a running process by name. | Generated prerm and unpacked DEB contain matching helper/executable paths; relocation requires reconfigure/repackage, not changing runtime environment. |
| `overlay-ports` (vcpkg-configuration.json) | Microsoft vcpkg manifest configuration, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/vcpkg/reference/vcpkg-configuration-json / DOC | Adds local port directories before registry recipes. Include overlay bytes in source/build-material bundle. |
| `CPACK_DEBIAN_PACKAGE_CONTROL_STRICT_PERMISSION` | CMake 3.28 CPack DEB official https://cmake.org/cmake/help/v3.28/cpack_gen/deb.html#variable:CPACK_DEBIAN_PACKAGE_CONTROL_STRICT_PERMISSION / DOC | TRUE forces Debian-policy control-file permissions rather than inheriting builder filesystem permissions (important for source on a Windows mount). Default FALSE. | Inspect generated control archive modes: maintainer scripts executable 0755, not world writable; exercise prerm from actual unpacked package. |
| `DISPLAY` | X.Org X(7), Xvfb(1); local primary Xvfb man in eclipse-linux/hosttools/xroot/usr/share/man/man1/Xvfb.1.gz / DOC | X server display address selects the server used by X clients; isolated tests target a private Xvfb display. This does not start a server. | xdpyinfo/xdotool show the private display dimensions and its isolated client window; no production desktop is targeted. |
| `SDL_VIDEODRIVER` | SDL2 official https://wiki.libsdl.org/SDL2/FAQUsingSDL / DOC | Selects requested compiled-in video backend before SDL video initialization; availability depends on platform/build. Tests use x11 with private DISPLAY. | SDL initialization log and actual X window on that display; selecting an unavailable backend must fail, not count as successful UI evidence. |
| `XDG_CONFIG_HOME` | freedesktop.org https://specifications.freedesktop.org/basedir/latest/ / DOC | Absolute user configuration base directory; unset/empty default HOME/.config. Test override isolates preferences from real pairings. | Fresh process creates configuration beneath the isolated absolute directory; no writes to real profile. |
| `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` | Linux dynamic loader ld.so(8), https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man8/ld.so.8.html / DOC | Colon-separated library search directories for dynamic linker; ignored in secure-execution mode. Used only by private Xvfb/xdotool wrapper for extracted helper libraries. | ldd resolves the expected extracted library paths for the helper and helper launches; no global loader configuration or distributed app search path changes. |

| `URL_HASH` | CMake ExternalProject_Add / DOC | Archive checksum `<algorithm>=<digest>` checked on downloaded and cached source bytes; mismatch fails extraction/download. No fixed rate/format is inferred from this module alone. | Inspect actual sink name/sample format/rate/channel map and module inventory; a real client sink-input attached to that null sink demonstrates routing. It does not demonstrate audible output, device quality or system-wide isolation unless other modules/endpoints are also checked. |
| `cookie-file` | PulseAudio client.conf key, primary v15.99.1–v17.0 `src/pulse/client-conf.c` parse table and pa_client_conf_load_cookie / SRC | Configured authentication cookie path; lower priority than PULSE_COOKIE, X11 cookie and application-specified path, higher than default/legacy fallback. Launcher fallback /proc/self/fd/9 refers to a held private cookie descriptor inherited through exec; later original config/drop-ins may override it. Existing shared cookie and explicit configuration environment bypass the wrapper. | Real libpulse AUTH packet fixture must contain selected fixture cookie bytes; inspect FD9 inheritance, preserve existing auth/config hashes and verify no new global cookie. This does not prove daemon autospawn or imply cookie-based authentication when a server accepts same-UID peer credentials. |
| `PULSE_SCRIPT` | PulseAudio16.1 daemon `daemon-conf.c:725-727,735-756`, https://github.com/pulseaudio/pulseaudio/blob/v16.1/src/daemon/daemon-conf.c / SRC | Overrides daemon startup script filename. Isolated autospawn fixture supplies only native Unix protocol (auth-anonymous=0) and null sink modules; no hardware detection/playback modules. Does not itself start a daemon. | A successful automatically spawned private daemon must have only the supplied module configuration; fixture has private XDG directories and exact owned PID cleanup. |
| `autospawn` | PulseAudio client.conf primary15.99/16.1/17 parse table in client-conf.c and context.c / SRC | Permits libpulse to start its normal per-user daemon if connection attempts fail; context NOAUTOSPAWN blocks it, root UID also skips it. Private fixture explicitly enables it to exercise the path as ordinary existing nobody account; production wrapper preserves original setting. | PA_CONTEXT_READY with fresh private runtime and no prestarted daemon plus new private Pulse PID proves spawn/connect, not hardware playback. |
| `PA_CONTEXT_NOFLAGS` / `PA_CONTEXT_READY` | PulseAudio public src/pulse/def.h / SRC | NOFLAGS=0 requests normal context behavior (including configured autospawn); READY=4 is the ready state after protocol authentication/setup. Neither implies an audio stream/device has opened. | Fixture calls pa_context_connect with0 and waits for state4, then disconnects and frees context. Exact owned daemon PID and authentication state inspected separately. |

| `--launcher-dir` | scripts/linux/test_pulse_autospawn.py argparse definition / SRC (tool-only) | Selects the directory containing the launcher and package-functions.sh copied into the private nonroot autospawn fixture. It does not start the actual Eclipse executable or change the account HOME. | Fixture uses these exact copied files and reports authenticated READY plus owned daemon shutdown; conductor records their hashes before execution. |


## 51. Throwaway native webOS DB8 storage canary (2026-10-08)

These rows apply only to the undeployed canary under scratchpad/webos-db8-canary, not production storage. LG documentation is an API contract, not native permission or TV26 uninstall measurement.

| Parameter | Owner / evidence | Meaning and scope | Observable application |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `putKind` / `id` / `owner` | LG TV Database API https://webostv.developer.lge.com/develop/references/database#putKind / DOC | At luna://com.palm.db, registers the exact kind ID and owning app ID; changing a kind requires owner authorization. Canary fixes both IDs; it never accepts an arbitrary owner/kind. | Record exact request and response from app-manager-launched native owner. Successful registration is required; transport/schema failure is not permission evidence. |
| `private` | LG TV Database putKind, webOS TV3.0+ / DOC | true requests kind removal when its owning app is uninstalled; default false retains it. This flag is not itself a cross-app ACL test. | First create/query marker, then uninstall via actual supported path, reinstall same ID and query BEFORE any recreation; distinguish missing-kind result from access/transport error. |
| `sync` | LG TV Database putKind / DOC | false (default) does not opt the kind into LG daily backup/restore; true enables it. Canary explicitly false. | Exact submitted payload establishes requested setting; cloud behavior remains unmeasured. No personal data enters the canary. |
| `find` / `query` / `from` / `limit` / `watch` | LG TV Database find and Query object https://webostv.developer.lge.com/develop/references/database#find / DOC | Read query targets fixed kind via from; bounded limit; watch=false uses a single result, not subscription. No mutation is implicit in a missing-kind response. | Default launch emits only find; display raw results/error. Existing marker nonce must match across close/relaunch. Reader ACL result must be specific denial, not generic failure. |
| `put` / `objects` / `_kind` | LG TV Database put https://webostv.developer.lge.com/develop/references/database#put / DOC | Inserts the supplied JSON objects into their stated kind. Canary inserts only newly generated nonsecret marker data after explicit create confirmation and successful putKind. | Successful put response plus subsequent read of exact nonce; no inference from submitting a request alone. |
| `delKind` | LG TV Database delKind https://webostv.developer.lge.com/develop/references/database#delKind / DOC | Deletes specified kind and its contained records. Canary exposes this only for its fixed owner kind with explicit confirmation; reader binary has no mutation path. | Exact target displayed before second confirmation, raw result, then explicit find checks absence. Never use manual deletion to claim installer-driven cleanup. |
| `returnValue` / `errorCode` / `errorText` / `results` | LG TV Database response objects / DOC | Boolean operation success, native error code/text, and query result records. Documented find codes include -3970 missing kind and -3963 permission denied; do not conflate them. | Preserve exact JSON and classify response separately from transport submission/callback/timeout. On-screen state is an operation result, not an automatic acceptance verdict. |
| `APPID` | Native environment consumer: bundled SDL webOS Wayland `SDL_waylandwebos.c:137-144`; distinct Eclipse CMake compiled APPID macro at CMakeLists.txt:377 / SRC | Launcher-provided environment names the app to Wayland; canary checks equality with its compiled distinct ID but never sets it. Eclipse also uses a same-spelled C macro for compile-time identity. Neither string alone proves LS2 bus credentials. | Record compiled ID, env ID, native UID and actual launched package/process. Require owner success plus separately launched reader denial to measure identity separation. |
| `CANARY_SDL_PREFIX` | Canary CMakeLists.txt / SRC (tool-only) | Path to already built audited Eclipse webOS SDL prefix; used for exact target headers/shared library, never a runtime app setting. | Link command and packaged SDL hash must equal that input; inspect ARM ELF and runtime dependency paths. |
| `CANARY_READER` / `CANARY_APP_ID` | Canary CMakeLists.txt and canary.c / SRC (tool-only) | Two fixed builds: com.eclipse.release.db8canary owner and com.eclipse.release.db8reader read-only companion. Reader preprocessing excludes create/delete operations; both query the owner's fixed kind. No user override of DB target. | appinfo ID, compiled marker and launcher env must agree; reader ELF lacks mutation URI strings; native ACL still needs measured service responses. |
| `CANARY_TIMEOUT_MS` | Canary canary.c / SRC (tool-only) | 10000ms response deadline; timeout cancels callback and reports outcome unknown, especially for writes. Contexts remain valid until process exit so late replies cannot reuse freed memory. | Elapsed pending timeout and no automatic write retry; real helper cancellation/lifecycle remains native test. |
| `nonce` / `createdUtc` / `probeVersion` | Canary synthetic marker schema in canary.c / SRC (tool-only) | Nonsecret random16-byte hex marker, creation timestamp and fixed probe format version; no pairing/profile data is read. | Compare exact marker after relaunch/reboot; default find must not generate/recreate it. |
| `CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH` | Official CMake installed help, https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/variable/CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH.html / DOC | Initializes installed targets' library runtime search path. Canary uses executable-relative ../lib for its packaged SDL library, not a builder path. | readelf dynamic section on staged ELF shows intended relative RUNPATH/RPATH and no build-tree path. |

| `-3963` / `-3970` (DB8 find errorCode) | LG TV Database API find error reference https://webostv.developer.lge.com/develop/references/database#find / DOC, checked2026-10-08 | For find, -3963 means permission denied to read the existing kind; -3970 means kind not registered. Scope is this service/method, not every platform error domain. | Compare exact raw native-app reply, target kind, caller package and known owner readback. Reader denial is ACL evidence only after the owner successfully created/read the same kind; missing-kind is uninstall evidence only after previously verified marker and reinstall default query without recreation. Generic helper failure/timeout proves neither. |

Never signal a process solely from this property. |

Capture owner is the proprietary LG TV service, not Eclipse. Basis below is the recorded live test in memory/coop-tv-and-geometry.md:11-23 (2026-07-28), plus native-size capture measurement in scripts/coop/sample_expected_vs_actual.sh:47-50 (2026-08-02). This is historical TEST evidence, not an official LG compatibility promise or a new TV26 measurement.

| Parameter | Owner / evidence | Meaning and scope | Observable application |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `executeOneShot` / `luna://com.webos.service.capture/executeOneShot` | LG capture service / historical TEST above | Performs one display capture. This exact service endpoint was measured through the native helper; alternate service spellings and direct CLI caller permissions are not assumed. | Require a successful service reply AND a newly written decodable image with current TV mtime; open the image and verify the intended foreground app. |
| `path` / `format` / `method` (capture payload) | LG capture service / historical TEST above | Measured payload uses path=/tmp/tvshot.jpg, format=JPG, method=DISPLAY. The destination is a temporary TV file. No other formats/methods are asserted by this row. | Reply success, positive file size, current mtime and viewed JPEG contents establish capture; remove only the explicitly owned capture file during teardown. |
| `width` / `height` (capture payload) | LG capture service / historical TEST above | Requested output dimensions; 1920x1080 and 3840x2160 were measured. Omitting both historically yielded960x540. These are output sizing, not a claim of physical display scanout timing. | Check returned captured dimensions and decoded JPEG size, and visually inspect before scaling for review. |
| `capturedWidth` / `capturedHeight` / `writtenBytes` (capture reply) | LG capture service / historical TEST above | Reported image dimensions and encoded byte count accompanying returnValue=true. | Cross-check actual image dimensions/size and current TV file mtime; values alone cannot rule out stale or incorrect screen content. |
| `AURORA_TV_HOST` | scripts/tv/lgtv.py:_resolve_tv, scripts/wol/coop_wake_both.py / SRC | Nonempty lgtv override directly selects control endpoint before Wi-Fi preference/net_env resolution. The on verb separately uses it for the wake target. Diagnostic-only scripts; not packaged app defaults. | Observe the resolved socket destination and response; an address override bypasses discovery and must come from current resolution, never a remembered address. SSAP rejection alone does not prove TV power state. |
| `AURORA_TV_MAC` / `AURORA_TV_MAC2` / `AURORA_TV_MAC3` | scripts/tv/lgtv.py:99-114,447-479 / SRC; lead current-TV NIC read 2026-10-08 / TEST | Environment selects MACs included in native Windows UDP wake magic packets: defaults wlan0 00:00:5e:00:53:00, USB eth1 00:00:5e:00:53:00, built-in eth0 00:00:5e:00:53:00. Empty value omits that MAC. These are diagnostic rig endpoints, not packaged app defaults. Current wlan0/eth0 measured matching; eth1 absent in observed standby. Source wake comments are not proof of TV26 behavior. | Generated packet target bytes and sent trace show selected requests only; require actual SSAP Active and a fresh inspected panel capture to establish wake. Sending a magic packet alone is not success. |

| `@repeat` | src/app/platform/webos/app_webos.c:coop_probe_pass / coop_probe_thread, SRC | Diagnostic-build probe header `@repeat N ms`: first pass latches total pass count N and interval; initial pass always occurs, subsequent passes run for i=1 through N-1. Positive ms is used; nonpositive interval falls back2000ms. Script commands are re-read each pass, not the interval. Release builds without AURORA_TEST_HOOKS do not run this probe. | Use bounded N for the intended observation window (e.g.120 at3000ms spans about357seconds plus capture costs), verify advancing probe run timestamps and image mtimes, then close owned probe process/remove script. Changing header needs a restart to take effect. |
| `CANARY_LOG_PATH` / `CANARY_LOG_DIRECTORY` / `CANARY_LOG_NAME` | scratchpad/webos-db8-canary/canary.c:open_diagnostic_log / SRC (canary-only) | DIRECTORY fixes /tmp/eclipse-db8-evidence; NAME is owner.log or reader.log selected at compile time; PATH concatenates them. Conductor creates a fresh absent parent with measured shared group and0770; app opens parent no-follow, checks matching eGID/no world-write, then openat fixed basename. APPID guard precedes open. No-follow append, initial0600 creation, same effective UID/GID regular single-link validation, then0640 for existing-group read; FD closes on exec. Not production app storage. | Verify actual app UID/GID, file mode/owner, group readability and appended timestamped identity/request/reply records. Conductor archives then explicitly removes only these diagnostic logs; removal is manual evidence cleanup, not an automatic DB8 uninstall result. |

Primary source: LG Electronics Connect SDK Android Core commit `2f7ebbc7eb909321ff41b2c6bcdf6b0881427579`, [WebOSTVService.java](https://github.com/ConnectSDK/Connect-SDK-Android-Core/blob/2f7ebbc7eb909321ff41b2c6bcdf6b0881427579/src/com/connectsdk/service/WebOSTVService.java#L623-L639) and [WebOSTVDeviceService.java](https://github.com/ConnectSDK/Connect-SDK-Android-Core/blob/2f7ebbc7eb909321ff41b2c6bcdf6b0881427579/src/com/connectsdk/service/webos/WebOSTVDeviceService.java#L540-L582). SRC describes client request construction; current firmware acceptance and completed process teardown require measurement.

| Parameter | Owner / evidence | Meaning and scope | Observable application |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `closeApp` / `ssap://system.launcher/close` | LG Connect SDK WebOSTVService.closeApp / SRC | Sends a close request for the supplied LaunchSession app identity. This is not uninstall and does not select every application. | Record raw reply; independently check exact target app state and process disappearance before subsequent launch or cleanup. Request success alone is not synchronous completion. |
| `getAppState` / `ssap://system.launcher/getAppState` | LG Connect SDK WebOSTVDeviceService.getAppState / SRC | Queries the supplied app/session state; non-subscription method sends one request. | Record reply running/visible booleans and independently correlate target process/foreground image where needed; errors do not establish stopped state. |
| `id` / `sessionId` (SSAP close/getAppState payload) | Same LG primary source / SRC | id is LaunchSession.getAppId(), sessionId is LaunchSession.getSessionId(). SDK inserts both through JSONObject.put; null session value is omitted under [Android JSONObject.put semantics](https://developer.android.com/reference/org/json/JSONObject#put(java.lang.String,%20java.lang.Object)). No fabricated session identifier or id-only firmware support is established here. | Compare outgoing payload to the currently observed intended app/session identity and retain success/error response. An id-only attempt is a scoped compatibility test until measured. |
| `running` / `visible` (SSAP getAppState reply) | Same LG primary source getBoolean parsing / SRC | Application state booleans returned to Launcher.AppState, distinct from a PID or proof of complete filesystem cleanup. | Retain actual booleans and timestamp; correlate exact PID independently. visible=false does not imply running=false. |

Oversized input is rejected with EFBIG before copying or mutation. This bounds the helper buffer; it is not an RSA key-size setting. | Focused oversized-file fixture must return an error and preserve the original file bytes; successful normal key read/migration and key hashes establish no unintended identity replacement. |

## 61. webOS owned-profile atomic migration (2026-10-08)

| Parameter | Owner / evidence | Meaning and scope | Observable application |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `SYS_renameat2` / `__NR_renameat2` / `RENAME_NOREPLACE` | Primary Linux v6.12 [ARM syscall table](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/arch/arm/tools/syscall.tbl), [UAPI fs.h](https://github.com/torvalds/linux/blob/v6.12/include/uapi/linux/fs.h), [rename(2)](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/rename.2.html) / SRC | Linux directory rename via held parent descriptors; NOREPLACE bit0 refuses every existing destination. ARM syscall offset382 uses ARM __NR_SYSCALL_BASE where old SDK headers lack the name; fallback definition restricted to ARM. Kernel/filesystem support is required (Linux3.15/ext4 onward); unsupported, cross-mount EXDEV or denied requests retain existing external profile, never a copying or plain-overwrite fallback. Not a runtime tuning option. | Isolated real-filesystem fixture must preserve inode/content/mode after successful move, refuse a destination created immediately before syscall, retain external data on injected ENOSYS/EXDEV/EACCES, and remove only an empty owned external parent. Actual TV rename and subsequent app-manager uninstall require separate native-identity measurement; equal st_dev alone does not prove cross-mount rename permission. |

> **Merge note (2026-10-10, release/eclipse-1.3):** sections 50–110 below come from the co-op audio branch
> `codex/secondary-audio-latency` (1e32b2e5). Release-branch sections 50–75 above share numbers; references
> inside the audio sections (e.g. "§81") mean the audio-branch section of that number.
## 50. Passive-gate discovery and level controls (2026-10-08)

Owner: Eclipse, class **SRC** throughout. Source references below use
`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c` unless another file is named. These
are code definitions and proposed verification observables, **not new runtime
measurements**. Compile constants require rebuilding and loading that binary;
environment tuning requires `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING` and creation of a fresh gate.
API counters below are available through `coop_pgate_get_stats()`; not every
counter is printed by the shipping mixer. Inspect those counters or add scoped
instrumentation where indicated before claiming application from a test.

| name | Meaning and primary source (SRC) | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING` | Offline compile-time guard: defined enables `read_env()` to read `PGATE_*`; absent compiles an empty function (`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c:253-290`). The simulator refuses compilation without the macro (`tests/coop/sim_coop_audio_pgate.c:17-19`); compiling separate objects requires it on both simulator and gate translation units. It is not a runtime environment variable. | Verify both compile commands and inspect a fresh gate with a qualifying input under a registered environment override. Defining the guard only on the simulator does not enable gate overrides. Shipping builds without the guard ignore all these environment values; §12 records this trap. |
| `PG_BLOCK_MS` | `5` ms (`:18`); create computes block frames as integer `rate/1000 * PG_BLOCK_MS` (`:303`), latency returns those frames (`:401`), processing uses that block and increments `blocks` (`:764-769,:1310-1319`); hold conversion also divides by this constant (`:1138`). At 48 kHz this is 240 frames. | Read `coop_pgate_latency_frames()` and per-block hook positions/counts (`:1206-1208`), plus input/output impulse displacement. Changing only an environment value cannot change it. |
| `PG_SEARCH_MS` | Current SRC equals PG_BLOCK_MS (5ms nominal), schedules discovery each complete block in input sample positions. Replaced historical100ms on2026-10-09. | Trace actual searches and admissions independently; initial scheduled call can return without searching until decimated history exceeds search_win. Not wall-clock guarantee. |
| `PG_DEC` | `8` (`:28`): average consecutive groups of eight channel-averaged input frames into the search signal (`:1276,:1292-1298`). Used for decimated lag extent (`:309`), initial search (`:363`), peak spacing/coarse lag (`:676,:695`), and refinement radius (`:705,:736`). At 48 kHz the search signal is 6 kHz. | Instrument decimated frame count versus input position and search-candidate refinement. Candidate/output offsets alone do not prove the decimation value applied. No parameter named PG_DECIM exists here. |
| `PG_SEARCH_WIN_D` | `1024` decimated samples (`:29`), newest search window (`:633-640`); opposite history window adds the decimated lag extent (`:641-645`). Also sizes decimated history/FFT (`:312,:316`). First search is scheduled at `(PG_SEARCH_WIN_D + max_lag_frames/PG_DEC + 1) * PG_DEC` input frames (`:309,:363`): with 48 kHz / 1000 ms limit, 56200 frames = 1170.833 ms; newest window alone = 170.667 ms. | Record first `searches` increment and instrument the search window bounds. Warm-up is initial discovery readiness, not output buffering: output block latency is separately `PG_BLOCK_MS`. No parameter named PG_SN exists here. |
| `PG_PEAKS` | `3` (`:30`), maximum coarse candidates **per direction per search** (`:675-693`); both directions loop at `:670`. Refined candidates still require admission. | Instrument candidate count in `search()`; `admitted` is insufficient because candidates may fail admission or merge into an existing bank slot. |
| `PG_ADMIT` | `0.64` (`:31`), minimum refined share for `bank_admit()` (`:748-749`). Search refines mono bands 2 and 3 over 20 ms (`:698-730`); when their best share is below this bar it also checks bass over 40 ms, accepting bass candidates at or above the separate 0.90 bar (`:733-746`). Share is positive normalized squared correlation (`:450-490`), not ordinary correlation and not the gate-closing threshold. | Instrument best refined share and admission decision with candidates straddling 0.64; inspect `admitted` plus returned offsets. No closure or audible suppression follows merely from admission. |
| `PG_SAME_MS` | `12` ms (`:33`), bank merge tolerance converted with integer `rate/1000` (`:580-582`); a candidate within tolerance of either current lag **or home** is an existing entry. Such an entry only recentres after more than 0.3 s since last match (`:589-598`). **Since 2026-10-09 (§106) a candidate near a WORKING entry may instead get its own slot when it wins the 60 ms twin test (`PGATE_TWIN`, default on); with it off, behaviour and output are unchanged.** | Instrument candidate, matched slot, current/home lag and `recentres`; compare candidates just inside/outside the tolerance. Unchanged `admitted` alone cannot distinguish merge from rejection. |
| `PG_EXPIRE_S` | `60.0` s (`:34`); at the end of each search, used entries with `now_s - last_match_s > PG_EXPIRE_S` are cleared and `evicted` increments (`:753-757`). Separate prediction recency check uses its own literal 60.0 (`:1046`); changing expiry does not change that check. | Observe an unmatched offset disappearing after the expiry threshold on a search boundary; instrument the expiry branch, since `evicted` also counts full-bank replacement (`:605-612`). |
| `COOP_AUDIO_GATE_MAX_LAG_MS` | Mixer compile constant `1000` ms (`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c:103`), passed into gate creation (`:201,:217,:485,:721`). Gate rejects constructor values outside 5..1000 (`coop_audio_pgate.c:293-295`); refinement restricts positive magnitude to 1..max_lag_frames in both directions (`:705-717,:736-737,:749`). | Verify constructor result and accepted offsets using paired input containing known offsets just inside/outside the bound. Editing only the mixer to exceed 1000 causes creation failure; it does not widen the gate. At 48 kHz and 1000 ms, raw/band ring allocation computes 65536 frames, decimated ring 8192 samples, FFT 8192 samples (`:309-316`); these storage sizes do not widen the accepted lag bound. |
| `COOP_PGATE_MAX_OFFSETS` | `16` bank slots (`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.h:36`, used in gate state `:102`); discovery fills the first unused slot, otherwise replaces the slot with oldest `last_match_s` (`:600-619`). | Inspect returned offset count and instrument replacement on admitting a seventeenth non-near candidate. `evicted` includes both replacement and timeout. No parameter named PG_BANK exists here. |
| `PGATE_PRED` | Offline environment integer -> `P_PRED`, default 1 (`:42,:262`), only when compiled with `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING` (`:253-256`). Enables onset-based predicted closing at known offsets; requires lag at least three blocks, match newer than creation, match age below a separate 60 s literal, and configured failure/pending guards (`:1046-1074`). | With fresh gate and input producing qualifying onsets, compare hook reason 3 (`:1201,:1206-1208`) and `pred_confirmed`/`pred_failed` between 0 and 1. No changed prediction counters when no onset qualifies is inconclusive. Shipping `read_env()` is empty: exporting this variable there does nothing. |
| `PGATE_BALANCE` | Offline environment integer -> `P_BALANCE`, default 1 (`:46,:266`), under the same compile guard. After at least 20 learned samples, clamps learned peer/self level difference to +/-12 dB then sets self gain `10^(d/40)` and peer gain `10^(-d/40)` (`:1212-1215`); gains interpolate across the block and multiply filtered/raw output (`:1228-1242`). | With fresh gates and unequal-level matching input, inspect `balance_samples`, `balance_db`, `balance_applied` (`:417`) and actual per-stream output gains. A balance estimate can exist while application is disabled; statistics alone do not establish audible quality. Shipping environment has no effect. |

## 51. Mixer per-orientation audio-buffer setting (2026-10-08)

**Current source remains the release policy below.** The proposed 5 ms default /
5 ms headroom change was shelved before build or test on 2026-10-08; see §53.
Neither that prototype nor its added diagnostics reached an installed binary.

| name | Meaning and primary source (SRC) | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_audio_depth_ms`, `coop_audio_depth_ms_h`, `coop_audio_depth_ms_v` | Eclipse configuration member `app_settings_t.coop_audio_depth_ms[2]` (`src/app/app_settings.h:253`), default zero (`app_settings.c:686`). Writer emits separate `_h`/`_v` keys (`:425-435`); parser reads each orientation or the unsuffixed legacy key into both (`:541-565,:578`). `coop_tunables.c:49-50,:72` writes the selected orientation to `/tmp/coop-audio-depth`; values <=0 remove the sentinel (`:26-35`). UI range is 0..400 ms in steps of 5, zero labelled built-in 40 (`src/app/ui/settings/panes/coop.pane.c:1616`). Coordinator reads the sentinel once: only integer 5..400 is accepted, otherwise default 40 ms (`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c:461-469`). This sets peer-ring prime depth; maximum depth is prime +80 ms (`:470-471`). Excess is trimmed back to prime, and an underrun re-arms priming (`:640-647,:674-678`). | Reconnect after changing the correct orientation, inspect the coordinator startup line's `Nms jitter buffer` (`coop_audio.c:481-482`), then observe actual `depth`, `under`, `trim` telemetry (`:783`) and audio/visual timing. The startup value proves the coordinator read the setting; it does not measure end-to-end latency. The buffer applies to the secondary/peer stream, which need not be PC2; passive-gate look-back/search history is separate. A live sentinel rewrite does not change an already-created coordinator. |


## 52. Co-op audio diagnostic fields (2026-10-08)

Owner: Eclipse, **SRC**. These rows define what the code counts; they do not
establish audible quality, source-game timing or end-to-end latency. References
use `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c` (mixer) and `coop_audio_pgate.c` (gate).
Mixer counters reset at coordinator start (`coop_audio.c:479`). Gate counters
start at zero on gate allocation (`coop_audio_pgate.c:297`), and the mixer can
recreate the gate on filter switches/reactivation (`coop_audio.c:185-220,716-724`),
so a gate-counter decrease is not automatically a new stream session.

| name | Meaning and primary source (SRC) | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `pgate`, `COOP-AUD.pgate` | The `pgate:` diagnostic is emitted only when `filter_active && gate_on && gate != NULL` (`coop_audio.c:790-803`); `filter_active` controls actual processing at `:714-737`. Printed every 1000 accepted mixer calls (`:629-632,:778`), not by a wall timer. | A fresh changing `pgate:` sequence proves this code path is executing; startup preference alone is insufficient. Row cadence cannot be converted to seconds unless actual frames per mixer call and clock continuity are independently known. No wall timestamp is included in this diagnostic. |
| `offsets`, `COOP-AUD.pgate.offsets(ms)` | Current `used` bank slots' `lag` samples are enumerated in slot order (`coop_audio_pgate.c:408-415`), then converted to milliseconds with the coordinator sample rate (`coop_audio.c:796-799`). At processing `lag > 0` selects peer as the later/slave stream, otherwise self (`coop_audio_pgate.c:801-802`). `none` means no returned active bank entries. | Compare `coop_pgate_get_stats().offset_samples` with the printed values, and paired known-offset input with admissions. These are retained candidate offsets, not the currently dominant sound, the hosts' network delay, or proof of gate closure. `fast_tracks` and expiry can alter them. |
| `balance`, `peer-vs-self`, `COOP-AUD.pgate.balance` | Printed `balance_db` is the learned peer/self level estimate, not an output attenuation: mid-band matches with share >=0.90 and positive fitted gain contribute signed `20*log10(gain)` (`coop_audio_pgate.c:845-853`). Estimate exported at `:416`; `(learning)` is printed unless `P_BALANCE && bal_n >=20` (`:417`, `coop_audio.c:801-802`). Applied balancing clamps the estimate to +/-12 dB and splits correction between both streams (`coop_audio_pgate.c:1212-1215`). | Observe contributing samples, `balance_applied` and measured per-stream output gain. A +15 dB printed estimate does not mean a +15 dB output boost; a missing learning suffix shows the application predicate, not an audible-quality result. |
| `pred_confirmed`, `pred_failed`, `COOP-AUD.pgate.predictions` | `predictions ok` / `failed` are gate-lifetime totals for completed pending offset predictions. A qualifying `any_match` while pending sets the confirmation flag (`coop_audio_pgate.c:995-1003`); when the pending countdown reaches zero it increments exactly one result counter (`:1007-1015`). | Exercise a qualifying prediction whose copy does/does not match before expiry and observe the corresponding counter. Counts are per completed offset prediction, not per audio block, onset, clipped word, or measured listening error. Pending predictions are absent from both totals. |
| `dropouts`, `COOP-AUD.pgate.dropouts` | Gate-lifetime count of stream/block detections: previous four-block mean band energy exceeds -60 dBFS and either current-block energy or final 2 ms energy is below -90 dBFS (`coop_audio_pgate.c:1118-1135`). Both streams are checked separately; detection increments regardless of whether dropout handling is enabled. | Supply a known audible-to-digital-silence transition and inspect increment plus block input samples. This is not the network-loss counter, not necessarily one count per physical outage, and does not establish why samples became silent. |
| `env-lines`, `fast-tracks`, `COOP-AUD.pgate.env-lines`, `COOP-AUD.pgate.fast-tracks` | Gate-lifetime branch counts. `env-lines` increments for each offset/channel/block with >=3 qualifying changing/matching band envelopes (`coop_audio_pgate.c:936-952`); `fast-tracks` increments for each successful local waveform re-search adjusting an offset (`:861-879`). | Inspect qualifying envelopes or the matched local search candidate and resulting offset. Neither is a count of spoken sentences nor proof of useful audible suppression. |
| `depth`, `COOP-AUD.depth` | Post-consumption peer ring occupancy printed as integer `ring_count()/48` (`coop_audio.c:706,786`). The literal 48 makes the `ms` label correct at 48000 Hz only. It is an instantaneous snapshot after draining incoming messages, any over-depth trim and this call's peer consumption (`:636-647,:674-706`). | Check known-rate PCM ring occupancy before/after consumption against the snapshot. It excludes secondary decode/IPC backlog not yet drained, primary/backend/device queues and gate latency, so it is not end-to-end secondary delay. Log-sample min/max do not bound all between-log queue states. |
| `under`, `COOP-AUD.under` | Coordinator-lifetime count of mixer calls where the peer ring was primed but available frames were less than the requested block; that branch unprimes the ring (`coop_audio.c:674-678`). Unprimed waiting calls do not increment it. | Induce a known insufficient primed block; observe increment, silence in the unavailable peer tail, and subsequent re-prime behavior. This is a shortage event count, not missing frames/milliseconds or all upstream packet losses. |
| `trim`, `COOP-AUD.trim` | Coordinator-lifetime count of over-depth corrections: if occupancy exceeds `ring_max_frames`, advance peer tail by occupancy minus `ring_prime_frames`, then increment once (`coop_audio.c:640-643`). Ring maximum is prime +80 ms (`:470-471`). | Observe pre-trim occupancy, tail advancement and increment on a known overshoot. Counter counts correction events, not discarded frames or duration; periodic depth is after trimming and consumption. |
| `msgs`, `COOP-AUD.msgs`, `bad`, `COOP-AUD.bad` | `msgs` counts accepted valid peer PCM messages after writing their frame payload to the ring (`coop_audio.c:620-624`); `bad` is `stat_dropped`, incremented for oversized/short/bad-magic/non-PCM-or-format-refused/size-inconsistent messages (`:575-620`), printed at `:786`. | Inspect accepted/rejected message headers and counter deltas. `msgs` is not samples, wall time, packet-arrival time, or audio content; payload frame counts vary and are not printed here. `bad=0` does not rule out network loss before decoded PCM reaches this channel, nor the uncounted oldest-frame eviction if the ring physically fills (`:528-539`). |

The generic `lag`, `rho`, `amount`, and `quieting` text on the separate mixer
status line is populated from **the legacy dedupe object**, even while the
passive gate is active (`coop_audio.c:779-789`). It must not be read as passive
gate matching strength or evidence that the gate has zero delay. Use the
`pgate:` row and its registered fields instead. No wall-clock conversion is
registered here: recorder UTC timestamps and these untimestamped rows have no
verified exact alignment. These read-only counters introduce no new §12
silent-setting case; the applicable depth-setting trap is already in §51/§12.

## 53. Shelved secondary-queue draft — historical definitions, WITHDRAWN (2026-10-08)

**NONE OF THESE ADDITIONS IS CURRENT.** The draft was shelved before build or
test, source/UI restored to the release baseline, and its helper/test removed.
The registered names are retained here and in WITHDRAWN to prevent accidental
reuse. The rows below describe the **abandoned draft only**; their source line
numbers refer to the saved draft, not the restored working tree. Every proposed
verification below is unexecuted. Archive:
`~/aurora-tv/scratchpad/audio-gate-review-20261008/shelved-tight-queue-draft/`
(`candidate.diff`, `coop_audio_queue.h`, `test_coop_audio_queue.c`, `STATUS.txt`).

The same draft proposed changing the existing default-depth constant from 40 to
5 ms and changing existing depth/trim/under semantics. Those changes were also
reverted: **the existing constant remains live at 40 ms**, and §§51–52 again
define the restored target +80 ms policy and literal-48 depth conversion. It is
the proposed value/behavior that was abandoned, not those existing names.

| name (WITHDRAWN) | Historical meaning and saved-draft source (SRC only) | Historical proposed verification — not performed |
|---|---|---|
| `COOP_AUDIO_HEADROOM_MS` | Eclipse compile constant `5` ms (`coop_audio.c:63`), converted by ceiling to frames at coordinator start (`:471`). Added to the per-callback floor to produce the trim threshold (`coop_audio_queue.h:23-26`). With default target and a 240-frame callback at 48 kHz, floor=240 and limit=480 frames (5/10 ms); a 960-frame callback instead gives floor=960 and limit=1200 frames (20/25 ms). These are source arithmetic, not measured delays. | Rebuild/load/reconnect; startup headroom=5 and printed frame-valued `limit` must agree with actual rate/block/target. A controlled queue excess must increment `trim` and `trimmed` by the predicted event/frame amounts. The threshold acts after drain and before consumption, not on all upstream queues or total A/V latency. |
| `target`, `COOP-AUD.secondary-queue.target`, `headroom`, `COOP-AUD.secondary-queue.headroom` | Millisecond values printed at coordinator start (`coop_audio.c:485-486`): accepted configured/default target and compile-time headroom. Runtime callback size may raise the actual floor above target. | Compare startup values with configured orientation and new binary identity, then `rate`/`block`/`limit`. Startup target=5 does not guarantee 5 ms callback sizes or an end-to-end delay of 5 ms. |
| `rate`, `COOP-AUD.rate` | Coordinator sample rate in frames/second (`coop_audio.c:473,:788,:795`), printed on the ordinary mixer status line. | Compare the accepted primary/peer PCM format with printed rate; use it to convert frame-valued `block`, `limit`, and `trimmed`. This is audio sample rate, not video/frame-delivery rate. |
| `block`, `COOP-AUD.block` | Frame count requested by the particular `coop_audio_mix()` call that emits the diagnostic (`coop_audio.c:632-635,:788,:795`). It participates in that call's queue floor. | Feed known callback sizes and observe the logged value and matching `limit`. It is not the passive gate's fixed processing block and does not describe all earlier callbacks in the interval. |
| `limit`, `COOP-AUD.limit` | **Frames**, not milliseconds: current call's pre-consumption queue trim threshold `max(target_frames, callback_frames)+headroom_frames` (`coop_audio_queue.h:23-25`, `coop_audio.c:795`). Strictly greater occupancy triggers discard; equality does not. | Compare the printed value with target/headroom/rate/block and instrument queue occupancy just at and above limit. Convert with `1000*limit/rate`; it does not bound unprocessed upstream backlog, temporary pre-trim occupancy, or end-to-end latency. |
| `depthhi`, `COOP-AUD.depthhi` | Highest **post-consumption** peer-ring occupancy among mixer calls since the preceding diagnostic (or coordinator start), converted to integer ms using actual rate (`coop_audio.c:707-708,:796`). Accumulator resets at start and immediately after each 1000-call diagnostic (`:481,:798`). | Insert a known queue excursion between diagnostic calls and verify this maximum while the later instantaneous `depth` may be lower. It does not include pre-trim/pre-consumption maxima or times the mixer was not called; unprimed calls still contribute their retained queue. |
| `trimmed`, `COOP-AUD.trimmed` | Coordinator-lifetime **frames** discarded by the queue policy, accumulated in a 64-bit total (`coop_audio.c:108,:480,:647,:797`). No diagnostic-interval reset. | A known policy discard must increase this total by the exact discarded frame count; compare deltas and convert to duration using rate. This excludes independent physical-ring overflow eviction in `ring_write()` (`:532-543`), upstream loss and missing frames during underruns. |

## 54. Opus negotiated frame duration and packet-loss concealment (2026-10-08)

**DOC + local SRC + bounded offline TEST; no live/runtime-cause claim.** Opus owns the
API contract; moonlight-common-c owns its negotiated configuration and missing
packet callback. Upstream references below are to the inspected owner checkout
`/mnt/c/Users/<user>/eclipse-src/core/moonlight-common-c/src/`, based on
`c86e0537d11566f9825d6323711ec61e3e702365`. Its local Limelight.h/SdpGenerator.c
changes concern input blocking and video intra refresh; the cited audio-duration
logic is unchanged. Eclipse references use `src/app/stream/audio/` in this tree.

| name | Owner / meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `opus_multistream_decode`, `opus_multistream_decode.frame_size` | **Opus / DOC.** [Official multistream API 1.5](https://opus-codec.org/docs/opus_api-1.5/group__opus__multistream.html): normally the frame-size argument gives available output capacity in samples per channel; storage must fit that count times channels. With NULL data (packet-loss concealment) or FEC enabled, the argument instead must specify the missing duration, in a multiple of 2.5 ms. A successful return gives decoded samples per channel; negative returns are errors. | On equally initialized decoders, submit identical valid packets, then a NULL/zero-length loss call with the negotiated missing-frame count versus buffer capacity. Measure returned frames and cumulative output frames relative to the missing interval, then the following valid-packet result. Do not infer which duration a live session used from buffer size or an API comment. The Linux libopus 1.6.1 regression below measures this contract; it does not attribute a recorded-session fault. |
| `samplesPerFrame`, `OPUS_MULTISTREAM_CONFIGURATION.samplesPerFrame` | **moonlight-common-c / DOC, local mapping SRC.** Owner `Limelight.h:332-337` defines the Opus configuration field; its duration capability contract (`:262-266`) requires clients to read it rather than assume 240. Owner `AudioStream.c:438-440` sets it to `48 * AudioPacketDuration` before calling renderer init. Eclipse primary stores it at `session_audio.c:201`; secondary at `coop_audio.c:904`. This row is specifically the Opus configuration field, not the separate SS4S audio-info field with the same label. | Record the actual init configuration, decoder call argument and decoded/forwarded frame counts. 240 samples/channel denotes 5 ms at 48 kHz, 480 denotes 10 ms; these are arithmetic examples, not observed negotiation for a particular session. Equal field names in separate structures do not prove equal values. |
| `AudioPacketDuration` | **moonlight-common-c / DOC.** Owner `SdpGenerator.c:536-559` chooses 5 or 10 ms according to its audio negotiation branches and writes the SDP packet-duration attribute where supported (`:554-555`); owner `AudioStream.c:438` converts it to negotiated samples per channel. It also appears in startup resync calculations (`AudioStream.c:248,:302,:305`). Exact identifier is AudioPacketDuration; no upstreamAudioPacketDuration variable was found or registered. | Observe chosen SDP duration and `samplesPerFrame` supplied at renderer init, then decode output counts; do not assume the same duration on every negotiated session. This is not secondary ring depth or passive-gate processing latency. |
| `frame_size`, `session_audio.frame_size` | **Eclipse / SRC.** Primary decoder state set from negotiated `opusConfig->samplesPerFrame` (`session_audio.c:201`), used to allocate its interleaved output buffer (`:202-203`) and passed to `opus_multistream_decode()` (`:285-286`) with FEC disabled. The identically spelled Opus API argument has the DOC contract above. | Instrument primary init value, NULL/valid-packet decode arguments and returned frames. Check output-buffer capacity and missing duration separately. Returned decode length drives mixer/backend submission (`:301-302`). |
| `sec_frame_size` | **Eclipse / SRC.** Secondary state declared at `coop_audio.c:817`, set from negotiated `samplesPerFrame` (`:904`), and checked against the 960-frame capacity (`:905-909`). **Installed release `06fe12b1`:** direct Opus call used capacity 960 for valid packets and NULL loss callbacks alike. **New local source:** `coop_audio.c:953-954` passes capacity and negotiated duration separately to `coop_audio_decode_opus()`; its NULL branch selects negotiated duration. Positive returned `decoded` becomes header frames and payload size (`:959-967`). | Capture init value, loss callback, actual API argument, returned frames and forwarded header/payload count. The local helper is covered by the Linux regression below; full-app/deployed runtime application and live queue/latency impact remain unmeasured. |
| `sampleData`, `sampleLength`, `AudioRendererDecodeAndPlaySample` | **moonlight-common-c / DOC.** Owner `Limelight.h:355-356` declares the Opus payload callback with byte length. `AudioStream.c:162-168` explicitly calls it with NULL and zero when the queued packet has zero size. Eclipse forwards those values to Opus in both primary `session_audio.c:285-286` and secondary helper call `coop_audio.c:953-954` (`coop_audio_opus.h:10-13`). | Observe the NULL/zero callback and associated negotiated frame count, then count decoded and forwarded samples. A generic packet-loss log alone does not establish the number of these callbacks or the duration synthesized by the downstream decoder. |
| `coop_audio_decode_opus`, `capacity_frames`, `packet_frames` | **Eclipse / SRC.** New internal helper `coop_audio_opus.h:10-13`: NULL input selects packet_frames; non-NULL input selects capacity_frames; FEC stays disabled. Secondary caller passes buffer capacity 960 and negotiated sec_frame_size separately (`coop_audio.c:953-954`). This helper does not alter ring depth or output scheduling. | Compare helper output and state with an independent direct Opus call using correct missing duration, across valid/lost/recovery sequences; separately ensure a non-NULL 960-frame packet still decodes into available capacity. Linux regression below covers these paths; installed-binary application is not established. |

**Bounded offline regression (2026-10-08):**
`tests/coop/test_coop_audio_opus.c` uses the production helper against real Linux
libopus 1.6.1, with 20 encoded stereo packets and three omitted packets (including
two consecutive losses) for each duration. The helper returns the reference frame
count and its PCM matches the direct correctly-sized decoder byte-for-byte,
including packets after loss. At 120/240/480/960 frames per packet, old-capacity
calls produce 2520/2160/1440/0 extra frames over the 20-packet sequence
(52.5/45/30/0 ms at 48 kHz). For 240-frame packets: expected/helper total 4800,
old total 6960. A non-NULL 960-frame packet also decodes fully when the negotiated
loss duration passed to the helper is 240. Evidence:
`~/aurora-tv/scratchpad/audio-gate-review-20261008/opus-linux-test.txt`.
These are isolated-library measurements, not Windows/TV deployment or proof of
October8 causality. The unchanged release queue policy remains §§51–52.

## 55. Requested backend block and client monotonic clock (2026-10-08)

| name | Owner / meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `SAMPLES_PER_FRAME`, `SS4S_AudioInfo.samplesPerFrame` | **Eclipse SRC + backend DOC.** Eclipse `session_audio.c:19` defines 240 and supplies it as SS4S audio-info samplesPerFrame (`:173`). This is separate from negotiated Opus samplesPerFrame (§54). Inspected SS4S owner checkout `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/eclipse-src/third_party/ss4s/`: `modules/sdl/sdl.c:36,:50` uses it for ring-size arithmetic and desired SDL sample frames, opens into `have` (`:56`), then sizes its read buffer from obtained samples (`:100-101`). SDL documents that the [requested audio-buffer size may be adjusted](https://wiki.libsdl.org/SDL2/SDL_OpenAudioDevice). | Record requested samples, obtained samples/rate, and actual callback byte lengths converted with channels/sample width. 240 at 48 kHz is a requested 5 ms block, not a measured callback interval, hardware buffer, speaker latency or negotiated Opus packet duration. Changing the constant cannot prove that the driver accepted it. |
| `clock_gettime`, `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` | **POSIX/Linux DOC + Windows wrapper SRC.** [Linux clock_gettime manual](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/clock_gettime.2.html): this clock is system-wide, not UTC, excludes suspension on Linux and can be frequency-adjusted. Windows project wrapper `src/app/platform/windows/coop_win_compat.h:450-458` derives timespec directly from QPC/QPF without subtracting process start. [Microsoft QPC documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/sysinfo/acquiring-high-resolution-time-stamps) supplies that timer's contract. | Compare timestamps only from the same client system/boot and clock namespace/implementation. Check cross-process timestamps using a bounded local message exchange before interpreting transport ages; do not compare to host game clocks, UTC recorder starts or process-relative SDL_GetTicks. Use signed/order checks before unsigned subtraction; report invalid/future timestamps rather than presenting them as zero-age evidence. |

## 56. Audio conservation, decode counters and IPC timing (2026-10-08)

**SRC; application tests pending.** Owner Eclipse: `coop_audio.c` coordinator
reset in coop_audio_coord_start, receive in coop_audio_drain, snapshot in timing_log,
mix in coop_audio_mix, secondary decoder in coop_secondary_aud_feed; primary `session_audio.c` decoder/log
functions. Frames are samples per channel. These are local sample accounting,
not host-event time, speaker presentation or audio/video delay. Most totals are
uint64; mix_frames reuses unsigned sess_frames, and queue occupancy is unsigned.
Use role/start/stop boundaries to separate resets; there is no unique session ID.
Coordinator emits each 1000 mixer calls and at stop; decoder emits at init/cleanup
and successful calls whose cumulative call count is divisible by 1000. Periodic
secondary emission precedes the current call's forwarding/rejection decision.

| name | Meaning and primary source (SRC) | How to prove application / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `COOP-AUD.timing` | Coordinator snapshot emitted by timing_log. New totals reset at coordinator start; mix_frames resets at coordinator stop. | Fresh log plus known input must satisfy received = consumed + trimmed + overflow + link_discarded + queued within one coordinator lifetime. No runtime proof yet. |
| `mix_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.mix_frames` | sess_frames: frames processed by active mixer, including silence-pump calls. | Reconcile primary decoder frames that actually reach mixing plus pump contribution; this is demand/processing, not device consumption. Unsigned counter can wrap. |
| `peer_received_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_received_frames` | Sum of validated PCM header frames inserted into ring, including PLC-tagged PCM. | Sum accepted header lengths; message count alone is insufficient. |
| `peer_consumed_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_consumed_frames` | Sum of take, actual peer-ring frames selected by mixer; excludes shortfall zero-fill. | Compare ring-tail movement and known PCM length; gating does not subtract from this count. |
| `peer_queued_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_queued_frames` | Current ring_count at snapshot. | Reconcile the conservation equation; instantaneous occupancy is not historical peak or speaker latency. |
| `peer_trimmed_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_trimmed_frames` | Exact excess discarded by existing target+80 ms queue policy. | Trigger a known excess and count discarded frames, not trim events. Default policy remains §§51–52. |
| `peer_overflow_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_overflow_frames` | Oldest frames discarded by ring_write when physical capacity is exhausted. | Fill beyond usable ring capacity; each overwritten old frame increments once, separately from policy trim. |
| `peer_link_discarded_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_link_discarded_frames` | Existing ring_count discarded when a replacement peer connection is accepted. | Reconnect with known residual occupancy; verify that count moves to this term before ring clear. |
| `peer_gap_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_gap_frames` | Positive difference between next received first_frame and previous packet end within a continuous accepted connection. Sequence tracking resets at peer accept. | Reject a known decoded packet after sequence begins; next accepted index exposes its frames. Does not count gaps before first packet/reconnect, failed decodes, overlapping/backwards indices, or loss already concealed upstream. Not part of ring conservation. |
| `peer_short_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_short_frames` | Sum of requested frames minus take, including unprimed/startup waiting. | Known peer shortage changes this count; it is not proof that game sound was lost. |
| `time_us`, `COOP-AUD.timing.time_us`, `COOP-AUD.decode.time_us`, `elapsed_us`, `COOP-AUD.timing.elapsed_us` | audio_time_us / primary equivalent uses CLOCK_MONOTONIC sec*1000000+nsec/1000 (§55). time_us is system-clock-origin microseconds; coordinator elapsed_us subtracts its start. | Check elapsed against start and same-clock cross-process exchange. Not UTC or source sample time. Clock call errors are not checked in current implementation. |
| `ipc_age_max_us`, `COOP-AUD.timing.ipc_age_max_us` | Lifetime maximum of validated packet receipt/drain time minus ready_us. Only nonzero, nonfuture ready_us contributes. | Delay a known local transfer. Includes post-decode scheduling/framing/IPC; missing/future timestamps contribute to invalid_timestamp_frames and are excluded from both age maxima. |
| `queue_age_max_us`, `COOP-AUD.timing.queue_age_max_us` | Lifetime maximum sampled at mixer consumption from ready_us of the oldest selected frame. Includes IPC age, not just residence in ring. | Delay known PCM; compare sampled oldest-frame age. Only one selected frame per call is sampled, so this is not an exhaustive maximum over all consumed frames if timestamp order changes. Zero/future values are excluded. Does not bound unconsumed or downstream audio. |
| `invalid_timestamp_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.invalid_timestamp_frames` | Accepted PCM frames whose ready_us is zero or later than coordinator receipt time (`coop_audio.c:665-667`); counter resets at coordinator start. Their stored timestamp becomes zero, excluding both age metrics. | Deliberately missing/future timestamps must increment by payload frames. Age claims require zero invalid count plus a complete sample ledger; zero alone cannot prove that an otherwise plausible clock is correct. |
| `peer_plc_frames`, `COOP-AUD.timing.peer_plc_frames` | Accepted PCM frames whose header reserved field has bit mask 1u set. Subset of received. | One tagged payload increments received and PLC totals by its frame count. |
| `COOP-AUD.decode`, `role`, `COOP-AUD.decode.role` | Independent primary/secondary decoder counters; role=pump is a separate stop-only contribution row. | Match actual process roles and lifecycle, not host names; pump row does not carry the ordinary decoder schema. |
| `COOP-AUD.decode.packet_frames` | Negotiated frame_size / sec_frame_size from Opus config (§54). | Compare NULL decode request and returned samples; distinct from buffer capacity and SDL callback size. |
| `packets`, `COOP-AUD.decode.packets` | ALL decode attempts after decoder-present guard, including NULL PLC and failed decode calls. | Known valid/malformed/NULL sequence increments once each. Not non-NULL packet count or UDP reception count. |
| `plc_calls`, `COOP-AUD.decode.plc_calls`, `plc_frames`, `COOP-AUD.decode.plc_frames` | NULL decode attempts, and sum of positive return frames from those attempts, respectively. | Inject known NULL calls; errors increment calls but add no frames. |
| `decoded_frames`, `COOP-AUD.decode.decoded_frames` | Sum of positive decoder returns including PLC; secondary counts these before IPC connection/backoff/send. | Sum actual returns, not packets times buffer capacity. Bare decoded_frames also names a video-analysis output in §20; use qualified audio context. |
| `rejected_frames`, `COOP-AUD.decode.rejected_frames` | **Secondary only:** historical baseline counted decoded frames abandoned by retry/connect/send failure. Current retained-transport candidate counts failed producer FIFO push; disconnect itself retains accepted data. | Fill sender FIFO and check later/final snapshot; periodic row precedes current push outcome. Positive decode is required. Distinguish rejected producer frames from transport attempts/ACKs (§71). |
| `pump_pcm_frames`, `COOP-AUD.decode.pump_pcm_frames`, `pump_frames_fed` | session_audio.c pump_stop logs pump_pcm_frames as uint64(pump_frames_fed)*frame_size after thread join. Existing pump_frames_fed increments once per pump block; old “frames mixed” text actually counts blocks. | Count known pump blocks and their negotiated frame_size. New PCM count is stop-only, inherits possible unsigned block-counter wrap, and is not primary decoded audio. |
| `ready_us`, `coop_audio_hdr_t.ready_us` | uint64 monotonic microseconds sampled immediately after successful secondary decode, carried through ring_write to per-frame metadata. CONFIG initializes zero. | Compare sender-ready and receiver receipt in the common local clock domain (§55). Excludes earlier host/network/decode execution. |
| `first_frame`, `coop_audio_hdr_t.first_frame` | PCM: uint64 index of first frame in cumulative successful secondary decode output, including PLC. ACK: exclusive coordinator-accepted end. Index resets on decoder init, now scoped by epoch (§71). | Known positive returns give contiguous indices unless input was omitted. Failed decode calls produce no indexed frames; do not compare indices across epochs or interpret ACK as playback. |
| `coop_audio_hdr_t.reserved` | PCM flag bit **mask 1u (bit index 0)** denotes NULL-input PLC; other bits unused. CONFIG zero. | Compare deliberate NULL/non-NULL callbacks with received PLC count. This flag is not inferred from waveform silence. |
| `coop_audio_hdr_t` | Local native IPC struct now includes ready_us, first_frame and epoch uint64 fields; COOP_AUDIO_MAGIC is CA4S. Historical telemetry baseline used CA3S without epoch/ACK. | Both processes must load matching protocol/layout; check CONFIG/PCM/ACK acceptance (§71). Version rejects earlier magic, not differing native ABI layouts using the same magic. |

No receiver peer_rejected_frames or ambiguous self_frames field was implemented.
They were transient design proposals and are not registered runtime counters.

## 57. Offline passive-gate simulator controls and final-decision trace (2026-10-08)

Owner Eclipse, **SRC only; tests pending**. Simulator references are
`tests/coop/sim_coop_audio_pgate.c`. These controls exercise offline analysis,
not host encoders or installed-client settings. Source comments about speech
realism remain hypotheses, not evidence that synthetic sounds represent all games.

| name | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `TRUTH_CLASS` | Simulator environment string selects gen_pair at `:384-432`: voice, s, sh, f, h, breath, whisper, speech_s, plosive, nasal, stretch, positional, clipped; unknown values exit2. Default voice in truth, whisper in truth-unseen (`:490`). **Legacy truth overrides the first scheduled occurrence of every offset to voice regardless of this variable** (`:491`). Noise classes use separately seeded noise and shared generated envelopes (`:388-399,:433-449`); master normalization occurs at `:495-496`. | Enable TRUTH_LIST and inspect each scheduled class. A whisper environment value in legacy truth does not make its first-offset trial whisper or untrained. Inspect generated components before claiming realism. |
| `TRUTH_PEER_DB` | Simulator-only environment dB value parsed with atof in tests/coop/sim_coop_audio_pgate.c:471; absent defaults0. lv_peer=10^(value/20). Applied at:499-501 only to generated shared voice assigned to source1, whether it is earlier master or later copy; independently generated bg[1] is unchanged. Both truth and truth-unseen use this. | Source summary prints20log10(lv_peer), but confirm generated shared component ratio and unchanged unique background before claiming application. This is shared-material gain imbalance, **not** a whole-host volume change. Final bg+voice int16 clipping can alter waveform/ratio. No numeric range/finite validation or runtime-client effect. |
| `TRUTH_LIST` | Presence of this environment variable prints per-scheduled-event SENT lines to stderr (`:505`), including later-copy interval in input seconds, offset ms, slave role, first-scheduled label and actual class. Even value 0 is present/ON. | Count emitted events and compare stated interval/class/offset with constructed arrays. This is scheduler truth, not evidence the gate admitted that offset. |
| `TRUTH_STRETCH` | Percent used only by stretch class: source interpolation factor `1 + atof(value)/100`, default2 (`:413-415`). No input validation/range guarantee; generated copy still occupies the same allocated duration. | Inspect generated source-to-copy sample mapping; do not infer admissible negative/arbitrary values or a longer output allocation. No shipping-gate tuning. |
| `truth-unseen` | New CLI mode selected at `:456,:584`; schedules each of seven configured offsets at most once in order (`:475-485`), defaults to whisper and omits legacy first-occurrence voice substitution (`:490-491`). Short requested duration may stop before seven (`:486`). | TRUTH_LIST should show unique scheduled offsets and requested/default classes. Background audio still runs and may incidentally teach offsets; the name does not establish an empty bank when an event arrives. No truth-untrained selector exists. |
| `real-trace` | New CLI selector `real-trace SELF PEER SECTIONS OUT CSV FROM TO` (`:164-166,:582-585`). Normal whole-file gate processing is retained; only selected hook rows are written (`:197-199`). | Compare ordinary real and real-trace output for the same full inputs; expected audio/stat equivalence, with additional CSV. This is an offline instrument, not a live acceptance run. |
| `real-trace.CSV`, `real-trace.FROM`, `real-trace.TO` | CSV path is opened with w (`:187`), replacing an existing file. FROM/TO are finite input seconds satisfying 0<=FROM<TO<=shorter input duration; converted to frame positions at fixed simulator48kHz (`:183-191`). Hook writes blocks whose start lies in the half-open interval (`:110`). | CSV frame column must fall within requested boundaries; inspect first/last row, not wall-clock creation time. Filtering trace output does not reset gate history at FROM. No SIM_GATE_TRACE environment knob was added. |
| `real-trace.frame`, `real-trace.frames`, `real-trace.offset_samples`, `real-trace.g0`, `real-trace.g1`, `real-trace.why` | Hook CSV columns (`:110-118,:192-194`): input block start/count; semicolon-separated retained offsets in samples; final per-stream/channel/band start/end gains and decision reason. Indexed columns are s0/s1 self/peer, c0/c1 L/R, b0..5. Hook occurs after final decisions but before balance output update (`coop_audio_pgate.c:1206-1215`); gains exclude balance.
