## 58. SDL output-ring accounting and aligned mixer capture (2026-10-08)

**SRC; application tests pending.** Backend owner is SS4S: inspected local
`third_party/ss4s/modules/sdl/sdl.c:27-44,:138-164,:171-202`, persisted through
`patches/client-deps/ss4s.patch`. These are backend ring/callback observations,
not device presentation timestamps. ReportTiming copies counters and occupancy
under the ring mutex, then formats/logs outside it and outside the device callback.
It runs at open, each 1000 Feed calls and after the device closes. A snapshot may
fall between chunks of a callback; callback totals and reads need not describe
an entirely finished callback at that instant.

| name | Meaning and primary source (SRC) | How to prove application / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `AUDIO-OUTPUT` | Per-open SDL backend accounting record. Totals start zero in allocated instance. | Obtain a fresh record from the actual selected backend and reconcile submitted = consumed + cleared + queued. Other audio backends do not emit this schema. |
| `AUDIO-OUTPUT.elapsed_us` | Microseconds since opened_counter, using SDL_GetPerformanceCounter/Frequency; sampled after the counter snapshot unlocks. | Compare same-instance elapsed progression. Relative clock, no common absolute time_us field; not exactly simultaneous with ring snapshot. |
| `AUDIO-OUTPUT.rate` | Obtained have.freq after successful SDL open. | Compare actual record to desired rate; code rejects mismatching rate/format/channels. Not evidence of speaker presentation timing. Use qualified name, not withdrawn COOP-AUD.rate. |
| `frame_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.frame_bytes` | sizeof(short)*obtained channels: bytes per interleaved PCM frame. | Divide byte counts by this stride before comparing audio frame totals. |
| `callback_frames`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.callback_frames` | Initial have.samples from SDL open; current clock candidate overwrites at each callback with actual len/frame_bytes (`modules/sdl/sdl.c:183`). Historical Oct9 baseline only emitted obtained size. | Compare requested, initial obtained and latest actual callback values using identified source/build. A single latest value does not establish cadence or every callback length. |
| `callbacks`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.callbacks` | Number of completed callback fill loops, incremented under mutex at callback end. | Known callback invocations change it once; idle callbacks count too. |
| `submitted_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.submitted_bytes` | Sum of successful ringbuf_write return sizes. Ring writer is all-or-zero (`modules/utils/ringbuf/ringbuf.c:25-46`). Current clock candidate no longer clears/retries on overflow; historical Oct9 baseline did. | Feed known byte lengths and compare accepted count. Current SDL Feed returns OVERFLOW for rejected writes and adds zero, preserving accepted PCM. No explicit rejected-byte counter; caller must retain/retry or account for rejection. |
| `consumed_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.consumed_bytes` | Sum of ring reads copied into SDL callback stream. | Known input removed from ring increments it; not hardware playback or acoustic output. |
| `silence_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.silence_bytes` | Zero-filled callback remainder len-done, including startup/idle shortages. | Known underfilled callback increments by remainder. Decoded PCM containing zero samples does not increment it; nonzero count alone is not a game-audio dropout verdict. |
| `cleared_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.cleared_bytes` | Historical Oct9 baseline counted ring occupancy discarded on failed-write overflow before retry. Current clock candidate removes this clear path, leaving zero during open lifetime. | Force rejected write and confirm queue preserved, cleared count unchanged and OVERFLOW returned. Does not include unread occupancy freed at Close; final pre-close ledger is required. |
| `queued_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.queued_bytes` | Current ring_size copied under mutex. | Same-instance conservation check; dividing by stride/rate gives ring audio duration, not complete output latency. |
| `peak_queued_bytes`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.peak_queued_bytes` | Lifetime maximum sampled after Feed writes, under mutex (historical baseline also retried after clear). | Known burst changes maximum; does not observe buffering beyond this ring. |
| `/tmp/coop-audio-dump`, `coop-audio-self.wav`, `coop-audio-peer.wav`, `coop-audio-output.wav` | Eclipse coop_audio.c dump_start `:318-335`: sentinel read at coordinator start, integer seconds 5..600 else default120. Opens/truncates three PCM16 WAVs beside AURORA_DIAG_LOG. Input blocks captured before gate/gain by dump_write; output after filter/mix/limiter by dump_write_output, before backend. Output writer advances shared frame budget and closes all three. | Restart/reconnect after sentinel change; validate all three actual payload lengths, headers, rate/channels and equal frame counts, plus matching known block alignment. fwrite/fseek errors are unchecked: matching header claims alone cannot prove complete files. Output is processed mixer PCM, not device/loopback capture or A/V proof. Existing historical captures had only the two input files. |

## 59. Co-op launch snapshot and host readiness fields (2026-10-09)

These names describe state, not new configurable knobs. **SRC** applies to
Eclipse objects and selector; **DOC** applies to inspected Umbra-owned source.
Host source inspected at `/mnt/c/Users/<user>/umbra-release`, Git HEAD
`76a730a86673287b59cb38e8d51aa7b03e54471f`; nvhttp.cpp SHA256
`23ee82ff7281a53eb50deecabfdfed1c30582437f530860b1125621c14d87ec9`.
Source inspection does not establish which build a running host has loaded.

| name | Meaning and primary owner/source | Application observable / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_session`, `coop_session_t`, `coop_session_set`, `coop_session_get` | **SRC, Eclipse in-memory object/API, not an INI key or sentinel.** `src/app/stream/video/coop_session.h:41-72` holds pair UUIDs, host-local app IDs, orientation and resolved codec/HDR state. `coop_session.c:54-70` copies into g_session, sets valid, publishes codec/orientation environment; get returns NULL unless valid. Dialog populates it and calls set (`coop.dialog.c:2144-2168`). Script preparation also builds this object from peer sentinel plus settings (`coop_session.c:115-153`), unless one already exists. | Verify the actual launch path, resolved pair/codec/orientation and resulting stream; writing a file named coop_session does nothing. `docs/conducting-live-tests.md:61-64` saying the UI “writes coop_session” means populating this snapshot, not writing a setting. The script path also ultimately fills the object; UI/script launch differences still need separate coverage. |
| `/tmp/coop-audio-gate` | **SRC, Eclipse runtime filter selector.** `coop_audio.c:171-224` checks every 200 refresh calls: exact token off or 0 selects legacy single-offset dedupe, any other nonempty token selects passive gate. Absent file restores passive default; empty/unreadable file preserves selection (non-ENOENT error). Selection change logs and recreates newly selected filter. | Check selector-change log and active filter/status, not mere file presence. off does **not** disable duplicate suppression. Actual filtering additionally needs dedupe enabled, MIX mode, neither pane local-host, and available filter (`coop_audio_mix`, around `:790-817`). Gate creation failure can fall back to dedupe. Read cadence is call-based, not guaranteed one wall second. |
| `currentgame`, `serverinfo.currentgame` | **DOC, Umbra** `src/nvhttp.cpp:1064-1072,:1101-1104`: HTTPS reports proc.running() app ID, except input-only mode may hide it as zero; HTTP always reports zero. `src/process.cpp:630-673` running() returns retained app ID for placebo/detached launch or running app/process group; it does not query connected stream count. | Fetch through the paired authenticated HTTPS path and retain transport/build provenance. Positive ID identifies host-local application state, not connected client identity. Zero from HTTP is a privacy placeholder, not proof of availability. Verify live ownership/active-session evidence before treating a retained app as safe to resume. |
| `state`, `serverinfo.state`, `SUNSHINE_SERVER_BUSY`, `SUNSHINE_SERVER_FREE` | **DOC, Umbra** same serverinfo owner: HTTPS chooses BUSY iff reported current_appid>0; HTTP hardcodes FREE. This schema is distinct from all other uses of state. Input-only hiding can also produce FREE with an app present. | BUSY does not establish active streaming, paused-token identity or a user's absence/presence. FREE alone does not establish safe takeover. Correlate authenticated state with current app, existing client/session evidence and current host logs; permission remains independent of this field. |
| `rtsp_stream::session_count` | **DOC, Umbra** `src/rtsp.cpp:543-546,:670-675`: synchronized session-slot container size, separate from proc.running(). `src/nvhttp.cpp:1582-1607` resume separately requires running app and checks this count for no-active-session behavior. No serverinfo field emits this count. | Source distinguishes retained app from registered stream sessions. For runtime readiness use actual current-session/log evidence with start/end boundaries; do not substitute serverinfo BUSY/FREE or infer count directly from one disconnect line. No new read-only API has been added here. |

Client normalization is another layer: Eclipse's libgamestream
`core/libgamestream/src/client.c:892-894,:1002-1007` reads currentgame and forces
its parsed currentGame to zero when state lacks _SERVER_BUSY. Keep raw HTTPS
response separate from this normalized client value. Stock Sunshine upstream
[serverinfo source](https://github.com/LizardByte/Sunshine/blob/master/src/nvhttp.cpp)
uses application state too, but the inspected Umbra fork's HTTP privacy behavior
is the owner contract relevant here; do not substitute upstream master for the
installed fork.

## 60. Windows capture guard and saved-counter validator (2026-10-09)

**SRC plus bounded mock/parser checks; live application not yet established.**
These are local script controls, not client/host settings. The Windows guard
uses the Windows profile/runtime and PowerShell process APIs; the TV SSH guard
cannot own this Windows process or restore its runtime sentinel. The conductor
still owns host readiness, stream launch, listening and live admissibility.

| name | Owner / meaning | Application observable / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `audio_capture_guard`, `audio_capture_guard.py` | `scripts/windows/smoke/audio_capture_guard.py`: launches candidate UI, temporarily writes the existing capture sentinel as 600 seconds, then closes owned client and preserves evidence in finally. Does not itself launch host streams. | READY confirms UI-launch path only; verify actual owned process, binary provenance, sentinel contents and fresh captured files. Revised source rejects duplicate/conflicting quitappafter, rechecks before launch, persists candidate hash/profile provenance and kills its returned process if ownership persistence fails. No host-preservation runtime proof yet; keep the profile unchanged during the controlled run. |
| `audio_capture_guard.--exe`, `audio_capture_guard.exe` | Required Windows executable path, existing file on mounted C:. Source requires basename moonlight-tv.exe, matching its process filters. | Verify intended binary SHA256/path and no running client; renamed binaries are rejected. Close verifies PID/path/start-time identity and bounded process exit; a changed PID is refused, not killed. |
| `audio_capture_guard.--out`, `audio_capture_guard.out` | Required fresh run directory on mounted C:, receives preflight/launch/close scripts, PID ownership record, pre-run backups, fresh evidence and markers. | Directory must not preexist; inspect ownership record and complete preservation output. This directory is not the profile used by the client. |
| `audio_capture_guard.--seconds`, `audio_capture_guard.seconds` | Guard lifetime, default900, accepted60..1800; monotonic deadline starts after launch. Separate from fixed600-second capture budget armed at coordinator start. | Observe deadline/STOP-triggered teardown; early exit may yield a shorter capture. No implication of 600 seconds of actual live input. |
| `audio_capture_guard.STOP`, `audio_capture_guard.READY`, `audio_capture_guard.FINISHED` | Files in output directory: STOP requests teardown; READY follows UI launch; FINISHED means evidence-preservation block reached its final write. | FINISHED alone is not successful close or accepted evidence; inspect guard exit/close status and owned-process absence. Startup/cleanup exceptions can prevent markers. |
| `check_audio_timing`, `check_audio_timing.py`, `check_audio_timing.log` | `scripts/coop/check_audio_timing.py` accepts one positional saved primary log slice; checks coordinator and SDL ring conservation, invalid timestamps, positive live-sample evidence, mixer interval advancement, output format stability and output clock/counter monotonicity, emitting JSON and nonzero on detected errors. | Use a fresh single-session slice with complete schema. Independent fixtures now reject zero-only records, output resets, bad ledgers, missing output and invalid timestamps, while a balanced advancing fixture passes. The revised constant coordinator-origin check (time_us minus elapsed_us) also rejects the independently reproduced mixed-lifetime fixture. These checks establish only the stated ledger/lifetime invariants. Passing conservation never proves correct audio, low acoustic latency or preservation of unique sounds. |

Capture mtime is only a freshness hint. Validate actual WAV payload/header lengths
and equal frame counts for all three files (§58); matching mtimes/headers alone
cannot establish complete audio. Revised guard hashes the old log prefix before applying offsets; mismatch captures
the whole current file and records unverified append provenance. The conductor
must then isolate a demonstrably fresh single lifetime. These are evidence
conditions, not new runtime parameters.

Bounded check on 2026-10-09: guard main ran with psfile mocked and disposable
profile/runtime/nonexecutable fixture paths; no candidate or live sentinel was
used. Actual Windows PowerShell Parser::ParseFile accepted generated preflight,
launch and close scripts without executing them. This proves syntax and the
mocked normal sentinel-restoration path, not Windows process teardown behavior.

## 61. Staged Windows window/loopback recorder controls (2026-10-09)

master inspection; successful mux does not establish first-view gate quality.

## 62. Passive-gate sampled matching and bass admission constants (2026-10-09)

Owner Eclipse, **SRC only**, `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c`.
Registered for quantitative cold-admission diagnosis; no values changed.
Matching share is squared uncentered positive correlation; negative/zero
cross-product is returned as zero, not a high anticorrelation match.

| name | Meaning and primary source | Application observable / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `PG_STRIDE` | Per-band sample steps {8,8,4,2,1,1} (`:25`), used by slave_energy and share_x (`:434,:449`). For window w, n=w/stride sampled positions are accumulated. Samples, not milliseconds or decoder packet sizes. | A trace at known lag/window must agree with independently summing those exact selected band samples. Full-rate waveform correlation is not this score; changing stride requires rebuild and changes score sampling, not window duration by itself. |
| `PG_FLOOR_MS2` | Mean-square reference `32768²*1e-7` (`:26`). share_x returns−1 when slave sampled energy is below floor*n (`:476`); loud precheck uses half-floor times window length (`:788`), prediction rise uses floor*block*10 (`:1056`), landing comparison adds floor*block (`:1160`). These are distinct predicates sharing one constant. | Exercise each cited boundary and observe its actual branch/result. A share−1 means too-quiet slave window, not failed positive correlation. The nominal−70dBFS reference does not establish input audibility or a universal closure threshold. |
| `PG_BASS_ALONE` | 0.90 squared-share threshold (`:35`). Bass-only admission fallback tests a40ms mono bass window around coarse candidate±decimation step when mid-band admission evidence is insufficient (`:735-745`). Separately, existing-offset per-channel bass score reaching this threshold increments consecutive bass_run (`:983-987`). | Trace true-lag bass score, admitted bank entry and bass_run separately. Admission does not itself close a gate; an existing-offset run and closure/witness logic are different stages. No first-view success follows from the constant alone. |
| `PG_BASS_RUN` | Three consecutive qualifying bass evaluations (`:36,:983-990`) permit bass without a higher-band witness when bass-alone option is enabled; score below threshold resets run. | Observe run0→1→2→3 and bass eligibility at an existing offset. Three qualifying5ms blocks cover15ms of block content (their first/third decision instants are10ms apart), not a measured acquisition or audible response delay; other history/admission/closure conditions still apply. |

## 63. External offline gate-admission trace schema (2026-10-09)

**SRC, offline diagnostic only; not production telemetry or controls.** Owner
`~/project-backups/audio-gate-review-20261008/search-2x2/gate-trace-5-1024.c`,
SHA256 `dccd678c99d03e520bc78816458d7022cad9a7824602d47714d2c38fbc8aa2ca`.
The names below label positional CSV columns; source prints tag plus values,
not header field names. Capture/source/config provenance remains necessary.
All three shapes are restricted to inclusive sample positions93600..148800,
i.e.1.95..3.10s **for the48kHz offline inputs**. Two oracle lags are fixed at
13056 and43200 samples; they are not supplied to production admission.

| name | Meaning and emission source | Application observable / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `gate-trace.ORACLE`, `gate-trace.ORACLE.phase`, `gate-trace.ORACLE.end`, `gate-trace.ORACLE.lag_samples` | diag_oracle `:494-507` emits ORACLE,phase,end,lag,score2,score3. phase search is before search FFT/candidate evaluation (`:641`); phase block is before process_block (`:1349`, only non-PGATE_TIMING branch). end is absolute exclusive input sample count (g->pos or g->next_block), not wall time. Positive lag means later stream1 against earlier stream0. | Inspect emitted sequence and caller/compile mode. Absence of block rows under timing instrumentation is not no gate processing; range restricts coverage. Fixed lag is diagnostic prior knowledge only. |
| `gate-trace.ORACLE.band2share20ms`, `gate-trace.ORACLE.band3share20ms` | Temporarily sets each band's matching window to rate/1000*20, calls share(g,band,1,−1,end,lag,NULL), then restores it. Bands2/3 are mono scores at fixed lag using existing sampled matching (§62). | Independently recompute same band/stride/window score. share−1 means below floor; other values use positive squared uncentered correlation. Score is observable match evidence, not a selected candidate, admitted offset or closed gate. Window restoration prevents these diagnostic scores altering later matching state. |
| `gate-trace.CORR`, `gate-trace.CORR.end`, `gate-trace.CORR.lag_samples`, `gate-trace.CORR.best_index_near_oracle` | `:691-703`, after inverse FFT and only later=1 with search-energy guard passed: center=ld−lag/PG_DEC; best_index is highest corr within center±1. end=g->pos; lag is fixed oracle lag. | Compare actual array indices; best_index is decimated correlation-array index, not full-rate lag. Scope is only emitted search/direction/window, not all candidates or all elapsed audio. |
| `gate-trace.CORR.raw_rank`, `gate-trace.CORR.PHATvalue` | Rank is1 plus count of all corr[0..ld] values strictly greater than best-near-oracle value. PHATvalue is that signed, unnormalized inverse-FFT correlation value. | Recompute strict greater-than count. This is **not** rank among separated local peaks; nearby samples of one peak count individually. PHATvalue is not Pearson correlation, squared share or an admission threshold. |
| `gate-trace.CANDIDATE`, `gate-trace.CANDIDATE.end`, `gate-trace.CANDIDATE.peakordinal`, `gate-trace.CANDIDATE.coarse_lag`, `gate-trace.CANDIDATE.refined_lag`, `gate-trace.CANDIDATE.refined_share` | `:779-780` immediately before admission predicate. end=g->pos; ordinal is0-based candidate iteration0..2; coarse=(ld−selected index)*PG_DEC in full-rate samples; refined is current blag; share is bsh after mid-band refinement and any bass fallback. Only later=1/range-limited. | Distinguish coarse discovery, refined score and bank mutation. The highest refined score may come from bass fallback rather than the oracle's bands2/3. Diagnostic ordinal is not global raw-rank. |
| `gate-trace.CANDIDATE.admit_request` | Boolean bsh>=PG_ADMIT printed before possible bank_admit call (`:780-782`). | It establishes that the call is requested, **not** a newly accepted bank entry. Deduplication/merging/recentering decisions require bank state/counters separately. |

These definitions close a source-schema gap only. They do not validate the
external experiment's conclusions, runtime CPU cost, or a proposed gate fix.

## 65. Optional SS4S output-clock contract (2026-10-09)

**SRC implementation; tests and installed-backend application pending.**
Owner is the local SS4S extension in `third_party/ss4s/include/ss4s/audio.h:49-66,:84`,
`src/audio.c:26-67`, and `modules/sdl/sdl.c:157-206`. These rows describe
the candidate source, not a claim that an installed backend supplies it. Only frame-count fields use interleaved sample frames;
sample_rate isHz, observed_us is microseconds, generation is an identity counter.

| name | Source meaning / owner | Application observable and limit |
|---|---|---|
| `SS4S_AudioDriver`, `SS4S_ModuleOpen` | SS4S owner layout `modules/interface/include/ss4s/modapi.h:49-75` ends AudioDriver at Close; Module and LibraryContext have no size/version extension guard. Internal loader `src/module_win32.c:36-65` / module_libdl.c:8-38 now resolves the optional clock export on the same successful module handle; existing module-open ABI is unchanged. | Verify old/new module compatibility without reading appended driver/Module fields. New optional extension must resolve on the same successful module handle, not re-open a display-name guess. These names are APIs/layouts, not config knobs. |
| `SS4S_AudioHasClock` | Core bool capability query (`third_party/ss4s/src/audio.c:26-28`, audio.h:82) returns whether selected module supplied optional clock function. Requires selected initialized module and caller serialization against module selection/quit; no audio instance query/open is performed. | Test old/unsupported vs clock-export module. True advertises API capability only: output open can fail, format can be unsupported, and instance GetClock must still succeed with valid rate/quantum/epoch. It does not prove hardware timing or target qualification. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock`, `SS4S_PlayerAudioGetClock` | Typed snapshot and bool query. `src/audio.c:54-67` holds player mutex through module query, overwrites generation on success, zeroes non-NULL output on false. Unsupported/closed audio returns false; caller keeps player alive. Describes backend consumption boundary, not speaker presentation. | Test known callback demand/PCM, unsupported module, closed instance and repeated open. Implementation must be inspected and loaded identity verified before runtime claims. Stop/join external worker before player destruction. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.sample_rate` | uint32 negotiated output sample rate inHz. | Compare successful open/backend record; reject zero/format change rather than dividing by zero. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.quantum_frames` | uint32 latest actual callback demand length in interleaved sample frames; obtained quantum before first callback. | Drive a callback length different from requested/obtained initial size and inspect next completed snapshot. Not a hardware-latency bound. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.demand_frames` | uint64 cumulative completed output demand, including positions filled with underrun silence. | Underflow must still advance demand; compare sum of actual callback lengths divided by frame stride. Do not clock output from real-PCM consumption alone. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.consumed_frames` | uint64 accepted PCM frames removed for output. | Known queued input advances this count; synthesized underrun silence does not. Does not establish acoustic presentation. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.queued_frames` | uint64 accepted frames still held by backend, including feeds accepted since latest callback. | Same-generation accepted = consumed + queued + discarded. Test feed-between-callback snapshot; counters must not expose partially completed callback accounting. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.discarded_frames` | uint64 accepted frames removed without output. | Exercise any implemented clear/discard path and reconcile; rejected input never accepted must not be added here. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.observed_us` | uint64 timestamp of most recently completed callback, or open before first callback, in backend monotonic epoch. | Unchanged during feed-only updates; advances after completed callback. Compare same-backend deltas only, not host UTC, coordinator timestamps or hardware position. |
| `SS4S_AudioClock.generation` | uint64 per-player successful audio-open epoch. | Successful reopen changes generation, failed open does not establish a new valid clock. Compare counters only within same player/generation; reset must not be treated as negative rate/drift. |

| `SS4S_ModuleAudioGetClockV1Function`, `SS4S_ModuleAudioGetClockV1_SDL`, `SS4S_ModuleAudioGetClockV1.clock_size` | Optional standalone export ABI declared in modapi.h:43-47: bool(instance, clock, size_t clock_size). SDL `sdl.c:189-206` rejects NULL or size != sizeof(SS4S_AudioClock) before access; one ring mutex snapshots completed callback counters plus current queue. Core supplies generation. | Test wrong-size output unchanged, NULL, supported SDL, and old/unsupported modules. No appended driver fields; absence returns unsupported, not an invented timer clock. Direct module query does not provide core generation. |
| `SS4S_AUDIO_FEED_OK`, `SS4S_AUDIO_FEED_NOT_READY`, `SS4S_AUDIO_FEED_OVERFLOW`, `SS4S_AUDIO_FEED_ERROR` | Existing SS4S enum audio.h:27-32 values0,1,2,−1. Core returns NOT_READY for closed audio. Candidate SDL Feed: zero size OK; NULL nonempty or unaligned bytes ERROR; size>=ring capacity or insufficient remaining space OVERFLOW; successful whole write OK. Definitions alone do not impose SDL behavior on another module. | Independently fill known ring, reject excess, verify existing indexed PCM and accepted ledger unchanged, then drain/retry exactly once. OVERFLOW is backpressure, not acceptance; retain the pending block. Other backends require their own source/behavior qualification. |

The candidate SDL callback publishes demand, consumption, silence, quantum and
observation timestamp under one ring mutex, with no partially completed callback
visible to query. This is the callback handoff boundary, not speaker delivery.
NDL and Pulse currently expose no optional export: query false is expected.
A new app still requires the new core SS4S library even though old modules remain
loadable. Stop/join the audio worker before close: legacy Feed itself releases
the player mutex before calling the module and cannot provide that lifetime guard.

## 66. Passive-gate complete-block API and epoch boundary (2026-10-09)

**SRC candidate; independent contract tests and live application pending.**
Owner `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.h:61-79`, implementation
`coop_audio_pgate.c:404-419,:1340-1390`. These are APIs/state, not runtime knobs.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_pgate_block_frames` | Returns g->block (rate/1000*PG_BLOCK_MS), zero for NULL. Required per-stream interleaved-frame quantum for complete-block processing. | Check returned size at supported rate and pass exactly that many frames. Integer rate/1000 truncation applies; do not infer exact5ms for arbitrary non-kHz rates. Caller still collects a whole block. |
| `coop_pgate_process_ready_block`, `coop_pgate_process_ready_block.frames`, `coop_pgate_process_ready_block.result` | Bool API requires non-NULL gate/both buffers, frames==block, no legacy-mode epoch, pos==out_pos and block-aligned pos. Valid call ingests once and copies processed absolute input indices [start,start+block) in place, then out_pos=pos. Invalid call returns false before state/buffer writes. | Indexed independent inputs must return current block without legacy initial held silence; compare against legacy output shifted by exactly one block, including an actually admitted/closed interval. Wrong size/mode must preserve inputs and hook/state sequence. No extra held block is not zero total collection, algorithm execution or backend latency. |
| `coop_pgate.io_mode` | Internal per-gate epoch state:0 unused,1 legacy streaming,2 caller-complete blocks. First valid processing selects mode; opposite API refuses further input in same epoch. | Mixed-mode calls leave buffers/state unchanged; successful reset permits either mode again. Internal state is not a user-selectable parameter. Neither process API is reentrant. |
| `coop_pgate_reset` | Allocation-free in-place reset preserves allocated buffers, coefficients and hook/context; clears sample/filter histories, indices, validity, bank, gains, stats and mode. Returns false only for NULL. | Reset after established matching must produce fresh stats/indices with preserved hook, then support either processing API. Must serialize between worker blocks: clearing retained arrays has nonzero CPU cost and is not device callback work. Verify complete reset against a fresh gate; NULL does not establish a new epoch. |
| `coop_pgate_latency_frames` | Existing legacy streaming delay returns one g->block; it does not add that delay to the complete-block API. Existing implementation requires non-NULL gate. | Legacy first block is delayed silence and later indexed blocks align one block behind ready output. Do not subtract this value twice or describe removal as eliminating input collection time. |

## 67. Indexed PCM FIFO storage contract (2026-10-09)

**SRC candidate; tests/application pending.** Owner
`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_fifo.h:6-33` and `coop_audio_fifo.c:6-91`.
Storage preserves arrival order. Source indices do not align gaps to output
time, detect duplicates, reject stale epochs, or establish a playback clock.
Caller owns serialization, epoch transitions, valid initialized objects/buffers
and allocation lifetime. These fields/APIs are not configuration knobs.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_audio_fifo_t`, `coop_audio_fifo_init`, `coop_audio_fifo_destroy` | Init requires non-NULL object, nonzero capacity and1..2 channels; allocates PCM plus per-frame stamps, clears state/counters. Failed allocation destroys fresh allocations; destroy frees and zeroes object. | Check mono/stereo allocation and failure cleanup. Init is for fresh/destroyed storage, not an in-place reset of a populated FIFO. Allocation/destruction belongs outside device callback; object access is caller-serialized. |
| `coop_audio_fifo.capacity`, `coop_audio_fifo.channels`, `coop_audio_fifo.head`, `coop_audio_fifo.count` | Capacity/count/head use interleaved frames, channels is1or2. Head is next read ring slot; count is accepted frames currently stored. | Fill/wrap/drain indexed PCM and verify order, count<=capacity and channel alignment. Capacity is a safety bound, not a priming depth or measured latency. |
| `coop_audio_stamp_t`, `coop_audio_stamp.index`, `coop_audio_stamp.ready_us`, `coop_audio_stamp.flags`, `coop_audio_fifo_push.first_index`, `coop_audio_fifo_push.ready_us`, `coop_audio_fifo_push.flags` | Every pushed frame stores first_index+i and copies ready_us/flags unchanged. Timestamp is intended local decoded-ready microseconds, not host capture time. FIFO itself neither validates timestamp nor interprets flags. | Verify exact stamp preservation across wrap/peek. Index discontinuities and changed flags/timestamps split peek; they do not insert silence or skip PCM in render. Caller must validate timestamp/epoch and define flag semantics. |
| `coop_audio_fifo_push`, `coop_audio_fifo_push.frames` | Copies complete incoming block if non-NULL initialized queue/PCM and frames<=remaining capacity; otherwise false and adds attempted frames to rejected when q exists. Success increments count/accepted by frames. No accepted frame is overwritten. | Full-queue rejected push leaves stored PCM/order/count unchanged; later retry must be accounted once as accepted. rejected counts attempts, so repeated retries can count the same source frames repeatedly; it is not uniquely lost source audio. |
| `coop_audio_fifo_peek` | Non-consuming copy up to requested/count, stopping before first nonconsecutive index or changed ready_us/flags; optionally returns first stamp. Empty/zero request returns0. | Boundary fixture preserves metadata and count; a short peek can mean metadata boundary, not starvation. On return0 first stamp/output are not populated. Caller provides valid object/output. |
| `coop_audio_fifo_consume`, `coop_audio_fifo_consume.now_us` | Removes min(request,count), advances head/count, adds consumed. Samples age only from first removed stamp when ready_us!=0 and now_us>=ready_us. | Over-request cannot consume nonexistent PCM. Consume means removed from this FIFO, not backend acceptance or speaker output; downstream pending ownership must be accounted separately. Same-clock microseconds required. |
| `coop_audio_fifo_render`, `coop_audio_fifo_render.frames`, `coop_audio_fifo_render.now_us` | Copies all available PCM up to requested count in arrival order, zeroes only missing tail, increments missing and consumes copied frames; returns real copied count. No priming threshold/whole-buffer reset after shortage. | Indexed partial-read fixture proves available prefix survives and only unavailable tail is zero; following input remains usable. Render ignores index/metadata boundaries and does not remap source-index gaps onto output time. |
| `coop_audio_fifo_clear` | Adds current count to discarded, empties ring, retains cumulative counters and allocations. | Verify accepted=consumed+discarded+count before/after clear; clear is explicit loss and must accompany justified caller epoch handling, not ordinary queue control. It does not reset accounting epoch. |
| `coop_audio_fifo.accepted`, `coop_audio_fifo.consumed`, `coop_audio_fifo.rejected`, `coop_audio_fifo.discarded`, `coop_audio_fifo.missing` | uint64 frame counters: successful pushes, removals, failed push attempts, explicit clear removals, and synthesized render-tail positions respectively. Init/destroy zero them; clear retains them. | Conservation is accepted=consumed+discarded+count. Missing positions are output demand without stored PCM; rejected attempts are outside accepted ledger. Neither proves audible missing sound or preservation by downstream stages. |
| `coop_audio_fifo.max_age_us` | Lifetime maximum of valid now_us−ready_us for the first frame of each nonempty consume call; ignored for zero/future timestamp. | Known oldest stamp changes maximum. This is a sampled FIFO residence/decode age, not maximum over every consumed frame, total A/V delay, or a clock-validity counter; malformed timestamps need caller diagnostics. |

## 68. Passive-gate learned closing threshold controls (2026-10-09)

**SRC, offline override controls; no threshold change or runtime claim here.**
Owner `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c:38-44,:253-297,:586-591,:1038-1060`.
Environment parsing exists only with COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING. read_env runs on gate
creation and writes process-static P_* values; absent variables do not restore
previously overridden values. Use fresh processes for independent offline cases.
No environment override applies to a shipping build without the compile guard.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / trap |
|---|---|---|
| `PGATE_CLOSE_REL`, `P_CLOSE_REL` | Offline environment atof into static float, default0.70. close_thr multiplies this by per-band learned ceiling when enabled, otherwise0.81. | Trace exact computed threshold and compare to score at known band before attributing closure. No range/finite validation; malformed or extreme offline override is not supported evidence of normal behavior. Fraction of squared-match ceiling, not an amplitude gain or probability. |
| `PGATE_CLOSE_MIN`, `P_CLOSE_MIN` | Offline environment atof into static float, default0.55. close_thr returns max(P_CLOSE_MIN, P_CLOSE_REL*selected ceiling) for finite inputs. | Sweep an independently known score across the resulting threshold and inspect final gate decision/hook; this is threshold floor, not proof admission/closure prerequisites passed. |
| `PGATE_CEIL`, `P_CEIL`, `coop_pgate.close_thr` | Offline atoi into static int, default1; nonzero enables learned ceiling in closing/opening thresholds and updates. Zero fixes threshold reference0.81 and disables learning. Fresh gate initializes every ceiling0.81. Default initial closing threshold is max(.55,.70*.81)=.567. | Inspect stats ceiling per band and computed close threshold. Learning requires hi_match plus a band1..5 score>=.85; skips negative/envelope-forced scores, update coefficient.05 upwards/.002 downwards with floor.75. Learned ceiling is a model statistic, not maximum achievable runtime similarity or reliable-copy proof. |

These controls can be used by a future candidate-validation guard through the
existing close_thr function, but that proposed guard is not established by this
registration. Admission, direct closing, predictions and hold behavior remain
separate decisions; observe final gains and preservation of unique material.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_audio_output_start` | Called after successful backend open/coordinator start, before decode callbacks. Requires active coordinator, no existing worker, successful matching-rate nonzero-quantum clock, supported block size and gate-block agreement. Allocates two FIFOs/mutex, records generation, starts one worker; false leaves legacy callback/pump path selected. | Confirm start record, backend identity/clock capability, no pump start and primary decode callbacks submitting instead of directly feeding output. False is not device-paced success; NDL/Pulse without qualified clock retain legacy behavior. |
| `coop_audio_output_submit`, `coop_audio_output_submit.frames`, `coop_audio_output_submit.first_frame`, `coop_audio_output_submit.ready_us`, `coop_audio_output_submit.plc` | Clocked-only primary/source0 submission: serialized all-or-none FIFO push; frames per channel, first_frame=primary decoded total−current decode length (includes positive PLC returns), ready_us=local monotonic time after decode, plc maps to flags mask1. False means not clocked or rejected storage. | Indexed source PCM and stamps must survive exactly once to consumption. Rejected block is currently logged, not retained by primary caller; continuity acceptance requires no unexplained rejections. False is not necessarily queue full despite current caller warning wording. |
| `coop_audio_output_stop` | Clears atomic run flag, joins worker, destroys input mutex and clears clocked/player state. Coordinator stop logs final timing before destroying retained input FIFOs, and invokes stop before backend close. | No worker Feed/GetClock after backend close; caller must also quiesce submit/decode callbacks before FIFO destruction. Join is not drain: pending mixed block and remaining FIFO frames can remain at stop and must be recorded/accounted before free. |
| `output_clocked`, `output_run`, `output_generation`, `output_player`, `output_thread` | Internal ownership state, atomic run request, borrowed player and worker handle; historical output_generation is now withdrawn in favor of output_owner.generation (§72). Worker checks clock availability and owner validates epoch/rate/quantum each step, exits on error and clears output_run. | Exercise clock loss/epoch change and verify submit rejects after stop. output_clocked is selected path, not health; caller must still quiesce producers before destroy. No automatic fallback on a failed active worker. |
| `output_block_frames`, `COOP-AUD.output.block_frames` | coord_rate/200 integer frames, bounded by COOP_AUDIO_MAX_FRAMES and equal to gate block when gate exists. At48k this is240 frames; gate uses rate/1000*5, so arbitrary rates can fail agreement. | Start record plus indexed output lengths establish chosen block. This is worker collection/processing quantum, not actual end-to-end delay or device callback size. |
| `coop_audio_output.target`, `COOP-AUD.output.quantum_frames`, `COOP-AUD.output.rate`, `COOP-AUD.output.generation` | target is max(latest backend quantum_frames, output_block_frames); logged quantum/rate/generation are startup snapshot. Worker polls current occupancy; it renders one block when queued_frames<target, re-queries after each successful Feed. SDL_Delay(1) is bounded polling request, not the output clock or precise wakeup guarantee. | Fake backend consumption must control production independently of primary arrival. A feed can raise queue from just below target to less than target+one block; backend quantum may exceed5ms. Startup record is not later quantum history, total buffering or acoustic latency. |
| `output_submitted`, `COOP-AUD.output.submitted_frames` | Mixed frames successfully accepted by backend Feed, one block per OK, now stored in output_owner.submitted_frames (§72); historical standalone output_submitted is withdrawn. Includes synthesized missing-source silence. | Compare backend accepted frames over same open epoch, accounting preexisting/other writers. This count is output frames, not sum of both sources, real-PCM count or acoustic presentation. |
| `output_retries`, `COOP-AUD.output.retries`, `COOP-AUD.output.pending_frames` | Every non-OK Feed increments owner retries (§72; historical output_retries withdrawn); unchanged mixed pending block retried without re-rendering or re-running gate. Terminal pending_frames is one block if not accepted, else0. ERROR exits; other failures wait/retry. | Forced overflow must preserve exact pending PCM and consume neither source again on retry. Retries counts failed attempts including final ERROR, not unique lost frames. Terminal pending is removed from input FIFO but not backend accepted; include it in downstream accounting. |
| `COOP-AUD.output.self_accepted`, `COOP-AUD.output.self_consumed`, `COOP-AUD.output.self_queued`, `COOP-AUD.output.self_rejected`, `COOP-AUD.output.peer_accepted`, `COOP-AUD.output.peer_consumed`, `COOP-AUD.output.peer_queued`, `COOP-AUD.output.peer_rejected` | Periodic and worker-exit snapshot of respective FIFO accepted/consumed/count/rejected (§67), before stop destroys storage; periodic cadence is submitted audio (§73). Source0 producer uses input_lock, source1 receive/render belongs to worker. | Read complete records and reconcile accepted=consumed+queued while no explicit clears occur. Consumed is FIFO removal and may be pending backend acceptance. Exit copies source0 under input_lock after clearing run; submit rechecks run under that same lock before push. This orders the acceptance cutoff and snapshot. Caller still must quiesce producers before mutex/FIFO destruction. |

Current integration limitations requiring resolution/explicit separation in tests:
source FIFO order does not align index gaps to output time. CA4S now handles
reconnect epochs/ACK retention (§71); full coordinator FIFO retains one pending
packet rather than rejecting it. Ready processing analyzes gate continuously but
uses processed output only under filtering policy; clocked gate-selector OFF
is explicitly logged bypass rather than delayed legacy dedupe. Clocked policy
toggles no longer recreate gate. ring_count uses allocated peer FIFO, including
final timing before destruction. Re-check old timing checker assumptions against
current transport/validity fields before treating it as proof of this path.
These are current source bounds, not accepted final architecture behavior.

## 70. Passive-gate input validity contract (2026-10-09)

**SRC candidate; tests/application pending.** Owner `coop_audio_pgate.h:76-86`,
`coop_audio_pgate.c` validity allocation, range_valid, ingest, process_block and
coop_pgate_process_ready_block_valid. This metadata is caller-supplied evidence
eligibility, not a waveform-silence test or independent verification of input.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `COOP_PGATE_VALID_SELF`, `COOP_PGATE_VALID_PEER`, `COOP_PGATE_VALID_BOTH`, `coop_pgate_process_ready_block_valid.valid_streams` | Bit masks1,2,3 identify complete-block source0/source1 validity.0 marks neither. Missing/concealed samples should invalidate their whole source block. Unknown bits reject before writes. | Test each mask and invalid high bits with indexed PCM/hook. Actual source may contain digital silence and still be valid; synthesized or PLC data remains audible input but must not establish matching evidence. Caller propagation must be tested separately. |
| `coop_pgate_process_ready_block_valid` | Same exact-block, mode/alignment and in-place index contract as §66 with explicit validity; plain ready API wraps BOTH and legacy ingest also marks BOTH. Each invalid current source passes unchanged, including bypassing level balance, and cannot supply matching evidence. Other valid source can still match valid past samples from an idle current leader; reference-validity checks govern held/predicted closing. Complete-block path disables the legacy current-digital-silence dropout heuristic. | After established closure/balance, inject invalid source and verify bit-exact current PCM plus indexed continuity. Positive guard: valid late copy still matches valid past leader when current leader is missing. Old ready/legacy callers do not automatically infer missing/PLC metadata. |
| `coop_pgate.valid_blocks`, `coop_pgate.valid_len`, `coop_pgate.valid_mask`, `coop_pgate.range_valid` | Preallocated power-of-two bitmask ring covers raw-history blocks; ingest stamps block validity. range_valid rejects nonpositive lengths, unavailable/future/expired range, or any overlapped block lacking the source bit. Waveform/envelope share and onset prediction reference checks use it. Successful reset clears validity with other history. | Matching windows spanning an invalid block must not confirm a match, including later lagged use after current block becomes valid. Conservative whole-block validity may exclude good partial samples. Ring is internal metadata, not another PCM buffer/latency stage. |

## 71. Acknowledged secondary PCM transport (2026-10-09)

**SRC candidate; transport fault tests/live application pending.** Owner
`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c` header:30-48, coord_commit_pending/
coord_flush_ack/coop_audio_drain, secondary_send_main and secondary decoder
init/feed/cleanup. Both processes require the same CA4S native ABI.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `COOP_AUDIO_MSG_CONFIG`, `COOP_AUDIO_MSG_PCM`, `COOP_AUDIO_MSG_ACK` | Header type values0,1,2. CONFIG establishes matching rate/channels and nonzero epoch; PCM carries indexed interleaved frames; ACK carries epoch and exclusive accepted end in first_frame. | Known CONFIG/PCM/ACK exchange must agree on sizes/version/epoch. ACK proves coordinator storage accepted frames, not worker consumption, backend acceptance or audible output. |
| `coop_audio_hdr_t.epoch`, `sec_epoch`, `peer_epoch` | Sender initializes decoder-lifetime identity from local monotonic audio_time_us once; preserves across connection retries. New matching CONFIG epoch explicitly clears queued peer audio, counts link discard and resets sequence; replacing a previous nonzero epoch also resets gate. Initial epoch leaves the fresh gate intact; same epoch reconnect preserves queue/sequence. | Force reconnect with same epoch and new decoder epoch separately. New epoch discards must be explicit; timestamp-derived identity is not a globally unique UUID or host media timestamp. Current reset is allocation-free and returns false only for NULL (§66); coordinator retains defensive failure handling. |
| `COOP_AUDIO_RETRY_MS` |10ms timeout for poll(POLLOUT) completing nonblocking connect, plus10ms SDL_Delay after failed connect; dedicated sender retries independently of decode arrivals. Completion also requires getsockopt(SO_ERROR)==0 and POLLOUT. Replaces withdrawn one-second callback redial. Loop otherwise uses1ms polling delay. | Delayed coordinator availability must connect while source quiet with retained frames still queued. Delays are scheduling requests, not measured exact reconnect latency or playback buffering. |
| `sec_fifo`, `peer_pcm_pending`, `peer_ack_pending`, `peer_pending_hdr`, `peer_pending_pcm` | Sender FIFO retains accepted PCM until matching ACK; one packet is in flight. Coordinator retains one full received packet while peer FIFO lacks space and pauses further receive. Send/reconnect errors discard pending transport state, while sender retains the underlying unacknowledged PCM. | Fault before acceptance, after acceptance/before ACK and after ACK; compare indexed coordinator accepted stream with source. Full FIFO must not overwrite accepted data. Finite sender capacity still permits producer rejection under sufficiently long disconnection/stall. |
| `COOP_AUDIO_ACK_TIMEOUT_MS`, `coop_audio_sender.sent_us` |250ms deadline while awaiting one packet ACK, measured from audio_time_us when framed channel accepts the PCM send. Sender checks elapsed>=timeout before receive, disconnects and retries same retained FIFO head. This measures send-acceptance to ACK wait, including any channel flush time. | Withhold ACK after coordinator accepts, cross deadline and reconnect: source data remains retained, coordinator suppresses duplicate enqueue, matching ACK releases sender block once. Bound actual scheduling/reconnect separately; deadline is not measured latency or guaranteed exact250ms wakeup. |
| `peer_duplicates` | Frames in completely earlier/already-accepted packet (end<=timing_next_frame) increment duplicate counter and trigger ACK without second enqueue. Partial overlap is rejected and connection dropped. | Lose an ACK and reconnect: duplicate count may rise, coordinator received/queued PCM must not double. Counter currently internal, not emitted telemetry; inspect dedicated fixture state/trace rather than inventing a log field. |
| `COOP-AUD.transport.epoch`, `COOP-AUD.transport.accepted_frames`, `COOP-AUD.transport.acknowledged_frames`, `COOP-AUD.transport.queued_frames`, `COOP-AUD.transport.rejected_frames` | Sender cleanup after sender join prints epoch and sec_fifo accepted/consumed/count/rejected. Consumption occurs only for exact epoch and pending_end ACK. Decoder positive returns advance sample index regardless of FIFO acceptance. | Reconcile accepted=acknowledged+queued when no sender clears. Rejected means producer FIFO failure; ACKed frames may still be queued/missing at later stages. Decoder producer must be quiescent for coherent final snapshot and storage destruction. |

Coordinator successful commit advances accepted counts once and then schedules
ACK. Same-epoch fully duplicate PCM gets cumulative accepted-end ACK; bad magic,
format, epoch, lengths, integer overflow and partial overlap are rejected.
A pending ACK now has the deadline below; timeout reconnects with the source
block retained. Repeated timeout/disconnection can still fill finite sender FIFO.
Unit fault coverage must include pending data at teardown and lost ACK after
acceptance, not just the happy path. Replacing epoch reset clears retained histories without allocation on the output
worker; its CPU time still belongs in replenishment-budget measurement.

Bounded component TEST (2026-10-09): `tests/coop/test_coop_audio_transport.c`
compiled actual included coordinator code with real channel/FIFO/gate and passed
local SOCK_SEQPACKET and SOCK_STREAM socketpairs. Covered exclusive ACK,
unread ACK lost at disconnect/same-epoch exact dedup, full FIFO pending with no
premature ACK, initial/replacement epoch behavior, partial-overlap rejection,
and indexed conservation17 accepted=15 consumed+2 explicit epoch discard.
Fixture manually consumes FIFO/updates timing, so it does not test worker render,
sender timeout/connect behavior, thread scheduling or live audio. Logger
preprocessing verified /dev/null; no app/host/device/socket pathname opened.
Evidence: external audio-gate-review-20261008/transport-component-20261009.

coop_audio.c output_render/output_feed/output_main. Caller supplies coherent
backend snapshots, serialization and object lifetimes. This is occupancy-based
production pacing, not a timer, independent resampler or source-clock synchronizer.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_output_t`, `output_owner`, `coop_output_init`, `coop_output_destroy` | Init validates non-NULL state, nonzero rate/block/generation and1..2 channels, allocates one interleaved block and clears counters/state. Destroy frees and zeroes; fresh/destroyed object required for init. Parent worker owns one instance. | Allocation failure and stop cleanup must preserve caller ownership. Destroy is not pending drain; terminal counters/PCM ownership need snapshot before free. |
| `coop_output_clock_t`, `coop_output_clock.rate`, `coop_output_clock.quantum_frames`, `coop_output_clock.generation`, `coop_output_clock.queued_frames` | Per-step view mapped from SS4S sample_rate, latest quantum, successful-open generation and queued accepted frames. Rate isHz; quantum/queued are interleaved frames. | Drive fake backend demand by decreasing queue, then verify one-block production. Nonzero quantum/matching rate+epoch required; a frozen but nonzero backend clock can stall production without becoming an error. No direct use of demand/observed counters in this owner. |
| `coop_output.block_frames`, `coop_output.channels`, `coop_output.rate`, `coop_output.generation` | Immutable configured block, channel count, rate and accepted open epoch after init. | Different rate/epoch or zero quantum makes failed sticky and returns ERROR before render/feed. Unit tests must distinguish invalid clock from ordinary full queue. |
| `coop_output_step`, `coop_output_render_fn`, `coop_output_feed_fn` | If no pending block and backend queue>=max(quantum,block), returns IDLE. Otherwise render exactly once into owned PCM, then Feed. Pending retries skip render and reuse exact PCM; one call attempts at most one block. | Independently indexed render and recording fake backend must prove no duplicate/drop/re-render across WAIT and eventual OK. Feed caller must return OK only for complete acceptance. Parent adapter maps SS4S OK→OK, ERROR→ERROR, all other results→WAIT. |
| `coop_output.pending`, `coop_output.pcm`, `coop_output.failed`, `COOP-AUD.output.failed` | pending means rendered block not yet accepted; pcm is owner buffer. failed becomes sticky on rate/epoch/quantum mismatch or Feed ERROR; parent also sets it on unavailable GetClock. Log prints failed at worker exit. | Fault with pending block preserves it for explicit terminal accounting, not hidden replay after new epoch. NULL invalid arguments return ERROR without necessarily setting failed; this differs from clock/feed failure. No implicit recovery/drain. |
| `coop_output.submitted_frames`, `coop_output.retries` | Successful full Feed adds configured block to submitted_frames and clears pending; any non-OK increments retries, including terminal ERROR. | Sum fake backend accepted indexed blocks and compare; repeat WAIT must not consume more source frames. Submission includes zeros from missing-source rendering and is not speaker playback. |
| `COOP_OUTPUT_ERROR`, `COOP_OUTPUT_IDLE`, `COOP_OUTPUT_PROGRESS`, `COOP_OUTPUT_RETRY` | Step results−1,0,1,2. ERROR invalid/fatal, IDLE sufficient queue, PROGRESS one accepted block, RETRY still owns pending block after nonfatal failed Feed. | Assert exact return plus callback counts/state; parent loops immediately only after PROGRESS (fresh backend query), otherwise yields1ms or exits on ERROR. |
| `COOP_OUTPUT_FEED_ERROR`, `COOP_OUTPUT_FEED_OK`, `COOP_OUTPUT_FEED_WAIT` | Feed callback result−1,0,1. Step treats any nonzero except−1 as retry;−1 sticky failure. | Verify adapter status mapping, not just enum spelling. WAIT must mean no acceptance; partial-accepting backends cannot use this whole-block contract without an adapter. |
| `coop_audio.render_input.valid_streams` | Parent render_input declares source valid only if queue holds whole requested block and every consumed stamp flags==0, then always renders available PCM. Source bits combine for gate valid API (§70). | Inject one PLC-flagged frame or short block; entire source block must be ineligible matching evidence while audible available PCM remains. Digital zeros with valid metadata stay eligible. Current check also rejects source-index discontinuities within/across consumed blocks (§73); it does not validate timestamps or align gaps to output time. |

The target admits less than one processing block of queue overshoot relative to
max(current quantum,block) when the supplied snapshot is current and Feed adds
only that block. Rejected/pending blocks remain owned even if queue later rises.
This bound excludes upstream FIFO/IPC, gate collection, OS/device and acoustic
latency; no5ms end-to-end guarantee follows from block_frames.

retries, generation rejection, and FIFO wrap/reject/metadata/clear accounting.
Fixture uses production FIFO/output owner with fake render/feed; its internal
heard counter counts **backend acceptance**, not device consumption or hearing.
This does not exercise actual IPC ACK, gate validity integration, thread races,
SS4S callbacks, firmware, acoustic continuity or latency. Those remain separate
integration/live checks. Registrar did not independently run this component test.

## 73. Ready-block policy ramps and worker telemetry (2026-10-09)

**SRC candidate plus bounded component TEST below; live application pending.** Owner
`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c` coordinator initialization, mix_blocks,
output_log and output_main. Applies to complete-block worker path; legacy path
retains its existing immediate gain/held-filter behavior.

| `output_role_gain` | Two retained source-policy gains, initialized to COOP_AUDIO_MIX_GAIN (.75 each) on coordinator start. Ready mixer interpolates previous→new mode/local-host gains at fraction(frame+1)/frames for each channel, then retains endpoints. One worker block is5ms at48k. | Constant distinct sources must yield monotone mix→primary→secondary transitions with exact endpoint and equal stereo gain within each frame. No held block is inserted; first sample takes1/block step, not exactly the old endpoint. This changes amplitude during transition and is not proof of perceptual click removal. |
| `output_filter_wet` | Retained dry/processed mix weight, initialized0. Target1 only for active permitted passive gate;0 for bypass/role/local-host policy. If gate ready processing succeeds, interpolate previous→target over same input-indexed block; gate analysis still runs across bypass. | Use actual attenuating gate output and raw input to compare expected per-frame blend, separately from role gain. Zero/identical gate output only tests history advancing, not audible crossfade correctness. Failed/unavailable processing falls back raw and cannot guarantee a wet transition. |
| `coop_audio.limiter_before`, `coop_audio.limiter_gain` | Before block update snapshots old limiter gain. When limit_mix is true, target is min(1,COOP_AUDIO_LIMIT/peak); ready attack stores target immediately, legacy attack factor.5, release.005 per block; steady single-source modes set endpoint1. Ready output interpolates old→new gain but caps each sample gain at full-block safe_gain before int16 conversion/clamp. | Known constant inputs and chosen prior gain can verify monotone recovery/attack within rounding and clamp limits. Full-block safe cap prevents the former ready attack ramp exceeding its current peak bound. Check integer rounding explicitly. This is not proof of perceptual transparency or unchanged single-source transition amplitude. |
| `coop_audio.safe_gain` | Ready mixer per-sample gain cap min(1,COOP_AUDIO_LIMIT/current full-block summed peak), applied only when limit_mix is true, after role/filter interpolation established that peak. Existing int16 clamp remains final defense; limiter attack endpoint is immediately safe in ready MIX/role transition. Fully settled single-source output retains full int16 range. | Regression prior gain.825 with summed block40000: every ready output sample must be at most32000 magnitude without saturating at int16 extrema. Legacy path is unchanged. Attack may be immediate rather than perceptually smooth; actual audio quality remains separate. |
| `coop_audio.limit_mix` | Captured before retained role gains are updated: true in MIX or during ready-mode gain transition (old source gains differ from new targets). Selects limiter update and safe_gain ceiling; steady solo bypasses32000 ceiling. | Transition fixture must remain bounded, then settled primary-only/secondary-only ±32767 must be bit-exact with other source nonzero. Do not call the transition/recovery block settled: prior limiter gain may still interpolate toward1. |
| `output_queue_peak`, `COOP-AUD.output.queue_peak_frames` | Lifetime max of backend queued_frames sampled by worker before each output-owner step; initialized0 at coordinator start. | Controlled backend queue burst must appear if observed; it is not backend's own post-feed maximum and can miss intervening peaks. Units interleaved output frames; no upstream/OS/hardware buffering included. |
| `COOP-AUD.output.self_age_max_us`, `COOP-AUD.output.peer_age_max_us` | Respective FIFO max_age_us (§67); source0 struct copied under input mutex, peer owned by worker. Includes decoded-ready→FIFO consume elapsed time for sampled first-consumed frames only. | Compare known ready/consume timestamps; not maximum over every frame, source-network latency or acoustic timing. Invalid/future stamps are excluded rather than proving low age. |
| `coop_audio.output_log` | Emits worker owner/pending/failure, queue peak and both FIFO accounting/age snapshots after each additional rate*5 submitted frames and once on exit. Existing startup record contains rate/quantum/generation separately. | Five seconds refers to submitted sample count, not wall-clock timer; stalled submission can stop periodic reports. Join/producer cutoff ordering is required for final totals. Read whole records and correct session; periodic snapshots alone do not establish final conservation. |

| `output_source_next`, `output_source_indexed`, `output_invalid_index_blocks` | Per-source render_input sequence state: after first consumed stamp, each next stamp must equal previous index+1. Any mismatch makes current source block invalid for analysis and increments invalid_index_blocks once, while retaining all PCM in arrival order. Start clears all arrays; replacement peer epoch clears peer indexed flag. | Real input FIFO fixture must cover initial contiguous, inter-block gap, following recovery, intra-block gap, PLC and short tail. Sequence adopts each actual consumed index, so one jump does not invalidate all later contiguous blocks. Counters are internal, not emitted telemetry. No source-time resampling/gap insertion follows from this metadata check. |

## 74. External desktop gate processing benchmark schema (2026-10-09)

**SRC offline measurement definition, not a performance finding.** Owner
`~/project-backups/audio-gate-review-20261008/search-2x2/bench-gate.c`
plus bench-ready.c / bench-legacy.c adapters. Input loader assumes, rather than
validates, a44-byte header with48kHz stereo int16 PCM. Use only independently
validated equal nonempty frame counts divisible by240 and record binary/source/
input hashes plus compilation mode. Every timed call consumes240 frames.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `gate-bench.blocks` | Number of240-frame calls from (file bytes−44)/4 interleaved input frames; equal input frame totals asserted. | Confirm externally validated format/payload and emitted block count. Loader does not parse RIFF chunks or verify sample rate/channel/format. Invalid or empty inputs cannot support a timing finding. |
| `gate-bench.wall_us_p50`, `gate-bench.wall_us_p95`, `gate-bench.wall_us_p99`, `gate-bench.wall_us_max` | Per-call CLOCK_MONOTONIC elapsed microseconds around bench_process; independently sorted wall samples use zero-based floor(N*.50/.95/.99), max=N−1. Includes clock-call/wrapper overhead and scheduling interruption. Output prints wall_us_p50 then positional p95/p99/max. | Record full labeled context; repeated p95/max tokens elsewhere on same line belong to CPU series. Desktop elapsed tails do not prove target firmware deadline safety; CPU pressure/preemption and warmup are included, not filtered. |
| `gate-bench.max_start_frame` | Absolute input frame index b*240 for first strictly largest wall-time call, captured before sorting. | Correlate same source/input hash and block interval to search cadence; it is not wall-clock timestamp or separately timed CPU maximum. |
| `gate-bench.cpu_us_p50`, `gate-bench.cpu_us_p95`, `gate-bench.cpu_us_p99`, `gate-bench.cpu_us_max` | CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID elapsed, sampled outside wall timestamps for each bench_process call; independently sorted with same quantile rule. Includes wall clock reads and wrapper overhead, excludes descheduled time. | CPU/wall quantiles need not refer to same block. Does not include loader, allocation, sorting, file I/O or gate teardown; no integrated mixer/backend/network cost or total latency claim. |
| `gate-bench.bench_process` | Ready adapter asserts coop_pgate_process_ready_block success; legacy adapter calls coop_pgate_process. Which gate implementation is linked depends on external build command, not adapter name alone. | Hash linked source/binary and compile options. A prototype legacy build and production ready build differ in algorithm/API; label both rather than implying a clean API-only performance comparison. No live host or target device is driven. |

Native Windows source variant `search-2x2/bench-gate-windows.c` uses QueryPerformanceCounter/Frequency deltas around the same actual ready call; reports only the existing wall-time fields and block/max-start index. Includes timing-call overhead and preemption; no CPU-time values. Loader asserts canonical44byte PCM16 stereo48k and payload/equal-length/block alignment. Assertions must remain enabled because API calls occur inside assert. Native device/real-time scheduling is not measured by this finite component benchmark.

Application session.c forces co-op stereo. | Check actual subsequent negotiated rate/channels against prepared format; aud_init rejects mismatch rather than silently consuming wrong-rate PCM. This is a preparation assumption tied to inspected upstream source, not permission to ignore future upstream changes or an adjustable device-rate knob. |
| `session_audio_prepare_coop_output` | Requires context/player, co-op request, stereo config and SS4S_AudioHasClock. Opens PCM S16LE stereo48k with requested SAMPLES_PER_FRAME, then requires successful instance clock/rate and output_start validation of quantum/epoch/block. Creates coordinator and worker before LiStartConnection; partial failure closes/stops its opened resources and returns false. | Fake/native lifecycle tests must verify false capability does not open; failed open/clock/worker leaves no worker/device; success opens once and aud_init reuses it. Runtime readiness requires exact start/ready records and actual instance clock, not capability true alone. No host is launched by this helper itself. |
| `output_prepared`, `device_paced` | Static session flags set only after successful prepare; prepared aud_init checks negotiated48k/stereo and skips second output/coordinator open. Normal init may set device_paced after negotiated output succeeds without output_prepared. Cleanup clears both. | Count actual Open/Close/start/join over success, failure before audio init, failure during init and ordinary cleanup. Flags identify selected path, not decoder activity, worker health, sample continuity or audible output. |
| `session_audio_finish_output` | Worker thread cleanup invokes before SS4S_PlayerClose; if non-NULL target matches current audio player, calls aud_cleanup to stop/join coordinator/output, close audio and free decoder/buffers. After normal cleanup player is NULL, so repeat finish has no effect. | Cover failure before AudioCallbacks.cleanup plus ordinary cleanup/finish sequence; no worker query/feed may access a destroyed player. Requires connection callbacks/producers already quiesced as part of session lifetime; helper is not concurrent cancellation API. |
| `COOP-AUD.output.ready before host connection and secondary launch` | Qualified label for literal readiness message after successful preparation; no numeric ready field is emitted. Startup code calls helper before LiStartConnection, under primary-only branch. | Actual loaded log establishes helper reached success at that point; it does not prove subsequent host connection/secondary launch or first audio preservation. Worker can submit synthesized silence before host audio begins, so submitted-frame/elapsed intervals include that preparation period. |

## 76. External additive-residual observer schema (2026-10-09)

**SRC external prototype only; experiments/results not established.** Owners
`~/project-backups/audio-gate-review-20261008/search-2x2/`
`gate-residual.c:1244-1273,:1313-1316` and `sim-residual.c:98-174,:566-572,:629-635`.
No production control or accepted gate architecture is introduced by these rows.
The simulator's additive component bookkeeping differs from multiplicative-gain
energy attribution: own components retain coefficient1 and subtract references.

| name | Source meaning | Application observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `residual_observe`, `residual_observe.start`, `residual_observe.frames`, `residual_observe.lag`, `residual_observe.coef` | Callback reports absolute48k input block start/length plus [2][2][6] source/channel/band lag and coefficient arrays before unity gain hook. Positive lag refers to other-source past frames. Coef is positive finite sampled least-squares sxy/syy for current window when midband and per-band current evidence pass; zero means no subtraction. | Independently reconstruct actual correction at those indices; selected lag alone does not mean subtraction. Coefficients are not gain on own unique material. Hold/prediction gains are replaced by unity in this external observer path, so old multiplicative attenuation metrics are insufficient. |
| `residual.unique_error/input` |10log10((sum squared opposite-unique reference correction+1)/(sum squared original own unique components across both sources+1)), whole truth interval, before balance/clipping. Positive or negative value describes relative correction energy. | This is unique-reference contamination/error relative to original unique energy, **not** unique energy removed, perceptual damage or output SNR. Original unique component remains coefficient1. Reconcile full predicted waveform before trusting component attribution. |
| `residual.shared_injection_energy` | Sum squared subtracted opposite shared-family reference signal at target times event_kind==0 (neither scheduled later copy nor earlier event). Observer also stores this negative component for waveform reconciliation. Prebalance/preclip, unnormalized amplitude²-sample sum. | Nonzero means modeled shared-family injection outside target schedule; zero does not exclude errors inside scheduled intervals or opposite-unique contamination. Compare same length/scale; not dB or audible verdict. |
| `residual_output_sample` | Truth-only observer for channel0, both sources, t<n. Compares clip16(predicted component sum times supplied actual balance) with clip16(actual already-balanced gate sample), saves indexed actual samples. | Whole waveform reconciliation includes earlier/copy/unique and out-of-event shared injection. Mono duplicated truth only, not all stereo scenes. Input quantization/component filtering can produce rounding residual; large unexplained disagreement invalidates attribution. |
| `residual.reconcile.rms_pcm`, `residual.reconcile.max_pcm`, `residual.reconcile.samples` | RMS and largest absolute PCM-unit difference of those clipped predictions vs actual, count of observed mono-role samples (expected2*n for complete truth processing). | Check expected coverage, zero/nonzero count and bounded residual; no division-by-zero-safe fallback is supplied. CPU/gate output quality is not established by reconciliation alone. |
| `residual.reconcile.indexed_mismatches` | Count where saved actual indexed channel0 differs from returned legacy streaming output shifted by coop_pgate_latency_frames; compares both sources only while i+delay<n, excluding final unavailable held block. | Zero proves indexed observer agrees with returned covered samples, not unavailable tail, component prediction or removal quality. Keep this distinct from RMS/max component reconstruction error. |

Truth event-kind construction aborts if any two scheduled events label the same
source/sample; no overwritten schedule labels are accepted. All energy metrics
are conditional on actual source/input/binary provenance and successful waveform
reconciliation. They are not production mixer measurements or live acceptance.

## 78. External per-band attribution and event residual diagnostics (2026-10-09)

**SRC external offline definitions only; no experiment result or production knob.**
Owners under `~/project-backups/audio-gate-review-20261008/search-2x2/`:
`sim-attribution.c:127-142,:543-554`, `gate-attribution.c:1255-1267`, and
`sim-wide.c:169-175,:641-642`. Attribution requires exactly seven unseen scheduled
events and refuses overlapping later-event labels. Output is OUT-bands.csv.

| name | Source meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `gate-attribution.frame`, `gate-attribution.event`, `gate-attribution.lag_ms` | Absolute48k input block start, event index0..6, and signed scheduled lag from {272,294,353,−180,34,60,−143}ms. Row event is selected by label on current block's **last sample**, only for later-copy role. | Check scheduler intervals and signed role against CSV. Energy spans entire block, so boundary blocks are not clipped to the event interval. These labels are prior truth, not inferred/admitted offsets or wall-clock times. |
| `gate-attribution.bank_present` |1 when actual gate stats contain any offset within±1full-rate sample of signed scheduled lag*48; otherwise0. | Compare emitted bank state to exact signed offset. Nearby pitch aliases outside1sample are false here; present does not mean used for this band/decision or successful subtraction. |
| `gate-attribution.band`, `gate-attribution.copy_energy`, `gate-attribution.remaining_copy_energy` | Band0..5, channel0 known later-copy component energy sum(x²) over current full block, and sum((x*final interpolated gain)²), using same g0→g1 per-frame ramp as gain hook. Before balance and clipping. | Recompute from that independently split component and actual hook gains. These are per-band model energies; sum across bands is not automatically broadband reconstructed energy because cross-band terms are omitted. Not total mixed-stream loss, acoustic loudness or source-independent quality. |
| `gate-attribution.true_share`, `gate-attribution.threshold` | Read-only share query at scheduled true lag for target source/channel0/current configured band window, and actual close_thr after normal decision logic. Observer query never supplies oracle offset to search/admission. | Match sampled window/stride/source/lag and threshold to source; too-quiet share can be−1. This is available truth-lag waveform evidence, not score of chosen candidate or explanation of prediction/hold/other-band decisions by itself. |
| `gate-attribution.g0`, `gate-attribution.g1` | Final existing gain-hook start/end values for later role, channel0, band; remaining_copy_energy uses gain=g0+(g1−g0)*(i+1)/frames. | Verify interpolation and indexed coverage; endpoint closed alone does not mean whole block muted. Excludes balance, clipping, output mixer policy and acoustic delivery. |
| `residual.event_unique_error/input` | sim-wide sums squared opposite-unique reference correction and original own-unique energy only where target role event_kind==1 (scheduled later-copy interval), then10log10((error+1)/(input+1)). Uses same additive bookkeeping as §76, prebalance/preclip. | Compare masks and denominators to event schedule, separately from whole-trace metric. Earlier-event role/time and outside-event intervals are excluded. This measures reference-unique contamination relative to own unique energy, not unique energy removed, net SNR or perceptual damage. Require full-waveform reconciliation before attribution claims. |

The linked wide variants change the meaning of residual_observe.coef relative
to §76's original per-band regression: gate-wide-ls assigns one coefficient to
all six bands, sqrt(gain2*gain3); gate-wide-energy divides that by
(mid2*mid3)^(1/4), using the two witness midbands' positive regression gains and
squared shares. Both require each witness share>=its close_thr and sufficient
reference energy, then choose by the smaller witness score; they do not retain
an independent every-band closing-threshold test. Neither coefficient isolates
reference-unique sound. Record exact linked variant/source/input before comparing
reported metrics, and do not reuse original per-band coefficient semantics.
