## 79. SDL callback and delay cadence diagnostic (2026-10-09)

**DOC + SRC, finite local silent probe; no timing result yet.** Primary owners:
`~/eclipse-linux/deps-build/sdl2/include/SDL_audio.h:183,590-607`,
`include/SDL_timer.h:111-147`, and `src/timer/windows/SDL_systimer.c:123-165`
under that same SDL source root. External probe owner:
`/mnt/c/Users/<user>/eclipse-tools/audio-latency-review/callback-cadence/cadence.c`
(SHA2569c8a0d0289b48804fc60fd2b4c824b8d43bfeae849c2c2f271366e77cba97d03).

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `SDL_Delay` | SDL timer API requests an unsigned millisecond wait; header permits longer waits due to scheduling. Inspected Windows implementation initializes ticks if needed then calls Sleep(ms), except legacy WinRT branch. | Measure elapsed performance-counter deltas around each call; a request1 does not establish1ms elapsed or worker wake cadence. Record loaded DLL/version, driver and timer-init context; local source is not proof another DLL implements the same path. |
| `SDL_GetPerformanceCounter`, `SDL_GetPerformanceFrequency` | SDL high-resolution tick count and ticks/second; inspected Windows owner calls QueryPerformanceCounter/QueryPerformanceFrequency directly. | Convert tick differences with measured frequency; these are host elapsed-time readings, not sample-clock, acoustic or cross-host synchronization proof. Check positive frequency and monotonic sample order. |
| `SDL_AudioSpec.samples` | SDL audio structure buffer size in sample frames (all channels at one instant), not scalar channel samples/bytes. Desired field is a request; owner documentation permits driver adjustment. Callback receives actual byte length. | Print obtained spec and every callback length; samples/rate expresses nominal duration, not observed interval or guarantee callbacks are evenly spaced. Probe requests240frames48k stereoS16 with allowed_changes0 but must inspect actual result. |
| `cadence.device`, `cadence.SDL`, `cadence.dll`, `cadence.driver` | Probe requires one exact device-name argument; prints requested name, SDL_GetVersion result, Windows loaded SDL2.dll path and current audio driver. No unnamed fallback is attempted. | Verify actual loaded module path/hash and named endpoint against intended candidate environment. Open success/spec alone is not acoustic delivery or default-endpoint identity proof. |
| `cadence.obtained_rate`, `cadence.obtained_channels`, `cadence.obtained_samples`, `cadence.obtained_format` | Obtained SDL_AudioSpec freqHz, channel count, buffer frames, numeric SDL format from actual successful open. | Compare with request48000/2/240/AUDIO_S16LSB; byte-to-frame conversion requires returned format/channels. Different format/device voids direct same-configuration comparison. |
| `cadence.callback_index`, `cadence.callback_us`, `cadence.callback_bytes` | Zero-based captured callback index; elapsed microseconds from first recorded callback using SDL performance ticks/frequency; exact callback bytes argument. Fixed2048-entry array records timestamp/length before zero-filling output. | Differences expose delivery intervals/bursts; sum bytes gives requested callback payload, not played speaker frames. If count reaches2048, capture is truncated and completeness cannot be assumed. Read only after CloseAudioDevice; callback timing includes recording overhead. |
| `cadence.delay_index`, `cadence.delay_us` | Zero-based index0..255 and measured tick delta around each SDL_Delay(1), converted to microseconds. Calls run after unpausing audio; then an additional SDL_Delay(2000) before close. | Report distribution of measured waits separately from callback intervals. Audio capture lasts those256 waits plus requested2000ms and scheduling/close overhead, not exactly2seconds. Probe uses SDL_Init(0) plus AUDIO, not explicit TIMER subsystem initialization; compare runtime context before attributing app behavior. |

Probe produces only zero PCM, opens/closes one named local output, never changes
its default endpoint. Measurements may distinguish callback bursts from delay
oversleep; neither alone establishes the application worker's root cause.

## 81. Gate discovery history sizing (2026-10-09)

**SRC landed2026-10-09; live application pending.** Owner
`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c`, current SHA2569158c7d6c69f581537cb25e3a366e18aa092f8ffacb7be68c3be6f88d07821fa,
following discovery, quiet-window optimization and valid-suffix matching,
lines306,368,416,489-533,702-715. §77 updated to this source; removed fixed-window macro is WITHDRAWN above.

| name | Current source meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_pgate.search_win` | Instance count in decimated samples: floor(block/PG_DEC), with block=(rate/1000)*5. At8k/44.1k/48k/96k gives5/27/30/60 respectively. Sizes search buffers and current correlation window, replacing fixed1024 there. | Inspect instantiated count for each rate, buffer coverage and search invocation/admission positions.44.1k uses integer220-frame block, so don't describe it as exact5ms. Shorter window alone does not prove selectivity or cold-start acquisition. |
| `coop_pgate.matching_window` | Internal helper now takes minimum contiguous valid retained suffix of target/current and opposite/lagged window, capped by configured window; refuses shortened bass or result shorter than fullblock. Returns0 for absent/future/overwritten/invalid history, end<=lag or negative lag. share_x treats0 as no evidence, disables cached full-window energy for shortened windows, and still validates both actual source ranges. | Focused tests must exercise startup, lag signs through caller's absolute-lag mapping, valid history and independent-noise controls. Availability is necessary but not evidence a correlation is true. Ordinary full-history windows remain §80 defaults; temporary search windows still apply. |

Current create/reset schedules next_search at first full block. Search returns
without incrementing searches while dpos<=search_win; otherwise effective lag
extent is min(configured ld,dpos−search_win), preserving configured long-term
range. This is an available-history mechanism, not proof a first-block search or
admission occurs. Record invocation positions and resulting offsets/gains across
fresh/reset cases before describing behavior. Candidate ranking uses unweighted FFT cross-correlation divided by FFT length,
then positive cross²/(current-window energy*matched historical-window energy),
clamped1 and requiring energy floor; nonpositive/quiet candidates score−1.
Admission additionally requires original refined score>=PG_ADMIT and both
midbands at the same lag >=their close_thr, or near-exact40ms bass corroboration.
This is a source mechanism, not proof of mixed first-view quality.

§81 source follow-up2026-10-09: current gate skips FFT when both current
decimated-window energies are below the unchanged existing floor*search_win.
It jumps to the existing expiry tail, preserving searches increment and bank
expiry traversal; no new threshold/API. This is source intent; gate-owner
byte-identical fixture evidence is recorded in RUN, not new live acceptance.

Two tones are independent test stimuli by role/frequency, not a duplicated scene
or passive-gate success test. Secondary quiet-primary/resumed-primary continuity
requires aligned input/output capture, ledgers and frequency-selective coverage;
hearing a tone or observing backend counters alone does not prove preservation.

Pull mode changes existing §58 accounting application: submitted_bytes and
consumed_bytes both advance by returned prefix bytes in callback; silence_bytes
counts zero-filled unreturned tail (also any nonframe-aligned remaining bytes),
not intentional zero PCM inside the prefix. No ring exists, so queued_bytes,
peak_queued_bytes and clock queued_frames are0. These counters describe callback
handoff, not speaker delivery. Legacy push ring semantics remain unchanged.

Application DSP runs without the player/ring mutex held. Callback owns a
private cumulative ledger and tries ringbuf_mutex without waiting to publish.
Contention skips publication, retaining all private totals for the next success.
Non-callback GetClock may report an older coherent completed snapshot; close
joins callback then flushes exact final totals. ReportTiming copies only
published fields under the mutex and prints outside it. Render duration still
excludes later bookkeeping/publication and is not full-callback timing.

§92 pull accounting refinement: new private ledger is whole-frame based:
silence_bytes=(demand_frames−delivered_frames)*frame_bytes. SDL callback lengths
are expected frame-aligned; any trailing nonframe bytes are zeroed but are not
represented in this whole-frame ledger. Current callback_frames is actual
latest completed callback request, not only initial obtained.samples; compare
startup negotiated values separately. No physical output claim.

## 100. PulseAudio pull output (2026-10-09)

**SRC + TEST (WSLg PulseAudio, silence).** Owner `third_party/ss4s/modules/pulse/pulse.c`, shipped through
`patches/client-deps/ss4s.patch`; fixture `third_party/ss4s/tests/test_audio_pull_pulse.c`. CORRECTED
2026-10-09 (diff-sentinel, verified in source): pulse and alsa tie at weight 10 and ties sort by group name
(`third_party/commons/media/ss4s/modules-list/ss4s_modules.c:228-233`), so `auto` tries ALSA first and takes it
whenever `snd_pcm_open("default")` succeeds (`alsa.c:94-105`) -- most PipeWire/Pulse desktops. PulseAudio is
chosen only where ALSA's default cannot open (e.g. WSLg distros, where the Linux client has been tested). So this
export moves Linux co-op to the pull mixer only when PulseAudio is the selected module; ALSA has no pull export
yet. Not yet run inside Eclipse or live.

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `SS4S_ModuleAudioOpenPullV1_PULSE` | Pull open for the pulse module: async libpulse threaded mainloop; the stream write callback renders exactly the requested bytes into PulseAudio's buffer (`pa_stream_begin_write`), zero-filling a short tail; the underflow callback counts; Close disconnects under the loop lock, then `pa_threaded_mainloop_stop` joins the thread before the final ledger. Feed returns ERROR in pull mode; the push Open/Feed path is unchanged. | Exported by ss4s-pulse.so (`nm -D`). On a Linux co-op start: `[COOP-AUD.pull] callback-owned` plus `[AUDIO-OUTPUT] backend=pulse pull=1 opened`. No export = `SS4S_AudioHasPull` false = legacy path. |
| `PULSE_PULL_TARGET_BLOCKS` | Compile-time 4: requested tlength = 4 client blocks (samplesPerFrame x frame bytes; 240 frames = 20 ms at 48 kHz), minreq = 1 block, `PA_STREAM_ADJUST_LATENCY`. The server may grant different values: WSLg PulseAudio granted tlength 2880 B (15 ms), minreq 960 B, prebuf 1924 B on 2026-10-09. | Read the GRANTED values on the opened line, never the constant. |
| `AUDIO-OUTPUT.backend`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.phase` | `backend=pulse` marks pulse lines (SDL lines carry no backend field); `phase=opened` is logged once after PA_STREAM_READY with a counter snapshot taken under the loop lock, `phase=final` after the mainloop thread is joined. Both carry the SDL §92 ledger fields with SDL meanings (callback_frames = last request in frames; submitted = consumed = pull_frames x frame_bytes; silence = short frames x frame_bytes; cleared/queued/pull_publish_skips = 0) so check_audio_timing.py parses them. | Exactly two pulse lines per open. Schema verified 2026-10-09 by splicing real fixture lines into a run log: no schema error. |
| `AUDIO-OUTPUT.stream_failures`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.context_failures` | Stream/context FAILED or TERMINATED transitions not caused by our own teardown (a `closing` flag is set under the lock first). After one, PulseAudio stops requesting data and render stops silently. | Final line; nonzero = the run lost its output mid-session. |
| `AUDIO-OUTPUT.demand_frames`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.underflows`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.write_errors`, `AUDIO-OUTPUT.max_request_frames` | Pulse ledger: frames requested by the server's write callbacks (= pull_frames + pull_short_frames); stream underflow callbacks (the server played silence); `pa_stream_begin_write`/write failures (a failed write ends that callback, and a granted block is clamped to the request); largest single request in frames. pull_*, callbacks, rate, frame_bytes keep their §92 meanings; elapsed_us = open to close. | Final line only, logged after the mainloop thread is joined. WSLg 2026-10-09: 3 s of silence, 397 callbacks, 144,236 frames, underflows 0, write_errors 0, max request 720 frames. |

## 101. RTP audio queue first-block start (2026-10-09)

**SRC + TEST (offline fixture); not yet run live.** Owner `core/moonlight-common-c/src/RtpAudioQueue.c`
getFecBlockForRtpPacket / `RtpAudioQueue.h` (shipped through `patches/client-deps/moonlight-common-c.patch`);
fixture `tests/coop/test_rtp_audio_queue_start.c`. Applies to every platform's receive path.

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `RTPA_DATA_SHARDS`, `RTPA_FEC_SHARDS` | Upstream constants 4 and 2: data and parity shards per audio FEC block. With 5 ms packets one block = 20 ms of audio. | Not tunable here; read from RtpAudioQueue.h. |
| `RtpAudioQueue.firstPacketSeen`, `RtpAudioQueue.start_policy` | Upstream started every stream on the NEXT FEC boundary, discarding the whole block holding the first packet, and used `oldestRtpBaseSequenceNumber == 0` as its "not started" marker. Now a dedicated `firstPacketSeen` flag marks the start; if the first packet is the FIRST DATA SHARD of its block, that block is kept, otherwise (mid-block start, FEC shard first) upstream's skip is kept. Also removes upstream's wrap case where base+4 wrapped to 0, re-armed the marker and lost two blocks. | Per stream, `[AUDIO-RECEIVE] received_data_packets` minus `[COOP-AUD.decode] packets` should fall from 4 to 0 on a clean start. Measured BEFORE the fix, 2026-10-09 live run recv63df-c: 1004/1000 primary, 6262/6258 secondary -- consistent with this discard (upstream drops the rest of the first received block: 4 packets only when the first packet is shard 0), not proven to be it. |
| `test_rtp_audio_queue_start`, `test_rtp_audio_queue_start.c`, `RTPAQ_SOURCE`, `EXPECT_UPSTREAM_START` | Fixture includes the actual RtpAudioQueue.c (RTPAQ_SOURCE selects another copy). Scenarios: boundary start at 0, mid-block start, non-zero boundary, FEC-first, wrap start at 65532, and a kept first block with a missing shard released after the OOS wait. EXPECT_UPSTREAM_START switches to upstream's expected results. | The fixture defines LC_DEBUG itself (Platform.h sets NDEBUG otherwise, which silently disabled its asserts). 2026-10-09: fixed code passes (ASan/UBSan, -O2, with and without -DLC_DEBUG); upstream code passes upstream expectations (including the two-block wrap loss) and aborts on the fixed ones. |

## 105. Secondary sender catch-up (2026-10-09)

**SRC + offline TEST; not yet run live.** Built from the 2026-10-09 14:46-15:28 TV session (build 7760420a, SKYNET
primary + PC2 secondary), where PC2's sender queue held ~10-60 ms for 25 min and then, after a PC2 delivery burst at
~15:11 (TV ring underran 3 times, sender queue jumped to ~9,400 frames), ~165-200 ms until the session ended, while
`drain_max_msgs` stayed 1 (2 in 33 of 504 windows). Cause (SRC): `coop_audio_fifo_peek` stops at every ready_us change,
so each IPC message carried one decoded packet (240 frames), and the coordinator takes one message per mix call, so the
link could only keep pace and a backlog never reached the ring, whose trim is the only latency bound. Owners
`src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio.c` (secondary_send_main), `coop_audio_fifo.{c,h}`.

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `coop_audio_fifo_peek_run` | Non-consuming copy like `coop_audio_fifo_peek`, but the run continues across packets with different ready_us; it still stops at the first non-consecutive index (a rejected block stays a gap) or flags change (PLC keeps its own message). `*first` = the OLDEST frame's stamp, so downstream ages (ipc/queue/pull age) read up to (run length - one packet) high for the later frames. Used only by the secondary sender, capped at `COOP_AUDIO_MAX_FRAMES` (960 = 4 packets). | `tests/coop/test_coop_audio_fifo_run.c` (run across stamps, index gap, flags change, wrap-around). Live: `COOP-AUD.secondary.msg_frames_max` > 240 in a window where `fifo_now_frames` was > 240, and a burst no longer leaves `fifo_now_frames` standing; the excess now shows as `peer_trimmed_frames` / `[COOP-AUD] trim=` on the primary. |
| `COOP-AUD.secondary.msg_frames_max` | Largest PCM message (frames) the secondary sent in the ~5 s window; 240 = one packet, up to 960. 0 = nothing sent. | Proves the run peek applied: on 7760420a and earlier builds it would always have been <= 240. |

## 106. Passive-gate pitch-twin test (2026-10-09)

**SRC + offline TEST on real session audio; not yet live.** User, listening to the 7760420a session's dialogue scene
through the current gate offline: an audible echo at the end of a marines' "yes sir". Measured (worktree RUN.md
"TWIN"): the two copies were near-identical (1.5-3.5 kHz correlation 0.99) at 385.9 ms (PC1 later), but the bank held
381.0 ms (one ~200 Hz voice period off) and 396.6 ms, and `PG_SAME_MS` kept the true lag out because those were
"working"; PC1's copy leaked only 6-13 dB down at the end of the line. Owner `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c`
(`bank_near`, `twin_match`, `bank_admit`).

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `P_TWIN`, `PGATE_TWIN` | Default 1. A search candidate within `PG_SAME_MS` of an entry that matched in the last 0.3 s (which used to be ignored) gets its OWN slot when its `twin_match` is >= `PG_ADMIT` and beats every entry within `PG_SAME_MS` by `PG_TWIN_MARGIN`; the working entry is never moved (it may be a second source, e.g. music). `bank_near` returns the NEAREST entry when on, the FIRST within tolerance when off. `PGATE_TWIN` is read only in `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING` builds. | Off = bit-identical output to the gate before it (sim, 250 s live2 inputs, `cmp`). On: the "yes sir" line 56.3-57.1 s in `live2` had 0 open PC1 band-channel blocks (was 92), tail leak -30..-47 dB (was -6..-13); 23 splits in 250 s; combat excerpt holes 14.9 s (was 14.5), flips 62.5/s (66.3); truth bench default offsets unchanged; all six `test_coop_audio_pgate_*` identical/PASS. |
| `PG_TWIN_MIN_MS`, `PG_TWIN_WIN_MS`, `PG_TWIN_MARGIN`, `twin_match` | 1 ms (closer = sample refinement, left to drift/fast tracking; pitch twins are >= 2.5 ms apart), 60 ms window, 0.20 margin. `twin_match` = mean mono share (r^2) of bands 2-3 (600-3500 Hz) over the last 60 ms at a signed lag; -1 when either band is unmeasurable. | The 60 ms window fits the band ring (`rlen` >= max_lag + 160 ms). Margin/window are first values, not tuned beyond the one scene. |
| `coop_pgate_stats_t.twin_splits`, `COOP-AUD.ingest.gate_twin_splits` | Count of twin-test admissions (cumulative in the stats; per ~5 s window delta on the ingest line, 0 when the gate was recreated or inactive). | Proves the test fired live: on builds before it the field does not exist; a live session with shared dialogue should show occasional non-zero windows. |

## 107. Passive-gate band incumbency ("music lock") (2026-10-09)

**SRC + offline TEST on real session audio; not yet live.** User, listening to the 7760420a combat excerpt (mission-start
music, louder than usual, plus a sniper scope and plasma pistol): every gated version "is jumping between them leaving
audible distortions each time". Trace 180-187 s: the music's two copies were 476 ms apart (correlation 0.73-0.83 per
half second, then the lag jumped to 372.5 ms at ~184 s); the band-closing side swapped between PC1 and PC2 164 times
per second (12 band-channels), 736 of the 839 immediate swaps by a PREDICTION on the new side, because the conflict
rule kept "this block's better match" and wiped the holder's hold. Owner `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c`
(conflict block and prediction arming in `process_block`).

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `P_STICKY`, `PGATE_STICKY` | Bits, default 3. Bit 1: when both streams want a band and exactly one held it last block, the holder keeps it unless the challenger's match is >= the close threshold while the holder's is < the open threshold for `PG_STICKY_BLOCKS` blocks in a row (then it switches). Bit 2: no prediction is armed on a stream's band while the OTHER stream's `closed_avg` for that band is >= `P_STICKY_BED`. 0 = the conflict rule and arming exactly as before. `PGATE_STICKY` is read only in `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING` builds. | Both 0 (with `PGATE_TWIN=0`) = bit-identical output to the pre-§106 gate (`cmp`, 240 s combat excerpt). On (combat 180-187 s): side swaps 164 -> 5.4 /s, short re-openings of the closing side 72.6 -> 40.4 /s, PC2 closed 2680 -> 947 band-blocks; whole excerpt flips 66.3 -> 44.4 /s, failed predictions 24.6k -> 12.6k, holes 14.5 -> 14.2 s. Cost: truth bench line onset median 10 -> 15 ms, 90th 50 -> 60 ms; echo -20.6 -> -20.5 dB. Dialogue clip 50-80 s: output identical to §106 alone. |
| `PG_STICKY_BLOCKS` | 3 blocks (15 ms) a challenger must win in a row. | A real change of leader (the next line from the other PC) switches within 15 ms. |
| `P_STICKY_BED`, `PGATE_STICKY_BED`, `PG_STICKY_AVG_MS`, `coop_pgate.closed_avg` | 0.3; per stream/channel/band moving average (time constant 300 ms, updated each 5 ms block) of "the band is closed". >= 0.3 = a sustained bed (e.g. music) that predictions on the other stream may not take. | Sweep 0.3/0.45/0.6/0.75/0.9: swaps 5.4/10.3/17.4/26.6/38.3 /s; onset 15/60 ms at 0.3-0.6 (15/50 at 0.75+). A strict "closed within 200 ms" version gave 5.9 /s but onset 20/75 ms (brief false closes blocked real line onsets). |
| `coop_pgate.challenge`, `coop_pgate_stats_t.side_switches`, `coop_pgate_stats_t.sticky_holds`, `COOP-AUD.ingest.gate_side_switches`, `COOP-AUD.ingest.gate_sticky_holds` | Challenge counter per stream/channel/band; conflicts resolved for the challenger (switched) / for the holder (kept); per ~5 s window deltas on the ingest line. | Combat excerpt 240 s: kept 4137, switched 192. Live evidence: sticky_holds non-zero in windows with sustained shared music. |

|---|---|---|---|
| `ECLIPSE_PI_HUB` | ours / SRC + TEST | CMake `option()` in `CMakeLists.txt`, default OFF. When ON it adds compile definition `ECLIPSE_PI_HUB=1` to `moonlight-lib`. When OFF, `coop_pi_scan_begin()` in `src/app/stream/input/coop_pi_pads.c` returns false with `fd = -1` and opens no socket: no `XLINK_PI_QUERY_V1` broadcast to UDP `COOP_PI_PORT` (48212) from the co-op dialog (every 2 s) or Settings → Co-op. `coop_pi_pads_route()` returns false. The pad list then never holds a hub pad, so no hub UI or route path is reached. | TEST: `tests/coop/test_coop_pi_pads.c` built without the flag checks no scan, `fd == -1`, no pads, poll inert and route refused. Built with `-DECLIPSE_PI_HUB=1`, it runs the original routing tests (4 pass). On a built binary, check BEHAVIOUR, not strings: the `XLINK_PI_QUERY_V1` literal stays in a release binary because the probe code still compiles. It can't be sent, though, because the scan sends only after opening a socket and with the flag off it never opens one. TRAP: rig/beta builds must pass `-DECLIPSE_PI_HUB=ON`, or the Pi's controllers silently disappear from the co-op picker. |

## 112. Host Opus encoding of each co-op stream's audio (2026-10-10)

Needed to simulate what the TV's co-op mixer receives (offline gate tests must Opus-code the hosts' audio the same way).
Owners: Umbra/Apollo `src/audio.cpp` (encoder), `src/rtsp.cpp` (SDP parsing); moonlight-common-c `src/SdpGenerator.c`
(what the client asks for).

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `stream_configs`, `config_t::HIGH_QUALITY`, `x-nv-audio.surround.AudioQuality` | Umbra (Apollo) / SRC + LOG | Stereo Opus at 96000 bps normally, 512000 bps when HIGH_QUALITY (`audio.cpp:38-53`); CBR (`OPUS_SET_VBR(0)`), `OPUS_APPLICATION_RESTRICTED_LOWDELAY`, 48 kHz. HIGH_QUALITY comes from the client's `AudioQuality` SDP attribute (`rtsp.cpp:1015`). | Host log line `Opus initialized: 48 kHz, 2 channels, 512 kbps (total), LOWDELAY`: seen on BOTH SKYNET and PC2 for every co-op stream of 2026-10-10 14:20-15:31. |
| `x-nv-aqos.packetDuration`, `AudioPacketDuration` | moonlight-common-c / SRC | 5 ms when the client sends `AudioQuality` 1 (`SdpGenerator.c:529-536`); 5 or 10 ms otherwise by bitrate. Host frame = packetDuration x 48 (`audio.cpp:116`). | Implied by the 512 kbps log line (high-quality branch); not separately logged. |

## 113. Passive gate: played-copy guard, copy level check, hold release (2026-10-10)

**SRC + offline TEST + user listening; not yet live.** Run `audio-long-20261010-1423-live`: the user heard the 1.3.3 gate cut
gunfire and reload sounds ("hollow", "gaps of weak or no noise"). Scored against Halo CE's own sound files (local only,
`~/halo-ce-sounds`, memory `halo-ce-sound-ground-truth`), 38 x 120 s chunks, hosts' audio Opus-coded as in §112: sounds only one
PC played that were cut >= 6 dB 7% -> 3% (238 -> 98 of 3442); shared pairs with one copy removed 63% -> 68%, both copies cut
20 -> 4. User, A/B listening 2026-10-10: this version "was the best", the same as the original where 1.3.3 missed reload shells.
Owner `src/app/stream/audio/coop_audio_pgate.c` (`process_block`).

| name | Owner meaning | Required observable / limit |
|---|---|---|
| `P_PLAYED_GUARD`, `PGATE_PLAYED_GUARD`, `coop_pgate.played`, `coop_pgate.played_tag`, `coop_pgate.played_len` | Default 1. Each output block's final band gains are kept by output block number (record long enough for max lag + 100 ms look-ahead). A match at `lag` is refused (share set to 0) when the other stream's block `lag` earlier was closed in that band/channel: the copy it would be removed for was not played. | Offline: "both copies cut" pairs 20 -> 4 with the other two changes. 0 = the §107/§108 behaviour for this rule. `PGATE_*` read only in `COOP_PGATE_ENV_TUNING` builds. |
| `P_COPY_MAX_DB`, `PGATE_COPY_MAX_DB` | Default 6 dB (0 = off). A match whose least-squares slave/master gain, normalised by the learned balance (`bal_db`), is above this is refused: a much quieter earlier copy (a distant version of a near sound) must not remove the louder later one. Also keeps such matches out of the balance learning. | Offline alone: wrong-removal share 16.7% -> 14.3%, correct removal unchanged (energy metric). |
| `P_HOLD_RELEASE`, `PGATE_HOLD_RELEASE`, `coop_pgate.close_lag` | Default 1. While a band is held closed (`P_HOLD_MS`) after its last match and the current match is below the close threshold, the hold ends when the stream's band energy over the band window in the block about to play exceeds 4x (6 dB) the other stream's at the lag that closed it (`close_lag`, recorded when a match >= the close threshold sets the band's best), plus the -70 dBFS floor. | Offline: with guards on, 80-93% of the remaining wrong removal was hold time; after the release 3-15% of removed energy in the worst chunks. User-heard: reload shells and gun noise kept. |

## 114. Umbra signed-build packaging options (2026-10-10)

These are Umbra build options used by the signed-release workflow (`.github/workflows/release-windows-signed.yml` on Umbra branch `ci/signpath`, 6e9e1273). Owner: the Umbra repository (an Apollo fork). Measured on SKYNET.

| name | owner / class | Meaning and primary source | How to prove application / trap |
|---|---|---|---|
| `UMBRA_PACKAGE_PRESTRIPPED` | Umbra / SRC + TEST | CMake `option()`, default OFF, in Umbra `cmake/packaging/common.cmake`. OFF keeps the inherited `set(CPACK_STRIP_FILES YES)`. ON leaves it unset, so CPack packs the installed binaries as built. It exists for signed builds, which strip first and then sign, because stripping after signing removes the signature. | MEASURED 2026-10-10 by packing a signed stand-in `sunshine.exe` (git.exe, 46920 B, Valid) with `cpack -G ZIP`. OFF: packed file NotSigned, 32256 B. ON: Valid, 46920 B, byte-identical. Per-build proof: the workflow step "Check the packed binaries are still signed" (`Get-AuthenticodeSignature` on the 7 signed files inside the ZIP). TRAP: the CMake cache keeps the last value, so a local build reconfigured with ON and then packed unsigned ships UNSTRIPPED binaries (`sunshine.exe` about 57 MB instead of 35 MB). |
| `UMBRA_GPU_HEVC_DIR` | Umbra / SRC | CMake cache PATH, Umbra `cmake/packaging/windows.cmake` (the GPU HEVC block near the top). The folder must hold `aurora_gpu_hevc8.dll`, `aurora_gpu_hevc10.dll` and `kernels/`, which are installed to `gpu-hevc\` with `COPYING`, `COPYING-GPL-3` and `BUILD-PROVENANCE.json`. Empty: a configure warning, then a FATAL_ERROR at packaging. | Applied only if the package contains `gpu-hevc\*.dll` AND the installed host reports `UmbraCaps` bit 3 (8) in serverinfo. 0.5.0 hosts report 31. The 0.5.0 release used `C:\Users\<user>/umbra-release-deps/gpu-hevc`. The signed workflow uses `_deps/gpu-hevc`, built in CI from Eclipse `experimental/gpu-hevc` @4d73b9e9 with pinned x265 and OpenCL-Headers. A 2026-10-10 dry-run produced the same 6 exports but DLLs about 7 KB smaller, so treat the first CI build as unproven until a co-op test. |
